Absolute Regression
===================

Chapter 251: Now Is the Time for Everyone to Become Stronger.
-------------------------------------------------------------
Nothing changed just because I had become the Young Cult Master.

The path I had to take had already been decided, and so had the things I had to do.

Become stronger and kill Hwa Mugi.

But in reality, there had been changes.

The first change to come upon me was standing in a line in the courtyard of my residence.

I could tell who they were from the emblem engraved on their chests.

A demonic visage painted upon a shield.

“We have come from the Heavenly Demon Hall Guard.”

Now that I had become the Young Cult Master, guard martial artists had been dispatched from the Heavenly Demon Hall.

“I am Jeokyeon, and from now on, I will be responsible for Your Excellency’s safety.”

He wore an eyepatch over one eye. It was rare for a guard martial artist to be one-eyed, so he looked quite extraordinary.

The guard martial artists standing behind him each gave their names and offered their greetings.

There were twelve in all.

They explained that they would protect me in shifts, six by day and six by night.

I knew this Jeokyeon and the other guard martial artists well.

Jeokyeon. He had lived a fate that shone like flame, then vanished.

They had been the guards of my elder brother, who had been the Young Cult Master, and on the day Hwa Mugi attacked, they had all died together with him. Not one of them had fled; they had offered their lives in loyalty.

‘Thank you.’

In my heart, I conveyed my gratitude to them.

“From now on, we will protect Your Excellency.”

That loyalty was now directed toward me. More fates I had to change.

The only problem was that I did not need them.

They would try not to leave my side for even a single moment, but I had many things that required me to move swiftly. Moreover, there would be quite a few actions of mine that they would not be able to understand.

Somehow, I had to make them keep their distance from me.

To think such a task would be given to me on the very first day I became Young Cult Master.

There was no need to say to them, “I have no need of you, so withdraw.” They were not the sort to go back just because I said that, and this was not a problem that could be solved that way.

It was a problem I would have to resolve by meeting Hwi, the commander of the guard and Father’s personal bodyguard. Of course, it would not be easy.

Whether in the orthodox, unorthodox, or demonic factions, the most conservative people were always the guards. If they were not conservative, it would be impossible for them to protect their charge.

The stronger a master was, the more they tended to overestimate their own ability, and protecting martial artists with unruly temperaments was truly difficult. Was I not proof enough of that? I was already thinking of a way to send them back.

“Rest for a while.”

I was about to go meet Hwi and settle things, but Jeokyeon seemed to have read my mind.

“The Guard Commander said this: The Young Cult Master may try to send you back. But there will be nowhere for you to return to. If Your Excellency sends us back, we will have to leave the guard.”

Hwi had anticipated that I would send them back and had made the first move. It meant he understood my personality that well, and it was also an expression of his will that withdrawing my guards was absolutely unacceptable. Whether things went well or poorly, he was telling me to keep them with me.

“Then have you all become completely my people now?”

“Our official affiliation is the Heavenly Demon Hall Guard, but in practice, that is the case.”

I said to Jeokyeon,

“Remove your eyepatch.”

Jeokyeon flinched at my command, but soon removed the eyepatch without hesitation. He had not been wearing it because his eye was injured. The eye that had been hidden was red as blood.

I stepped closer to him, looked into that eye, and said,

“You have trained in the Ghost Eye Art.”

The Ghost Eye Art was an eye technique passed down among guard martial artists for detecting those in hiding. However, the process of training it was extremely painful, and in daily life, one could not use that eye in this way. For the sake of a single moment of crisis that might come once in a lifetime, one had to give up one eye for life.

“Does it hurt?”

“It is bearable.”

“Stay still.”

I placed my palm over his eye. A strand of fine internal energy flowed in and caressed his eyeball.

When I withdrew my hand, Jeokyeon bowed his head.

“Thank you.”

Bewilderment settled on Jeokyeon’s face as he looked at me. He probably had never imagined that I would ease his pain like this.

“It is only a temporary measure.”

Ian had been like that, and now Jeokyeon was as well.

The act of protecting someone demanded far too great a sacrifice. This was likely not a problem unique to our cult, but the burden our cult placed upon its guard martial artists was too harsh.

“I know that if one achieves great mastery of the Ghost Eye Art, one can use that eye even in ordinary times.”

“That is correct. However, achieving great mastery is an exceedingly difficult task.”

“I will help you.”

Jeokyeon’s red eye widened in shock, as if he were looking at a ghost.

“How old are you?”

“I am twenty-nine.”

He was young. The guards who had come with him were either younger than him or around the same age.

“Though I am young, this is my twelfth year since entering the guard.”

That meant he had passed into the guard at seventeen. The Heavenly Demon Hall Guard was not a place one could enter with ordinary skill, so it also meant Jeokyeon’s talent was exceptional.

“The juniors who came with me are young as well, but they are people whose abilities I can vouch for.”

Of course they were. They had been sent to guard the Young Cult Master. Hwi must have sent me the young masters who would bear responsibility for the next generation. Young masters with both skill and loyalty.

Uncle Hwi, you truly chose well.

For these were people who had already proven who they were through death.

“How much do you know about me?”

“We have grasped almost everything that has happened recently.”

“Then you must know my personality to some degree.”

“I have a very slight idea.”

“Tell me.”

Jeokyeon hesitated for a moment over how to answer, then spoke carefully.

“I believe you are not an easy person to serve.”

I nodded and said,

“If you let go of just one thing, you will be able to get along with me better than anyone.”

“What would that be?”

“Haste. I mean you need only let go of your haste to become my guards right this instant.”

Jeokyeon and the other guard martial artists looked puzzled. They had already been assigned as my guards, so what was I talking about? That was what they seemed to wonder.

“I do not understand what you mean.”

Instead of answering, I asked a question.

“What is your ultimate dream?”

“To become the finest guards.”

“Then is your dream to guard the Young Cult Master? Or to guard the Cult Leader?”

After a brief pause, Jeokyeon answered.

“Naturally, my final dream is to become the Cult Leader’s guard.”

Not only him, but the other guards likely had the same goal.

“That is why I mean you must let go of haste.”

They still wore expressions that said they could not understand what intention lay behind my words.

“Bring thirteen wooden swords.”

Realizing that I intended to test their abilities, Jeokyeon immediately had a subordinate bring wooden swords.

A short while later, each of us held a wooden sword. I coated the tip of my wooden sword with white powder.

“Now, think of that friend at the end as me, and protect him from this moment on.”

With the person I had pointed to at the center, they instantly assumed formation.

They were tense.

“There is no need to worry that I might be hurt. So stop me with all you have. Here I come.”

I charged at them.

They tried to stop me no matter what, but how could they stop me when I came at them in earnest?

I unleashed the Wind God Four Steps without reservation and burrowed into their midst. They were pushed aside and pulled off balance, and the swords they swung missed. They gave me openings where no one should have been able to break through, tripped over legs, and for an instant lost sight of me.

When the thought, What in the world is happening? flashed once through each of their minds, I lightly launched myself and landed back in my original spot.

The twelve guards stared at me with blank expressions.

What exactly did you just do?

None of them had dropped their swords. None of them had been struck.

Then one among them was startled.

“Hah!”

Only then were all of them shocked. They were more horrified than if their arms or stomachs had been pierced. At some point, white powder had been smeared over all of their hearts.

It was the same for their leader, Jeokyeon, and the same for the comrade they had been trying to protect. What was even more shocking was that none of them knew when or how it had been applied. In the blink of an eye, they had all died.

“With your current abilities, can you guard me?”

Jeokyeon’s face, as well as the faces of the others, turned red. Apart from the gap in ability, it must have been unbearably humiliating for guard martial artists to be defeated so helplessly.

“I am not trying to humiliate you. I am showing you reality. If you want to protect me, you must be far stronger. Because the people I will face from now on will be masters like this.”

I approached them and met each of their eyes as I spoke.

“You could not stop me today, but next time, you must. Alone, you cannot stop me, but if twelve of you have gathered, you should be able to. Do not waste your time guarding me while I eat or guarding me while I sleep. During the time you would stand such meaningless watch, train in martial arts.”

There was nothing more meaningless than following me around now to guard me.

“Then are you telling us to do nothing but train in martial arts?”

At Jeokyeon’s question, I shook my head.

“You have your position to consider, so that cannot be. For official events, whether internal or external, you should move with me. But when I am going about my daily life or spending time with the Demon Venerables, there is no need to waste time guarding me even then.”

In truth, it was also a heaven-sent opportunity for them. They were people who cut down on sleep to find time to train amid their difficult guard duties.

“The guard’s rules are like this. It goes against principle. From now on, abandon those thoughts. We will establish new rules and principles. If I tell you to withdraw and rest, accept my order without question. If I do not call you separately, that time is training time. Train with the mindset that you will become the greatest guard martial artists in the murim.”

At my unexpected words, the eyes of Jeokyeon and the martial artists wavered.

“I need guards like that.”

I slowly approached Jeokyeon and brushed the white powder from his heart. Beneath it, the emblem of the guard was visible.

“I need this shield drawn on your chests. A shield of ten-thousand-year cold iron that no one can pierce.”

I could feel Jeokyeon’s emotions surge. They were still young. My words moved them. Becoming the finest guard martial artists was surely their dream as well. An opportunity had come to them.

“If you keep your promise with me, I will promise one thing as well. I will take you with me until the end. When I become the Heavenly Demon, my guards will be you.”

Jeokyeon and the guard martial artists were startled. Usually, when one became the Heavenly Demon, the guards were replaced by martial artists who guarded the Heavenly Demon. But now I had declared it. That I would take all of them with me.

“If trouble arises because of this method and you are expelled from the guard, I will take responsibility and accept you. But such a thing will not happen.”

Jeokyeon could not decide whether this was something that would truly work.

“In exchange for giving you a great deal of time, I will check your abilities frequently. Anyone who grows lazy will be sent away immediately. Perhaps this may become even more difficult than guarding me.”

Now was the time for everyone to become stronger. In particular, I had to make my guard martial artists strong no matter what.

Jeokyeon looked around at his subordinates. He was not making the decision arbitrarily, but asking their opinions. I sensed sound transmissions quickly passing back and forth. I liked the way he handled matters.

“I trained in the Ghost Eye Art to see ghosts… but right now, I feel as though I have been possessed by one.”

Are we really allowed to decide something this important so emotionally? With that expression, Jeokyeon added,

“We have decided to follow Your Excellency’s words.”

The guard martial artists behind him shouted in unison.

“We will become stronger. We will entrust our future to Your Excellency.”

I could feel their resolve.

“Become stronger. And in the future, I hope you will become the finest Heavenly Demon Guardian Corps in the murim.”

They would be able to do it. Because I would help them.

And if only to survive, we had to succeed.

“Where are your quarters?”

“They have been prepared nearby.”

“I will have them rebuilt with the finest facilities. With a training ground attached as well.”

All of them wore moved expressions.

“Now then, even so, today is the first day. Shall we all go together?”

* * *

With the guards in tow, I headed for the Heavenly Demon Hall.

The guards following proudly behind me treated the demonic practitioners with cool and dignified gazes, unlike when they dealt with me.

They were men with the authority to cut down anyone if it meant protecting me. During escort duty, they even had the authority to dispense with greetings to the Demon Venerable. They had only been given a harsh lesson by me; they were not men who fell short anywhere else.

As we walked, the demonic practitioners parted like water splitting before us.

Far from daring to block my path, even the way they looked at me had changed completely.

Until now, they had revealed their emotions. Whether their eyes held support or disapproval, I had been able to read their hearts to some extent.

But now that I had become the Young Cult Leader, they did not dare show their feelings before me. When they saw me, they expressed absolute respect and bowed courteously. Whether they liked me or not was irrelevant. All they thought was that being marked by the next Heavenly Demon meant death.

The guards waited at the entrance, and I entered the Heavenly Demon Hall alone.

As always, Father was seated in the grand chair.

I walked down the Path of Blood and stood below the steps.

“May Your Exalted Person be ever hale and strong, may you have been free of all troubles since last we met, and has your precious body been at peace?”

“Why are you doing something you never do?”

“As the Young Cult Leader, I should at least offer this much greeting.”

The corners of Father’s mouth curled upward.

“Do it as you usually do.”

I smiled in good spirits and asked Father,

“Did you and my brother have your fill of bad-mouthing me?”

“We didn’t. Your brother isn’t the sort to curse people behind their backs like someone I know.”

Father, the way you looked after my brother yesterday was truly wonderful.

“I kept my promise up to the part where we came holding hands, so from here on, please help a little too, Father.”

Father gave no answer, but I believed that when it came to my brother, he would help in one way or another.

“You kept singing about being successor, successor, but from now on there will be many things you must do.”

I knew that as well. There were many internal matters to deal with, events I had to attend, and in particular, a whole series of outside functions I would have to attend in Father’s stead.

“This handsome face that takes after you, Father—I’ll show it off to the world a little.”

In truth, what I was relying on was the Sicheon Secret Art. Somehow, I had no choice but to cultivate the Sicheon Secret Art and secure more time.

Father, who had been looking down at me in silence, suddenly asked,

“Are you ready?”

I knew by instinct.

At last, the moment I had so long awaited had come.

“I am ready.”

At my calm answer, Father rose from the grand chair.

“Follow me.”

For that thing more important to me than the position of successor, I followed after Father. Though I was walking slowly, my heart began to pound as if it were racing.

Chapter 252: That Is the Nine Flowers Demonic Art.
--------------------------------------------------
I walked an unfamiliar path, following my father.

The rear of the Heavenly Demon Hall was a path I had never once walked before. Even when I returned to the cult before my regression to gather materials for the great art, I had never come to this place.

When we reached a beautifully tended garden, my father informed me that a formation was laid here.

“Step exactly where I step and follow me.”

The formation spread across the garden was the Nine Springs Evil Spirit Maze Formation. It was a dreadful death formation in which, if one failed to find the correct life gate, one would be chased by evil spirits until the moment of death.

I carefully followed behind, stepping precisely where my father had stepped.

Once we passed through the Nine Springs Evil Spirit Maze Formation, the view before us changed completely. In the place where there had been nothing as we walked through the garden, there now stood a massive building rising like a castle.

More secret than any other place within the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult—this was the Nine Calamities Demon Palace.

My father placed his palm on a small plate beside the door. When he infused internal energy into it, the enormous doors began to open slowly. This was a place that only those who had learned the Nine Calamities Demonic Art could enter.

I stepped into a new world.

The moment I entered, a single sight overwhelmed me.

Directly ahead, a gigantic stone statue in the form of the Heavenly Demon Soul sat in place. It looked as though my father himself were seated upon the grand preceptor’s chair.

And that Heavenly Demon Soul was extending its hand forward. I could instinctively tell. The place atop that palm was the seat meant for the Heavenly Demon.

The moment I saw the Heavenly Demon Soul, a thrilling shudder ran through my entire body. I wanted to climb onto that outstretched hand and stand tall. I was seized by the desire to meet my own Heavenly Demon Soul as soon as possible. Truly, on this day of all days, it was not easy to regain my composure.

Then my father spoke a single sentence that made my heart tremble even more.

“I, too, received the Nine Calamities Demonic Art from my father here.”

Perhaps he was remembering that day, for a memory of the past brushed across my father’s eyes.

‘What kind of person was Grandfather to you, Father?’

My father had never once spoken to me about my grandfather.

‘I have gained so many things to tell my own children about you, Father.’

I slowly looked around.

In that vast place, the past and the present coexisted.

The murals painted on one wall depicted the Demonic Cult at its very beginning. It was frightening, grotesque, and primal, and the paintings and stone statues were so vivid that they seemed as though they might leap out and move at any moment.

Beside them was another depiction of the Demonic Cult. As the years passed, the Demonic Cult had changed. The demonic humans and demons alike were portrayed differently.

“The Nine Calamities Demonic Art has been passed down through countless ages, changing and improving little by little, and at times being lost before being carried on again.”

The Nine Calamities Demonic Art was also recorded in detail upon the walls.

There were records from when the Nine Calamities Demonic Art was first born, and detailed accounts of the era when it consisted of nine techniques.

There was also a time when it was divided into the Nine Calamities Demon Sword Art, the Nine Calamities Demon Saber Art, and the Nine Calamities Demon Fist Art, becoming demonic arts of seven techniques each in the first and second halves. There was even a time when it was lost and buried in darkness.

Every step of the process up until the present, when it had once more become nine techniques, was recorded in detail on the walls.

“The Nine Calamities Demonic Art has continued to change.”

“It could not have been otherwise. In every era, fools and geniuses must have continued to appear.”

As he said that, my father’s gaze returned to the stone statue of the Heavenly Demon Soul.

“Amid all those changes, the only thing that never changed was the Heavenly Demon Soul.”

The techniques changed according to the era, but the Heavenly Demon Soul did not.

It was the ultimate existence that could be summoned only when one achieved great completion of the Nine Calamities Demonic Art.

Before coming here, I had already seen the mental image of the Heavenly Demon Soul several times. In the cave of the Lord of All Things, I had felt as though the Heavenly Demon Soul was staring at me, and I had seen it while enduring the domineering aura of the Martial Alliance Leader.

I could not forget the look in the Heavenly Demon Soul’s eyes as it gazed at me then. A gaze that seemed to find me laughable, seemed to long for me, and seemed to pity me all at once.

“Have you summoned the Heavenly Demon Soul?”

At my question, my father nodded.

It meant he had achieved great completion of the Nine Calamities Demonic Art and could summon the Heavenly Demon Soul.

Father, did you lose to Hwa Mugi even after summoning the Heavenly Demon Soul? Or did you fight without summoning it? Just what kind of battle took place that day?

Even in this moment, it was difficult to imagine defeat from my father’s profile, which looked so unshakably firm. I did not know whether I would one day learn what happened that day or remain ignorant forever, but one thing was certain.

That would never happen again.

“If I achieve great completion of the Nine Calamities Demonic Art, would both you and I be able to summon the Heavenly Demon Soul separately?”

“That is so. It is said that the Heavenly Demon Soul differs slightly depending on the one who summons it. The Heavenly Demon Soul I summon and the Heavenly Demon Soul you summon will be different.”

A Heavenly Demon Soul different from my father’s. Imagining those two Heavenly Demon Souls standing tall and facing each other made me look forward to that moment beyond measure.

“My Heavenly Demon Soul… may be rather talkative.”

A faint smile formed at the corner of my father’s mouth. For him, that reaction was practically roaring with laughter.

“I do not know if I will be able to see it in my lifetime.”

It was a teasing remark, but it also contained the meaning that achieving great completion of the Nine Calamities Demonic Art was just that difficult.

My father lightly launched himself into the air and landed atop the palm of the Heavenly Demon Soul statue.

“Come up here.”

As though I had been waiting, I also launched myself up and stood there.

I reached out and touched the statue’s hand. The feel of cold stone.

At the center of the palm was engraved the symbol of the Heavenly Demon Soul, and it was shaped so that a person could sit there. It seemed my father, too, had received the Nine Calamities Demonic Art here.

My father looked at me and asked again.

“Are you ready?”

I looked at my father and answered.

“I wish to feel the true touch of the Heavenly Demon Soul.”

Then my father’s dignified aura filled the entire hall.

At last, from my father’s mouth came the words I had so desperately longed to hear.

“From this moment, I will transmit the Nine Calamities Demonic Art to you.”

Before a great destiny, my body trembled of its own accord. My heart, which had sunk into calm, began to pound once more.

I sat there with my back straight, in proper posture.

I breathed thinly and long, yet steadily. My breath and consciousness became one, and at last I was ready to accept my fate.

The place atop this palm was no ordinary location. I did not know by what principle, but the movement of internal energy and demonic energy was far smoother here.

“A technique is merely a process aimed at the single result of slaughter, and a movement that optimizes that process. Your achievement will depend on how well you understand this.”

Beginning with the profound principles of martial learning, my father started to recite the oral formula of the Nine Calamities Demonic Art.

With my eyes closed, I began to memorize the formula of the Nine Calamities Demonic Art.

It was more difficult than any martial art I had encountered until now. In most cases, I could at least roughly understand upon hearing it, but the Nine Calamities Demonic Art was composed of entirely new concepts.

I only memorized what my father was saying. Understanding and interpreting its meaning would come later. I focused my entire mind and memorized.

“…Shengjue must be cold, and Shuifen must be hot. If Xiawan is the leaf, Jianli must become the root, and only when chaos becomes order, and thunderous noise becomes silence, shall Zhongwan turn to life and Juque to death. When Zhongting is clear and still, Tanzhong shall…”

Unfathomable formulas continued endlessly. After a long while, the final formula of the Nine Calamities Demonic Art concluded like this.

“…Standing alone and towering above this world, that is the Nine Calamities Demonic Art.”

When my father’s recitation ended, an exclamation escaped me before I knew it. How much I understood or failed to understand was a matter for later. Instinctively, I knew. I knew just how extraordinary the Nine Calamities Demonic Art was. The majesty and dignity of an absolute demonic art became an overwhelming emotion that wrapped around my entire body.

“How much have you memorized?”

“I have memorized the formula itself in its entirety.”

My father was different from usual. Normally, he would have said, Then let us go, and risen from his seat. But my father repeated the formula.

“Now, listen again.”

At least in this moment of teaching the Nine Calamities Demonic Art, he was more serious than ever before. Since the Nine Calamities Demonic Art was transmitted directly rather than through a secret manual as a matter of principle, even a single misremembered or misinterpreted character could endanger one’s life.

After transmitting the formula by repeating it three times, my father told me to recite it.

I began to chant the formula of the Nine Calamities Demonic Art.

When I recited it accurately from beginning to end, my father wore a satisfied expression.

“It is correct.”

I performed a deep bow to my father.

“I am truly so happy. Thank you, Father.”

Emotion welled up within me, and no other words came to mind. I was simply joyful and grateful.

I thought the transmission of the martial art would end like this. I have taught you, so now learn it on your own.

But it did not.

My father transmitted several key principles and essentials of the Nine Calamities Demonic Art to me. He did not teach me the whole of it, but precisely pointed out only the specific parts where I might become confused.

“Have you memorized all of this as well?”

“Yes, I have memorized it.”

“I am telling you this so that you do not wander the path needlessly.”

“Thank you, Father.”

My father sat down on the tip of one of the Heavenly Demon Soul’s fingers. I also sat beside him.

“My father lectured me on the entire essence of the Nine Calamities Demonic Art. From the beginning of the formula to the end. He taught me how to interpret it and in what manner I should train. Even so, it took me a very long time to achieve great completion.”

My father’s gaze turned toward me.

“I will not teach you the entire essence.”

I was not particularly surprised, as it was like my father. But the reason he intended to do so was truly astonishing.

“You, too, must know that even the same martial art can differ greatly depending on how it is interpreted.”

Then he said the most astonishing thing he had said since I met him.

“I want to see the Nine Calamities Demonic Art as interpreted by you.”

“!”

I was startled. Truly startled.

And I could understand. Those words just now meant that my father acknowledged me as a martial artist, one standing before another martial artist. No, it was more than that.

“Show me your Nine Calamities Demonic Art.”

He was saying that he wanted to see a Nine Calamities Demonic Art even more outstanding than the one he had learned. My proud father was saying that now.

“And so, engrave your history in this place!”

My heart surged. In this moment, the joy of being acknowledged by my father was greater than the joy of receiving the Nine Calamities Demonic Art. The tears that had not come then were now about to fall.

Before hearing my answer, my father rose from his seat.

“Let us go.”

It was the moment he returned to being my usual taciturn father.

I did not say that I would show him.

Because I truly would.

Thank you, Father.

I really think I have returned well.

* * *

After leaving the Heavenly Demon Divine Palace, I climbed to the summit of Great Heavenly Mountain.

Standing there alone, I recited the formula of the Nine Calamities Demonic Art again and again. I strove to engrave it into my mind forever.

My life depended on this. Everyone’s lives depended on this.

And I reflected again and again on the key principles and insights my father had taught me. Had I not known these principles, I would have needed far more time to learn the Nine Calamities Demonic Art.

Forgetting even hunger, I immersed myself there solely in the Nine Calamities Demonic Art. My mind was clear and clean, and I felt joy.

There was no me, and no father. In the end, even the Nine Calamities Demonic Art disappeared. In a true state of selflessness, I perfectly engraved the Nine Calamities Demonic Art within me.

* * *

The first place I went after descending Great Heavenly Mountain was the Pungryu Tavern.

When I entered, Jo Chunbae ran out and welcomed me.

“Young Cult Leader! Truly, congratulations! When I heard the news that you had become the Young Cult Leader, I wanted to go into the cult and pay my respects to you.”

Truly, he looked as though he wanted to embrace me.

So I suddenly embraced him. Jo Chunbae was so startled he looked as if he might faint.

“You must not do this to someone as lowly as me.”

“This is the last time you will say such things as ‘lowly.’ It is rude to say such things to a friend, isn’t it?”

At the word friend, Jo Chunbae cried, “Good heavens,” and waved his hands in refusal. What more needed to be said? Jo Chunbae’s wrinkled face was filled with emotion.

“There has never been anyone like you before, Young Cult Leader, and there never will be again.”

“Show me that emotion through your cooking! I’m starving to death!”

I went up to the second floor.

I looked at the wall where Demon Buddha and the Poison King had left their marks before, and beside it, one more person had left something behind.

Blood Heaven, sharing a drink with his disciple before departing.

Astonishingly, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had come with Seo Daeryong and left those words.

It was impressive enough that they had come to drink together, but in the writing he left behind, he had even mentioned his disciple. Thinking of the two of them, I smiled pleasantly.

A little later, Jo Chunbae came up carrying wine and side dishes.

Looking at the words newly left on the wall by the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, he said,

“Senior Blade Demon came by recently and left that for us. It is all thanks to you, Young Cult Leader.”

“It may have been thanks to me at first, but now it is because of our tavern master’s wine and cooking. Do you know how picky those people are?”

As I said that, I tugged at Jo Chunbae’s sleeve.

“Have a drink with me.”

“Yes, I shall offer you a congratulatory cup.”

“Do you know that? This is the first congratulatory cup I’ve received.”

“Good heavens! Should someone as low—no, should I be the one to offer you the first cup?”

“That’s what I’m saying. I thought of the tavern master first, before all the Demon Venerables. Why do you suppose that was?”

Jo Chunbae answered wittily.

“Could it be because you were hungry?”

“To be honest, I did go without food for a few days.”

We laughed together.

I was different from before.

I had gone from Second Young Master to Young Cult Leader, and I had even learned the Nine Calamities Demonic Art.

But I would live an unchanged life.

I would live a life where I shared cups of wine with Jo Chunbae like this.

The Ten-Star Great Completion of the Nine Calamities Demonic Art could likely be achieved in the training hall.

But I do not think the Twelve-Star Great Completion can be achieved in the training hall. I believe the path to the Twelve Stars can be found at a drinking table with Jo Chunbae, in a single line carved on the wall by the Extreme Evil Young Demon, and on a moonlit mountain path walked with Ian.

My absolute principle regarding martial learning, unchanged as ever.

Only when the person changes can the martial art change.

And I know something else as well.

That the demonic path that does not break the table is ten times harder than the demonic path that does. Jo Chunbae is the milestone of the demonic path I must protect. When laughter disappears from his face, my demonic path will be walking the wrong road.

And so, this is enough for my celebration. A grand one, with just the two of us.

“Now, let us have a drink, tavern master!”

Chapter 253: The Thing Harder Than Embracing.
---------------------------------------------
There were other things that changed once I became the Young Cult Leader.

I was moved to new quarters deep within the inner compound. The house was large, the training ground was large, and there were even gardens and walking paths around it. It was a residence incomparably better than my old one. The number of maids attending to me increased greatly as well.

The first thing I did in my new quarters was change the interior to suit my tastes.

In truth, even before my regression, I had camped so often that I could sleep well anywhere. But now, where I slept had become important.

I would be busy as the Young Cult Leader, and I would be busy with training. That was precisely why this space was all the more precious. It would be my place of rest amid all that busyness.

I replaced the bed with a large one and spread soft, cozy, pure-white bedding over it.

By the sunlit window, I placed a desk. I also put in a chair where I could sit comfortably and read.

The bookshelf was filled with books I had bought on the recommendation of the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. My goal was to read them all within the year, though I did not know if I would even manage half.

I pulled out a book and sat in the chair by the window.

Then I took the book and threw myself onto the bed. I rolled around on the fluffy, pleasantly soft white blanket.

“Yes, this is it.”

Then I lay there and read.

Did reading poetry anthologies help with martial training? Of course it did. Up to a certain level, focusing solely on martial arts would be helpful, but once one reached my level…

Thump!

“Ow!”

I had dozed off, and the book fell on my face. Yes, I could dodge the sword of an expert, but not this.

I lay on the bed for a while and savored the brief peace. Once I left the bed, I would have to train like mad and run forward again. That was why I was truly grateful for this moment. Life became happier when one was grateful for the things taken for granted.

My quarters were not the only thing that changed.

As promised to my guards, I had their old, cramped lodgings torn down and built them a large, spacious residence with a training ground attached. When all the guards joined in to help the workers, the lodging was completed in no time.

I also made sure the beds and furniture inside were of the best quality possible. Since I took care of even those details, it was only natural that everyone was pleased.

“Thank you very much.”

Jeok Yeon, their captain, greeted me on behalf of them all. By keeping my promise, I was introducing myself to them as the kind of person I was.

“I’ll be devoting myself to training for the time being, so don’t come looking for me unless it’s important.”

With that, I entered my personal training ground.

I had two training grounds in total: a large outdoor one, and a personal indoor training ground that was smaller than the outside one.

I trained in the indoor training ground.

It was narrower than the outside one, but it was larger than any other indoor training ground. It was probably the largest and most finely appointed after Father’s training ground.

As the saying went, one could know the whole from a single part—the eighteen types of weapons lined up against the wall were far too fine in quality to be mere training weapons.

The walls, ceiling, and floor had also been built sturdy enough not to collapse under any impact. Truly, everything changed once I became the Young Cult Leader.

Yes, new wine in new wineskins, and new martial arts in a new training ground!

My heart trembled as I prepared to truly begin training in the Nine Transformations Demonic Art.

An absolute demonic art against which no evil energy or demonic energy could prevail.

At last, the training of the Nine Transformations Demonic Art I had longed for even in my dreams had begun.

The Nine Transformations Demonic Art consisted of nine forms in total.

Six were forms used by a person, and three were forms used by the Heavenly Demon Soul. Even if one mastered only the forms used by a person, it was possible to reach great completion.

However, in that case, the Heavenly Demon Soul would be unable to unleash the forms.

The Heavenly Demon Soul was powerful in and of itself, but it was even stronger when using forms.

Imagine something as enormous as a demon king executing forms as swiftly as a human attacking!

That was why I had to master all nine forms perfectly and achieve great completion.

The Nine Transformations Demonic Art was a completely different martial art from the Flying Heaven Sword Art. If the Flying Heaven Sword Art was swordsmanship, then the Nine Transformations Demonic Art was, quite literally, a demonic art.

Each and every form consumed an immense amount of internal energy, and naturally, its power could not be compared.

I began training calmly, starting with the mental cultivation method.

This was the most important moment in the training of the Nine Transformations Demonic Art.

If I made even the slightest mistake here, I could fall into qi deviation, so I moved my true qi slowly and cautiously.

This was the first time in my life I had ever practiced a mental cultivation method with such care and concentration.

Fortunately, I succeeded in circulating my true qi through one heavenly cycle according to the口诀. If not for the key principles Father had taught me, I would have been blocked from the mental cultivation method itself.

I did not yet train the forms. First, I would wait until I could circulate the mental cultivation method comfortably. Only then would I begin training in earnest.

I circulated my true qi once, twice, and then three times.

After continuing to circulate my energy like that, I came to my senses and realized that three full double-hours had already flown by. I had been so absorbed in the mental cultivation method of the Nine Transformations Demonic Art that I had not even noticed my hunger.

Each time I circulated my energy, the internal energy in my dantian reacted differently. I felt my pure, majestic internal energy slowly beginning to heat up.

I was glad to have found a martial art I could immerse myself in like this, yet at the same time, I felt fear. I sensed that the road ahead would be very long.

“I’m hungry, so let’s eat first.”

Today, I cooked for myself.

I chopped firewood, lit a fire, and prepared the meat.

I could not simply make do with a quick meal and hurry back to training. The more important the training, the more I had to eat well, rest well, and strive to maintain my body and mind in the best possible condition.

I grilled plenty of meat and called the guards over to eat with me.

They were surprised that I had cooked the food myself, and surprised again that I had called them to share it with me. They were even more surprised by the fact that the dishes were actually edible.

“I’ll cook for you again next time. Look forward to it.”

Before they returned, I called Jeok Yeon, their captain, aside.

“How is the training?”

“Everyone is doing their best.”

“I’ll take a look at you all soon, so tell them to work even harder.”

“They will all be pleased.”

“Wait. Let me see your eye.”

“It is all right.”

“I’m not all right with it.”

Jeok Yeon removed his eyepatch.

It was redder than when I had seen it before. The pain must have been considerable.

“If you keep enduring pain, your personality will turn nasty.”

I placed my palm over his eye and injected internal energy to treat it. I could have taught him the method, but with his current martial level, it would be difficult for him to achieve this effect. Since it was the eye, it had to be handled with extreme delicacy.

“It feels even more comfortable than when you treated it before.”

Perhaps that was true. There might have been some difference I was unaware of between before and after I learned the Nine Transformations Demonic Art.

Jeok Yeon bowed his head.

“Thank you, Young Cult Leader.”

To me, it was no great technique, but to him, it was not so.

“I always used to say this to the former guard I drove out. That building affection with one’s guards is just earning points so they’ll die in your place. That it’s a despicable thing to do.”

I said it because I was curious what he would answer.

Jeok Yeon covered his eye with the eyepatch again and said,

“Whether there is affection or not, I merely fulfill my duty.”

It sounded cold, but I could tell. Rather than protecting someone because he liked them, he liked the version of himself that protected someone. Yes, that was right. Ian had been the unusual one.

“The meat was delicious.”

Jeok Yeon bowed and turned away.

He seemed blunt and devoted to his work, but I still did not know what kind of person he truly was.

When looking at someone, one had to look closely. Closely enough to even remove their eyepatch.

* * *

In the evening, I trained in the Sicheon Secret Art.

The problem was the Sicheon Secret Art. With the Nine Transformations Demonic Art, I felt as though I was somehow taking one step after another, but the Sicheon Secret Art was nothing but darkness.

I tried this and tried that. I kept attempting all sorts of methods. If even I found it this difficult, it was understandable that the Cult Leader of the Wind Heaven Sect would shake his head and say it was absolutely impossible.

All I needed was for someone to say, “Ah, that’s this!” Just that one sentence. Then again, it was probably because no one had ever heard that one sentence that no one had succeeded until now.

Still, I did not give up. In order to bring the Nine Transformations Demonic Art to the twelfth star and achieve great completion, I absolutely had to learn the Sicheon Secret Art. And to a level where there would be a clear difference in time.

“Ugh, I don’t know either!”

Snap!

I snapped my fingers and opened the space I liked.

A transparent sea and a pure-white sandy beach, a blue sky and a broad-leaved tree, a comfortable chair beneath it, and even a crab crawling beside me.

“Good. This is good!”

I flopped down there. I did not know why I liked this place so much.

When I lay here and closed my eyes, I felt so good. The gently blowing breeze lulled me to sleep.

Still, what gave me hope was that ever since I learned the Nine Transformations Demonic Art, the utter impenetrability of the Sicheon Secret Art felt, in practical terms, a little easier.

Jeok Yeon saying his eye felt more comfortable was the same. Was the Nine Transformations Demonic Art having some effect without my knowing?

* * *

Jeok Yeon came to Geom Mugeuk’s quarters.

“Young Cult Leader!”

Geom Mugeuk’s reply came from within the training ground.

“Come in.”

When Jeok Yeon entered the training ground, Geom Mugeuk was nowhere to be seen. Instead, incense was burning at the center of the training ground.

‘I’m certain I heard his voice from inside.’

Just then, Geom Mugeuk suddenly appeared behind him. He was standing there holding a stick of incense.

“Where were you?”

He wondered if Geom Mugeuk had been training in stealth techniques, but Geom Mugeuk gave an incomprehensible answer.

“I had hoped I was in a different time from you.”

“Pardon?”

Geom Mugeuk walked over and compared the incense in the training ground with the incense he was holding. Seeing that they were the same length, he let out a sigh. The Sicheon Secret Art was a succession of failures.

“What is it?”

Jeok Yeon, who had been staring at the incense in puzzlement, quickly reported.

“We have received word from the Valley of Villains. The Cheonhwa Pavilion Master is leaving today.”

* * *

When I arrived at the Valley of Villains, the Faceless Guests who would be escorting the Cheonhwa Pavilion Master were preparing the carriage in the yard.

“I hope I have not summoned you when you were busy.”

“Since the Pavilion Master is leaving, of course I had to come and see you off.”

The Cheonhwa Pavilion Master had not yet come out, and only the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon was in the yard first.

“Are you not sad to see her go?”

“I am.”

I was startled. I had expected an answer like, “Now that her body has recovered, we should each live our own lives,” from the Lesser Demon.

“Then why send her away? You should tell her to stay longer.”

“She is a woman with great dreams. I believe the best thing I can do for her is watch over her from one step away.”

I had not expected the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon to think that way.

“You truly are remarkable.”

Just as the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon was about to say something, the Cheonhwa Pavilion Master emerged from the building.

Before leaving, she had put on beautiful makeup and worn lovely clothes, wanting to show the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon her best appearance one last time.

“I have been greatly indebted to you all.”

As she said farewell, I spoke with a regretful expression.

“Because you were here, Pavilion Master, our gloomy sect shone brightly. Now it will become a world of darkness again.”

At my words, the Cheonhwa Pavilion Master smiled broadly.

“Even though I know that isn’t true, it still makes me feel good. I suppose I really am getting older.”

“Stay and enjoy yourself a little longer.”

She glanced at the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon and said,

“Brother has fully recovered, and he must be busy with work. It is time for me to go.”

Her eyes were waiting for the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon’s answer.

This was another test!

And the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon avoided the many wrong answers and chose the correct one.

“Stay longer. The work can be postponed.”

A smile appeared on the Cheonhwa Pavilion Master’s face.

“If you had said you were busy, I would have unpacked my luggage again.”

She gazed at the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon, conveying the sorrow of parting.

The two looked at each other without a word.

That was why the farewell was all the more poignant.

She said farewell to me as well.

“Second Young Master, you will become an excellent Young Cult Leader.”

“Can you perhaps see my future as well?”

She shook her head.

“It is not because the future is too terrible to speak of, is it?”

“If that were the case, I would tell you all the more. You are my benefactor, after all.”

The moment I saved the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon, I became her benefactor.

“I cannot see the futures of those who bear a destiny too great. Young Cult Leader, you possess a destiny I cannot handle.”

She added one final thing.

“Please take good care of our brother.”

As always, I answered with the same greeting.

“That is what I should be saying to you!”

And so the Cheonhwa Pavilion Master left.

Since I had come all this way, I did not want to leave immediately, so I walked through the Valley of Villains with the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon.

I complained to him.

“My martial training has been completely blocked lately.”

“If even you are blocked, Young Cult Leader, it must be an extraordinary martial art.”

He probably thought I meant the Nine Transformations Demonic Art, but the problem was the Sicheon Secret Art.

After walking in silence for a while, the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon suddenly brought up the Cheonhwa Pavilion Master.

“Earlier, you said my feelings for the Cheonhwa Pavilion Master were remarkable.”

“Because watching over someone with such feelings is no easy task.”

“It is not that difficult either. We are not clinging to each other.”

We naturally stopped walking. I could not understand what the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon meant.

“When I first met her, it was for my own benefit. I wanted to obtain information through the courtesan houses. It is the same even now. If she takes control of every courtesan house under heaven, then all that information will become mine. I have a selfish purpose.”

“If you look at it that way, the Pavilion Master is the same.”

It was something she could not easily accomplish without the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon’s backing.

In that sense, perhaps the two of them had an ideal relationship. They had feelings for each other, while also having clear business goals they each desired.

“The Cheonhwa Pavilion Master and I stand one step apart. That is why we can see each other better than when we are clinging to one another.”

The Extreme Evil Lesser Demon looked at me with clear eyes.

“Young Cult Leader, try taking just one step away from that martial art as well. Just one step.”

The moment I heard those words, I realized it. Until now, in my impatience, I had been clinging to the Sicheon Secret Art. I had believed I could only obtain it by embracing it so tightly I could barely breathe.

“Taking one step away may be more difficult than clinging to it.”

At that moment, he and I were standing one step apart, facing each other. Perhaps that was why I could see him so well. Not only the clear eyes behind the mask, but even the Lesser Demon’s desire to help me however he could.

Filling my expression with gratitude, I said to him,

“You see? I told you it is me you should be asking her to take care of.”

Chapter 254: Yesterday Is Like Today.
-------------------------------------
I decided to actively take the Wicked Little Demon’s advice.

Take a step back from the Sicheon Secret Art.

Of course, it was not as easy as he made it sound. We were martial artists, people who had always run forward.

What I chose first was to reduce the time I spent training the Sicheon Secret Art and use that time to cultivate the Ninefold Demon Art.

Right. Now wasn’t the time for me to be worrying about some other martial art.

With that thought, I focused on the Ninefold Demon Art.

Honestly, if not for the Sicheon Secret Art, I would have wanted to spend the entire day practicing only the Ninefold Demon Art.

The Ninefold Demon Art was truly abstruse and high-level, and precisely because it was difficult, it possessed a charm that drew people in.

Even its inner cultivation method alone could not be compared to other martial arts. The internal energy gathered through its cultivation was far greater than that of existing methods.

If Father had been in front of me, I definitely would have made this joke.

—You mean to say you were practicing such an excellent martial art all by yourself!

I became completely immersed in training the Ninefold Demon Art.

For now, rather than practicing its forms, I tried to make my body completely accustomed to the breathing method of the Ninefold Demon Art, and even that alone was enjoyable.

Of course, that did not mean I had completely turned my attention away from the Sicheon Secret Art. The more I practiced the Ninefold Demon Art, the more I realized I needed the Sicheon Secret Art.

And I realized something else as well.

That reducing my training time was not the answer to taking one step back.

This method was wrong.

* * *

When I arrived at the residence of Nam Dojong, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, he was reading a book.

I stood some distance away and watched him.

Strangely, whenever I saw the Blood Heaven Blade Demon sitting by the window reading, my heart grew calm. I wanted to read books but could not read many, so was I perhaps getting vicarious satisfaction from it?

Just then, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s sound transmission flew into my ears.

—Stop peeping and come here.

—You send your transmissions quite far. Don’t tell me you can use Thousand-Li Sound Transmission too?

—Look who’s talking!

I walked over to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“Aren’t you busy? This is the first time I’ve seen such an idle Young Cult Leader.”

“I am busy.”

“And yet?”

“No matter how busy I am, isn’t this where I belong? Right beside our elder!”

I leaned against the window frame with my arm on it.

“To be honest, I’m rather free these days.”

“If you are the Young Cult Leader, you should find work to do.”

“I don’t want to. Soon enough, everything will come crashing down all at once. I’m just going to rest while I can.”

“Then rest. Why did you come here?”

“Because being with you puts my heart most at ease. I came to rest.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon shook his head, but he was clearly pleased.

As I entered the room, I made another pointless joke.

“Ah, how nice! How is it that even at your age, you still smell this good?”

“No need to feed a fish you’ve already caught.”

“I’m telling you, this isn’t flattery. I mean it.”

I looked over the books on the shelves. Among them, I committed the ones that looked interesting to memory. I planned to buy them later and place them on my own bookshelf. If I bought them, I would read them someday.

“Have you read all of these books?”

“I don’t buy them and use them as decorations like someone I know.”

“Someone once said that reading begins with buying the book.”

“You like looking at books lined up more than actually reading them, don’t you?”

“I will read them all someday! I’ll read even more than you, Elder.”

“Do you think that’s all I’ve read?”

Startled, I looked at the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“No, but you are a Demon Venerable in name and deed. If you open a secret warehouse and find books piled up where weapons or miraculous elixirs should be, what are we supposed to do?”

“If a sword flies at me, I’ll try blocking it with a book.”

At first, it had seemed completely unfitting, but now he looked more natural holding a book than the Heaven-Destroying Great Saber.

“There’s tea over there that I just brewed. Drink it if you want.”

“I was just desperately craving a cup of tea.”

I sat at the tea table and drank.

“It’s the same tea, so why does tea brewed by you taste better, Elder?”

“That’s because you want to make an old man serve you.”

Pretending I had not heard him, I stroked the tea table.

“I should put one exactly like this in my room too.”

Then the Blood Heaven Blade Demon suddenly asked,

“Do you have something troubling you?”

As expected, he knew me better than anyone. I fiddled with my teacup and asked him,

“Have you ever taken a step back?”

At my question, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s gaze left his book and turned to me.

“From what?”

“From the thing you wanted most, Elder.”

He seemed to consider the intent behind my question for a moment, then soon answered firmly.

“No.”

His expression seemed to say that his life had only ever gone straight ahead.

Of course, to someone who did not know him, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon did look like that sort of person. That stiff old man certainly did not look like someone who would ever step back.

But he had already stepped back several times.

By accepting me.

If anything, I was the one who had charged straight ahead. To open the door to his heart, I had advanced straight toward the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, and by accepting me, he had stepped back for me.

If he too had only charged straight at me, our relationship would have been different.

“Why are you suddenly asking that?”

“I was wondering how one could take just one step back.”

Having gone through all manner of hardships, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon would be able to guess what I meant.

“Among the books you’ve read, is there perhaps one titled How to Take Only One Step Back?”

“As if such a thing would exist.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s gaze was about to return to the book he had been reading, but it came back to me. He was curious after all.

“Seeing a clever child like you worry about it, it seems the object of your concern is no ordinary thing.”

“It is a martial art.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon would think that martial art was the Ninefold Demon Art. After all, it was a concern that had arisen after I became the Young Cult Leader.

“But why are you trying to step back?”

“It’s so abstruse and difficult that I’ve hit a wall. I want to look at the martial art from a little distance. I also wonder if it isn’t working out precisely because I want it too desperately.”

“Is it not that you are less desperate?”

“That’s probably not it. If nothing else, I’m confident in my effort.”

After a short pause, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon asked,

“But trying to step back from that martial art in order to find a method like this… isn’t that actually bringing you even closer to that martial art?”

“!”

In that instant, a certain realization flashed through my mind.

Unable to restrain myself, I sprang to my feet.

“That’s right! I wasn’t stepping back. I was actually clinging to it even more fiercely.”

I ran toward the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“If I’m going to embrace something, it should be you, Elder! Elder!”

“Don’t come near me, it’s disgusting!”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon used spatial grasping to push me back and seat me at the tea table again.

Then, deliberately lowering his voice, he said,

“Young Cult Leader, please maintain your dignity.”

I noisily gulped down the remaining tea and answered.

“No, I’m going to throw away even more of it. I’ve found the answer.”

* * *

“The prodigal son has returned! I have rejoined you!”

The three members of the late-night training gathering stared at me.

I had been too busy to attend at all, and then today I had suddenly shown up.

Ian said to me,

“You still intend to hold the late-night gathering even after becoming the Young Cult Leader?”

“Yeah. I’m going to keep doing it even when I become the Cult Leader.”

“We would hate that.”

Thanks to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, I had realized something.

To take one step back from the Sicheon Secret Art, I had reduced the time I spent practicing martial arts, and I had also tried to find other methods.

And now the conclusion I had reached was simply to do things as I had before.

Like before I learned the Ninefold Demon Art or the Sicheon Secret Art.

Back then too, I had trained diligently in martial arts. The difference from now was that there had been no burden in my training. I had vigorously clashed with the Demon Venerables and saved scraps of time to train. It was something I naturally did, and there was no burden of thinking I had to accomplish this by some set time.

Even in that process, I had continued to grow stronger and improve.

I had to step back. Not from martial arts, but from the burden. From impatience. If I simply lived frantically as I had before, then at some moment, it would come to fruition.

“Please bestow your teachings upon me after so long.”

As if to say we should do this properly, the Fist Demon took off his upper garment. A terrifying face and a perfectly muscled body. It had truly been a long time since I had seen him like this.

I took off my upper garment as well. Compared to him, mine were baby hands and a baby body.

I felt sorry to the Fist Demon that I had neglected fist arts training all this time. After all, I had pestered him so much to let me become his disciple.

Together with the Fist Demon, I performed the Thunderbolt Asura Fist. Normally, with my worries over the Sicheon Secret Art, I would not even have thought of practicing the Fist Demon’s martial art.

Ian and Cheon Sohui also devoted themselves to training beside us. Ian’s skill had improved yet again in the meantime. I could tell how much she was dedicating herself to training just by watching her for this brief moment.

When our eyes met for a moment while we were performing our forms, I smiled at Ian. She smiled faintly as well.

Cheon Sohui looked at the Fist Demon with an expression that said, “Did you just see them rubbing salt in a maiden’s wounds, Lord Fist Demon?” then startled herself. She was shocked that she had unconsciously conveyed such a sentiment to the formidable Fist Demon.

The Fist Demon pretended not to notice and continued training.

And as we trained like that—

Kwarrrung!

The sound of thunder came from the fist I had thrust out.

Not only I, but Ian and Cheon Sohui, who had been training beside me, were startled as well.

“Was that sound… from me?”

“Yes, it came from your fist.”

Everyone was surprised, but I was the most surprised of all.

“What happened? To be honest, I’ve been neglecting fist arts lately. Today is the first time in a while I’ve even performed the fist art. Why in the world did thunder sound?”

The Fist Demon wore an expression that said, How would I know? Then he silently continued training.

Had I become stronger in the meantime? Or was it because, without realizing it, I had taken a step away from fist arts?

One thing was certain.

After learning the Ninefold Demon Art, my martial arts had begun to surge.

Kwarrrrung!

This time, thunder sounded from the Fist Demon’s fist. It was a louder thunder than the sound from my fist.

“No, why are you making the sound now after not doing it all this time? You never did it when we trained before!”

At my protest, the Fist Demon pretended not to hear and continued training.

“No, why did you have to do it on the very day I made thunder for the first time? Could it be that you’re jealous—”

Kwarrrung!

My words were buried beneath the Fist Demon’s thunder.

“This is going too far—”

Kwarrrrrrrrung!

* * *

As I headed back to my residence with a tired body, someone suddenly came to mind, so I turned my steps.

Someone who seemed likely to still be awake even at this late hour. As expected, he was awake.

“What are the stars saying to you, Brother?”

Then the Drunken Demon, who had set a boat afloat on the lake and was looking up at the night sky, answered.

“The stars are saying this: You have the best lot in life in all the martial world!”

The Drunken Demon turned to look at me and smiled warmly.

“You came?”

Standing by the lakeside, I crossed the water in an instant using the Water-Treading Step and climbed onto the boat.

“Now you can show off your skills as much as you want.”

On the day I was chosen as successor, I had displayed my aura before all the demonic practitioners. Now that I had even become the Young Cult Leader, there was no reason to hide my abilities.

“I was never the sort to hide things in the first place. I’m the type who likes stepping forward and receiving attention.”

“To be honest, so am I.”

“Do you think we became sworn brothers for nothing? Next time, let’s go to the Central Plains and get some attention!”

“I know lots of good places. Let’s go!”

Could he possibly know as many as I did? I would have to share a drink with him somewhere with good wine and fine scenery.

We filled each other’s cups to the brim.

“Congratulations on becoming the Young Cult Leader.”

“Your help was great, Brother. Thank you.”

“About the fifth greatest, maybe?”

“You’re drunk, Brother. Seeing you belittle yourself again.”

After toasting together, we emptied our cups.

“Ah, that’s good!”

“As expected! Wine must be drunk with my elder brother.”

It felt so good to drink with him after so long.

We exchanged drinks and talked about this and that.

I also spoke comfortably to him about how hard it was, being stuck in martial arts.

The solution was just like the Drunken Demon.

“Drink! The answer is in wine. Once you’re drunk, everything will work itself out.”

“You really are the one with the best lot in the world, Brother.”

“Drink. There is life in wine, and there is the jianghu too. Everything is in it.”

We drank again. Drinking with the Drunken Demon after so long, the wine went down like water.

The Drunken Demon talked about all sorts of things. He gossiped about the Demon Venerables, talked about women he had met when he was young, and spoke of martial arts.

It seemed as if he was drunk and speaking recklessly, but he never crossed the line. That was why I always had this thought: the moment the Drunken Demon made a mistake was when he was sober.

Splash!

In the end, the Drunken Demon jumped into the water, and I jumped in with him.

After swimming here and there across the lake, we floated on our backs, looking up at the night sky.

“This place is still nice.”

“Playing at being immortals here makes it feel as though time has stopped. One shichen ago feels like now, and now feels like one shichen from now. Yesterday feels like today, and today feels like tomorrow.”

Brother. That time—I have to stop it.

Perhaps because he had spoken of time, I thought of the Sicheon Secret Art.

Over the night sky, the meridians within my body rose up, overlapping it. I had tried every method until now. Sending true qi from this meridian to that one, then from here to there. There were still far too many possibilities left to attempt.

‘Stop it. Why think about that at a moment this good?’

I was here playing around with the Drunken Demon precisely to take a step back from the Sicheon Secret Art.

Splash!

I dived back down into the water.

But the very next moment, I lifted my head above the surface again.

Once more, I imagined my meridians over the stars and overlaid them.

Not here, not there. Where was it earlier?

Then there was a place where the meridian I recalled and the star fit together exactly.

‘I found it!’

A shudder ran through my entire body.

Was it mere coincidence? Or?

What if I circulated my true qi in the order of those constellations? It felt as though something might happen.

Could it be that the Sicheon Secret Art had been created by observing the constellations in the heavens?

“Brother!”

“What is it?”

“I think I solved that problem I mentioned earlier.”

The Drunken Demon was startled. Then he burst into hearty laughter, delighted.

“I told you, didn’t I? Drink, and everything gets solved!”

“I’ll go ahead! Brother, thank you. Let’s talk again later.”

From within the water, I shot straight up into the air.

Changing direction in midair, I flung myself toward the lakeshore.

The Drunken Demon’s voice came from behind me.

“This brother of yours is always here.”

Starlight was pouring down over the Drunken Demon as he floated upon the water.

* * *

After returning to the training ground, I prepared two sticks of incense.

I lit both sticks at the same time, placed one in the incense burner, and then unleashed the Space-Time Displacement Art.

The space that was created was an empty one, containing nothing at all, in order to heighten concentration and minimize the internal energy consumed.

And there, I recited the formula of the Sicheon Secret Art. Unlike before, I moved my true qi in the order I had realized earlier from looking at the constellations.

Had it worked?

For now, I could not tell whether it had worked or not.

At my current level, I would only know once I left this place and compared the time. Even the slightest difference would be enough. As long as the Sicheon Secret Art had begun!

Please!

It was my desperate wish to achieve the twelfth-star grand completion of the Nine Flames Demonic Art, no matter what.

When the incense had burned down to a certain point, I released the Space-Time Displacement Art.

Sssssss.

The world of the Space-Time Displacement Art vanished, and after emerging from within it, I slowly walked toward the incense I had lit.

I crouched in front of the incense burner and inserted the incense stick to the same depth.

My heart trembled as much as the gaze with which I stared blankly at it.

The two sticks of incense had burned down to different lengths.

Chapter 255: You Decide
-----------------------
“I did it!”

I cheered. I was truly happy. So happy that I hopped up and down right there on the spot.

Finally, I had taken the first step in the Time-Differential Secret Art.

Though the difference in time was not yet significant, the fact that I had accomplished it held meaning.

If I continued to refine the Time-Differential Secret Art and could double the time difference? I would be able to have twice as much training time as others. Three times, four times… What would happen if the Time-Differential Secret Art reached its pinnacle?

The important thing was internal energy. The greater the time difference, the more internal energy it would require.

Gather more internal energy, and raise the realm of the Time-Differential Secret Art even higher.

It all depended on my effort alone.

* * *

The next day, I gathered all the money remaining on the battlefield.

I even sold all the remaining night-glow pearls.

The place I headed to with that money was the Thousand Poison Forest.

“Greetings, Young Cult Leader.”

Today, Sangseon personally came out to guide me. It seemed he had come out to greet me, now that I had become the Young Cult Leader.

“You may treat me as usual.”

“That would not be possible.”

And it wasn't just him. The poison folk I passed by also greeted me with polite bows, unlike before.

“It was truly a wise decision not to engage in fraternal strife with the Eldest Young Master.”

“Thank you for saying that.”

Arriving at the Poison King’s residence in such a manner, Sangseon withdrew.

The Poison King was not at home. Looking around, I found him refining his poison arts in the nearby forest.

The moment I saw that sight, a smile formed at my lips.

Two venomous snakes were coiled nearby, watching the Poison King train in his poison arts. They were the very snakes the Poison King had named long ago.

Black smoke puffed from the Poison King’s fingertips, forming a shape. Surprisingly, the shape the smoke formed was a rabbit.

As the rabbit-shaped smoke flew forward, the snakes chased after it. They looked just like excited puppies running after a ball their master had thrown.

When the snakes pounced on the rabbit, the black smoke vanished with a pop.

New smoke rose from the Poison King’s fingertips. This time, a butterfly fluttered by. Again, the snakes eagerly chased the butterfly, crawling with a speed and agility no ordinary serpents could compare to. Again, the smoke vanished.

The snakes raised their heads and flicked their tongues, as if wondering what he would send next.

Then, a massive burst of smoke flew from the Poison King’s hand. As the tiger-shaped smoke charged at the snakes, they fled into the forest in surprise.

I thought he had chased the snakes away because he knew I had come.

That was not the case. The Poison King paced around, deep in thought. Even when training, he existed in his own world.

Then he saw me and started in surprise.

“When did you get here?”

“Since the rabbit was hopping. Surely that wasn’t real poison?”

“If it were, the kids would all die.”

It meant that if he had used his extreme poison, even spiritual venomous snakes couldn’t endure it.

“It’s harmless, used only for practice. If I used real poison every time I trained, what would become of the forest?”

He wasn’t sparing the poison; he was sparing the Thousand Poison Forest.

“But the Poison King trains as well?”

“Didn’t you say the martial artists fear me the most?”

The Hyeolcheon Doma was the same, as was the Geukak Soma who trained facing a wall. The Demon Lords were each training in their own way. The swan’s legs beneath the water’s surface were this busy.

The Poison King sat perched on a rock and wiped his sweat as he asked,

“What brings you here?”

“I came because I missed you.”

“If you missed me, you would have come sooner.”

“I’ve been busy.”

“But you had time to drink with the Drunken Demon?”

“Has word already spread? What heavy lips people have.”

I sat down beside him.

“How does it feel to become the Young Cult Leader?”

“It’s exciting. A new martial art, a new house. People are respectful, and I don’t have to be on edge fighting with my older brother. Isn’t it only natural?”

“That is unexpected. I somehow thought you wouldn’t be excited.”

“I love being the center of attention. Do I usually talk so much otherwise?”

As we talked, the Poison King fell into his own thoughts again.

Whether he was thinking about me, the poison arts he had just displayed, or the snakes that had disappeared, I couldn’t tell. By now, such moments didn’t bother me at all. It seemed I, too, was beginning to understand his world.

After a moment, the Poison King’s gaze shifted to the small leather pouch I was holding.

“What’s that?”

I handed the leather pouch, filled with banknotes, to the Poison King.

The Poison King was startled when he checked the amount.

“That’s quite a large sum.”

“Please use this money to acquire highly toxic spirit medicines.”

The elixirs circulating on the market were too expensive. So expensive that the effects didn’t justify the price.

Instead, elixirs refined from toxic herbs and poisonous substances were relatively cheap. This was likely because they were mostly used for special circumstances or for treatment.

Thanks to that, it was an opportunity for me, with my immunity to all poisons, to acquire elixirs at a low price. I had sufficient internal energy, so I hadn’t intended to spend money on elixirs, but the Time-Differential Secret Art changed my mind.

“Why elixirs? You already have plenty of internal energy, don’t you?”

“I am greedy for internal energy.”

After a brief pause, the Poison King asked,

“Are you planning to conquer the martial world or something?”

“Yes, that’s the plan.”

He must know well that I am not that kind of person.

“Give me the money. I’ll kill them all for you.”

A smile formed at my lips involuntarily.

“Just hearing that makes me feel secure.”

“Then I’ll take the money.”

“And the elixirs will make me even more secure.”

A dragon formed at the Poison King’s fingertips.

It let out a dragon’s roar in the air before flying toward me.

Pop!

Behind the smoke that vanished upon colliding with my face, the Poison King said,

“Come back in twenty days.”

The Poison King would spend the money far more economically and effectively than I could.

Of course, I didn’t forget to make sure.

“Don’t get lost in thought and forget to buy the elixirs. Look at me, look me straight in the eyes. If you forget, I won’t do poison experiments with you.”

The Poison King didn’t reply and resumed his training.

Looking back from afar, I saw crows flying away in all directions. Watching the black smoke bursting here and there, I wondered what would happen if it were real poison. It was only right that everyone feared the Poison King the most.

* * *

I divided my training time anew.

Five parts to the Time-Differential Secret Art, four parts to the Nine Transformations Demonic Art, and the remaining one part to alternating between the Heavenly Demon Guardian Art and the Fist Demon’s martial arts.

The central martial art was important, but the supplementary arts were crucial as well. The Wind God Four Steps would complement the Nine Transformations Demonic Art; in a desperate moment when my sword left my hand, the Thunderclap Asura Fist would save me; at the final moment, the Heavenly Demon Guardian Art would protect me. What mattered was the balance of these martial arts.

It had already been ten days since I began serious training in the Nine Transformations Demonic Art.

The mental cultivation method had become quite familiar to my body now. The amount of innate true qi accumulated through the mental cultivation of the Nine Transformations Demonic Art was incomparable to the previous method, and the speed at which my consumed internal energy recovered was much faster as well.

After finishing my training, I sat by the window drinking tea when a message arrived summoning me to the Heavenly Demon Hall.

* * *

When I arrived at the Heavenly Demon Hall, my father and Sima Ming were waiting together.

Whenever Sima Ming was present, a mission was always issued.

I slowly walked into that place where tension hung in the air.

After greeting my father and Sima Ming, I spoke with gusto,

“Has the time finally come to show the world this handsome face I inherited from my father?”

The corners of my father’s mouth rose slightly.

“Your banter never tires, does it?”

“I have to at least try to be funny. No matter how serious the world is, we must live laughing. Where is it? I’ll take care of everything.”

Then Sima Ming smiled and said,

“It seems the Young Cult Leader’s handsome face must be shown at the next opportunity. Today, you have been summoned for a different reason.”

The reason they called me was something I had not expected.

“Now that you have become the Young Cult Leader, you should step down from the position of Hwangcheon Pavilion Lord and select a new one.”

I had left most of the pavilion lord duties to Seo Daeryong lately. Partly because I was busy. And partly for another reason.

“Whom are you thinking of appointing as the new Hwangcheon Pavilion Lord?”

At my question, Sima Ming looked at my father.

Unexpected words flowed from my father.

“You decide.”

I was startled. I truly hadn’t expected him to tell me to decide the next Hwangcheon Pavilion Lord.

“Now that you are the Young Cult Leader, you ought to have that much authority.”

It was truly a moving statement. My father was not looking at me as his son right now. The Heavenly Demon was looking at his successor. The Cult Leader was looking at his Young Cult Leader.

And regarding the Hwangcheon Pavilion Lord, my thoughts were firm.

“I cannot speak for other positions, but the Hwangcheon Pavilion is the most important organization for maintaining the sect’s discipline. Therefore, the right person must sit in that seat.”

That is precisely why the person I recommend is—

“I wish to make Seo Daeryong, special investigator of the Hwangcheon Pavilion, the next Hwangcheon Pavilion Lord.”

My father and Sima Ming were not particularly surprised, perhaps having expected this to some degree.

“He is competent. He has a strong sense of responsibility. And even his background is excellent, as he is the chief disciple of the Hyeolcheon Doma. Ah, that’s right. He also entered the Hwangcheon Pavilion as a full member.”

Sima Ming interjected cautiously.

“As you said, Investigator Seo is a fine person. However, his experience seems somewhat insufficient to take on the role of pavilion lord.”

“That lack of experience should not be much of a problem within the Hwangcheon Pavilion itself. He has already demonstrated sufficient capability while taking on my duties.”

That was also why I had left my duties to him: to show that he was fully capable of serving as pavilion lord.
