“What matters more than experience for the head of an organization is that person’s character. Look at the Demon Army of the past and the Demon Army now. Did it not become a completely different organization once Jangho became its leader?”

That was something neither my father nor Sima Ming could deny. I spoke to them with conviction, pouring my heart into my words.

“I recommend Seo Daeryong! I vouch for him.”

My father, who had been looking down at me quietly, said,

“Do not offer your guarantee so lightly.”

Even the Heavenly Demon was still a father.

* * *

That afternoon, while I was in the middle of training, Seo Daeryong came to find me.

Seo Daeryong was greatly excited.

“I am coming from the Heavenly Demon Hall.”

It seemed my father and Sima Ming had thought it necessary to speak with Seo Daeryong personally.

“I thought I would faint. Even now, I can barely breathe.”

Staggering, Seo Daeryong walked over and leaned against a wooden dummy standing in the corner.

“I heard from the Chief Strategist. That the Young Cult Leader recommended me for the position of next Hwangcheon Pavilion Lord.”

Seo Daeryong clutched his head.

“Me, a pavilion lord? And of the Hwangcheon Pavilion at that! Ah, just thinking about it again makes it hard to breathe.”

Anyone watching would have thought he had just escaped a heavenly net of snares, the way Seo Daeryong gasped for breath.

“If it’s that hard for you, you don’t have to do it. You can do it next time.”

“Right? That’s right. No. No! When would an opportunity like this come again! Ah! Me, a pavilion lord.”

Seo Daeryong paced around the training grounds like a madman.

He tripped over a weapon rack that had been set up, then sat down on the floor and hung his head low.

“So did you come to tell me you couldn’t do it?”

Then Seo Daeryong took something out from within his robe. He had his back turned, so I couldn’t clearly see what it was.

“What is that?”

“The new Number Two.”

“What do you mean?”

“Until now, the Number Two in the Hwangcheon Pavilion duty room was the flower pot gifted by the Yihua Jianzun. Now this is the new Number Two.”

He rose from where he was sitting, walked over to me, and held out what was in his hand. It was an identity plaque. On the ornate plaque engraved with the symbol of the Heavenly Demon Hall, four characters were written in bold calligraphy.

Hwangcheon Pavilion Lord (黃泉閣主).

“The formal appointment ceremony will be held later.”

The position of Hwangcheon Pavilion Lord had been bestowed upon Seo Daeryong. My father and Sima Ming had acknowledged my first decision as Young Cult Leader.

Seo Daeryong’s pupils trembled as he looked at me.

“How could I refuse? You pushed for me and even vouched for me. How could someone like me decline? I shouted that I would work hard with my life on the line. I don’t really remember what happened after I shouted. I don’t even know how I got out of the Heavenly Demon Hall. Ah! I wonder if I properly greeted the Cult Leader. When I came to my senses, this identity plaque was in my hand.”

I embraced Seo Daeryong firmly and said,

“Congratulations, my right hand. Congratulations, Lord of the Hwangcheon Pavilion.”

Seo Daeryong’s face was filled with intense emotion as he looked at me. A mere investigator had become the chief disciple of the Hyeolcheon Doma and was now even the Hwangcheon Pavilion Lord. It was difficult to even imagine what he must be feeling.

“Later, you might resent me when you’re trembling before a formidable foe. You might say, ‘Why didn’t you just let me live as a plain investigator!’”

“Will you accept that resentment?”

“As much as you want.”

“Then I’ll give it a try.”

“It will be even harder to learn martial arts while serving as the Hwangcheon Pavilion Lord. Still, work yourself to the bone. When you get older, you’ll want to work yourself to death but your body won’t keep up, and that will be even harder.”

“You speak as if you’ve experienced it yourself.”

“I’m the Young Cult Leader who knows everything, aren’t I?”

We looked at each other and laughed pleasantly.

“That’s another thing I can tell my grandchildren.”

“I didn’t recommend you for your sake, or for mine.”

“Then for whose?”

“It’s for the people that the Hwangcheon Pavilion you lead will protect. For those who must endure injustice simply because they have no strength.”

Seo Daeryong’s eyes sparkled. The dark and gloomy man of the past was no more. Now there was only a man who seemed capable of handling anything entrusted to him.

Seo Daeryong, who had been gazing at me with fervent eyes, broke into a grin and said,

“Did you know? The Number Two—no, Number Three—I raised, that flower pot, has even borne fruit.”

That’s right, Daeryong. Raise the Hwangcheon Pavilion well, just like that flower pot.

* * *

On the day promised with the Poison King, I headed to the Thousand Poison Forest with an eager heart.

I had immersed myself in martial arts training. I trained after eating. I slept and trained, ate and trained. I slept soundly in the new bed, woke up, and cooked one meal a day myself. Occasionally, I would bring guards and eat with them.

And I trained all day long. I trained as hard as if this were my final training session. At the very least, my failure must not be due to a lack of effort.

“How has the Poison King been?”

Sangseon answered my question,

“He has been busy. This is the first time he has gone out for such a long time.”

I felt sorry and emotional for no reason. I knew too well how much he hated going outside.

Thus, I arrived at the Poison King’s residence.

As I opened the door and entered, a scent unlike the usual one tickled my nose.

Chapter 256: The Martial World Will Be on Edge.
-----------------------------------------------
Elixirs were laid out on the long table.

It was as if I had come to a shop that sold elixirs. Each of them had its own scent, and those scents mingled together to give off a peculiar fragrance.

The Poison King was absorbed in mixing medicine at another table beside it.

I waited for the Poison King to finish what he was doing as I looked over the elixirs displayed on the table.

There were some elixirs I recognized, but most were ones I did not know. There were far more of them than I had expected.

At last, the Poison King finished mixing the medicine and placed it on the table.

“You’re here?”

“What were you doing?”

“I mixed the ones that work better together.”

Combining these highly poisonous elixirs to increase their effect? Truly, only the Poison King could say such a thing.

“I heard you went out into the Central Plains yourself?”

“If I didn’t, how would I have gathered all these goods in twenty-one days? I mobilized my entire network for the first time in a while.”

The word network sounded awkward coming from the mouth of the Poison King, who lived shut away in the Thousand Poisons Forest. That meant the relationships were that rare and reliable. And saying he had mobilized his network meant he had incurred a debt to someone.

“I owe you a great debt.”

The Poison King came up before me, his eyes gleaming. His gaze seemed to say, So how are you going to repay me?

“Whenever you need my help, just call for me.”

“It won’t end with just once or twice.”

“I’d be glad to see you often, Poison King.”

The Poison King went to the end of the table, picked up the first elixir, and handed it to me.

“Here. Take them in order, starting with this.”

It seemed he had even decided the order of consumption.

“What happens if I take them all at once?”

“You’re immune to all poisons, so you won’t die, but there’ll be chaos inside you. Take them as I give them to you.”

Before eating it, I asked the Poison King.

“Please tell me how much each of these cost.”

“Why? You don’t trust me?”

“If I didn’t trust you, I wouldn’t ask.”

When it came to someone who would never have deceived me, asking like this was, rather, a matter of courtesy.

The Poison King told me the price of each elixir. When I added them all together—

“It exceeds the money I gave you.”

“The last elixir was too good to pass up, so I added a bit of my own.”

“That doesn’t seem like just a bit. It seems like you pulled out one of the foundation pillars of the Thousand Poisons Forest.”

“Do you even know how many pillars the Thousand Poisons Forest has? Just hurry up and eat.”

He, who hated going out more than death, had gone out personally, and on top of that had added a huge sum of money. How could I not like the Poison King?

“Then I’ll take them with gratitude.”

“The first one is the Black Ocean Pill. Take it and circulate your qi through one full cycle.”

The moment I put the Black Ocean Pill in my mouth, my face twisted on its own.

“Uwaaah! It’s bitter. Why is it so bitter?”

“It’s poison. Did you expect it to be sweet?”

I began dissolving the medicinal effect of the Black Ocean Pill. Now, I dissolved it using the mental cultivation method of the Ninefire Demonic Art. Since the amount of internal energy it accumulated was great, the amount of medicinal effect I absorbed would also be great.

“Next is the Purple Star One Poison. It’s compatible with the Black Ocean Pill, so there shouldn’t be any clash of internal energy.”

He was not giving them to me starting with the weakest medicinal effect. He was giving them according to the compatibility and nature of each elixir.

“Next, take the Soul-Seizing Pill.”

Just when I thought the Purple Star One Poison had made things a little better—

“No matter what, this is too much. Ugh.”

When I tried to spit out what was in my mouth, the Poison King covered my mouth and stopped me from doing so.

After forcibly swallowing it, I said to him,

“You’re taking revenge on me right now because I asked you to buy me elixirs, aren’t you?”

“If you want to fill this much internal energy with mild and fragrant elixirs, you’d have to give me several times more money.”

This, too, was a privilege only someone immune to all poisons could enjoy.

“And the bitter medicine hasn’t even come out yet.”

I sprang to my feet, gave a cupped-fist salute, and bid him farewell.

“I should learn to fight well even without internal energy. That’s what a true master is.”

When I tried to flee, the Poison King forced me back down.

Half of it was a joke, and half of it was real. With some exaggeration, it felt as though my tongue and intestines were melting away.

When the elixirs dissolved in my body and their medicinal effects spread through all my blood vessels and meridians, I took another elixir.

“I knew you’d be like that, so this time I prepared something a little sweet. Drink it.”

To think that the Myriad Blood Poison Water was one of the sweet ones!

Even after that, I continued taking elixirs.

The Demon Spirit Divine Pill, the Hundred Spirits Purification Powder, the Thousand Scorpions Essence Blood Liquid, the Profound Yin Pill, the Yin-Yang Two Poison Grass, the Heavenly Outer Paradise Fragrance…

They were elixirs whose names I had only heard, or whose names I had never even heard before. Some came in multiples, while others existed as only a single piece.

This was the first time I had ever taken such a variety of elixirs one after another. Not just me—there was probably no one in the entire jianghu who had.

“Are you sure it’s all right for me to eat all of these?”

“I don’t know either. This is the first time I’ve seen such a case.”

The Poison King looked excited. He, too, had likely never seen so many poisons being dissolved at once. In fact, he was more excited than I was.

Someday, in the book he compiled as the culmination of his knowledge, this moment today would be recorded as well. What title would it have?

The Correlation Between Immunity to All Poisons and Poisonous Elixirs?

No. It would be something like this.

How the Poison King Tormented One Immune to All Poisons.

Judging by how delighted he looked today, he was more than capable of choosing a title like that.

It took a long time to consume all the prepared elixirs.

I would take one, circulate my qi through one full cycle, then take another and circulate my qi again. I carefully dissolved the medicinal effects. Since I dissolved the medicine with the mental cultivation method of the Ninefire Demonic Art, I absorbed far more of the medicinal effect than before.

There were several times midway when it was so poisonous and bitter that I truly felt I might die. Even if it was the same poison, being poisoned by it and eating it like this were completely different matters.

“Here, this is the last one—the Myriad Poisons Divine Pill.”

This was the medicine the Poison King had bought by adding a great sum of his own money. It was the poison that boasted the most powerful effect among all the poisons I had taken so far.

I dissolved even the medicinal effect of the Myriad Poisons Divine Pill with the mental cultivation method of the Ninefire Demonic Art.

The medicinal effect surged through every blood vessel and meridian in my body, then, like water from a valley flowing into a river and ultimately into the sea, it became internal energy and gathered in my dantian.

Even after taking all the elixirs, I circulated my qi several more times and regulated the internal energy inside my body.

Only after confirming that all the medicinal effects had become pure internal energy did I slowly open my eyes. An enormous amount of internal energy had been added to my dantian.

If not for the Poison King, I would not have gained even a quarter of this internal energy with that money. He must have bought good-quality medicine cheaply, and even the order of consumption had been perfect.

Now, in terms of internal energy, I was confident against anyone in the martial world. It was not merely because the quantity had increased. The purity of my internal energy had also deepened beyond comparison with before. Of course I had grown stronger, but more than that, it would be of great help in training the Sicheon Secret Art.

How deep my gaze had become now could be seen from the Poison King’s expression. The next moment, the Poison King was even more surprised. Through Return to Simplicity, that deep gaze had already become an ordinary one.

“How strong are you planning to become?”

I answered calmly.

“I will become strong until I can protect everyone.”

As I rose from my seat, the Poison King said to me,

“We are the ones who protect you. The Demon Venerables protect the Young Cult Leader.”

I stared intently at the Poison King, then suddenly said,

“Poison King.”

For an instant, the Poison King flinched. Instead of my usual polite attitude, I revealed a weighty dignity.

“Then grow stronger!”

“!”

After saying that, I strode out.

As soon as I left, I immediately stuck my head back through the open door. The Poison King was still staring this way with a blank face.

“You’re not angry, are you?”

“Are you already acting like the Heavenly Demon?”

“If not with you, Poison King, where else could I play this joke?”

“There are plenty, aren’t there? The Little Demon, the Blade Demon, the Drunken Demon!”

I lowered my voice again with solemn severity.

“The one this sovereign wished to play this joke on was the Poison King.”

At the end of that banter, I added my sincerity.

“Poison King, I will never forget the grace you have shown me today.”

* * *

Even long after Geom Mugeuk had left, the Poison King remained lost in thought.

“Did you call for me?”

When Sangseon arrived after being summoned, the Poison King said something he had not expected.

“Report to the Heavenly Demon Hall that we will double the Thousand Poisons Forest’s poison production.”

Sangseon was startled. And that was not all.

“Raise the training intensity of the Poison Men as well. Teach the children, and train yourself too.”

“If we do that, the martial world will grow tense.”

In particular, the Martial Alliance might go on emergency alert. Everyone was keeping a sharp eye on the poisons entering the Thousand Poisons Forest. The fact that they had gathered highly poisonous elixirs this time might already have been reported to the Martial Alliance or the Evil Dao Alliance. That was how sensitive matters involving poison were.

The Poison King rose from his seat. Around his body, a black aura began to rise like a shimmering haze. It was the Heaven-Slaying Poison Qi he emitted when revealing his presence.

Wrapped in black mist, the Poison King said in a low voice,

“Tell them to be tense.”

* * *

On the day of the appointment ceremony for the Pavilion Master of the Yellow Springs Pavilion, the Yellow Springs Pavilion investigators and enforcement martial artists came to Seo Daeryong’s residence to escort him.

On the day Geom Mugeuk had taken office in the past, Seo Daeryong had gone with the enforcement martial artists. Now, the situation had changed. The investigator who came today was Gok Myeong, the most senior special investigator.

“Pavilion Master, let us go.”

Seo Daeryong thought that since a far junior colleague had become Pavilion Master, Gok Myeong could not be feeling very pleased.

“Investigator Gok, I look forward to working with you.”

“I will serve you with loyal devotion.”

“After entering the Yellow Springs Pavilion, I learned a great deal from you, senior. What I learned from you, I will now put to use.”

Seeing Gok Myeong smile pleasantly, Seo Daeryong suddenly had this thought.

‘I wasn’t this kind of person.’

He had been someone who could barely meet another person’s eyes. Someone who first had negative thoughts. Even now, he would have thought, That man may be smiling, but he must be cursing me inside, right? That sort of thought would have come first.

And yet now he was trying to convey his sincerity to others. He was trying to understand the hearts of others.

Thus, escorted by Gok Myeong and the enforcement martial artists, Seo Daeryong arrived at the Yellow Springs Pavilion, where the investigators and enforcement martial artists stood in a line.

“We greet the Pavilion Master!”

Seo Daeryong’s rise to the position of Pavilion Master was an unprecedented appointment. Among them were some who were inwardly displeased, but on one point, they shared the same view. Since Seo Daeryong had worked so hard, everyone acknowledged his ability at least.

And at that moment, someone had come to lend him strength.

“Pavilion Master, congratulations.”

Hearing the voice from behind, Seo Daeryong turned and saw the Demon Army Lord Jang Ho walking toward him. Since he had not expected him to come here, Seo Daeryong was startled.

“My lord! You came in person.”

“Of course I had to come.”

When Ian had not been there, the two of them had drunk together at the Pungryu Tavern.

Now they stood facing each other as the Demon Army Lord and the Pavilion Master of the Yellow Springs Pavilion.

“Do you know this? I always wanted to become a martial artist like you, Lord Jang. That thought still hasn’t changed.”

Jang Ho grinned with his unique, manly smile. Whenever Seo Daeryong saw Jang Ho, he always thought of him as someone like a mountain standing firmly in place. He, too, would surely become such a martial artist.

When the Demon Army Lord came personally to congratulate him, the investigators and enforcement martial artists wore expressions of surprise.

Just then, another person appeared there.

Ian had come to congratulate him. With the appearance of the beautiful woman, it felt as though the surroundings brightened. Moreover, as her martial skill had reached a new realm and she had truly entered the ranks of masters, the enforcement martial artists there were astonished by her beauty, then astonished once more by her bearing.

“Congratulations, Pavilion Master of the Yellow Springs Pavilion.”

“Martial Artist Lee, thank you.”

“I knew a day like this would come.”

At Ian’s words, Seo Daeryong said as if whispering,

“I didn’t!”

Jang Ho and Ian laughed together.

At that moment, yet another person arrived there. Seeing the one who had appeared, the investigators and enforcement martial artists all bowed in unison and paid their respects.

“We greet the Young Cult Leader!”

Seo Daeryong, Jang Ho, and Ian also greeted him respectfully.

Geom Mugeuk walked over, paid his respects politely, and said,

“Congratulations, Pavilion Master of the Yellow Springs Pavilion.”

“Thank you, Young Cult Leader.”

Seo Daeryong looked at Geom Mugeuk with trembling eyes.

To Seo Daeryong, who was too choked up to know what to say, Geom Mugeuk gave a single nod with eyes full of trust. That was enough.

Gok Myeong came over and said to Seo Daeryong,

“Everyone is gathered, so please say a few words.”

With a trembling heart, Seo Daeryong stood before everyone. He had thought he might have to say something and had even practiced at home in advance, but he was still terribly nervous.

“The former Pavilion Master was someone who had surpassed the limits of humanity, but now, at last, a human has come to take charge of Hwangcheongak.”

At those words, everyone burst into laughter.

“On the day the former Pavilion Master took office, he said this in this very place: I do not know whether we will become happier or more miserable. But one thing is certain. Our hearts will beat faster than before. The Pavilion Master kept those words. My heart is beating faster than it did before. I believe all your hearts are the same.”

Everyone wore expressions of agreement. Geom Mugeuk’s Hwangcheongak had been an organization entirely different from what it was before. All of them had received the gratitude of countless people who had been spared injustice.

“I cannot make your hearts beat faster than the Young Cult Leader did. Instead, I am confident I can do this much. I will strive to ensure that the hearts the Young Cult Leader set aflame do not cool.”

Applause and cheers erupted.

After giving his greeting, he was about to go inside when the last person who had come to congratulate him arrived.

At his appearance, not only Seo Daeryong but also Geom Mugeuk, Ian, and Jang Ho were startled.

Astonishingly, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had revealed himself. With the Myeolcheon Great Saber slung across his back, he walked over with proud composure. At the sight of him approaching while exuding an overwhelming aura, all the martial artists of Hwangcheongak grew tense.

“Master!”

Instead of offering words of congratulations, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon drew the great saber he had brought along with the Myeolcheon Great Saber on his back. It was a great saber, but it was slightly smaller than the Myeolcheon Great Saber.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon handed it to Seo Daeryong.

“Use this from now on.”

At a glance, it was no ordinary saber.

“It is the blade I used when I was young.”

Seo Daeryong was shocked. A gift from his taciturn master? And if it was the blade he had used in his youth, then its value was beyond words. Overwhelmed, Seo Daeryong felt as though tears might fall.

After giving him the saber, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon turned and walked away without another word.

Seo Daeryong bowed deeply to his master’s retreating back and conveyed his gratitude.

“Thank you, Master.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon left just like that, without looking back.

The inspectors and execution martial artists watched the scene with astonished eyes. Geom Mugeuk was so close to Seo Daeryong and always kept company with him, so it was understandable that he might come, but they had never imagined the Blood Heaven Blade Demon would come here.

No one looked down on Seo Daeryong. Even if they wanted to, they could not. The Young Cult Leader had come, a Demon Venerable had come, a Demon Army Lord had come, and the greatest beauty under heaven had come to congratulate him—who would dare look down on him?

Seo Daeryong respectfully greeted Geom Mugeuk, Jang Ho, and Ian, then turned around.

After turning, he strapped the great saber to his back just as the Blood Heaven Blade Demon did.

Then, leading the inspectors and execution martial artists, he entered Hwangcheongak.

From the sight of his back as he walked proudly with the great saber strapped on, Geom Mugeuk saw the back of the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

Chapter 257: All Men Must Die.
------------------------------
“Investigator Seo was impressive.”

Ian said as we returned to my quarters together. I was taking her there to show her the residence I had moved into after becoming Young Cult Leader.

“She seems like a completely different person from when I first saw her.”

“She’s worked very hard in the meantime.”

Since Ian was someone who would be offended to be called second to anyone when it came to effort, I added one more thing.

“But don’t start thinking that means you need to work even harder too. You already overdo it enough as it is.”

“No. I’m not going to let my effort overflow halfway. I’m going to make it gush out in torrents.”

I could guess what Ian was thinking right now. With everyone else surging ahead, she might be feeling as if she alone were falling behind.

“I’m sorry.”

“For what?”

“I’m only visiting your new residence today. In the past, I would have come to see you on the very first day you moved.”

It seemed she felt sorry for coming so late. Her already drooping shoulders sank even further.

“If you put it that way, I’m the same. I moved into a new home and didn’t even invite you, didn’t I?”

“You were busy, Young Master.”

“You were busier than I was. Is touring a room more important, or is our work more important? The answer is obvious. So there’s no need to feel sorry at all.”

I took her inside. She was startled by how much larger it was than before.

“Ah! It really is big.”

“This body is the Young Cult Leader of our cult!”

The maidservants passing by came out politely and greeted Ian. Ian accepted their greetings with a smile.

When she entered my room, she was even more surprised.

“It’s so different from the room I expected.”

The bookshelves lining the walls, the large bed with pure white bedding, the desk and chair placed by the sunlit window. It seemed different from what she had imagined.

“This isn’t your taste, Young Master.”

“This is my taste.”

“I didn’t know. You should have told me it was like this.”

Before my regression, I had had no such thing as taste.

Whether the bed was big or small, whether there were bookshelves or wardrobes, I had not cared. My only concern had been becoming the successor.

That was why I had drifted away from becoming the successor. Because I saw nothing. I did not see people properly, and I did not care what was in my own room.

At the time, I had thought I was passionate, but now that I thought back on it, I wondered if perhaps it had been an escape into passion.

“You have so many books.”

She took interest in the bookshelves first.

“Now that I’m Young Cult Leader, I need to look learned.”

“This book is incredibly thick.”

“Don’t take it out! That one’s for decoration. It’s hard to put back in.”

“It won’t come out even if I want it to. A book you need inner energy to pull out!”

She examined another bookshelf.

“There are a lot of books here that Elder Blade Demon has, aren’t there?”

At one time, she had borrowed books from the Blood Heaven Blade Demon to read.

“Yes. I wrote down all the books he had there and filled this shelf with the same ones. The Elder knows books well.”

She took a book from another shelf and studied it closely.

“You even have this kind of book.”

The book she was holding was titled Escaping the Heavenly Net and Earthly Snare.

“Have you read it?”

I nodded and answered.

“I have.”

“Did it seem like you could escape if you followed it?”

“I didn’t read it to escape. I read it to keep others from escaping.”

She laughed at my words.

“Young Master, you’re someone who can say, ‘Lay out the Heavenly Net and Earthly Snare!’ but I might end up trapped in the Martial Alliance’s Heavenly Net and Earthly Snare, so please lend me this book.”

“As much as you like.”

“Please lend me this one and this one too.”

She picked out several books. She used to mostly read collections of poetry and paintings, but the books she chose now were all books that recorded secret methods for surviving in the martial world.

“Do you still read books these days?”

“I try to read at least for one gak before going to sleep. I have to build indirect experience, if nothing else.”

It seemed she was still worried that she might become a frog in a well.

“Ian.”

“Yes.”

She turned toward me.

“You think having a lot of worldly experience is better, don’t you?”

“Of course. Isn’t it?”

Ian’s eyes went round.

“It is. People with a lot of worldly experience somehow seem impressive and dependable. They have insight, and they can offer new teachings and experiences to those around them. But to become that way, it also means they’ve gone through and suffered all kinds of filthy things. They’ve endured dirty, petty things, seen people at their very worst. They even come to harbor distrust and disgust toward humanity. Do you think such experiences are only good things in a person’s life?”

Before my regression, I had gone through many things. Yes, thanks to that, I had become stronger and survived.

And so? Could I say my life had been a better one than Ian’s, who had silently walked only the path of a bodyguard warrior? If I were thrown somewhere, perhaps my chances of survival would be higher, but that did not mean it was a better life.

“I’m saying you don’t need to feel too regretful about the life you’ve lived, walking one path in silence. You don’t have to deliberately take on all sorts of miscellaneous wounds and pains wrapped up in the name of experience. You can fight without them, and you can win without them. I believe it. A moment will come when the life you’ve lived through effort will protect you more powerfully than any other experience.”

When I reached out, one of the poetry and painting collections on the shelf slipped free.

The collection floated through the air and was delivered to her.

“So read what you want to read as well.”

I had seen her glance at that book twice.

Ian sidled over to me and stared intently as she said,

“I guess bloodline really is a thing.”

I stared back at her with a serious expression that suited those words. Then, perhaps feeling needlessly embarrassed, Ian avoided my gaze and pretended to look elsewhere.

“The bed is really wide and nice. Is it soft?”

“If you’re curious, lie down.”

“You thought I’d say, ‘How could I dare lie on the Young Cult Leader’s bed!’ didn’t you?”

She threw herself onto the bed.

But her body floated above it.

“Not in the clothes you wore outside! Now I understand why Elder Blade Demon wouldn’t let you lie down.”

I floated her back to where she had been. Sitting on the edge of the bed, she spoke with a serious face.

“A while ago, I selected five people for the Ghost Shadow Unit. I looked at their character and their skills. As you said, I’m aiming for an elite organization rather than focusing on numbers.”

“You’re doing well.”

“Ah, and tomorrow I’m going to leave the cult with Cheongmyeon and my subordinates. We’re going to search for people to recruit into the Ghost Shadow Unit. I should have gone out earlier, but I delayed leaving the cult so I could watch the inauguration ceremony for the Lord of the Yellow Springs Pavilion. While I’m out, I’ll also visit Strategist Go and Cult Leader Pungcheon.”

She was stepping into the jianghu in earnest.

Perhaps sensing the worry in my gaze, she smiled and said,

“Don’t worry. I have nine tails, don’t I?”

“Come back stronger!”

She rose from her seat, cupped her fist politely, and said,

“Yes, Young Cult Leader!”

* * *

I immersed myself in martial arts training.

Across both before and after my regression, this was the period when I was training in martial arts the hardest.

Perhaps because he knew I was in a state of total absorption in martial arts, Father postponed all kinds of events within the cult.

The Guhwa Demonic Art and the Sicheon Secret Art.

They were martial arts I could not help but devote myself to earnestly.

First, the more I trained in the Sicheon Secret Art, the more there seemed to be a difference in the flow of time between the outside and inside of the Space-Time Transposition Art.

However, the difference was still minute, so it was not yet at a stage where I could use the Sicheon Secret Art for actual training.

Use the Space-Time Transposition Art, then use the Sicheon Secret Art again, and then train in martial arts there.

Inner energy would be consumed in three overlapping layers.

Compared to that, the time difference between outside and inside was not yet meaningful, so I was in a situation where recovering inner energy took even more time.

My heart was impatient, but I did not rush. It was not a martial art that could be accomplished overnight, and the life of training was consistency. Repeat, and repeat again. If I did that, one day I would find myself standing on the next stage.

Next was the Guhwa Demonic Art.

Once I became familiar with the mental method of the Guhwa Demonic Art, I began training in earnest from the First Form. It was a difficult and profound martial art, but I calmly analyzed the chants and advanced little by little.

Today, I intended to execute the First Form for the first time.

Guhwa Demonic Art, First Form: Human Annihilation Form.

From the very first form, it was simply annihilation. It meant that if one were human, one would surely die.

I gazed at the wooden dummy set up in front of me and steadied my mind. When I felt that both body and mind were ready, the Black Demon Sword was drawn.

Saaak.

In a single stroke, the dummy’s head fell off. It was an even faster sword form than the Azure Heaven Form, the swift-sword form of the Flying Heaven Sword Art.

So had I succeeded in the First Form? No. I had failed.

I walked over slowly and examined the wooden dummy whose head had fallen.

A faint trace of a sword line remained on its back. There were no marks at all on the left or right.

The Human Annihilation Form was a technique in which, the moment the form was unleashed, four sword energies flew in simultaneously from the opponent’s front, rear, left, and right.

Just imagine it.

In a flash, sword energy flying in not from one place but from four places at the same time. And they were not all aiming for the same spot. They came from four directions, each targeting a different place.

The sword energy from the front aimed for the neck, the one from behind for the back, the one from the left for the waist, and the one from the right for the legs.

However, for now, only the sword energy from the front flew properly, while the sword energies from the other three directions did not.

I closed my eyes for a moment and recalled the chant.

After organizing the chant in my mind, I unleashed sword energy toward a new wooden dummy.

I examined the wooden dummy again. This time, its back had been cut away, while its neck remained intact.

I repeated and repeated, but the First Form did not unfold as I wanted. I was missing something.

Father had said that he wanted to see the Guhwa Demonic Art as I interpreted it.

At this rate, could my Guhwa Demonic Art ever surpass Father’s? The only thing I could trust was my Heavenly Martial Body, but even I could not be certain whether I would be able to wield a Guhwa Demonic Art that surpassed Father’s.

As I agonized over it, clutching my head, I suddenly realized something.

The greed of wanting to learn a Guhwa Demonic Art stronger than Father’s was instead holding me back. Added to that greed was the desire to be acknowledged by Father, and it was weighing even more heavily on my ankles and dragging me down.

This isn’t my way.

I immediately left the training ground.

* * *

When I entered the Heavenly Demon Hall, Father was standing with his hands clasped behind his back, looking out the window.

“Come up here.”

“Yes.”

I walked the Path of Blood, climbed the stairs, and stood side by side with Father. Whenever I stood here with Father and looked out over the cult’s scenery, I always felt good, and now I stood beside him as Young Cult Leader.

“The Thousand Poison Forest has decided to double the amount of poison it produces.”

“Why do they say that is?”

When I asked while pretending not to know, Father looked at me. Since he knew I had been interacting with the Poison King recently, he must have guessed that I had influenced this decision as well.

Father’s gaze turned back out the window. After a brief silence, Father spoke in a low voice.

“Do not provoke the Poison King. He is someone who can become the beginning and the end of everything.”

I could understand what that meant. It meant that a war could begin because of him, and that he could also end a war. Martial artists had no choice but to be sensitive to poison. A lifetime of training could be ruined by a single poisoning.

“I will keep that in mind.”

Father turned to me and asked,

“Why have you come today?”

“I came because I have a request to make of you, Father.”

“What request?”

“Please use the Human Annihilation Form on me.”

At the unexpected words, Father flinched in surprise.

“You finally became the successor, and now you want to die?”

“Of course not.”

I loosened my chest garments.

“I’m wearing supreme-grade heavenly silkworm silk and even the Ghost Tiger Robe. I’m asking you to use it only to the extent that I won’t die.”

“Your reason?”

“I want to experience it directly and grasp the feeling.”

Originally, I should have learned much more from Father. But since Father had only given me the bare minimum of interpretation because he wanted to see the Guhwa Demonic Art as I interpreted it, this was a request he could grant.

“Very well.”

Once Father gave his permission, I stepped back and put some distance between us.

Are you ready, here I come. All such words were omitted.

The Heavenly Demon Sword was drawn at once from Father’s hand.

Swish.

A truly short and light sound of wind.

In that instant, I was shocked.

Four demons attacked simultaneously from every direction.

Father’s Human Annihilation Form was not merely four streaks of sword energy flying in. It was a sword energy in which terrifying apparitions of four demons, enough to freeze the body just by seeing them, appeared simultaneously from east, west, south, and north and swung their swords.

Kaaang! Kaang!

I blocked two, one struck me shallowly, and the other hit my body squarely.

Pak! Paaak!

As the sword energy collided with my protective energy, I flew through the air and rolled across the floor.

“Uuugh!”

It truly hurt. I had blocked with supreme-grade heavenly silkworm silk, the Ghost Tiger Armor, and protective energy on top of that, but my waist hurt as if it would snap in two.

It was fortunate Father had adjusted his inner energy. If he had unleashed it to the limit, I might have died from that one move. Even if I had not died, I would have suffered a fatal injury.

It was the first moment I truly felt the terror of the Guhwa Demonic Art.

And what I was curious about at that moment was this.

“If my realm in the Guhwa Demonic Art rises, will those demons manifest in my Human Annihilation Form as well?”

Father nodded.

“Similar demons, but different ones, will appear.”

Similar, but different.

He meant that because I would interpret the Guhwa Demonic Art differently and complete the martial art in my own way, different demons would appear.

My heart began to pound. Seeing the Heavenly Demon Soul was still a distant, distant matter, so I wanted to see my own small demons through the Human Annihilation Form at least.

“Will that do?”

“It is enough.”

I rubbed my waist, gave my greeting, and was about to turn away when I asked Father,

“By any chance, did you name those demons?”

That sneer appeared at the corner of Father’s mouth.

“The Poison King likes naming things. I’ve been spending time with him often lately, so I suppose I’ve been influenced.”

After greeting Father politely, I turned away.

The attainment of my martial arts was not solely my own attainment. If a Human Annihilation Form different from Father’s emerged, Father too would receive some inspiration from my Guhwa Demonic Art. Influencing each other, we would become stronger.

At the end of the Path of Blood, I said to Father,

“The next time I come to see you, I’ll bring four friends with me.”

Chapter 258: Someone to Discuss Swordsmanship With.
---------------------------------------------------
After leaving the Heavenly Demon Hall, I headed for the Northern Heaven Sword Clan.

When there was someone I could discuss swordsmanship with, why would I need to struggle over it alone?

Yes, this is my way. If I don’t know, I ask. If I’m talking about something else, I come to a realization. If I truly can’t figure it out, then to hell with it—I go have fun.

Because of the other Demon Venerables, and because I’d been busy with various incidents, I intended to make up this time for having neglected the Ilhwa Sword Venerable.

It was the return of the sparring friend who had run away from home.

Just as there had been changes in me during that time, there had been changes in the Ilhwa Sword Venerable as well.

When I arrived at the Ilhwa Sword Venerable’s cottage in the Northern Heaven Sword Clan, I was startled.

Her cottage had changed. The modest little cottage from before had now become a large estate.

The yard had grown wider, and so had the flower garden she tended.

The only thing that had not changed was the Ilhwa Sword Venerable’s beautiful appearance, which still seemed to run backward against the flow of age. She welcomed me warmly.

“Young Cult Leader, welcome.”

“Have you been well?”

“I’m the same as ever.”

“Your house has changed.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable looked around with me and smiled calmly.

“I tried changing the atmosphere.”

The old cottage had been a house meant to show others. To say: I am a lofty person who has cast off worldly desires and walks only the path of martial arts.

And now, she had cast aside pretense and was revealing her honest desires. Why had her heart changed?

“You must be busy these days. What brings you here?”

“I came because I wanted to see you, Sword Venerable.”

“You did well to come. Come in.”

I followed the Ilhwa Sword Venerable into the house. The interior was decorated splendidly.

“May I look around a little? These days, I’ve also become quite interested in decorating my room.”

“Look as much as you like.”

I slowly looked around the room. The furniture and ornaments were ornate and luxurious. She made no attempt to hide the fact that she had spent money on decorating the interior.

“It’s truly wonderful.”

Then something caught my eye.

‘This is?’

What lay on the table was a book. It was the collection of poems and paintings that had been on the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s bookshelf.

Of course, it was possible for there to be another copy of the same book.

But the reason a single book drew my attention so strongly was likely because it involved the Blade Demon and the Sword Venerable.

“I’ll have to get that display cabinet for my room as well. I like it very much.”

“I didn’t know you had such tastes, Young Cult Leader.”

After looking around the room like that, we sat facing each other at the tea table.

As I drank the tea, I recognized the taste. It was the same tea I had drunk when I went to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s house.

“Elder Blade Demon seems to drink this tea as well.”

“Is that so?”

I did not miss the moment the Ilhwa Sword Venerable flinched. The book, and the tea too.

‘These people!’

I laughed inwardly. If the book and tea were not coincidences, then something must have changed in the relationship between the two of them.

“You’re curious why I changed the house, aren’t you?”

“Did you have a change of heart?”

“I thought I wanted to live a little differently.”

I looked around once more and said,

“To be honest, this room suits you better than the old one did, Senior. It’s splendid and beautiful.”

“Is that so?”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable laughed in good spirits.

Though we were laughing and conversing like this, she and I maintained a certain distance. Not very close, but not very far either. Definitely farther than a single step. But not so far that it felt uncomfortable.

About five steps. That was the distance between the Ilhwa Sword Venerable and me, as I saw it.

“I came to visit you today as a sparring friend.”

I asked her for a spar. Fortunately, she readily accepted.

“I would be glad to. Though I don’t know whether a spar with me will be of any help to you.”

“I learn a great deal just from watching you draw your sword, Senior. If I thought it wouldn’t help, would I have asked you to spar?”

“Could you leave that mouth of yours behind?”

“There are many places where my mouth is needed, so that would be difficult.”

We went out to the training ground.

We stood facing each other about ten paces apart. As if we had never been exchanging jokes moments before, her aura changed.

It was not only her house that had changed. I could feel that her aura was different from before.

‘She’s devoted herself to training all this time!’

She had been honed sharp, like a treasured sword. I had once compared the efforts of the Demon Venerables to the feet of a swan, and the one who fit that comparison best was the Ilhwa Sword Venerable.

Behind her swanlike elegance was unseen effort.

What had changed her like this?

I did not hide my true aura either and revealed it. Tension appeared on the Ilhwa Sword Venerable’s face as she looked at me, and her gaze sank coldly.

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable moved first.

Shiiiiik!

A sword flew in. I could not see her figure. Completely hidden behind the sword, she and her sword had become one.

The Ilhwa Sword and the Black Demon Sword collided in midair, scattering sparks. Dozens of sword lines created by the two blades instantly embroidered the air, then vanished.

Though she had lost to me in our previous spar, a Sword Venerable was still a Sword Venerable. On top of that, her recent training shone brilliantly, raising this spar to an even higher level.

Flashes of sword light burst forth as we flew into the sky together. The Demon Swords of the Northern Heaven Sword Clan stopped in their tracks and watched the sword light shining in the air. The sword radiance glittering in the sunlight was truly a magnificent sight.

Chaaang!

With one final, long metallic ring, we descended to the ground.

Even though I had not sent her sword flying from her hand, and even though I had not placed my sword at her throat, the Sword Venerable must have known. That she had lost.

“You grow without end.”

She could not hide her bitter expression. Her training had failed to catch up to my growth.

I took care of her pride for her.

“It was thanks to your consideration, Senior. I revealed the bottom of my reserves, but you still have one final move left, don’t you?”

All Demon Venerables hid one move to use at the very end. The Ilhwa Sword Venerable was no exception.

“A move I hope never to use in my lifetime.”

In other words, it meant a trump card that required her to stake her own life as well.

“Let’s go inside.”

After we returned inside, we moistened our throats with cold tea and spoke about swordsmanship.

We discussed the spar we had just had. I went one step further from there. I told the Ilhwa Sword Venerable about what I had felt when I previously achieved Great Completion of the twelfth stage of the Flying Heaven Sword Art.

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable listened closely to my words and asked whenever she had something she was curious about.

With her level of skill as a Sword Venerable, she would naturally know. How sincerely I was speaking to her. That this was the essence of martial learning—something I would never tell anyone unless I cherished them.

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable’s face grew more and more flushed.

When I finished speaking, she rushed out to the training ground.

“Wait! Wait just a moment.”

She swung her sword alone in the middle of the training ground.

Then, suddenly, she stopped her sword and sank into thought. She swung again, then stopped again.

She had gained some sort of realization. Through our conversation, she had found a way to cross a wall she had been unable to overcome through solitary training.

After becoming a Demon Venerable, she must have trained alone for a very long time. And at the end of that road, which she had walked alone and lonely until it was blocked, she had met me.

After swinging her sword like that for a long while in the training ground, she returned, still flushed.

“Did you not come to me because you wished to gain some realization about swordsmanship?”

“That’s right. To be honest, my swordsmanship has been at a standstill lately.”

“Then why are you passing martial learning on to me?”

I calmly settled my aura and spoke with dignity.

“Because you are someone who will become my Demon Venerable.”

To her, who stared at me without a word, I continued calmly.

“There are two more reasons besides that.”

“Two more?”

“First, because I believe that if you break through your limits, that realization will return to me again. Aren’t we sparring friends?”

She nodded and asked,

“And the second reason?”

“Senior, you’re the only one among the Demon Venerables who uses a sword. You are the only person I can discuss swordsmanship with. You know my father’s personality well enough. From whom would I learn swordsmanship? On whom would I have an influence? I have no one but you, Sword Venerable.”

Of the three reasons, this seemed to touch her the most. A surge of emotion passed through her.

“That is why I should have come to see you first, and I should have come to see you most often……”

I spoke honestly.

“It felt as though you would always remain exactly as you were. It was a selfish and ugly thought. I should have cared for the good people first.”

At that, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable spoke in a somewhat self-mocking voice.

“I am not that good a person. Perhaps you were taking care of people in the proper order after all……”

A certain regret passed through her eyes.

For a moment, neither of us spoke, each lost in our own thoughts.

I did not force out obvious comfort in this situation, saying, No, Sword Venerable, you are a good person.

What was needed now was this.

“May I come again tomorrow?”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable nodded with an expression that said I could come not only tomorrow, but for the rest of my life.

* * *

“Since when did you like flowers?”

Even at the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s words, I continued staring at the flowerpot by the window.

“It looks like a somehow sly flower.”

On my way back to my quarters, I stopped by the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s place, and there was something by the window that I had never seen before. It was a flowerpot with flowers planted in it. If I had come here first, I would have simply thought the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had bought the flowers.

“What exactly is inside the head of someone who looks at a flower and calls it sly?”

“Where did you get the flower?”

“Someone gave it to me.”

When he gave the Ilhwa Sword Venerable the book and the tea as gifts, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable must have given him the flowerpot as a gift. Or perhaps it had been the other way around.

‘Brother Chwima, you’re amazing!’

It seemed Chwima’s efforts to reconcile the two of them had finally borne fruit.

“Who?”

“Someone I know gave it to me.”

“So who was it?”

“You’re noisy when I’m trying to read.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon did not say to the very end that the Ilhwa Sword Venerable had given it to him. I did not press him further. I also held back from joking around.

How happy must he be right now? And not with anyone else, but with the Ilhwa Sword Venerable. Resolving past misunderstandings and conflicts, even exchanging gifts.

Hold back. If I’m going to tease him, I can tease him later.

“Stop staring at it! If you release your qi wrong, the flower will die.”

If that happened, he might unleash a killing move at me.

Daeryong, the flowerpot here is more precious than the one in the office. If you drop it while training, you’ll have to undergo hell training for at least a year.

“I’ll be going.”

“Why are you leaving already?”

“I’m in the middle of hell training these days. I’m probably working the hardest in the whole cult.”

“You’ve even become Young Cult Leader. What more wealth and glory are you trying to enjoy, working so hard?”

Because my relationship with them had changed completely from before my regression, if I died now, the Demon Venerables would all die too. If I were dying, would the Blood Heaven Blade Demon simply stand by and watch?

That is why. To save everyone.

‘I’m doing this so that even if someone has to leap into hell, it’ll be me.’

As for that wealth and glory, please enjoy it yourself, Elder. Spend your later years happily with the Sword Venerable, all cozy together, traveling around the Central Plains.

* * *

The next day, I sparred with the Ilhwa Sword Venerable again.

Perhaps she had not slept all night, for her eyes were a little bloodshot.

“I lost some sleep training yesterday.”

It seemed she had spent the entire night trying to turn yesterday’s realization into skill.

I sparred with her again. Because we knew each other’s moves, we gradually began using more powerful techniques, and toward the end, the exchanges were almost indistinguishable from actual combat.

After the spar ended, we discussed the contents of today’s fight. Because the flow of the fight had been similar, much of it overlapped with what we had talked about yesterday, but we spoke as though it were the first time.

As we talked, the conversation sometimes branched into new topics. Stories of swordsmanship training in childhood, and even methods for raising flowers.

I went the next day, and the day after that too. I went with the thought that I should make up now for all the times I had not visited often enough. I went with the determination to keep coming until she grew sick of me and told me to stop.

The pattern of the spars was similar, but we were feeling changes.

Even with the same technique, the speed differed, and the internal energy infused into it differed. Some days it was fast, and other days it was slow. An opportunity to test one’s sword art to one’s heart’s content like this truly did not come easily.

And this morning.

I used the first form of the Nine Flames Demonic Art, the Annihilation Form.

Saaaaaak!

The sound was different.

Slice! Slice! Slice! Slice!

Startled, I ran over and saw that the wooden dummy had been cut into exactly four pieces.

“It worked!”

At last, I had succeeded in making sword qi fly in from four directions simultaneously.

It seemed my spars and conversations with the Sword Venerable had helped in some way.

From that day on, I devoted myself even more to training.

I had a desire. I wanted to see the lesser evil spirit in the Annihilation Form. That desire drove me to the utmost effort.

Once a day without fail, I stopped by the Sword Venerable’s place, sparred with her, and discussed martial learning.

If someone were watching us, they might think we were repeating the same thing over and over, but it was not the same.

Within the power granted by repetition, we grew.

The more the Sword Venerable grew, the more I grew as well. Influenced by me, the Sword Venerable grew, and I received back the influence of the Sword Venerable who had grown. We were burning with the fervor shared only by those who wielded swords.

And there was a change in our relationship as well.

“I changed the house because of Senior Blade Demon.”

She revealed her honest feelings.

“These days, I have been trying to forget the old grudges with Senior Blade Demon and get along well with him. In the process, I found myself thinking a great deal about the past. Back then, I liked splendid and pretty things. That was who I was. While thinking that, I suddenly changed it on impulse. It is embarrassing, but I did not change it after some grand deliberation.”

It must have been longing for her younger days. Those days she wanted so desperately to return to.

“You did well. Later, if you miss the cottage, you can change it again.”

“Would that not make me look too frivolous?”

“To me, you would look like someone with a free spirit.”

Yes, I am no longer at an age to live while worrying about others’ opinions, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable said, laughing pleasantly. It was the moment I realized that the distance between us had, before I knew it, gone from five steps to three.

Meanwhile, my training continued.

I trained day and night. Then, one day, after repeating the Annihilation Form countless times.

Saaak!

The sound of the Annihilation Form changed again.

At that very moment!

I saw it. A pitch-black being cutting off the wooden dummy’s neck.

‘Finally!’

One of the four evil spirits had appeared.

Chapter 259: What Father Wanted to See.
---------------------------------------
Regrettably, I could not see the evil spirit’s face.

Of all things, the first evil spirit to appear had been the one directly in front of the wooden dummy.

So all I could see was its back.

Four parts of the wooden dummy were sliced away, and immediately after, the evil spirit vanished.

There was a difference between the cuts made when an evil spirit appeared and those made by sword energy alone. Once the evil spirit manifested, the speed and power became faster and stronger. Considering even the demonic energy the evil spirits emitted, along with the visual and psychological effects, the true Annihilation Form was only complete when the evil spirits appeared.

I performed the Annihilation Form again.

Saaak!

The evil spirit appeared once more. But this time, too, I could only see its back.

The joy of finally having succeeded and the disappointment came over me at the same time.

‘I wanted to see what you looked like! I worked that hard to see your face, not your back!’

Then a thought flashed through my mind.

‘Ah! I can just set up a bronze mirror!’

I was about to go get a bronze mirror, but then I changed my mind.

I felt that going that far just to see it wasn’t right. I had decided to accept the order in which the martial art manifested. If you don’t want to show me, then fine.

‘I’ll look at the other evil spirits’ faces first.’

* * *

“Did something good happen?”

Ilhwa Sword Venerable could tell that Geom Mugeuk’s expression seemed especially bright today.

“I made progress in the sword art I’d been stuck on.”

“Ah! That’s good to hear.”

Ilhwa Sword Venerable was pleased. Through sparring with Geom Mugeuk and discussing martial arts with him, she herself had achieved great progress. At the level of a Demon Venerable, attaining new insight was truly difficult.

And yet, she had never dreamed that in such a short period of time, she would gain an achievement that would raise her to the next stage.

“You truly are impossible to predict.”

When Geom Mugeuk first came to visit, she had thought he would show up for a day or two and then stop. Since he had become the Young Cult Leader, she assumed he had come as a form of networking.

But that was not the case.

What Geom Mugeuk had said on the first day had been true.

You are the only senior I can talk to about swordsmanship.

Geom Mugeuk had sincerely wanted to discuss swordsmanship with her. He had tried to teach her the profound principles of martial learning, and also to learn from her.

Though she had practiced swordsmanship all her life, this was the first time she had ever pondered, debated, and discussed the sword so seriously.

Through this opportunity, she realized it. Just how much she had longed for such time.

A sparring friend.

Geom Mugeuk had approached her as a true sparring friend. From now on, those words would carry a deeper meaning to her than any other relationship.

And so, these words were sincere.

“Young Cult Leader, you are also the only person I have to talk to.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled and drew his sword.

“Then shall we share?”

When they flew up into the air, all the Demon Swords came out and watched them. Of course, since they rose so high to fight, their figures could not be seen in detail.

Only countless swift flashes of sword light flickered.

Even so, the Demon Swords became absorbed in the sight as though bewitched by something.

Whenever Geom Mugeuk finished sparring and left, he always said this to the Demon Swords he met.

“I learned much from the Sword Venerable today. I envy you.”

When Ilhwa Sword Venerable heard those words, she was happy. Whether it was flattery or sincerity. To receive such high praise from none other than Geom Mugeuk was an indescribably joyful thing.

A person who unstintingly shared his understanding of martial learning and said such words to her subordinates… how could she possibly not be won over?

* * *

My training continued.

The outside events the Young Cult Leader was supposed to attend kept being pushed back. It was Father’s consideration after hearing that I was obsessed with training.

The Demon Venerables did not seek me out either. The Drunken Demon might have come to ask me to drink, but even he did not visit.

Within the cult, all anyone talked about was the Young Cult Leader who had gone mad for martial arts.

I sparred only with the Sword Venerable, and after returning from the spar, I practiced the Annihilation Form like a madman.

So many wooden dummies were cut apart that a metal dummy arrived at my training ground. It had been made by mixing Ten-Thousand-Year Cold Iron and steel, a special steel dummy that would not be cut by sword energy.

Kaaang! Kang! Kaaang! Kaaang!

The refreshing sound of metal rang out again and again.

After seeing the evil spirit’s back so many times, I even began to think, Can’t you turn your head just once?

Repeating it, again and again.

And today, the gift granted by that tedious repetition arrived.

Saaaaak!

The sound of the Annihilation Form changed once more.

In that instant, I saw it.

Behind the steel dummy, another evil spirit had revealed itself.

It was the appearance of the second evil spirit.

And this time, I was able to see the evil spirit’s face. For a moment, I was startled.

It did not look frightening. Surprisingly, my first impression was this:

‘It’s handsome!’

To my astonishment, the evil spirit I had summoned was a handsome evil spirit.

It had the kind of face that might appear if I showed a painter the Poison King’s face and told him, “Draw an evil spirit based on this.”

‘Is this allowed?’

I truly was surprised.

Could it be that these evil spirits reflected the heart of the person using the martial art?

I had not thought that the evil spirit’s face needed to instill fear in the opponent. I had only focused on wanting to see the evil spirit’s face.

Was that why? Was that why this kind of evil spirit appeared instead of a terrifying one?

Now I became curious about what the evil spirit that showed me its back looked like. Did it look the same? Or did it look different?

In any case, to confirm whether all their faces were different, I would have to manifest the evil spirits on the left and right as well.

Curiosity was leading my yearning.

* * *

“I think today will have to be our last day of training.”

Ilhwa Sword Venerable nodded with an expression that said she had expected it.

“Are you planning to enter closed-door training?”

“How did you know?”

“Intuition. Recently, I could feel your fervor rising higher and higher. The moment has come for you to pour everything into it one last time.”

“As expected, you are remarkable.”

The look in the Sword Venerable’s eyes as she gazed at me was different again from when I had first come seeking a spar.

“Young Cult Leader, I truly thank you.”

“I am grateful as well.”

I had become incomparably closer to her than before. I now thought I stood two steps away from her. That distance came from her nature. No matter how much closer one became, there were people who would never allow anyone within one step.

“If I get stuck in my martial arts again, I’ll come find you.”

“I will prepare myself to receive you.”

I read determination in her eyes. If she grew once more from here, the cult would welcome a new Ilhwa Sword Venerable.

Geom Mugeuk looked around the training ground. For the time being, this place was at an end as well.

“The next time I come, it may have become the narrow yard of the thatched hut again.”

“Then it will be an even more thrilling spar with a free spirit.”

Ilhwa Sword Venerable smiled and looked over the training ground with him.

“Since it is the last day, would you spare me some time today?”

In truth, I had prepared something for her today.

“Seo Daeryong, who became the Hwangcheon Pavilion Master, is like my right arm. So I would like to hold a modest celebration for him today.”

“For something like that, I should make time.”

“I have also invited the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. Since he is Seo Daeryong’s master, I thought it only right to bring him.”

Ilhwa Sword Venerable understood. He was trying to take her there in order to naturally arrange a meeting with the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“Would you wait a moment? I will change and come out.”

“Yes, please take your time preparing.”

Today, it took her longer than usual to wash, put on makeup, and choose her clothes.

* * *

The place Geom Mugeuk and Ilhwa Sword Venerable arrived at was a hill with a green field beneath Daecheon Mountain. Wine and food had been prepared there. It was a banquet Geom Mugeuk had asked the cooks to prepare in advance.

Ilhwa Sword Venerable looked around and said,

“Was there such a place near the cult?”

“If you search every corner, there are many wonderful places. From now on, please travel and rest as well.”

“What fun is there in doing that alone?”

At that moment, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and Seo Daeryong arrived. With their great sabers slung over their shoulders, the two now looked like master and disciple to anyone.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon was wearing a neat long robe he did not usually wear. His hair was neater than usual as well.

Geom Mugeuk forcibly held back the urge to tease him.

“You’ve come?”

“What’s the point of making a fuss over a celebration?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon spoke curtly for no reason.

“Your disciple has risen to a great position, so why are you being so ill-tempered?”

At Ilhwa Sword Venerable’s words, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon said nothing.

Seo Daeryong politely greeted Ilhwa Sword Venerable.

“Thank you for gracing us with your presence.”

“Congratulations.”

“Thank you.”

Seo Daeryong poured wine for everyone.

After we had drunk a few cups like that, I went out for a walk with Seo Daeryong. I had deliberately left the two of them alone and stepped away. Today’s gathering was for the Blood Heaven Blade Demon rather than Seo Daeryong. That was why I had given Seo Daeryong a hint beforehand.

“How is the work as pavilion master?”

“For now, I’m too busy trying to adjust to think straight.”

Seo Daeryong stopped walking and looked at Geom Mugeuk.

“I’ve been having nightmares lately. Dreams where I’m wandering around looking for you, Young Cult Leader, in an urgent situation. It’s something I should be handling, but in the dream, all I do is search for you. And when I finally manage to find you, you look at me coldly and say this: ‘Why did you take that position when you don’t even have the ability?’”

“Will it really only happen in your dreams?”

“Ugh, I’m being serious.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled and said,

“Realistically, something like that may happen once in your lifetime, if at all. Don’t waste your mental strength on something that may never happen.”

“What if it does happen?”

“Then you solve it with this.”

Geom Mugeuk lightly tapped the handle of the great saber Seo Daeryong was carrying.

Seo Daeryong stared at the handle marked by his master’s touch and nodded.

“That must be why every day has to be hellish training.”

The two turned to head back.

“Let’s stay a little longer.”

In the distance, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and Ilhwa Sword Venerable were drinking and talking. Both of their expressions were bright.

“It’s a good time for them!”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Seo Daeryong laughed.

“When I went to bring him earlier so we could come together, it looked like he was dressing up. He seemed to have tried on several outfits too. It was the first time I’d ever seen Master like that.”

Seeing the Blood Heaven Blade Demon smile put Geom Mugeuk in a good mood as well.

Yes, please forget everything that happened in the past now. Lock the door that leads to the past, and there is no need to peek into the future either. Just live happily like that.

“You should have a spring breeze blow your way too.”

“It’s hopeless for me. Please go on ahead without me. I’ve gotten even busier now. My life is going to be nothing but work and training!”

Seo Daeryong gazed enviously at the fresh, youthful feeling in the distance that had forgotten age.

“Can I really go ahead first?”

“You have to take me with you! I’m your right arm!”

* * *

Time passed quickly.

I had entered wondering when I would come out, and before I knew it, I had finished one hundred days of closed-door training and was leaving the training hall.

Without even shaving my beard, the first place I headed was the Heavenly Demon Hall.

Father was guarding the Heavenly Demon Hall, unchanged as ever.

“What is that state you’re in?”

“I wanted to see you, Father, so I came running straight here. This way, it shows how hard I worked.”

Out of gladness, I started joking first.

“I nearly went mad wanting to eat something delicious. Please develop some food that can be used for closed-door training, not fasting pills or jerky!”

Father welcomed me with that same unchanging smile.

“A lazybones training for a hundred days. Impressive.”

“Those fellows were so terribly shy, it was truly hard to get a look at them.”

I had tried to abandon my desire to be acknowledged by Father. I thought that burden was holding me back.

Even so, in this moment, my heart trembled. It seemed I had not thrown it all away after all. The desire to be acknowledged by Father through martial arts.

“I will introduce you to my friends.”

After calmly settling my mind, I performed the First Technique.

Saaak!

A crisp sound of wind.

Four strands of sword energy cleaved through the air.

Four evil spirits revealed themselves.

They were my evil spirits, the ones I had wanted so badly to see.

They looked different from the evil spirits Father had shown me. To my surprise, my evil spirits all had different appearances and impressions.

There was one that was frightening, and one that was handsome. One gave off a strange sense of mystery, and another looked clever.

Father was surprised by their differing appearances. Naturally, he must have thought they would all look the same. In truth, I was surprised too. I had not expected evil spirits with such different impressions to appear.

The four evil spirits did not vanish immediately like Father’s had.

As though they were trying to commune with me, they seemed to look at me for an instant.

Seut.

The next moment, the evil spirits disappeared in a flash, just as they had appeared.

Father had risen from his grand chair. He did not hide his surprise.

“Is this what you wished to see, Father?”

The Guhwa Demonic Art, completed through my own interpretation, different from Father’s.

Father looked down at me in silence, then slowly nodded.

“Yes. It was something like this.”

I recalled the conversation I had with Father on the day he passed the Guhwa Demonic Art on to me.

—The Guhwa Demonic Art has continued to change.

—It could not have been otherwise. In every era, fools and geniuses must have continued to appear.

I would become that genius and unfold a further developed Guhwa Demonic Art.

I felt that Father’s aura had changed. At least when it came to martial arts, Father would not simply stand by in admiration. He would strive to show his own change. He would try to compete with me and win. He would sincerely help where he meant to help, and win where he meant to win. Father was Father.

“Thank you for postponing all the external duties the Young Cult Leader was supposed to handle.”

“The first button is always important.”

It had been Father’s consideration so that I could properly learn the First Technique of the Guhwa Demonic Art. Thanks to him, I had fastened the first button well. Now, as I learned the Sicheon Secret Art, I would calmly fasten the rest one by one.

“I’ll leave the cult at once and take care of everything.”

“Before you go…”

Father stepped down from the grand chair and said to me,

“Let’s eat together. You worked hard.”

Father had personally cast even the sorcery he supposedly hated so much. Because with that single sentence, all the hardship of those hundred days vanished.

Chapter 260: Your Names Are.
----------------------------
It had been a long time since I’d eaten with Father.

I like this time so much. Whether we are playing go or sharing a meal. This moment, quietly conversing with Father without words.

I usually joke around a great deal, but in truth, perhaps it is because this moment is what lies closest to my essence. After all, I had lived almost an entire lifetime in silence.

“Was the training not difficult?”

“My body did not suffer, but enduring the tedium of repetition was difficult.”

“How did you endure it?”

“In the end, I emptied my mind. This repetition will be my task for life, and even if I escape this boredom, another boredom will be waiting for me anyway, so I decided simply to do it. As I did, it felt as though the repetition and the boredom became one with me.”

Father smiled. It was not a sneer. It was a smile of sincere congratulations, as if to say I had found the right path.

I explained to Father in detail how I had interpreted the First Form, what trial and error I had gone through, and how I had trained it through repetition.

I spoke excitedly, as if boasting, but in truth, I was giving Father information.

For his reference.

Father would compare my interpretation with his own and strive to create an even better form. Please, let him succeed.

I did not regress in order to become stronger than Father, or to defeat him. I came here to survive together with him.

“If you’ve finished eating, let us go digest our meal.”

I followed Father outside. Father looked toward the summit of Mount Daecheon, visible far in the distance, and said,

“Let us see who reaches there first.”

For whatever reason, Father proposed a lightness art contest.

“You haven’t forgotten that I said we should have a match once I achieved great mastery in Rapid Step.”

“How about it? Are you confident?”

“Of course I am. For your reference, even the Demon Venerables were all sent flying by me.”

“You start first!”

“Then you will have to run while looking only at my back.”

The moment I finished speaking, I shot forward first. This way, even if I happened to win, Father would have something to say.

I raced ahead at a terrifying speed with Rapid Step.

Rapid Step, once brought to great mastery, was so fast that I wondered whether even Father could keep up with this speed.

It was just then. I thought I heard the fierce sound of wind from behind.

When I turned my head, Father’s figure was beside me.

Father was flying with his robes fluttering, standing upright in midair. Speed was not the issue.

‘So cool!’

If I was running as though my life depended on it, Father was flying with dignity and aloof grace. The Heavenly Demon Flight Art, brought to great mastery, suited him so perfectly it seemed like a lightness art made for Father himself.

Right. If it lacks style, it should not be slow too.

I raced with all my might. I truly thought I had run as fast as light, but the one who arrived at the summit first was Father.

“I lost. I lost in style, and I lost in speed. Even the god of wind is no match for the Heavenly Demon.”

When I lowered my head deeply, Father said,

“Rapid Step is fast as well.”

Though he never acknowledged other martial arts, the Wind God’s Four Steps was one martial art even Father recognized.

And then unexpected words flowed from Father’s mouth.

“If you achieve great mastery in the Heavenly Demon Flight Art, you will produce the same result.”

They were words acknowledging me, but there was a reason Father had suggested a lightness art contest, and a reason he had just said that.

“I will pass the Heavenly Demon Flight Art on to you.”

I could tell. It was because I had told him earlier about the First Form. Since I learned one thing, I should teach one thing as well. That was his mindset. Father never simply receives.

“Thank you, Father.”

I prostrated myself on the spot.

“With your current level, having grasped the ultimate principle of lightness arts, you should be able to learn it without difficulty, and great mastery will not take very long either. Now, listen carefully from this point on.”

Father transmitted the mnemonic formula of the Heavenly Demon Flight Art right there. Only after checking several times to see whether I had memorized it properly did the transmission of the formula end.

I gave Father a deep bow.

“I am so happy, Father.”

The reason I was this happy was not merely because the Heavenly Demon Flight Art was the greatest lightness art in the murim. Nor was it because of the possibility of combining the Heavenly Demon Flight Art and the Wind God’s Four Steps to perform an even faster lightness art.

I was happy because this was the moment I inherited all of Father’s unique martial arts. The Nineflames Demonic Art, the Heavenly Demon Body Protection Art, and now even the Heavenly Demon Flight Art. It was the moment I learned all the core martial arts Father had mastered. My heart swelled.

“Get up now.”

“Yes.”

Together with Father, I stood side by side on the cliff at the summit of Mount Daecheon.

Far in the distance, the full view of the Heavenly Demon Cult could be seen.

“When I became the Young Cult Leader, I came up here with my father.”

I looked at Father standing beside me. I pictured Father in his youth, and imagined Grandfather standing beside him.

“My father’s dream was the unification of the murim.”

That was a fact even I had not known.

After a brief pause, I asked Father,

“What about you, Father?”

Father gave no answer. I know. Father dreams of unifying the murim. Father also knows that I know that fact.

I brought out words that were difficult to say. They were words I would have to say at least once someday.

“Even if you accomplish the great undertaking of unifying the murim and come to receive the praise and respect of everyone in the world… you will not receive my respect.”

They were words that might have made him burst into anger, but Father said nothing. Because by now, he knew well what sort of feelings his son held.

In silence, we looked at the Heavenly Demon Cult far away, and beyond it, the horizon and sky stretching out.

To ease the heavy atmosphere, I spoke in a cheerful tone.

“Father, please give names to my friends. I thought hard about whether there were any cool names. With ghosts as the theme, I considered Night Ghost, Blood Ghost, White Ghost, and Heavenly Ghost, but when I thought about it, they seemed too gloomy. So I thought I might use the moon as a more elegant theme, like Bright Moon, Waning Moon, Hidden Moon, and Radiant Moon, or if not that, then colors as the theme…”

It was just then. Father abruptly said,

“East, west, south, north.”

For a moment, I flinched.

“You are joking, right?”
