Father looked at me with a face that held not the slightest hint of laughter.

“Not even Plum, Orchid, Chrysanthemum, and Bamboo, not even Clear Wind and Bright Moon, but east, west, south, and north?”

I could not tell whether he meant I should not give needless names to evil spirits, whether it was because of what I had said earlier about unifying the murim, or whether he truly thought east, west, south, and north were good.

“East, west, south, north!”

He spoke once more, as if confirming it.

What could I do when Father was acting like this?

Ah, I am sorry. My evil spirits!

“Oh, now that I hear it, it sounds good. East Ghost, West Ghost, South Ghost, North Ghost. Easy to memorize, too. Perfect. If anyone asks, I will tell them my father named them! I’ll boast about it too.”

I’m telling you, I’ll expose that Father named them! Even so, Father did not budge in the slightest.

In the end, the names of the four evil spirits were decided as east, west, south, and north!

* * *

After descending Mount Daecheon, I headed straight for Tongcheon Pavilion.

It was to hear from Chief Strategist Sima Myeong what I had to do next.

As I entered Tongcheon Pavilion, the strategists I encountered offered respectful greetings.

Their gazes were friendly. Given the nature of their work, they were people who knew me better than anyone, so there was a need to pay attention to the change in their gazes.

After passing through several layers of strict security and entering the operations room of Tongcheon Pavilion, Sima Myeong was in a meeting with the strategists in the conference room.

The strategist who greeted me said he would notify Sima Myeong, but I told him to leave it be, and waited in the operations room until the meeting ended.

That place was truly bustling.

Through the dozen or so openings installed in one wall and divided by region, dispatches continued to arrive, and the strategists sorted them according to the matters they concerned.

Some strategists ran here and there with documents in hand, while others were busily writing something down. Another strategist was moving flags of dozens of different colors stuck into a map of the Central Plains to other locations. There was not a single person who was not busy.

The murim was peaceful, but this place was a battlefield. No, perhaps the murim was peaceful because this place was the battlefield.

A short while later, Sima Myeong came out after finishing the meeting and was startled when he saw me.

“Young Cult Leader, when did you arrive?”

“A little while ago. I told them I would wait until the meeting ended, so not to disturb you.”

“Let us go to my office.”

“Please speak here. Seeing the strategists at work is wonderful. It also makes me think I should live more diligently.”

“That is not something someone who has just emerged from closed-door training should say. Please rest now.”

“That is also not something someone who surely has a whole pile of missions to assign me should say.”

Sima Myeong and I looked at each other and laughed.

“How was your training?”

“As soon as I came out, I ran to Father to boast without even shaving my beard.”

The word boast contained all the results.

“Congratulations.”

“It is thanks to you, Strategist, for coordinating the external schedule. I imagine there must be many things I have to do piled up. What should I start with?”

“First, you must visit the Hunan Branch and the Jiangxi Branch. The Hunan Branch is the place that would clash first if war broke out with the Murim Alliance, and Jiangxi is a key strategic point for dealing with the Unorthodox Alliance. Therefore, visiting those two branches and meeting the branch leaders there is the first task the Young Cult Leader must carry out.”

“Understood.”

“I will send the official mission order tomorrow morning.”

“Yes, I will see you after I return.”

I was about to turn and leave when Sima Myeong spoke.

“From now on, Young Cult Leader, you will have a great influence on the fate of our cult.”

I smiled and said to him,

“That is why I intend to visit you often, Strategist.”

* * *

I had left Tongcheon Pavilion and was walking back to my residence.

On a quiet path, a masked man appeared before me. The person who released his concealment and revealed himself was Hwi, the captain of the Heavenly Demon Hall guards.

“Uncle!”

“Young Cult Leader.”

“Please just call me Geuk, as you did when I was young.”

“I cannot do that.”

Seeing him after so long stirred my emotions.

“How long has it been since I last saw you? There is a lot more white hair on your head, Uncle.”

“I am aging too.”

“It’s because serving our father is hard. He is terribly exacting, isn’t he? He torments you, doesn’t he?”

“It is the greatest joy of my life.”

The Hwi I knew truly was such a person. A man for whom only the duty of guarding mattered. A man for whom Father was everything.

“You once carried me on your back on a snowy day.”

When Hwi smiled, his two eyes within the mask curved like moons.

“You remember that?”

“Of course. I loved it so much back then.”

After we exchanged greetings like that, Hwi revealed the reason he had sought me out.

“When you leave the cult this time, please take the guards with you.”

I could not answer readily. Originally, I had planned to go alone. He had expected that, which was why he had come to me.

“To be frank, I am not saying this for your sake, Young Cult Leader. I know as well that their skills are at a level where they may instead become a burden.”

Hwi did not speak in circles.

“It is for my juniors. If those children are with you, Young Cult Leader, they will grow into excellent guards. I have watched your actions of late. The Young Cult Leader I saw was the sea. A sea vast enough to embrace all of my juniors.”

Given his personality, it could not have been easy for him to come to me and say such things. That was how much he cherished the Heavenly Demon Guard and his juniors.

That day, they all died with us.

If it was the request of Hwi, who had devoted his entire life to Father, then yes, I should take not twelve but even twelve hundred with me.

“I will go with them.”

Hwi respectfully bowed his head.

“Thank you.”

“No, Uncle.”

I also respectfully bowed my head to Hwi.

When I raised my head again, Hwi had already disappeared. He was not a man who repaid debts with words, so someday he would try to repay today’s debt.

Uncle Hwi, there is no need. You and your juniors have already repaid it with your lives.

* * *

My guards were training in the martial training ground.

I watched them train for a while, then stepped inside.

They stopped training and greeted me all at once with cupped fists.

“We greet the Young Cult Leader!”

There was spirit in their resounding greeting.

“Have you been training continuously for the past hundred days?”

Jeokyeon stepped forward and answered.

“Yes, we trained without missing a single day.”

“Then why are there only six of you?”

“Since we do not know when we will be assigned to guard duty, we have divided our lives into a day shift and a night shift. And once a month, we switch days and nights.”

In other words, the night shift trained from evening until morning. I truly thought it was remarkable.

Since it had been a long time since I had seen him, I first checked Jeokyeon’s eyes.

Knowing that refusing would be useless, Jeokyeon obediently removed his eyepatch.

I looked into his crimson pupils and said,

“The realm of your Demon Eye Art has risen by one stage.”

At that, Jeokyeon was startled.

“That is correct. Could you have realized that just by looking at the color of my eyes?”

“The color has changed from before.”

“Even I did not notice despite looking in a bronze mirror every day.”

I had distinguished the difference with the Divine Eye Art.

The reason I kept being drawn to his eyes was also because of the Divine Eye Art. I had learned the Divine Eye Art for my own sake, while he had learned the Demon Eye Art for someone else. And that someone else had ultimately become me.

Though all of that was his own choice and fate, sacrificing one’s eyes for another was never an easy thing.

I poured true qi into his eyes and regulated them for him.

“It feels different.”

“How?”

“The pain has decreased much more.”

If so, it was likely because of the change in my internal energy. It had become far more profound and pure.

“Tomorrow, you leave the cult with me. An official mission order will arrive from Tongcheon Pavilion early in the morning.”

Jeokyeon’s and the guard warriors’ expressions brightened. At last, they would carry out their first mission.

Their excitement and joy, which they could not hide, reached me. Seeing how happy they were, it would have been a serious mistake to leave them behind.

“Thank you for trusting us.”

“Guard me well from tomorrow on. I am going relying only on you.”

With a resounding answer, Jeokyeon and the guard warriors bowed their heads. Yes, since we are leaving anyway, let us return after growing even further.

The next morning, two carriages left the cult.

It was my first departure from the cult as the Young Cult Leader, formally accompanied by guards.

Chapter 261: My Murim Is Not Far Away.
--------------------------------------
The carriage was different from the one I usually rode in.

It was a special carriage used by the Heavenly Demon Hall’s escort unit. Though light, it was sturdy, and would not break from ordinary external impacts. It was pulled by powerful horses, and even had horse armor ready to be fitted in an emergency.

As the day-shift guards rode alongside the carriage to escort it, Jeokyeon rode inside with me and explained the details of the escort mission.

“The night shift is riding in the carriage behind us. They will take turns resting, then switch duties with us in the evening.”

The same applied to the day shift, but I had no need for guards at night either. If someone approached while I was sleeping, the Heavenly Demon Protection Art would wake me.

Even so, I left things as they were. What Hwi wanted was for these men to become excellent guards, and the thing they needed most to achieve that was experience.

Of course, there was something I had to do to speed up that process.

“Have the carriage stopped for a moment.”

Jeokyeon ordered one of his subordinates on the coach box to stop the carriage.

Once the night-shift carriage had joined us, he gathered everyone in front of me.

Then I walked over to the carriage. The Heavenly Demon Hall’s emblem had originally been carved into its exterior, but that part had been covered up for now.

Shrrrraaaak!

I tore off the covering in one clean motion. The figure of a terrifying demon was revealed.

“From now on, we will travel without concealing our identities. You are to change into the official uniforms of the Heavenly Demon Hall’s escort unit as well.”

The guards had been wearing ordinary martial robes.

Jeokyeon said in surprise, “That is not permitted by regulations.”

“We make our own regulations. Do as I command.”

Conflict flickered in Jeokyeon’s eyes as he looked at me, but he did not deliberate for long. While guarding me, they had already strayed far beyond the existing rules.

He ordered his subordinates to change their clothes.

Jeokyeon and all the guards changed. The official uniform of the Heavenly Demon Hall’s escort unit was simple yet striking. A shield was embroidered over the left chest of their black martial robes, and within it was the demon symbolizing the Heavenly Demon Hall. The black masks they wore were embroidered with the single character 魔, “Demon,” in a bold hand. That alone was enough.

The moment they put on their official uniforms, their entire atmosphere changed.

I looked at them one by one and said,

“You look good. Of all the uniforms in our cult, I think yours are the finest.”

They smiled faintly and glanced at one another.

“I know that moving around like this will make things much harder for you. It will be more dangerous, and unexpected variables will arise. If you were ordinary guards, I would not go this far. But you are the men who will guard the Heavenly Demon from now on.”

At the mention of the Heavenly Demon, their eyes sharpened.

“When my father moves officially, has he ever concealed his identity? That is why. From this moment on, you will gain experience and practice as well. You are openly guarding the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.”

“Yes! We will protect you with our lives!”

“Please don’t give your lives. Just protect me. Understood?”

My joke eased the tension a little.

Perhaps Jeokyeon thought this was a good opportunity, with everyone gathered, because he told me the numbers each man had been assigned.

“During escort operations, we address one another as Guards One through Twelve. Young Cult Leader, please call us that way as well.”

Jeokyeon was Guard One, and the numbers had been assigned according to their years of service in the escort unit. The youngest was Guard Twelve. He looked young at a glance and said he was only eighteen.

I walked over and stood in front of Guard Twelve. He was far too young to die protecting someone else.

“Do your seniors bully you because you’re the youngest?”

“It’s hard because they treat me too well just because I’m the youngest!”

After patting the youngest man’s shoulder once for giving the right answer, I climbed into the carriage.

“Let’s go!”

The carriage set off again.

Jeokyeon could have ridden in the carriage with me, but he rode alongside the other guards on horseback. It seemed he had only boarded the carriage earlier to explain the escort mission.

Though he was their leader, he worked on equal terms with his subordinates. In fact, he took the lead and worked even harder. Perhaps Hwi had thought highly of Jeokyeon because of that aspect of him, and had come to meet me personally after seeing his potential to become an excellent guard.

Our first destination was the Hunan Branch of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

It would take a considerable amount of time to get there by carriage, so I settled in for a leisurely journey.

It was a good opportunity to practice the Heavenly Demon Protection Art.

The carriage was of such excellent quality that it barely shook. I should be able to focus comfortably on the Heavenly Demon Protection Art.

Only two stages remained before I completed it. My goal on this journey outside the cult was to advance by one stage.

How long had we been traveling like that?

Jeokyeon shouted from outside.

“We’ll take a short rest.”

The carriage came to a stop.

While the horses rested, I also climbed down from the carriage and sat on a rock.

The guards stood watch in every direction around me. They were concentrating fully as they scanned their surroundings.

What did I honestly feel?

If they were spread out in every direction like that, how was I supposed to protect them?

“Jeokyeon.”

“Yes, Young Cult Leader.”

Jeokyeon, who had been standing guard in front, approached me.

“Show me the martial arts you’ve learned.”

At my words, Jeokyeon was startled. He realized that I intended to examine his martial arts.

“This isn’t an opportunity that comes often, so hurry.”

“Yes, Young Cult Leader!”

The mission was important, but he was not foolish enough to let such an opportunity pass.

“The martial art I have learned is the Secret Shadow Sword Art.”

After stating the name of the sword art he had learned, he began performing its techniques.

As it was considered proper etiquette not to watch another person’s signature martial art, the guards turned their backs.

Jeokyeon performed all the techniques of the Secret Shadow Sword Art.

“It is a good sword art.”

I corrected his bad habits and posture, then told him how he should train from now on. I did not tell him many things. I pointed out only one thing—the single most important thing.

“Thank you!”

Jeokyeon expressed his gratitude with a deeply moved look, while the other guards could not hide their envy.

“There’s no need to be so envious. Guard Two, step forward!”

When I said I would examine everyone’s martial arts, Guard Two spoke in confusion.

“You’re busy. You can examine ours later.”

“You’re the ones who are busy.”

“Pardon?”

“The branch leader can be seen at leisure. We’re only visiting to pay our respects, after all. But your martial arts need to be improved as quickly as possible. If sword qi came flying at your back right now, could you block it? So come on—show me your martial arts!”

Without wasting any more time, Guard Two performed his martial arts. As I had done with Jeokyeon, I told him what he absolutely needed to correct and how he should train from then on.

I examined the other guards in turn as well. It did not take long. I could tell the answer the moment I saw them.

I could feel their excitement. They had never before received instruction at such a high level. They still had so much to learn that, with a little exaggeration, they would grow stronger with every word I said.

“Thank you. We will work harder and improve ourselves from now on.”

I was telling them not to die this time.

That night, I pointed out the same flaws to the night-shift guards and told them what they needed to correct.

* * *

On the third day after leaving the cult—

Krrrrumble!

Thunder rang out, and then rain began pouring down. There was so much rain that it became impossible to drive the carriage.

“We’ll have to stop for a while.”

Jeokyeon stopped the carriage beneath a great tree.

The guards stood around the carriage in the rain.

“Don’t stand there getting soaked like fools. All of you, get inside the carriage.”

“We’re fine.”

“That is an order.”

Jeokyeon tried to resist, but in the end he could not defy my will and came inside.

Six men entered, but it was a large carriage, so everyone could sit facing one another. I handed them things to dry themselves with and said,

“There’s no need to feel uncomfortable. Don’t worry about me. Just watch the rain outside.”

I heated the water in the kettle with the Samadhi True Flame and brewed warm tea for them. At the sight of this, they all opened their eyes wide.

“Thank you. We’ll drink it gratefully!”

They were still stiff and found it difficult to deal with me. Even when they looked out the windows, every one of them stared through the window opposite me.

We watched the falling rain in silence.

Whoooshhhhhh!

Sitting huddled together, sipping tea and watching the rain, had a certain charm to it. After dealing with the elderly Demon Venerables, being surrounded by these young men felt refreshing.

Jeokyeon spoke cautiously.

“We are supposed to protect you, Young Cult Leader, but for some reason, it feels as though we are the ones being protected.”

I smiled faintly and asked Jeokyeon,

“With enough skill to enter the Heavenly Demon Hall’s escort unit, you could have joined another organization as well. Why did you apply to become a guard?”

He answered without hesitation.

“I wanted to become a guard from a young age. Whenever I saw guards, they looked impressive. I thought protecting someone was an honorable thing.”

“Do you still feel the same way?”

Jeokyeon’s answer did not come as quickly as it had the first time.

“I feel the same.”

Because of the pain in his eyes, I wondered if he might regret his choice. It was difficult work even without that, and his eyes hurt so badly they felt as though they might die.

Just then, my eyes met Guard Three’s. Of the day-shift guards, he had the gentlest appearance.

“What about you?”

“I thought I had more talent for protecting people than killing them.”

“What does it mean to have a talent for protecting?”

“I don’t find protecting someone boring. I think it may be because I’m introverted by nature.”

Then Guard Nine answered without even being asked.

“I decided that escort duty was relatively safe. I want to live a long time.”

His companions must have heard him say that before, because they all smiled. Judging by his manner, he seemed to enjoy making his comrades laugh and bantering with them.

In any case, those were words I could never have heard from Hwi. He had lived through the Age of Devotion, and he was still living in it.

But these young martial artists were certainly different from their seniors. That did not mean I thought they had less of a sense of duty than Hwi, nor did I think Hwi old-fashioned compared to their rational and individualistic attitudes. I simply felt that the times were changing.

As I sat there with all sorts of thoughts running through my mind, the downpour began to ease.

As if he had been waiting for that moment, Jeokyeon said,

“We’ll depart now.”

Before they got out, I asked,

“Is anyone from Hunan Province?”

They looked bewildered at the sudden question.

“No one?”

Then Guard Three, who had earlier said that protecting people suited him, stepped forward.

“My hometown is here in Hunan.”

“Are your parents there?”

“My mother lives there alone.”

“When was the last time you saw her?”

“It’s been about three years.”

“Let’s stop by.”

Guard Three was startled.

“No, it’s all right. We’re on a mission. We can’t do that.”

“It’s fine if we’re a few days late. How eager could the Hunan Branch Leader be to see me? But how much must your mother want to see you? It would be rude to come all this way and leave without stopping by. This isn’t a request. It’s an order, so let’s go!”

And so the carriage began rolling toward Guard Three’s hometown.

I know well that all kinds of people live in every corner of the Central Plains, and that all sorts of things happen there. I know every kind of conspiracy and scheme, how far people can fall, and how cruel they can become. I also know how many martial artists lose their way and wander through that forest of drawn swords and perilous blades.

My martial world is not far away. Jochunbae at the Romantic Tavern is my martial world, and the hometown of Guard Three, who protects me, is my martial world. This is how I keep from losing my way in the martial world.

The carriage continued on, cutting through the clear sky as though it had never rained.

* * *

The carriage, traveling not far from Guard Three’s hometown village, began to slow down. I lifted the curtain and looked outside. A carriage lay smashed to pieces beside the road. Bloodstains remained here and there on the ground, along with traces of a fierce battle.

Jeokyeon’s voice came from outside.

“I’ll find out what happened.”

Guard Two went to ask a nearby resident what had happened here and returned with an answer.

“A few days ago, a convoy from the Hunan First Escort Agency was attacked and robbed. Several escort leaders, escort warriors, and convoy porters were reportedly killed in the process.”

“Isn’t the Hunan First Escort Agency the largest escort agency in this area?”

“That is correct.”

Ordinarily, no one dared to interfere with the escort convoy of the largest escort agency in the region. An establishment on the level of the Hunan First Escort Agency had deep ties with various martial arts sects, and anyone who rashly provoked it would face retaliation.

Something extraordinary had happened—something that normally would never occur.

Of course, it was not our concern, so I immediately said to Jeokyeon,

“Come, let’s pick up the pace.”

“Yes!”

The carriage began racing along at an even greater speed.

* * *

The carriage arrived at Samho’s hometown village.

“It’s over there.”

In the direction Samho pointed stood a small inn.

It was the Dohyeon Inn, named after him. On the way, I had heard that he had opened it for his mother with the money he earned after joining our cult.

“Running an inn was Mother’s dream.”

Just because someone was a demonic cultivator, did that mean there were no filial sons among them?

We stopped the carriage in front of the inn.

When we entered, there was neither a customer nor an owner in sight. Startled, Samho called out loudly for his mother.

“Mother!”

But no one answered. Since it was rare for an inn to close, worry flashed across Samho’s face.

I went into the kitchen and examined the traces left by the fire in the stove.

“This kitchen was still in business until yesterday. So do not worry.”

Just then, a bright-looking child entered the inn.

“We’re not open today. The proprietress and the cook are at Fengshui Manor.”

The child introduced himself as someone who came to work as a waiter whenever things got busy.

I asked him in a friendly tone,

“Do you know why they went there?”

“Martial artists from Fengshui Manor came this morning and took them there because they needed someone to prepare food.”

If that was the reason they had closed the inn, then it was actually a relief. When a martial arts sect needed a cook, they sometimes mobilized the people from nearby inns in this manner.

“Where is Fengshui Manor?”

“It’s at the end of the road to the west.”

“Thank you.”

When I pressed a coin into the child’s hand, he happily thanked me and left.

This time, I asked Samho,

“Do you know anything about Fengshui Manor?”

“Not much. After leaving my hometown when I was young to train in martial arts, I only visited Mother once every two or three years.”

He lowered his head deeply, but no one would think he had done anything wrong. Even amid his busy life, he had faithfully come to see his mother once every two or three years. He was the most filial son of all.

“Young Cult Master, please wait a moment. I will clean the guest room first.”

Samho acted calm, as though nothing was wrong, but how could I not know what he was thinking? He must have wanted to run straight to his mother’s side. Hearing that she had been taken by a martial arts sect, he must have been worried in more ways than one.

I rose from my seat and said,

“Mother is working while we young people sit around doing nothing. We should go help her and bring her back early.”

At my unexpected words, Samho waved his hands, insisting that it was all right, but I climbed into the carriage. All the guards mounted their horses, and Samho mounted his as well. Though he continued saying it was unnecessary, irrepressible joy briefly crossed his face.

“Let’s go—to Fengshui Manor!”

The carriage began racing toward Fengshui Manor.

Chapter 262: If It Tastes Bad, I’ll Die.
----------------------------------------
Hwasun’s hands trembled with fear.

“If it doesn’t taste good, you’ll be a dead woman too!”

The words spoken by the martial artist who had brought her here kept ringing in her ears as he left the kitchen.

When she had first been dragged to Pungsu Villa, she had never dreamed that the atmosphere would be like this. They said that the life of a merchant was to go when a martial household told them to go and leave when they told them to leave, but she had never imagined it would be so murderous.

And those words had not been a simple threat.

“Be careful. Two people have already died in this kitchen today.”

A maid had secretly told her that while bringing ingredients to the kitchen. Hwasun was acquainted with her. Whenever the maid went out to buy groceries, she would sometimes stop by the inn to buy noodles or dumplings. Hwasun had always given her generous portions, telling her to eat more.

“A terrifying master came to the villa, and because he was so picky about food, he pulled out the villa cook’s tongue and killed him. The cook from Bukhwa Village who was dragged here afterward died even more horribly. What are we going to do?”

The maid’s appearance as she trembled in fear and stamped her feet had seemed strangely unreal, as though it were someone else’s problem.

Hwasun kept making mistakes while cooking. She added the wrong seasonings, and her trembling hands even caused her to drop a bowl.

She knew how skilled the cook from Bukhwa Village was. If even his cooking had failed to satisfy them, she would meet the same fate. Had they really killed him?

What if she died?

Her chest grew heavy, and her son’s face rose in her mind. She missed him terribly. It was about time for him to come visit again.

That was when it happened.

“Mother!”

Startled, she turned her head. Her son was standing at the entrance to the kitchen.

“Hyun! Is that you, Hyun? Hyun!”

She was so shocked that he worried she might collapse.

Samho rushed over and embraced his mother.

“Mother!”

“Hyun!”

She clung to her son, too moved to get a word out.

But soon, she lowered her voice and spoke.

“Why did you come here? Go, quickly! Go wait at the inn!”

“Mother, what’s wrong?”

“Go! I’ll tell you later, so go now.”

Hwasun could think of nothing but somehow sending her son away.

Unlike his frantic mother, Samho calmly took her hand.

“You’ve gotten so thin.”

His mother had aged so much over the past three years. It was obvious that she had suffered greatly.

When she held her son’s hand, tears streamed from Hwasun’s eyes. Even amid her fear, she was so glad and happy to see him. She was so overjoyed that the tears would not stop.

Seeing his mother cry, Samho shed tears as well.

“I’m sorry I couldn’t come see you more often.”

“No, no. You must have been busy working. It’s all right, it’s fine. Now that we’ve greeted each other, go wait at the inn.”

She was in a hurry. If they stayed here and caught the villa’s people’s attention, something terrible could happen.

“Go! I’ll cook you a meal later.”

Hwasun tried to push her son away. Seeing his face was enough for her.

She knew only that her son had joined the Demonic Cult; she did not know what work he was doing there. He must be going through all sorts of hardships to establish himself. That was all she could assume.

Just then, more people entered the kitchen. It was Geom Mugeuk and his colleagues from the patrol unit.

Hwasun was startled, thinking that the villa’s martial artists had come.

“My son just came by for a moment. He’ll be leaving soon. We’re going now.”

Geom Mugeuk strode toward her and politely bowed.

“It is a pleasure to meet you, Mother.”

Hwasun stared at him in surprise, and Samho introduced him.

“He’s the person I serve.”

Caught off guard, Hwasun was startled again. She immediately tried to bow deeply to Geom Mugeuk, but he quickly stopped her and took her hand.

“My name is Mugeuk. Please treat me as you would your own son.”

The moment Hwasun looked at Geom Mugeuk, the turmoil in her heart, which had been like a battlefield, quietly settled.

It was probably because of his eyes. She had seen countless people while running her business, but she had never seen anyone with such clear eyes.

She felt comforted and reassured. With eyes like those, he was someone she could trust with her son.

“It is an honor to meet such a distinguished person.”

She did not know Geom Mugeuk’s identity, so she assumed he was the young master her son served.

“Please take good care of my son.”

“Thanks to your son, I have been doing very well. You have a wonderful son.”

“That is because you have looked after him so well, Young Master. Thank you.”

Samho then introduced his other companions.

“These are my colleagues.”

Jeok Yeon and the escorting martial artists greeted her.

Speaking on their behalf, Jeok Yeon said,

“Hyun is so capable that we rely on his help quite a lot.”

“Please take good care of my son.”

Hwasun felt sorry. If only they had met under different circumstances. She would have cooked for them herself and served them delicious food. This was the first time her son had brought his colleagues to meet her.

‘I have to get them out of here.’

She feared that if the villa discovered so many of them together, the situation would become even worse. Her own son was precious to her, but she might also put the precious sons of other families in danger.

They were all so young. There were dozens of martial artists in Pungsu Villa, and, on top of that, a terrifying master was said to have arrived.

“You should take the Young Master down to the inn and wait there. There’s food in the kitchen that can be served to him, so make sure he eats properly. Go! Hurry!”

At that moment, a man’s voice came from outside.

“Is the food finished? I told you clearly. If it doesn’t taste good, your life is—”

The martial artist entering the kitchen flinched.

The men inside were lined up on either side, staring at him.

“Y-you! Who are you people?”

The man tried to draw his sword, but his arm would not move. His blood-acupoints had been subdued before he knew it.

‘When did that happen?’

Geom Mugeuk approached him and casually draped an arm over his shoulder.

“You said you’d die if the food didn’t taste good? Why?”

His tone was friendly, but Geom Mugeuk’s eyes were colder than ice.

“I-I…”

“No matter. We’ll find out when we meet him in person.”

Geom Mugeuk went to Hwasun and asked,

“Mother, is the food ready?”

“It’s gone cold, so I think I’ll have to heat it up again.”

“That’s all right. I’m rather good at reheating things. Today, I’ll make it very hot.”

Geom Mugeuk placed the dishes she had made onto a tray and picked it up.

Then he gave an order to the escorts.

“Your mission today is to protect Mother. Guard her with the same devotion you would use to protect your own parents.”

Samho and the escorts formed up around Hwasun. It was the formal formation used when six people guarded a single person.

Geom Mugeuk kicked the martial artist whose blood-acupoints had been subdued.

“Let’s go.”

At some point, the martial artist’s blood-acupoints had been released. Terrified by Geom Mugeuk’s astonishing martial prowess, he took the lead.

‘As soon as we reach a place with more people!’

The martial artist led the way, followed by Geom Mugeuk carrying the tray.

Six escorting martial artists brought up the rear, surrounding Hwasun as they walked.

Hwasun was still anxious, but her son’s reassuring expression beside her put her at ease. She did not know what was happening, but she was simply happy to see her son like this. She prayed and prayed that everything would end safely today.

Many of the villa’s martial artists were gathered in the spacious front courtyard.

The martial artist in front sent them a meaningful glance.

‘What are you doing? Hurry and subdue these men!’

But something astonishing happened. The martial artists passing by, as well as those rushing toward them, all froze like stone statues.

Their blood-acupoints and speech-acupoints had been subdued before anyone noticed.

The martial artist leading the way was horrified.

‘He’s subduing them while carrying a tray? How?’

It was as if poison were spreading through the air. The moment they met Geom Mugeuk’s eyes, their blood-acupoints and speech-acupoints were subdued. Those nearby and those far away, experts and weaklings alike—no one was an exception.

“Ah!”

Jeok Yeon and the escorting martial artists cried out in astonishment at the incredible sight unfolding before them. They had heard rumors that the Young Cult Master’s martial arts had surpassed those of the Demon Venerable, but they had never imagined they were this formidable.

Thus, they entered the building and headed toward the residence of the master of Pungsu Villa.

The martial artists guarding the entrance froze as well. One had been about to ask, “Who are you people?” and was left standing with his mouth open, while the one beside him had stopped midway through raising his eyes, leaving himself in a bizarre pose.

Inside the room, two men were engaged in conversation.

Seated in the seat of honor was the Bloodhand Sword. Having committed all manner of evil deeds, he had already been designated a public enemy of the martial world by the Martial Alliance.

The man seated opposite him was Pungsu Villa’s master, Yi Eom.

“Even so, that is far too unreasonable a demand.”

“Not only the First Escort Agency of Hunan, but every escort agency in the Central Plains will regard me as an enemy. Shouldn’t I receive a larger share?”

“Didn’t we agree to divide it into three equal portions from the beginning?”

“Now that I think about it, that isn’t fair. You and that man can sit in your rooms and take a third each, can’t you?”

“I’m the one who provided the information for this operation.”

“Then reduce his share.”

“You know who he is, and you still say that?”

“Do you think I don’t know who I am?”

Yi Eom sighed inwardly. This was why one should never join hands with people like him.

He had vehemently opposed the plan, but that man had been so stubborn that it had come to this in the end. Perhaps he had anticipated this situation. Since his own share would not be taken away, he was telling Yi Eom to deal with the consequences himself.

At that moment, the door opened, and Geom Mugeuk entered.

Since Geom Mugeuk revealed no presence at all, the two men took him for a servant bringing in the food.

Geom Mugeuk set the dishes down on the table.

The Bloodhand Sword said to him,

“Wait there for a moment.”

He picked up his chopsticks and took a bite of the food.

“Ptui!”

He immediately spat it onto the floor.

Yi Eom frowned at the sight. The reason the Bloodhand Sword had already brutally killed two cooks was not that the food had tasted bad. He was pressuring Yi Eom. He was saying, I can kill you like this too if I take offense. Hand over more money.

The Bloodhand Sword was a level above him in martial arts, so Yi Eom had no choice but to endure this humiliation. The only reason the man could not act recklessly was that other person.

Even so, Yi Eom could not yield. If he gave the Bloodhand Sword everything he wanted, he would gain nothing despite involving himself in this extremely dangerous affair. After all, he was the one who had discovered that this escort mission was an enormous opportunity, the kind that came only once every few years, if that.

“Who made this?”

“Madam Hwa prepared the dishes.”

“Where is that woman now?”

“She is outside the door.”

“Tell her to come in at once!”

“Why are you doing this?”

“She made such tasteless food, so her tongue must be pulled out. What use is there in keeping such a useless tongue?”

Geom Mugeuk turned his head toward the door, and it opened as though it had been waiting for him.

Hwasun stood in the corridor outside. Three escorts stood on either side of her. At the front were Jeok Yeon and Samho.

The Bloodhand Sword assumed they were martial artists from Pungsu Villa.

He glared at Yi Eom and said,

“What are you trying to pull?”

Yi Eom could not understand what was happening. He had never ordered anyone to protect that woman. And now that he looked closely, those people were not his men.

Then Yi Eom noticed the emblem on Jeok Yeon’s chest.

‘That emblem… It couldn’t be, could it?’

Yi Eom grew tense inside. There was only one organization in the martial world that used a demon as its symbol. He had heard that ordinary members of the Demonic Cult were not permitted to wear that emblem; only its elite could.

‘Did that person send them? But why are they protecting that woman?’

Yi Eom’s thoughts became confused.

What happened next would astonish him even more.

Geom Mugeuk picked up a new pair of chopsticks and tasted the food.

“This is delicious. I think the problem is with your tongue.”

The Bloodhand Sword was so dumbfounded that he let out a hollow laugh. People did not suddenly go insane, so he assumed this too was something Yi Eom had arranged.

“What the hell are you doing?”

The Bloodhand Sword shouted furiously at Yi Eom.

Yi Eom said nothing, merely watching Geom Mugeuk and gauging his intentions.

Geom Mugeuk looked at the Bloodhand Sword and said coldly,

“Try it again.”

“Oh, you crazy bastard!”

Just as the Bloodhand Sword was about to cut down Geom Mugeuk first—

Geom Mugeuk fully unleashed his demonic qi. The immense demonic energy bore down upon Ieom.

‘Ugh!’

The Blood-Hand Sword could not move even a single finger.

‘What is this? Nghhhh!’

He had never encountered such terrifying demonic qi. The pressure of a mountain crushing down on him made it impossible to breathe. A fear that he might actually suffocate overwhelmed him. A master of his caliber dying from nothing more than someone’s aura? It sounded absurd, yet that was exactly what was happening.

He had lived his entire life without fearing anything, but in this moment, he felt the terror of death. Even if his opponent were a demon, he was not the sort to have his spirit broken. Yet now, he could not even dare to look this young opponent in the eye.

Then the demonic qi pressing down on him eased slightly.

“Eat it again.”

Unable to defy those words, the Blood-Hand Sword ate the dish.

“……It’s delicious.”

“Why are you telling me that? You should tell the person who cooked it.”

The Blood-Hand Sword turned his head and looked at Hwasun.

“It was delicious.”

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk spoke.

“Again!”

This time, he said it politely.

“It’s delicious, Madam Hwa.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled and said to Hwasun,

“He says it was delicious.”

Hwasun nodded once, her heart trembling.

“Please wait a moment. I have something to discuss with these men before I leave.”

The door closed, and the guards led Hwasun away to the end of the corridor.

The very moment the door shut—

Crack!

Geom Mugeuk drove the chopsticks he was holding into the Blood-Hand Sword’s forehead.

The Blood-Hand Sword collapsed then and there, smashing his head against the table as he breathed his last.

Ieom was so shocked that he felt as though his heart were about to leap out of his mouth. He had not even known that the Blood-Hand Sword could be killed with a single move. Geom Mugeuk had driven the chopsticks in without even looking at the man sitting beside him.

The Blood-Hand Sword had survived despite committing every sort of evil deed imaginable. To see him killed by a pair of chopsticks, without a word being spoken, was a tremendous shock.

“You said the stolen goods were to be divided into three shares?”

“I’ll give up everything! Take my share as well!”

“Who is the remaining person?”

Ieom spoke in a trembling voice.

“Wasn’t he the one who sent you? Was he trying to take this entire haul for himself?”

“I asked who that person is.”

When Ieom hesitated, wondering what on earth was happening, Geom Mugeuk picked up the remaining chopstick. There was no need for him to threaten Ieom by prodding his cheek or forehead.

Now that Ieom had seen it disappear into the Blood-Hand Sword’s forehead, he could not withstand the chopstick.

“It’s Branch Leader Yeo!”

Only then did Geom Mugeuk understand why they had mistakenly thought that he had sent them.

“He’s Yeo Sogwang, the Branch Leader of the Hunan Branch of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult!”

Chapter 263: The Food My Mother Makes.
--------------------------------------
“The Branch Master of the Demonic Cult is behind the escort caravan robbery?”

At the mention of a name that should never have come up, Geom Mugeuk’s aura turned cold.

“Branch Master Yeo agreed to handle the aftermath so that this matter wouldn’t grow any larger.”

As the Branch Master of the Heavenly Demon Divine Church’s Hunan Branch, he was a man with enough power in this region to make such a promise.

“What proof is there that he was involved?”

“Proof… There is none. He isn’t the sort of person who leaves evidence behind in matters like this. I am the witness.”

The moment I Eom uttered Yeo Sogwang’s name, he crossed a bridge from which there was no return. He had to abandon Yeo Sogwang and find a way to survive on his own.

“What if you’re slandering the Branch Master?”

“Then you may kill me.”

Judging by his attitude thus far and that desperate look in his eyes, he was telling the truth. In fact, it was not particularly shocking or surprising.

When he had first regressed, it was easy to understand if one thought of the Demon Lords. He had used the Demon Army for his own personal gain and amassed wealth. If until now he had merely kept the inside under control, the time had come to keep the outside under control as well.

“How did you learn about the Hunan First Escort Agency’s caravan this time?”

“I have one of my men among their escort leaders.”

“Who?”

“Yang Dae-nam.”

Once I Eom’s mouth had opened, he did not hesitate.

“Let’s start with what was plundered from this caravan.”

“Please, follow me.”

I Eom rose from his seat. His body felt heavy. He realized that, at some point, the inner power in his dantian had been suppressed.

‘I was subdued without even realizing it.’

I Eom realized that his opponent’s martial prowess far surpassed the realm of ordinary experts. The reason he had killed the Blood-Hand Sword with a single strike from a chopstick was certainly not because the Blood-Hand Sword had let his guard down. Perhaps he could have killed the Blood-Hand Sword with nothing more than a small wooden skewer used to pick one’s teeth.

‘He’s far stronger than Branch Master Yeo.’

His strength was one thing, but he showed not the slightest trace of fear toward Yeo Sogwang. A Branch Master of the Demonic Cult was a person of great power. Just who was this man?

When I Eom activated a mechanism hidden in the bookshelf, the wall revolved, revealing a secret chamber.

Inside were three fairly large chests, filled to the brim with gold and other valuables.

When Geom Mugeuk saw the wealth they had stolen, he became angry with both I Eom and Yeo Sogwang. This was worse than a group of penniless men committing a crime in hopes of striking it rich.

Both men already possessed considerable wealth, and there were countless ways for them to make money without resorting to such deeds. Yeo Sogwang in particular must have received a substantial amount of tribute from here and there.

And yet they had committed something like this?

Without revealing the anger rising within him, Geom Mugeuk asked calmly,

“Did the Blood-Hand Sword accomplish this alone?”

“Chief Escort Yang leaked the route in advance, which made things easy. We were able to set a trap beforehand and launch a surprise attack.”

“You even chose a despicable method.”

I Eom, who had been eagerly informing on everyone, shut his mouth and cautiously watched Geom Mugeuk’s expression.

“You have plenty of money. Why did you do something like this?”

“……”

“Not going to answer?”

“Money is… better the more you have, isn’t it?”

“If it were money you earned.”

I Eom could say nothing and lowered his head. He wanted to speak honestly. In a martial world where everyone preyed upon everyone else, wasn’t the one who got robbed simply an idiot? You think the same way, don’t you? You hypocritical bastard! He wanted to shout those words.

Geom Mugeuk left the chamber and issued an order to Jeok Yeon, who had been waiting in the corridor.

“Bring in the people in charge of the Yellow Springs Pavilion and Heaven-Penetrating Pavilion in this region immediately!”

“Yes, understood!”

I Eom was startled when he heard that. Through his dealings with Yeo Sogwang, he had heard enough to know that the Yellow Springs Pavilion and the Heaven-Penetrating Pavilion were core organizations of the Demonic Cult. He was summoning the heads of such core organizations just like that?

At that moment, I Eom’s gaze fell upon Jeok Yeon’s chest. When he had first seen him, he had noticed only the evil spirit. But now he could also see the shield surrounding it.

‘Could it be?’

He had once caught a glimpse of this symbol during a drinking party.

‘The Heavenly Demon Palace Guard?’

I Eom’s gaze turned toward Geom Mugeuk.

‘Then this man is… N-no way… the Young Cult Master of the Demonic Cult? Uwaaaah!’

I Eom’s heart sank. He had truly fallen into the worst possible predicament.

His legs gave out, and he prostrated himself flat against the floor.

“Please spare me! I failed to recognize such an exalted person!”

No matter how deeply I Eom bowed and begged, Geom Mugeuk’s gaze remained cold.

As if to prove that I Eom’s guess was correct, the Demonic Cult members in charge of the Yellow Springs Pavilion and Heaven-Penetrating Pavilion arrived like the wind.

Geom Mugeuk handled the matter without hesitation.

First, he explained the incident to the officials in charge. He informed them that, because the Branch Master of the Hunan Branch was involved, they could not use the men from that branch, and therefore the Yellow Springs Pavilion and Heaven-Penetrating Pavilion had to take action.

After finishing his explanation, Geom Mugeuk gave his first order to the special investigator from the Yellow Springs Pavilion.

“First, return the stolen escort goods and the Blood-Hand Sword’s corpse to the Hunan First Escort Agency and explain the situation. Also, deal with the escort leader named Yang Dae-nam, who leaked the information about this caravan.”

“Understood.”

I Eom watched the proceedings with a pounding heart when Geom Mugeuk turned to him and said,

“And send that man to the main cult and lock him in the prison.”

The prison of the Heavenly Demon Divine Church? I Eom’s vision went dark.

“No! Please, spare me!”

Geom Mugeuk asked indifferently,

“Did you really think you could commit such a grave offense and simply get away with it?”

“That’s why I confessed everything!”

“And that’s why I haven’t killed you, is it not?”

I Eom was rendered speechless. What he needed at a time like this was a bribe. But judging by the order to return the escort goods, it did not seem as though that would work either.

Geom Mugeuk gave him a choice.

“Fine, then choose! Do you want to go to our prison? Or do you want to go to the Hunan First Escort Agency?”

I Eom knew that both choices meant certain death.

“Please, spare me! I only came up with the plan!”

“This isn’t your first time doing something like this, is it?”

“It is.”

I Eom’s voice trembled, and he could not meet Geom Mugeuk’s gaze.

Could it really be his first time? Even when the Blood-Hand Sword brutally killed the cooks who had been dragged there, he had merely stood by and watched. That alone was enough to guess at the kind of life he had led.

“Of the three, you’re the worst of them all, in my opinion.”

Geom Mugeuk thrust out his fist without hesitation.

Baaam! Crack!

I Eom’s chest was shattered, and he died instantly.

Rumble!

Everyone there was startled by the sound of thunder that rang from the fist. It was as if the heavens themselves had passed judgment.

Geom Mugeuk continued issuing orders.

“Dismantle Wind-and-Water Manor, and distribute all the money obtained from it among the families of those who died this time.”

“Understood.”

He had wanted to help the families of the victims, but there was another reason the matter had to be handled in precisely this way.

“The Hunan First Escort Agency belongs to one of the orthodox sects, does it not?”

“Yes, that is correct.”

In some regions, the Heavenly Demon Divine Church held greater influence, while in others the Martial Alliance or the Unorthodox Alliance held sway. Naturally, the closer a region was to the main cult, the stronger its influence, and the farther away it was, the weaker it became.

Hunan Province was a place where the Heavenly Demon Divine Church’s power was strong.

But that did not mean every sect in the region followed and supported the Demonic Cult.

Though fewer in number, there were still many sects that followed the orthodox or unorthodox factions. Even here in Hunan Province, the Martial Alliance had branch altars and branches, and the same was true of the Unorthodox Alliance.

“Notify the Martial Alliance so there are no misunderstandings. Tell them that we will deal with Yeo Sogwang ourselves.”

The Heaven-Penetrating Pavilion maintained frequent contact with the Martial Alliance, so they would handle it properly.

“For the time being, place this village under special protection. It is where my guard’s mother lives.”

Jeok Yeon had been watching Geom Mugeuk handle the matter both decisively and meticulously from the side. Seeing this, he thought to himself:

The Young Cult Master truly was not a position that just anyone could fill.

After finishing his work, Geom Mugeuk went to Hwasun, who had been resting and waiting in the guest room.

“It’s all finished. Let’s head back now. I want to eat the food my mother makes.”

Hwasun felt relieved at those words. From Geom Mugeuk’s bright expression, she could tell that everything had been resolved successfully. The heart that had been pounding anxiously until the very end finally began to settle.

“Of course I should make it for you. Naturally, I should.”

“I told you to speak more comfortably.”

“I cannot do that. This is more comfortable for me, Young Master.”

When they went outside, the escort warriors once again formed a protective formation around Hwasun.

When they reached the carriage, Geom Mugeuk said to Samho and Hwasun,

“Only the two of you should ride in the carriage.”

Hwasun looked bewildered.

“So you can have a private talk with your son after such a long time apart. Come on, get in. We’re leaving.”

Geom Mugeuk climbed onto the driver’s seat. Samho protested that he could not do that, but the matter was settled with a single word from Geom Mugeuk:

“It’s an order.”

The carriage carrying Hwasun and Samho began heading toward the village.

How long had they been traveling?

Laughter could be heard from inside the carriage.

It was the most pleasant sound Geom Mugeuk had heard since leaving the cult.

Geom Mugeuk had no memories of his mother at all. She had died when he was very young. That was why, on days like this, he sometimes missed her.

‘Mother, your sons are living well without fighting one another.’

The succession struggle that had ended without bloodshed between him and his brother had also been for the sake of their late mother.

The carriage arrived at Hwasun’s inn.

When Hwasun stepped down from the carriage and saw her inn, she finally felt that she had returned safely.

Geom Mugeuk treated her warmly, like a son.

“Mother, we’re hungry!”

Hwasun rolled up her sleeves and went into the kitchen.

Geom Mugeuk and the escort warriors were able to enjoy a home-cooked meal prepared with a mother’s care for the first time in a long while. Her cooking was truly delicious.

Hwasun was delighted to see her son eating with his companions.

Overlapping with that scene was the memory of him coming home with the neighborhood children when he was young, all of them crowding into her house to eat.

‘When did you grow so much?’

Her little boy had become a grown man and was now eating with his companions. Her heart swelled with pride and emotion.

After cleaning out a bowl of rice, Geom Mugeuk said to her,

“I’d like to bring you to the main cult and eat the meals you make every day.”

She felt relieved. With her son serving such a good superior, she felt she no longer needed to worry about him.

She brought over more rice and side dishes.

“Young Master, you must have another bowl! I put in extra meat, too.”

Geom Mugeuk beamed at her words.

“I’ll cherish your son more than the other fellows!”

She laughed at his joke, and the guards laughed along with them.

Hwasun was sincerely grateful to Geom Mugeuk. She did not know in detail how the matter at the manor had been handled, but she could feel that it had been resolved because of him.

‘Thank you, Young Master.’

* * *

Geom Mugeuk and his party stayed there for another four days.

They should have left the next day, but he wanted to give Hwasun and Samho, who had reunited after three years, more time together.

Samho devoted himself to his filial duties to his heart’s content.

On the third day, he even took his mother to a nearby temple. They ate delicious food along the way and admired the scenic views. When his mother’s legs began to hurt, Samho carried her on his back.

When her son asked whether she would come live in Maga Village, she shook her head. She did not want to become a burden to her son, nor did she want to leave the hometown where she had spent her entire life.

Four days later, Geom Mugeuk and his party said a reluctant farewell to Hwasun and set off toward the Hunan Branch.

During a brief rest, Samho came to Geom Mugeuk.

“I heard that you ordered our hometown village to be placed under special protection.”

Jeok Yeon must have told him.

“Of course I did. From now on, visit your mother more often. That is an order, too.”

Samho felt it keenly this time. He had been far too neglectful of his mother, using his busy schedule as an excuse.

“Thank you so much. I will never forget the grace you have shown me.”

“Thanks to you, I got to eat my fill of your mother’s delicious food. I’m the one who should be grateful.”

When their rest ended, Jeok Yeon sat in the driver’s seat, saying that he would drive the carriage himself.

“I’ll travel as quickly as possible.”

“Let’s go slowly. We’re not in a hurry.”

Jeok Yeon asked with a puzzled expression,

“Shouldn’t we hurry?”

“Why?”

“If he realizes that his corruption has been exposed, might he not flee?”

There was no chance of that. The Yellow Springs Pavilion and Heaven-Penetrating Pavilion did not handle matters so carelessly. At least until they arrived, they would ensure that he knew nothing.

“Resign as Branch Master because of this incident? That would be unlikely. But if he did, that would be punishment enough in its own way. He’d spend the rest of his life regretting and lamenting it. Still, that won’t happen.”

I Eom had said that Yeo Sogwang had left no evidence of his involvement in this matter. There was no reason for him to become frightened and flee preemptively.

“They’re all men consumed by greed. They can’t distance themselves from this mass of avarice.”

And so the carriage traveled leisurely toward the Hunan Branch of the Heavenly Demon Divine Church.

* * *

Yeo Sogwang was staring out the window.

He had a comfortable, good-natured appearance and did not look like a member of the Demonic Cult at all.

“Have we still received no word from Chief Steward I?”

At Yeo Sogwang’s question, Hwang Pyo, his right-hand man, answered,

“Not yet.”

They should have received word that the goods had been sent, but there had been no news from I Eom.

“Could it be that he has begun harboring other intentions?”

Yeo Sogwang firmly shook his head.

“He isn’t that kind of man.”

Besides, the blade they had used this time was the Blood-Hand Sword. Yeo Sogwang had chosen that greedy bastard precisely because of the possibility, however remote, that something might go wrong. No one would dare harbor other intentions when dealing with that man, for he was not easy to handle.

“What of the Young Cult Master?”

“He is running later than scheduled.”

“He’s trying to engage in a battle of wills. He learned bad habits from a young age.”

“Have you heard the rumors? They say that the Second Young Master, who became the successor this time, is truly extraordinary.”

“Of course he is.”

Even so, Yeo Sogwang remained at ease.

“Even so…”

He was still merely a young man brimming with youthful vigor. No matter how hard he tried to hide it, the rashness that came with his age was unavoidable. Yeo Sogwang intended to gently melt him down with sweet words.

He would show the young man exactly how he had risen from an ordinary martial artist to become a Branch Master.

“So come quickly, Young Cult Master.”

Chapter 264: My Reputation Depends on You.
------------------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk entered the Honam Branch with guards flanking him on both sides, his presence imposing and majestic.

Yeo Sogwang likewise received the Young Cult Leader with all his subordinates in tow.

The demonic warriors of the Honam Branch, lined up on his left and right, shouted in thunderous unison.

"We greet the Young Cult Leader!"

Yeo Sogwang walked out to greet Geom Mugeuk. Today was the first time he had laid eyes on him.

*He really is handsome.*

Not merely handsome—he possessed a refreshingly striking appearance. And those eyes overflowing with confidence. This was actually for the best. The easiest types to exploit were those with excessive pride or, conversely, those with deep inferiority complexes.

Yeo Sogwang was especially adept at handling those with strong pride. Because he could match their moods better than anyone, he had managed to rise all the way to Branch Leader.

"It is an honor to meet you, Young Cult Leader."

Yeo Sogwang bowed deeply at the waist. His waist was as supple as the political acumen he had spent a lifetime refining.

Geom Mugeuk stared intently at Yeo Sogwang.

"You have a very pleasant face."

"I hear that from time to time."

"They say in the martial world that one should be careful when meeting a face like yours."

Despite the provocation in Geom Mugeuk's very first greeting, Yeo Sogwang smiled.

"Indeed. Everyone else should be careful. But in this murim, there are exactly two exceptions. One of them is you, Young Cult Leader. Now, please, come inside."

Yeo Sogwang entered the great hall alongside Geom Mugeuk.

A banquet had already been prepared.

As soon as Geom Mugeuk entered, the musicians began to play and the dancers began to dance. The chefs began warming and carrying out the dishes.

"Now, let us proceed to the seat of honor."

Normally, when receiving a distinguished guest, the first day was for rest and the banquet was held the following evening, but this time was different. Since he was the Young Cult Leader, he would have traveled comfortably anyway, and he was still in the prime of his youth.

He would show him from the very start. That life was enjoyable when one was with the Honam Branch Leader. If he said he was tired and wished to rest, another banquet could be held tomorrow. Yeo Sogwang was more confident than anyone in the martial world when it came to hospitality.

Geom Mugeuk sat in the seat of honor, and Yeo Sogwang sat in the adjacent seat.

Then Jeok Yeon drew silver needles and checked each dish one by one for poison. The other guards stood in all directions around Geom Mugeuk at a slight distance, maintaining their vigil.

Watching them, Geom Mugeuk smiled. When they were with him, they seemed like mere children, but seeing them standing among others like this, they were clearly different. Their momentum was no ordinary thing.

"Your guards are young."

"If they were chosen as guards for the Young Cult Leader despite their youth, how skilled must they be? They are individuals who would not be out of place in any elite organization of the main cult."

Geom Mugeuk said this in a loud voice so that all the guards could hear.

At that sight, Yeo Sogwang misunderstood.

*As expected! He has the bluster unique to the young.*

But he did not know. That Geom Mugeuk had offered praise to elevate his subordinates' pride.

"Truly, each and every one of them possesses extraordinary momentum."

Yeo Sogwang played along with Geom Mugeuk's praise.

This time, Geom Mugeuk offered formal pleasantries.

"The Honam Branch is a strategic stronghold of our cult to keep the Murim Alliance in check, is it not? That is precisely why I visited this place before the other branches."

"You have entrusted a grave mission to an inadequate man; my shoulders feel heavy."

"As long as they aren't heavy from shouldering other things."

Despite the meaningful remark, Yeo Sogwang did not waver in the slightest.

"I serve only with loyalty for the cult."

"As expected! The main branch was right to place its trust in Branch Leader Yeo. Let us have a drink."

The two men toasted and drank.

As he set down his cup, Yeo Sogwang spoke casually.

"The Young Cult Leader's illustrious reputation has resounded all the way here to Honam."

"What sort of rumors were they?"

"That you are the greatest talent among all successors in history."

Geom Mugeuk put on a pleased expression.

"Moreover, you are exceedingly handsome. I dare say with confidence that you are the most handsome among the younger generation of the martial world."

"Our Branch Leader Yeo is painting gold on an ugly face."

"I speak from the heart. Ask my subordinates. I am a man who cannot tell lies."

Yeo Sogwang called over his right-hand man, Hwang Pyo.

"What do you think?"

"Not merely among the younger generation, but among all martial artists, you are the most handsome."

At his words, Yeo Sogwang laughed heartily.

"Ah, I have made a mistake. Not the younger generation, but all martial artists."

This was something that had happened countless times before. Yeo Sogwang would offer praise, and Hwang Pyo would appear with even greater praise. They would laugh merrily and repeat the flattery.

It was truly their method for entertaining guests. There was no praise more effective than praise of one's appearance. Moreover, when the other party was this young, it went without saying.

Geom Mugeuk laughed cheerfully.

Yeo Sogwang thought he was laughing because he felt good about being called handsome, but in truth, Geom Mugeuk was laughing for a different reason.

"So this is how it felt. Everyone must have felt quite good."

"What do you mean?"

"It is nothing. Let us have another drink."

"Very well. To the birth of a new hero of the murim!"

Yeo Sogwang readily went along with whatever Geom Mugeuk said. Whatever came out of his mouth, Yeo Sogwang would agree it was right and play the co-adjutant, elevating Geom Mugeuk by saying he himself had failed to think of such things. He truly was a man with considerable expertise in prostrating himself flat.

Yeo Sogwang was skilled at flattery. But he did not know. That his counterpart, chuckling along at his flattery, was the true living embodiment of the Divine Art of Flattery.

"Our Branch Leader Yeo, have a drink!"

Hearing the word "our" attached before his title, Yeo Sogwang smiled inwardly.

Now was the time to add the finishing touch to the banquet.

As intoxication took hold and the atmosphere ripened, Yeo Sogwang beckoned a woman over.

She was one of the dancers who had performed earlier. The one who had been noticeably beautiful among the dancers slowly walked over.

Yeo Sogwang introduced the woman.

"She said she dearly wished to greet the Young Cult Leader upon your arrival, so if you would not find it rude, please receive a drink from her."

The woman came and politely greeted Geom Mugeuk.

"I am called Yak Ran."

She was beautiful. Her beauty was such that there likely was not a man who could resist falling if she set her mind to seducing him.

Yak Ran approached and lifted the wine bottle.

"I shall offer a cup to the esteemed one."

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk sprang up from his seat and swept her up into his arms. Everyone was startled by the sudden action.

"Now, we'll drink later. Branch Leader Yeo, wait right there."

Holding her in his arms, Geom Mugeuk ran out of the great hall. The guards bewilderedly ran after him.

Yeo Sogwang was also taken aback, but he soon called out loudly.

"As expected, heroes are bound to love beautiful women! The bedroom is in the building to the left when you exit!"

The thought that things were resolving themselves so easily brought a natural smile to Yeo Sogwang's face.

*Wine and beauty. They always work.*

Of course, he had not expected them to work this passionately or this easily.

Meanwhile, Yak Ran was greatly astonished.

At first, she was surprised by the Young Cult Leader's sudden action. Next, she was surprised by the fact that while running to the bedroom, she had been floating through the air. The Young Cult Leader had pretended to embrace her, but he had not laid a hand on her body.

Indeed, even after entering the room, an unexpected situation unfolded.

When he had urgently dashed into the room, it seemed as though he would throw her onto the bed, but Geom Mugeuk gently seated her in a chair. No, to be precise, an invisible energy seated her.

Then she saw. The Young Cult Leader's handsome appearance did not enter her eyes. It was his gaze. He was looking at her with eyes so clear. Could the eyes of a demonic person be this clear?

In a tone as soft as his gaze, Geom Mugeuk spoke.

"You know who I am?"

"Yes."

His words were gentle, but overwhelmed by an indescribable atmosphere, her voice trembled.

"How many times has this happened?"

"What do you mean?"

"In the past, Yeo Sogwang forced you to entertain guests like this, didn't he?"

Yak Ran was startled. She was surprised that he had noticed this, and surprised that the Young Cult Leader was trying to dig into Yeo Sogwang's secrets.

Fear surged within her. If she became entangled in a power struggle, she would die.

"It seems even my status cannot open your mouth."

How could she dare refuse to answer before anyone?

"No. It is only... if Branch Leader Yeo learns of what I have said, he will kill even my family in my hometown."

"Did that man threaten to kill your entire family?"

"Yes."

"How did you feel then?"

"I was terrified. And I was furious."

Was it because he was the Young Cult Leader of the formidable Demonic Cult? Or was it because of those deep eyes that seemed to peer directly into her heart? An answer she should have agonized over came out without difficulty.

"Yes, you should be furious. One must be furious at a bastard who threatens to kill one's family."

She had been threatened by Yeo Sogwang since she was young. If he told her to come to a drinking party, she came. If he told her to pour wine, she poured. She was even forced to share his bed.

But she could not refuse.

Yeo Sogwang held not her life, but her family's lives as collateral. Her sick father, her young siblings, her mother who toiled away.

The bastard had threatened and brainwashed a young girl. That the world had always been this way. That if one clung to power, one could earn more money. That if she sent more money for medicine, she could save her father.

She had hated it. Serving drinks to men she was meeting for the first time was something she hated to death. She had thought of taking her own life many times, but she could not even die because of her family.

Eventually, she begged Yeo Sogwang to stop. But he was never an opponent that a pure young girl could handle.

After hearing her entire story, Geom Mugeuk approached her. And after gazing at her for a moment, he spoke in a low voice.

"I am sorry. I came far too late, didn't I?"

The moment she heard those words, something surged up from deep within her chest. Tears came flooding out. She had thought she had no tears left, but once they began to flow, they would not stop.

She tried to cease her weeping, but it did not go as she willed. She wanted to shout at Geom Mugeuk. Why did you come only now? Why now?

If she were still the girl of her childhood, she would have shouted exactly that.

But that young girl was no longer here.

"...I am sorry."

There was only her, apologizing even though she was the victim.

The next moment, her head rose on its own and a gentle energy began to envelop her.

At that gentle energy, which she had never once felt before, her heart found peace.

Perhaps because of that, she summoned the courage to ask one thing she had been curious about.

"Why did you think I was dragged here against my will?"

Surely, Yeo Sogwang had introduced her as a dancer who had wanted to see the Young Cult Leader.

"Because of the look in your eyes when you walked up and looked at me."

"My eyes?"

"I saw the anger contained in your eyes."

She was startled. She thought he had looked at her gently. He had read the heart hidden within?

"My right-hand man looked at me with those same eyes at first. He was drowning in anger and defeatism, believing the organization would never change."

Yak Ran no longer wore the face that had trembled with fear earlier.

"What will happen to me if I do not speak of Branch Leader Yeo?"

"I will simply let you go."

"Really?"

"Yes. I will have to find another way. In any case, since he is a rotten bastard at the core, I will eventually find the evidence to make him pay for his crimes."

Yak Ran still hesitated, and Geom Mugeuk no longer pressed her. He rose from his seat.

"You need only forget this conversation and act as if nothing happened. I will make sure that bastard pays for his crimes."

As Geom Mugeuk was about to leave, she spoke.

"Wait a moment."

When Geom Mugeuk turned around, she asked.

"What became of the person who had the same eyes as mine?"

"He rose to the position of Hwangcheon Pavilion Lord. It is a position that punishes those who have forsaken their humanity."

Yak Ran's pupils trembled. After a moment, a surprising remark flowed from her lips.

"Please wait. If you leave now, they will doubt the Young Cult Leader's virility."

Geom Mugeuk's eyes widened; he had not expected such a jest. It was not in his character to let such a joke pass.

"You are the first woman to have safeguarded the most important reputation of the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult."

Yak Ran laughed at Geom Mugeuk's jest.

"My dream was to die on stage while dancing, yet it seems I shall die doing something mad like this."

She walked to the table and began writing down names with the brush and paper there. She started with the most recent names, and among them was a name that Geom Mugeuk would know.

Hyeol Sugeom.

The mere fact that he had received him as a guest was proof that Yeo Sogwang was involved in this affair. Of course, he would make every excuse under the sun, but with Geom Mugeuk as his opponent, it would not be easy for him to slip away.

Aside from the Blood-Hand Sword, the names of several other influential figures came out one after another.

And she also wrote in detail about what had happened to her. Writing about her own affairs meant she was staking her life on this matter.

Yakran handed the paper to Geom Mugeuk and said,

“This is… my life.”

“No. This is your new life.”

Because from now on, she would be able to live a different one.

Geom Mugeuk had Samho come in.

“Escort the young lady to Tongcheongak. Have the martial artists there take her back to her hometown. And see that a way is found to treat her ailing father as well.”

Yakran’s eyes widened in astonishment. It was so overwhelming that she could hardly believe it.

“He isn’t going to follow me and kill my whole family too, is he?”

At her question, half-jest and half-fear, Geom Mugeuk said coldly to Samho,

“Now then, go massacre this young lady’s household and come back!”

At that, Samho said to Yakran in an expressionless tone,

“Not long ago, I went to see my mother for the first time in three years.”

Yakran looked at Samho in surprise.

“She had aged greatly in that time. Money is important, but… you should go see them before it is too late.”

Geom Mugeuk gave Samho a faint smile. It was a moment in which he had glimpsed what kind of man Samho was.

As Yakran’s eyes reddened at the thought of her parents, Geom Mugeuk smiled and said,

“If you were that worried, you shouldn’t have written this for me in the first place. And do you think our cult wouldn’t be able to find your family home if you didn’t tell us?”

Yakran gave an embarrassed smile.

“Will you punish him today?”

“I will.”

“I want to see it too. The sight of that man dying.”

Even if he died, her wounds would remain for the rest of her life. If they were going to remain anyway.

“Then there’s something I want to see too. When he dies, show me your dance.”

Yakran slowly nodded.

“Yes. I want to show you, Young Cult Leader. Though I don’t know if I’ll be able to dance well in front of a corpse.”

Geom Mugeuk and Yakran walked out of the room.

The guards looked at Geom Mugeuk with reverence in their eyes. They had been startled when he first ran off carrying her, but after hearing the conversation inside the room, they were once again filled with admiration. The more they learned about Geom Mugeuk, the deeper their loyalty became.

Geom Mugeuk bantered with Yakran.

“My reputation for virility depends on the look on your face.”

Yakran smiled and said,

“Don’t worry. I’m someone who stands on stage.”

The two returned to the banquet hall.

Geom Mugeuk walked with his chest proudly out, while Yakran followed behind him with her head slightly lowered and a satisfied smile on her face.

Yeo Sogwang sidled up close beside Geom Mugeuk and asked casually,

“You seem to be in a good mood?”

Geom Mugeuk poked him in the side and grinned.

“Because today, I’m going to see the most wonderful dance of my life.”

Chapter 265: I’m a Demon, So What If I Kill a Few People?
---------------------------------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk returned to his seat at the head of the hall.

When Yeo Sogwang tried to sit beside him, Geom Mugeuk had Yakran take that seat instead.

Despite being displaced from his original seat, Yeo Sogwang did not feel offended in the slightest. On the contrary, he was filled with admiration for Geom Mugeuk.

With so many people watching, he had carried a woman into a room? And now he had openly seated her beside him? It was something Yeo Sogwang could never even have imagined doing.

In any case, everything was proceeding exactly as he had intended.

‘He’s completely fallen for it.’

If someone this unusual and impulsive could be won over, perhaps he might be able to seize the opportunity to advance to the main headquarters this time. Buoyed by that hope, Yeo Sogwang tried to sweet-talk Geom Mugeuk with even more flattering words.

“Just look at the dancer’s expression. She’s completely immersed in happiness. Then again, how could any woman not be captivated by our young cult master’s manliness?”

Yeo Sogwang’s gaze shifted toward Yakran.

Her expression was different from usual. She had always been afraid whenever she saw him, but now she looked strangely confident.

‘That foolish bitch. She thinks she’s secured the young cult master just because she shared his bed once.’

She did not realize that becoming entangled with the young cult master had shortened her life. When the young cult master eventually ended things with her, he would never leave her alive. Even if he did not deal with her himself, one of those guards would.

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk asked Yeo Sogwang,

“What do our Chief Yeo hate the most?”

Yeo Sogwang racked his brains, wondering why the young cult master was asking such a question.

But he could not discern his intention. It seemed like the answer should be obvious, yet the young cult master also had an unpredictable side.

“I…”

What did he hate? Of course there were things he hated. He hated everything that obstructed his rise. He hated anything that interfered with accumulating money. And he hated it even more when someone saw through his duplicity.

Since none of those were things he could say aloud, he answered,

“I hate those righteous sect bastards who seek to bring down our cult. I hate their hypocrisy.”

“As expected! It’s reassuring to have our Chief Yeo with us. Here, in that spirit, accept a cup of my wine.”

“Thank you.”

As Geom Mugeuk poured him a drink, he casually asked,

“Chief Yeo, have you amassed a lot of money?”

Yeo Sogwang flinched inwardly, but soon let out a pained groan.

“If only that were true. As you can see, I have more than a few subordinates to support. Perhaps I simply have no affinity with money, because whatever comes in leaks right back out.”

“Chief Yeo, don’t exaggerate. Lend me some money.”

Money? Was he asking for a bribe? Or was this merely some joke over drinks?

Yeo Sogwang tried to ascertain his true intentions, but he could not fathom the young cult master’s thoughts at all.

Smiling, Yeo Sogwang took out a money pouch from his robes.

“Our young cult master needs money, so of course I must even empty my personal purse.”

He opened the pouch and said,

“I have eighty nyang. I’ll lend you this, at least.”

Geom Mugeuk accepted the money, then shook his head.

“This isn’t enough.”

Did he really want a bribe?
