Yeo Sogwang had risen to his current position by offering countless bribes. From the way someone spoke, he could roughly estimate how much they wanted—or even calculate how much would satisfy them without their having to say a word.

But the young cult master wanted a bribe? And in such an open setting?

It was so far beyond common sense that no matter how hard he tried to understand the man’s intentions, he could not see what lay behind them.

‘Fine, then. Let’s see how far this goes.’

Yeo Sogwang rose with a smile.

“Please wait a moment.”

Yeo Sogwang left the main hall.

Yakran, seated beside Geom Mugeuk, looked at him with a puzzled expression, but he offered no explanation.

The guards exchanged glances as well, but none of them could guess what Geom Mugeuk had in mind.

After a short while, Yeo Sogwang returned carrying a small box.

“Here it is.”

On the way back, Yeo Sogwang had reached a conclusion. Carrying Yakran into the room, and now this.

‘The young cult master enjoys attracting attention. He likes standing out far more than I thought.’

He was the young cult master—what did he have to fear? Look at me. I take bribes this openly. He probably thought this was stylish.

‘You foolish young cult master. You’re leaving evidence of your own weakness. If this causes trouble later, do you intend to kill every person here to silence them?’

Geom Mugeuk looked inside the box.

“Oh, you’ve saved quite a lot.”

“I’m giving you everything I’ve saved.”

Geom Mugeuk closed the box and spoke again.

“Chief Yeo.”

“Yes, young cult master.”

“Give me more.”

Yeo Sogwang’s expression instantly hardened. But even faster than his features had tightened, he put on his usual good-natured smile. If keeping one’s expression from betraying one’s thoughts was a skill, then he had mastered it.

“Did I not tell you that this is every last bit of wealth I’ve saved?”

“I know you have assets set aside elsewhere.”

“I don’t.”

At the very moment Yeo Sogwang smiled and waved his hands in denial—

“You made money by joining hands with the Lord of the Cheonhwa Gate, didn’t you?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Yeo Sogwang was so startled that he nearly blurted out, How did you know that?

Of course, the well-seasoned Yeo Sogwang showed no sign of surprise as he responded.

“I don’t know what you mean.”

“Why are you acting like this between us? I understand everything. You have to look after your subordinates on a meager salary, so you need to earn some side income. Isn’t that right?”

The amount he had earned through the Cheonhwa Gate affair was far too large for that excuse to apply. Moreover, he had killed more than twenty people to bury the secret. It was something that absolutely must never come to light.

‘How much does he know?’

Yeo Sogwang flatly denied it for the time being.

“You seem to be mistaken. It’s true that I had dealings with the Cheonhwa Gate, but that was only to handle matters for the branch.”

He had prepared a false ledger for precisely such a situation. It made it appear as though the money that had come in had been spent for the branch’s sake.

Geom Mugeuk then gestured for him to bring his face closer. Yeo Sogwang leaned in, inwardly tense. He was afraid a fist might come flying at him at any moment.

Fortunately, that did not happen. But something more frightening than a fist came flying instead.

“How much did you take from the Lord of the Soya Gang?”

“!”

“Don’t remember? Then do you recall the money you received from the deputy lord of the Seochang Gate?”

Several more names followed in a steady stream.

Yakran, listening nearby, was secretly shocked. They were the names she had written down for him. When she had handed him the paper, he had seemed to glance over it casually, but he had memorized every single one precisely.

Yeo Sogwang’s pupils trembled. He was rarely flustered, but this time his composure was shattered.

‘What? How did he find out?’

No, that was not what mattered. What mattered was how he would escape this situation.

“Our Chief Yeo’s expression has stiffened. I always thought your smiling face was pleasant to look at.”

Yeo Sogwang forced a smile. This was a moment that called for quick thinking, but with so many names being brought up, his mind was in chaos, unable to decide what to say. The greatest crisis of his life had come crashing down upon him without warning.

“Now I understand what our Chief Yeo hates the most. Giving up his own money. Am I right?”

Yeo Sogwang’s lost gaze drifted toward Yakran, seated beside Geom Mugeuk.

At that moment, his eyes met hers. The instant he saw her confident gaze—

‘Damn it! That accursed bitch told him everything!’

He had done everything he was ordered to do for so many years. He had never dreamed a situation like this would arise.

“Did that dancer slander me to you?”

“Why are you suddenly saying that? Is there some connection between you and this woman, Chief Yeo?”

Yeo Sogwang realized at once that he had misspoken. He should have denied everything outright. Above all, he must never become entangled with Yakran.

But that was not the only thing tightening its grip around him.

“Where are the goods from the Hunan First Escort Agency?”

Yeo Sogwang’s vision went dark. The young cult master knew everything about his corruption.

“This is slander! Someone is plotting to bring me down!”

“Who?”

Yeo Sogwang’s gaze shifted toward Yakran again.

“Was it this dancer?”

He could not answer yes. If he did, everything would be revealed—including the times he had sent her to entertain others. More importantly, it would expose the fact that he had introduced such a woman to the young cult master. He had a better chance of surviving as a corrupt martial artist.

‘He came here already knowing everything. He came to arrest me. If so…?’

Yeo Sogwang dropped to his knees where he stood and bowed his head deeply.

The music that had filled the hall stopped, and everyone held their breath. It was an utterly unfamiliar sight to see Yeo Sogwang—the man who had wielded absolute power throughout Hunan—kneeling before someone.

“Please show mercy and forgive me. I was blinded for a moment and gave in to greed.”

He could only hope that the young cult master’s impulsive side and unique personality would reveal themselves. He prayed that Geom Mugeuk would burst into laughter and say, It’s nothing, really. Wasn’t he someone who loved standing out? Please! Forgive me, and make a spectacle of it! Act arrogant!

But at the very moment when he should have made a spectacle, Geom Mugeuk instead grew calm and cold.

“Where is the money you’ve accumulated over the years?”

“……It’s in a money house.”

“Not that. Not the money you set aside to put on a show for the inspection by the Hwangcheongak. I mean the money you scraped together with such desperation.”

Yeo Sogwang said nothing. No, he could not. To survive, he had to hand over the money immediately—but what kind of money was it?

Geom Mugeuk beckoned Hwangpyo, standing in the distance, to come over.

Hwangpyo hurried over with a taut, nervous expression and knelt beside Yeo Sogwang.

“You know where he hid the money, don’t you?”

Geom Mugeuk released killing intent at him. Every hair on Hwangpyo’s body stood on end. He felt as though blood were draining from his entire body. A chilling fear that he might truly die seized him.

“Tell me honestly, and I’ll let you live.”

Hwangpyo’s heart pounded. He had always believed he could die for Yeo Sogwang. But now that he was truly facing death, he was afraid. Had Yeo Sogwang really treated him well enough for him to die in his place?

Geom Mugeuk read his wavering heart exactly.

“You must have watched your master amass an enormous fortune. What did he give you?”

Crumbs. And even those had come with endless displays of generosity.

Hwangpyo looked at Yeo Sogwang, kneeling beside him. Seeing how haggard he looked compared to usual, Hwangpyo understood. His loyalty had only been valid while Yeo Sogwang held the power of life and death over him.

“……The wealth is hidden at a temple called Yangho-sa.”

Geom Mugeuk sent a telepathic message to Jeokyeon.

―Notify Tongcheongak immediately and have them recover all of it!

―Yes, understood.

Hwangpyo pleaded desperately.

“I confessed everything honestly, so please let me live!”

At that very moment—

Splurch!

Yeo Sogwang drew his sword and cut through Hwangpyo’s throat. The spurting blood splashed across Yeo Sogwang’s body and face.

Yeo Sogwang wiped the blood from his face. Regret came before resentment toward Hwangpyo. He should never have trusted him. He should never have trusted him to the very end. This one, that one—he should have gotten rid of them after using them for a few years, then found new ones.

Yeo Sogwang spat on Hwangpyo’s corpse and said coldly,

“Who do you take me for, some complete idiot? Did you think you could live after spouting that garbage beside me?”

His perpetually smiling expression had changed as though a mask had been stripped away. This was his true face. He had never imagined his mask would be torn off so easily.

All because of that strange young cult master.

“You never intended to let him live from the beginning, did you?”

“After spending your whole life flattering others and watching their every move, you still tried to read someone else’s thoughts at the very end?”

The sword clenched in Yeo Sogwang’s hand trembled. He spoke with a sigh.

“It’s unfair. I never even got to spend the money I saved all my life.”

“You were addicted to making money. You should have spent it, looked at people, and seen the world. But all you ever saw was money.”

Yeo Sogwang thought that perhaps it was true. He had felt a greater thrill earning money than spending it.

“I…”

He wanted to refute him somehow, but no words came out. He had been known for his eloquence, yet in front of that damned young cult master, he could think of nothing. Could the life he had built over so many years really collapse in a single instant? Was this truly happening? It felt like a dream.

Yeo Sogwang screamed at the top of his lungs.

“So what if it was! I’m a demon cultivator—what’s wrong with killing a few worthless lowlifes?”

Under ordinary circumstances, he would never have said such a thing. That was the sort of thing only lowly thugs said.

If only Geom Muguk had replied in agreement, saying, “Yes, I’m a demon too. So what if I kill a bastard like you?”—then she would not have felt this wretched. But Geom Muguk merely gazed at her with indifferent eyes.

Yeo Sogwang screamed as though possessed.

“Uaaaargh!”

Just as Yakran had been unable to bear him, he too was unable to bear Geom Muguk.

Yeo Sogwang threw himself forward. The sword he had sent flying toward Geom Muguk as though intent on killing him suddenly changed direction. It now pointed at Yakran’s heart. He placed the blame for everything that had happened on her.

Shk!

There was the sound of flesh tearing as the sword plunged in.

Yeo Sogwang’s sword stopped just before Yakran’s heart. In its place, Geom Muguk’s Heukma Sword was buried in Yeo Sogwang’s heart.

Geom Muguk said to Yakran,

“The specter that tormented you will now disappear from this world.”

He pulled out the sword, and Yeo Sogwang fell backward, dead.

“Send away the specter in your heart along with him.”

Yakran understood why Geom Muguk had said such a thing. He meant that she should not spend the rest of her life suffering from the wounds Yeo Sogwang had inflicted on her.

As she gazed at Yeo Sogwang’s corpse, she took a deep breath and said,

“I’ll dance.”

She had been waiting for this moment alone.

Then Geom Muguk said,

“Don’t dance here. Dance on a stage later. Dancing belongs on a stage, not in a place like this, where filthy blood flows.”

“But you promised.”

From Geom Muguk’s clear, deep eyes as he silently looked at her, Yakran understood. He had made that promise so he could say these words to her.

“There’s no need to take your revenge. He isn’t worth it. Perform your greatest dance on your stage.”

Tears welled up in her eyes.

“Why are you being so kind to someone as insignificant as me?”

Geom Muguk stood facing her.

“Because the Cheonma Divine Cult wronged you.”

Looking straight at her, Geom Muguk spoke with solemn courtesy.

“I will apologize in their place. I’m sorry.”

The tears Yakran had been holding back streamed down her face. The Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult was apologizing to her—a worthless life that could be snuffed out with a mere crook of his finger. If this was how things were, how could she remain bound to the wounds of the past?

She bowed deeply to Geom Muguk.

“I will never forget this kindness. Please come and watch me dance someday.”

The dancers and musicians who had been there also bowed their heads.

Watching them, Jeokyeon could feel it. The demonic way of Geom Muguk, which had lingered over the meals prepared by Samho’s mother, had today lingered over that woman’s dance.

And now, she thought she could see it just a little more clearly: what it was they had to protect. Her eyes, which had hurt so badly she thought they might fall out, no longer hurt quite as much.

Chapter 266: The Young Cult Leader No Matter What Anyone Says.
--------------------------------------------------------------
Only Geom Mugeuk and his guard warriors remained in the place where the banquet had ended.

After having only the corpses cleared away, he had deliberately sent everyone else out. He wanted to have a drink with his guards.

I sat on the edge of a table and picked up a bottle of liquor.

“Your shift changes soon, doesn’t it? Come on, everyone, have a drink.”

Jeogyeon and the guards silently accepted their cups. They no longer said, “We cannot do that while on guard duty,” in response to my orders. A relationship unique to us was forming.

“You’ve all worked hard.”

When I raised my cup high, everyone raised theirs and emptied them.

A cup of liquor drunk in the empty banquet hall after a storm had swept through it had a strange charm.

‘Rest a little, at times like this at least.’

Some people say guard warriors are impressive, but without a sense of duty, it is truly no easy task. They are neither the leads nor the supporting cast, not even bit players. They do work that requires them to become the background.

“Do you ever drink before sleeping?”

At my question, Jeogyeon answered.

“Sometimes. On a day like today, I probably would have.”

“A day like today?”

Instead of answering, Jeogyeon asked me a question.

“Do you not grow tired of punishing evil?”

“That’s why we lose. Because we get tired. That side is like weeds that have grown back the moment you turn around, while this side is a farmer who has to pull them out one by one.”

Jeogyeon’s eyes trembled slightly. Soon, his gaze turned to the bloodstain left where Yeo Sogwang’s corpse had been.

“When I first saw Leader Yeo, I did not know he was that sort of person.”

“You mustn’t be fooled by first impressions. Petty men are skilled at disguising themselves. If someone gives you an oddly excessive feeling of pleasantness at first glance, you should be wary of them for the time being.”

“In that sense, I suppose I am fortunate.”

With his single eye, he had a strong first impression. He could not compare to the Fist Demon, and could be called a gentler version of Jangho.

“When you take off your eyepatch, you’re actually quite handsome, aren’t you? Manly, too.”

“I am? That is the first time I have heard that.”

Just as Jeogyeon’s mood was about to improve slightly, I quickly added,

“That is exactly the sort of thing you should watch out for. Being made to feel good for no reason!”

Jeogyeon and the guards laughed together.

“Will you be leaving for the Jiangxi Branch tomorrow?”

“I’d like to, but… I should finish up the matters here before I go.”

Cutting someone down and getting rid of them is not difficult. It is always the cleanup afterward that is hard. You have to clean up what the bastard left behind, and find someone to replace him.

“There’s no hurry. Let’s go slowly.”

Would it be any different there? Good people and bad bastards would be living all mixed together.

* * *

The next morning, the rumor that the branch leader had died spread through the Honam Branch.

Because there had been so many people present when Yeo Sogwang died, the reason for his death was also made known in detail. Today it was only a rumor within the branch, but within a few days, it would spread throughout the Honam region.

Now all the branch leaders and division chiefs in the Central Plains would fear me. I did not think that was bad. Only if they were thoroughly cowed and tense would discipline be properly established.

That afternoon, the person in charge of the Honam region from Tongcheongak came to see me again.

“We have recovered all the assets Yeo Sogwang had hidden in Yanghosa. How should this money be handled?”

“Send all the money to the main sect.”

“Understood.”

Father, your son is working hard out here to earn money! I really should joke around with Father about it once.

“Until the higher-ups appoint an acting branch leader, at the very least, the Young Cult Leader will have to remain here at this branch for the time being.”

Since I had cut down the branch leader without even deciding on a successor, it could not be helped. Even if the Tongcheongak warrior had not said it, I had intended to stay and help settle the aftermath.

Just as he had entrusted the selection of the Hwangcheongak Lord to me, there was a possibility Father would accept my suggestion for the successor to this branch leader as well.

“When you return, immediately send me information on the executive-level personnel under the branch leader. Send all possible information.”

“Understood.”

After receiving information from Hwangcheongak as well, I immediately held interviews with the warriors who seemed suitable to become the next branch leader.

It was impossible to know what sort of person someone was from a single interview, but his past conduct allowed me to guess what kind of person he might be.

After recording my impressions and thoughts on each person one by one, I sent the contents to Tongcheongak. Whether they sent down a new person or chose from among those I had interviewed, the rest was for Father and Tongcheongak to handle.

After finishing what I had to do, I trained in the Heavenly Demon Protective Divine Art at the training ground. Lately, whenever I practiced the Heavenly Demon Protective Divine Art, I had instinctively felt a certain ticklish sensation, as though the time had come to cross into the next stage.

As expected, my prediction was correct. On the third day after I began training, the Heavenly Demon Protective Divine Art reached the next stage. Now only one stage remained before Great Completion. Once I crossed just one more stage, I would possess two lives.

Ordinarily, it was difficult to know the reason behind an advancement in such profound martial arts, but this time I could tell.

It was because of the Ninefold Demon Art. Learning the Ninefold Demon Art had undoubtedly influenced my training in the Heavenly Demon Protective Divine Art.

I immediately displayed the First Form of the Ninefold Demon Art. I was curious as to what effect the Heavenly Demon Protective Divine Art’s advancement by one stage had conversely had on the Ninefold Demon Art.

The First Technique, Annihilation Form.

The Eastern Ghost, Western Ghost, Southern Ghost, and Northern Ghost appeared all at once and simultaneously tore through space.

The terrifying one, the handsome one, the mysterious one, and the clever-looking one. All four were the same as ever.

And something had changed as well.

The time they remained had become just a little longer than before. As the Heavenly Demon Protective Divine Art rose by one stage, it had also influenced the Ninefold Demon Art.

Of course, that did not mean I was having an emotional exchange with them. For now, it was still the stage of my one-sided courtship.

East, west, south, and north, I look forward to working with you from now on as well!

Would the day ever come when those evil spirits responded to these feelings of mine?

It was already different now, but that day would be when my Ninefold Demon Art became a Ninefold Demon Art completely different from Father’s.

* * *

On the day Geom Mugeuk finished training and came out of the practice field, two men had come looking for him. One of them introduced himself first.

“My name is Chungsik. I have come here despite my discourtesy because I have an earnest request to make of the Young Cult Leader.”

Perhaps because he was nervous, Chungsik’s voice trembled.

And Geom Mugeuk recognized him.

“You were the one playing music at the banquet hall that day.”

When Geom Mugeuk recognized him, Chungsik was startled.

“You recognize me?”

“I do. I do not easily forget someone I have seen once. What brings you here, musician?”

At that, Chungsik looked back at the middle-aged man who had come with him.

“This friend here is my friend.”

The man who had come with him introduced himself.

“I am Haesong, and I sell vegetables in the market here.”

Haesong was even more nervous than Chungsik.

“Good to meet you. I am Geom Mugeuk.”

“It is an honor to meet someone so noble.”

“What brings you to me?”

Haesong could not easily speak. Even though he had practiced what he would say, when the moment came to actually speak, his heart trembled so much that the words would not come out.

Unable to watch any longer, Chungsik spoke in his place.

“Before the Young Cult Leader came here, this friend’s son suffered an unjust assault. He was injured so badly that the physician said he might become crippled.”

Haesong bit his lip tightly. That was the reason he could not bear to say it with his own mouth. Whenever he thought of his son, anger burst inside him, and his heart welled up so fiercely that tears threatened to fall.

“Who did it?”

“There is an organization made up of young warriors in the Honam region. They call themselves the Musinhoe.”

“Who led the assault?”

“The son of the Cheonhwamun Sect Leader and the son of the Soyabang Gang Leader.”

Cheonhwamun and Soyabang. They were sects that had joined hands with the dead Yeo Sogwang. They were also sects that had to be investigated because of this incident, and now they had revealed themselves through an unexpected matter like this.

Seeing that the heavens were not allowing those sects to slip through the net, I thought that perhaps their wicked deeds were even greater than they appeared.

“We know how discourteous it is for lowly people like us to come see the Young Cult Leader and make such a request. But that day, I saw the Young Cult Leader step forward for Yakran. I thought that if it were the Young Cult Leader, you might resolve this friend’s injustice as well.”

Chungsik’s gaze turned to Haesong.

“I am someone who has lived my entire life doing nothing but playing music. This friend bought instruments for me when I was poor and made it possible for me to learn music. I want to repay that grace even if I must stake my life on it.”

Haesong lowered his head. Though he was nothing more than a vegetable merchant in the market, he knew well how burdensome and difficult it was to come before the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult. For his sake, his friend had come here risking his life. In truth, he himself had also risked his life by coming. If it had been anything else, he never would have come.

Haesong opened his mouth in a trembling voice.

“To be honest, I had given up. How could someone like me, a mere market merchant, stand against Cheonhwamun and Soyabang? But this friend said that the Young Cult Leader’s arrival at this time was a chance the heavens had granted me.”

Haesong looked at Geom Mugeuk.

“I want to make them pay for their crimes.”

He had been trembling all along, but in that moment, his eyes were those of a father.

* * *

Haesong’s wife was startled. Even when the musician came and told her husband they should go meet the Young Cult Leader, she had thought it would be a wasted effort. She believed there was no way the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult would meet a mere musician. She had worried that they might go for nothing and only come back after being beaten.

But when the Young Cult Leader came in person and the guards stood watch around the house, she was so astonished she could hardly think straight.

When they entered the room, Haesong’s son Haejin lay there, groaning in pain.

At a glance, his injuries were severe. A physician had come and treated him, but he was still lying there unable to move at all. In his current state, let alone living as a warrior, even living as an ordinary person seemed difficult.

At the sound of the door opening, Haejin forced himself to endure the pain and said,

“Mother, I’m fine. I’m much better.”

Haejin was desperately holding back the pain so his mother would not worry. Haesong’s wife sat beside her son, suppressing her tears.

“Jin, a noble person has come to see you.”

Only then did Haejin open his eyes. But he was in too much pain to see properly.

“Let me examine him for a moment.”

Geom Mugeuk turned Haejin’s body to the side and placed his palm on his back.

After injecting true qi for a moment to regulate the qi and blood in his body, Haejin’s ragged breathing quieted.

At the sight, Chungsik, Haesong, and his wife were relieved and greatly delighted. It was the first time since his injury that he had appeared so peaceful.

Geom Mugeuk opened the blocked qi and blood channels and properly reset the broken bones. He carried out that difficult treatment in an instant with the least possible pain. If not for the cracking sounds, no one would have known what he was doing.

“What happened?”

Now Haejin, his face eased, answered about what had happened.

“I went to work at a banquet hall. If you carry wine and food at banquets for prestigious families, you can earn more money than working at an inn.”

Haesong let out a sigh. His son had gone to a martial academy with the money he earned by working so hard like that. If he himself had had more money, his son would not have had to go do such work. Chungsik had told him not to blame himself, but as a parent, how could he not?

“My dream is to become a warrior.”

“You have naturally good bones and muscles, so you will be able to become a good warrior. Be grateful to your parents.”

“Really?”

Haejin, now somewhat in his right mind, looked at his parents. The couple forced smiles while hiding their sorrow.

Geom Mugeuk asked again.

“What happened there?”

“I carried the liquor to be used at the banquet for a long while, and when I had a break, I practiced the footwork I had learned. I shouldn’t have practiced…”

After letting out a sigh full of regret, Haejin continued.

“At that time, Young Master Seo was passing by, saw me, and asked if I had learned martial arts. So I told him I attended a martial academy, and then he told me to have a spar with Young Master Seok, who was with him. I refused over and over, but…”

Haejin’s expression filled with resentment, and then fear appeared in his eyes. It seemed that when he thought of that moment, fury surged up in him so intensely he could hardly endure it. The terror of being beaten nearly to death had also left a deep wound in him.

Now it was clear what had happened. Since they had used a spar as an excuse to assault him, no one could hold them responsible. It was likely not something they had done only once or twice.

“How is my body?”

“Fortunately, your important blood vessels were not damaged. I just opened the vessels that had been blocked by your injuries. You do not have to give up on your dream.”

“Thank you so much.”

Tears flowed from Haejin’s eyes and ran down his cheeks.

Chungsik, Haesong, and Haesong’s wife were also greatly delighted. They had only hoped he would not become crippled, but now their son could dream of becoming a warrior again. They were even happier than their son.

“Are banquets held often?”

“They are held once every three or four days.”

“Now sleep deeply.”

When Geom Mugeuk tapped Haejin’s sleep acupoint, he fell comfortably asleep.

“Leave the rest to me, and focus only on treating your son.”

The three people bowed deeply to Geom Mugeuk. They had truly never imagined the Young Cult Leader would so readily help them to this extent. It felt like a dream.

Before leaving, Geom Mugeuk said to Haesong,

“I came because I saw the look in your eyes. You are the one who saved your son.”

When Geom Mugeuk came outside, the sound of Haesong’s sobs, which he had endured and endured, came from inside. His wife’s crying and his friend’s crying joined it.

Geom Mugeuk said to Jeogyeon, who had been waiting.

“Contact Tongcheongak again and tell them to send information on Cheonhwamun and Soyabang. And find out when the banquet is being held.”

“Yes, understood.”

As they walked together toward the carriage, Jeogyeon added,

“You are truly remarkable.”

“What is?”

“The fact that you personally look after each and every one of these matters.”

“I came in a carriage you people drove, treated him with a stream of true qi, then I’ll ride in another carriage you people drive to the banquet hall and scold some bastards who can’t tell shit from piss. Now, where in any of that is something remarkable? What is remarkable is the courage of that musician who suggested they go find the Young Cult Leader for his friend’s sake. It is the courage of a father asking that the children of great sects be made to pay for the sake of justice for his son. It is the endurance of a son pretending not to be in pain for his mother’s sake. That is where the word remarkable belongs.”

But Jeogyeon firmly shook his head and said,

“No. No matter what anyone says, the Young Cult Leader is more remarkable.”

Jeogyeon opened the carriage door. As Geom Mugeuk climbed into the carriage, he said,

“Now you’re being stubborn, too. You’re changing. You’re becoming somewhat rebellious.”

As he closed the carriage door, Jeogyeon said,

“And whose fault would that be?”

Looking at Jeogyeon through the window, Geom Mugeuk smiled. He could feel color gradually filling those who had once been a gray background.

And so the carriage began to run off to handle something remarkable, and yet not remarkable at all.

Chapter 267: You're Not on My Level.
------------------------------------
The next day, a Tongcheonggak martial artist came straight to me.

“Here are the documents regarding the Cheonhwamun and Soya Bang that you requested.”

According to him, in order to provide me with information quickly, they had opened a temporary Tongcheonggak branch near the Honam subdivision. Such was the power of the Young Cult Leader.

I looked over the documents the Tongcheonggak martial artist presented.

“The scale of the Cheonhwamun is considerable.”

I had known it was a large sect, but its scale and depth were substantial.

“Yes, it is truly the foremost sect in Honam. Four in the main sect, four in the Sadomaeng, two in the Martial Arts Alliance. It preserves its neutrality while interacting with this degree of influence.”

It was not just the Cheonhwamun. There were sects that maintained their neutrality so thoroughly while growing their own power. Mainly the larger sects, like the Cheonhwamun, did this.

“What was the business you had been pursuing with Yeo Sogwang?”

“We are investigating that matter, but have yet to uncover it. Both Faction Leader Yeo and the Cheonhwamun Sect Leader handled the affair extremely secretly.”

If Tongcheonggak’s information network could not uncover it, an external investigation seemed impossible.

“Very well. Pause that investigation for now and look into the succession structures of the two sects. When the current sect leader steps down, who will inherit the clan, and which heir would be favorable to us—investigate this in detail.”

“Understood.”

After the Tongcheonggak martial artist finished his report and left, I immediately called for Jeogyeon.

“The banquet?”

“It is this evening.”

“Select fifty of the skilled martial artists from the Honam Branch and have them wait near the banquet hall.”

“Understood.”

After Jeogyeon left, I silently gazed out the window.

‘While you were making your perfect deals, your children—who should have been the most perfect—were becoming the worst.’

* * *

Today, as always, the young men and women of the Musinhoe were drinking, laughing, and chatting in the banquet hall.

At their center was Xu Qing of the Cheonhwamun.

This was a gathering maintained purely by the logic of power. The greatest influence came from one’s family, and next was one’s personal martial prowess.

As a predator who stood at the very top in both regards, Xu Qing was the leader of the Musinhoe.

The second-in-command of the Musinhoe was Xi Feng of the Soya Bang.

But the second-in-command, Xi Feng, was drinking in a corner rather than in the seat beside Xu Qing.

They had once been as close as could be, but recently Xu Qing’s attitude toward him had changed. He had grown cold for some reason, and naturally, the relationship had become strained.

“Brother, give me a glass too.”

“Pour it yourself.”

There were many beauties around Xu Qing, yet he wore a listless expression.

Lately, drinking was no fun, and playing with women was no fun either. He needed something intense, something stimulating. Honestly, he was feeling a desire to kill someone.

A few months ago, he had quarreled with some Yonghwabang martial artists. Unable to control himself, Xu Qing had cut them down. Three people died then; that was Xu Qing’s first murder.

He and Xi Feng, who had been with him, secretly buried the bodies.

It began after that day. The wanting to kill someone. The thrill of the moment he stabbed a person would not be forgotten. Afterward, he killed two more.

The first was a middle-aged woman walking at night. She was someone who knew no martial arts, but unable to resist the sudden urge to kill, he impulsively cut her down. He rummaged through the woman’s belongings, staged it to look like bandits had attacked her, and left the place.

His heart did not race as fast as it had when he killed for the first time. So a few days later, he killed another. This time, it was a low-ranking martial artist from the Honam Branch of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. Perhaps because he had killed someone he shouldn’t have? This time, his heart raced even faster than at his first murder.

Once he had tasted blood, an uncontrollable desire for slaughter awakened.

Xu Qing emptied his cup and rose from his seat. As he walked out, he bumped against Xi Feng. Whether it was accidental or deliberate, Xi Feng could not tell, but he felt displeased.

Xi Feng knew. He knew when the attitude had changed. It was because of his slip of the tongue. While drunk, he had carelessly brought up the matter of the Yonghwabang martial artists Xu Qing had killed. It had been a passing remark—“those bastards”—but seeing Xu Qing’s eyes change completely at that moment, he had realized his mistake. Damn it! The grudge was dragging on far too long.

It was on Xu Qing’s way back from relieving himself in the privy.

Someone caught his eye, practicing footwork in a secluded corner.

There had been someone practicing footwork in the same manner not long ago. A liquor servant daring to train in martial arts? So he had made Xi Feng duel the man and crush him.

Thinking it was perfect timing to alleviate his boredom, Xu Qing beckoned to those with him. The group that crowded over exchanged mischievous glances, having found a new toy in the young man practicing footwork.

“Hey.”

The young man practicing footwork turned around at Xu Qing’s call.

Upon seeing him, Xu Qing startled. It was not the face he had expected. He had anticipated something dull and naive, but before him stood a handsome young gentleman. It was Geom Mugeuk.

“Who are you?”

For someone who looked like a liquor servant, there was an inexplicable dignity and bearing about him. Indeed, his response was not that of a laborer.

“Then who are you?”

When Geom Mugeuk asked back, laughter burst from around them. Their expressions showed they were already looking forward to seeing what would happen to someone who had provoked Xu Qing’s nerves.

“Prideful, aren’t we? Well, for a martial artist, pride is life itself.”

Xu Qing touched on pride. It was a method he always used. If he gently fanned the flames of pride, his opponent would always leap in like a moth to fire. The more eyes watching, as now, the better it worked.

“You came out like a man, so you should show your skill. How about a duel?”

“With whom? With you?”

Then laughter erupted again from the surroundings. Because among the younger generation of Honam, there was no one who could beat Xu Qing.

“We aren’t on the same level.”

Xu Qing shot Xi Feng a look that said, *You face him.*

Xi Feng’s expression hardened. It had been the same last time, and it was the same now.

Was he really on the same level as such a whelp? Yet making him face such a person was clearly meant to humiliate him in front of everyone.

“I did it last time, so this time—”

“You do it!”

Xu Qing cut him off coldly. With so many eyes watching, Xi Feng’s pride was wounded. But he had to endure. Not because of a difference in skill, but because of the Cheonhwamun’s prestige.

Then Geom Mugeuk asked Xi Feng,

“Then who are you?”

“I am Xi Feng of the Soya Bang.”

Then Geom Mugeuk exclaimed with exaggerated admiration.

“Oh! I have heard that the heir of the Soya Bang is a remarkable talent. That his skill makes him the top junior in all of Honam! So it was you! It is an honor!”

Xi Feng was bewildered by the unexpected reaction. It had been truly ages since someone had praised him like this in front of everyone. Especially lately.

Then Geom Mugeuk tilted his head and muttered as if to himself.

“Ah, or am I wrong? If you have the skill to be the best in Honam, you wouldn’t take orders from others, would you?”

In an instant, Xi Feng’s expression stiffened. Whether it was sincere or an intentional mockery, it was a perfect provocation.

This time, Geom Mugeuk spoke to Xu Qing.

“You are impressive. Commanding the heir of the Soya Bang as your subordinate. Who are you?”

The praise that had been directed at him shifted to Xu Qing in an instant, and Xi Feng’s face reddened. Seeing Xu Qing snicker, his anger flared even more.

“Shut up! What does a wretch like you know to run your mouth so freely?”

The very moment Xi Feng tried to rush at Geom Mugeuk—

“If you attack me like this, you are practically admitting that you are his subordinate.”

Xi Feng’s movements halted with a start.

Geom Mugeuk began to shake him up. Now Xi Feng was the one leaping into a blazing bonfire of pride.

Geom Mugeuk looked around and said,

“It seems to be true indeed. Everyone is looking at you with eyes that regard you as that friend’s subordinate?”

Xi Feng looked around.

Someone needed to step up and say they were friends rather than a superior and subordinate, but everyone was reading Xu Qing’s mood and could not say so.

Rather, it was Xu Qing who stepped forward.

“Why are you swayed by that punk’s sowing of discord?”

Xi Feng found Xu Qing, who was trying to stop him while using the word “swayed,” even more irritating. What followed was even more irritating.

“Stop sulking like a girl and duel that strange fellow first.”

He said this fully knowing how angry such words would make a person.

Then Geom Mugeuk asked Xi Feng,

“But if you really fought, you could beat him, right?”

*“!”*

It was something someone desperately wanted to be asked, yet also a question that absolutely could not be answered.

“You endure because the Soya Bang is weaker than the Cheonhwamun, right?”

It was the truth. If they fought properly, Xi Feng was confident he could beat Xu Qing. He had the better physique, and he did not think his martial arts lagged behind in the slightest.

Silence fell over the spot. A moment later, it was Xu Qing who broke it.

“Why don’t you deny it?”

But Xi Feng said nothing.

Xu Qing’s gaze grew cold.

“You won’t say anything to the end?”

Xi Feng still remained silent.

Vulgar words spilled from Xu Qing’s mouth.

“Look at this bastard. He must really think so?”

In truth, it was not that Xi Feng would not speak now, but that he could not.

His mouth would not open. It was strange. He was clearly not being suppressed at an acupoint, yet it felt as though some invisible force was pressing down on his jaw from below and his crown from above. He thought it was because he was so angry at Xu Qing.

Xu Qing grabbed him by the collar.

“Did you really think that?”

*Smack!*

Xu Qing’s fist turned Xi Feng’s jaw. In that instant, the force pressing down on him vanished.

“Th-that’s not—”

*Smack! Smack!*

The fist struck Xi Feng’s face in rapid succession.

Xi Feng, who had never imagined he would be struck, was furious to the very tips of his hair.

Dodging the fourth punch, Xi Feng threw a fist of his own.

*Smack!*

Having never expected Xi Feng to counterattack, Xu Qing was sent flying and crashed to the ground. Lying on the floor, Xu Qing’s eyes widened.

“How dare you! Have you lost your mind?”

The more surprised and flustered one was Xi Feng. His fist had flown out of its own accord. Once again, some invisible force had acted, but Xi Feng believed it was his long-suppressed rage exploding.

Xi Feng bellowed.

“You bastard! Stop looking down on people!”

The words he had held back until now burst out.

Xu Qing rose from his seat, radiating killing intent.

“It seems our young master Xi has harbored quite a few grievances until now.”

Xi Feng felt both fear and relief simultaneously. But gradually, fear began to grow.

*Damn it! Crazy bastard! What the hell did he do?*

Xi Feng knew full well what a cruel bastard Xu Qing was. Even with eyes watching, the bastard would try to finish him off.

Then, a voice transmission flew to him.

*—Shall I help you?*

The one who sent the voice transmission was Geom Mugeuk.

Xi Feng had completely forgotten his existence. Now that he thought of it, today’s incident had happened because of him. Annoyingly, the bastard was watching the situation with his arms crossed.

*—He’ll use the duel as an excuse to cripple you. With that method you guys often use.*

*—Who are you?*

*—Don’t want help? Suit yourself.*

Xu Qing cleared those around him and made space.

“Young Master Xi, I challenge you to a duel. Surely you aren’t going to run away like a coward?”

Xi Feng’s heart raced.

*—How would you help?*

*—Expose Xu Qing’s evil deeds! Then I’ll help you.*

*—Then he’ll really try to kill me.*

*—Isn’t that the case now, too?*

Xi Feng sensed the killing intent blooming in Xu Qing’s eyes. He realized Xu Qing might truly kill him. A man who lived and died by his pride had been struck in front of people. At the very least, he would try to sever an arm or a leg.

Conversely, if he fought back and killed or injured Xu Qing, that would also be a problem. If he mishandled the heir of the Cheonhwamun, they would not let it go.

Pushed into a life-or-death corner, Xi Feng looked at Geom Mugeuk. This had started because of him, yet no one was paying him any attention. He was a devil-like bastard. Perhaps that was exactly why Xi Feng felt he could trust him.

Just as Xu Qing approached, Xi Feng spoke in a loud voice.

“A while ago, you killed the Yonghwabang martial artists you quarreled with and buried their bodies.”

In an instant, silence flowed over the place. Until now, it had been merely an interesting occurrence; now the situation had become serious.

Xi Feng shouted again.

“You stabbed a passing traveler to death, and even killed a demonic agent.”

The words he had shared when they were close were now pouring out.

Resolved to kill Xi Feng without fail, Xu Qing looked around and said,

“There’s no one who believes that nonsense, right?”

Heavy silence was all that filled the place. A few who met Xu Qing’s eyes smiled awkwardly and said,

“Of course we don’t believe it!”

“We don’t believe it.”

Xu Qing was triumphant. Even if they believed it, there would be no one who dared speak of today’s events aloud.

Then, a single voice echoed through the banquet hall.

“I believe it.”

The one who spoke was Geom Mugeuk. Everyone’s gaze turned toward Geom Mugeuk.

The corners of Xu Qing’s mouth curled up.

“Fine, let’s say you believe that nonsense. So what are you going to do about it?”

To Xu Qing’s question, Geom Mugeuk replied with a smile.

“Something like this?”

Geom Mugeuk pointed at the door and flicked his finger.

Then the door began to open slowly.

*Creeeak.*

The first to enter was Jeogyeon.

Bodyguard martial artists followed, and behind them, fifty Honam Branch demonic agents filed in.

“What the hell are these bastards?”

The ones nearby tried to block them, but they were ruthlessly beaten by Jeogyeon and the bodyguards and sent flying.

Someone recognized the emblem emblazoned on Jeogyeon’s chest.

“It’s the Demon Cult! The demonic agents!”

At his cry, everyone froze.

Only then did they all see the fiendish emblem carved on the intruders’ chests.

Screams erupted everywhere. Some trembled, some watched on their knees, some tried to flee through the windows only to be caught, some hid under tables. It was a veritable cauldron of chaos.

Even Xu Qing was startled.

*They really are demonic agents!*

Arrogant as he was, he was not someone who could act big in front of the Demon Cult.

Geom Mugeuk spoke to his subordinates.

“Xi Feng has exposed all the crimes, so take Xu Qing to the dungeon and begin the investigation.”

Xu Qing reflexively shouted.

“Who do you think you are! You bastard!”

Then Jeogyeon roared, unleashing a cold demonic aura.

“Mind your tongue! He is the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult!”

At those words, Xu Qing was shocked to his core. Xi Feng and the others were also terrified.

Someone hastily knelt, and the others all knelt as well.

“Please spare us!”

Everyone shouted for their lives.

Amid that uproar, Xu Qing stood blankly, muttering with a vacant expression.

“...The Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult? Why? Why would the Young Cult Leader come here?”

Geom Mugeuk slowly walked toward him and said,

“We aren’t on the same level, but shall we have a special duel?”

Chapter 268: No One Really Knew
-------------------------------
Even at the words suggesting a martial contest, Seo Cheong did not back down and remained standing where he was.

He was the sort who lived and died by his pride. With Seok Pung watching, and the others watching too, he did not want to kneel alongside them.

Geom Mugeuk returned to Seo Cheong the very words he had spoken when they first met.

“You have a strong sense of pride? Well, for a martial artist, pride is life itself.”

The instant he heard those words, killing intent surged up in Seo Cheong. He pictured himself stabbing Geom Mugeuk before him to death with his sword. He imagined cutting down even the demonic cultists who rushed at him. The thought of standing tall, drenched in blood, while everyone looked on in shock and admiration, was thrilling enough by itself.

He did not like Geom Mugeuk’s clear yet deep eyes, which seemed to see right through that desire of his.

“How could I dare cross swords with the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult?”

No matter how wounded his pride was, he was not foolish enough to charge at the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult.

“When someone begged you like this, did you grant his request?”

At first, Seo Cheong wondered what he meant. But soon, he remembered. Not long ago, the man he had forced into a martial contest and smashed apart had begged in the exact same way. He had said he was no match for him. He had begged to be forgiven just once.

“Why on earth are you doing this to me?”

“Didn’t that fellow explain the reason earlier?”

“Everything that bastard said is a lie!”

At that, Seok Pung, who was kneeling, shouted.

“I know where the bodies were buried.”

Seeing him come at him as if to drag them both down together, Seo Cheong ground his teeth.

“That brainless son of a bitch!”

The two glared at each other as if they would kill one another.

Seo Cheong’s gaze turned back to Geom Mugeuk.

“They were all people of no importance. Low-ranking martial artists, passersby on the road. I will compensate them all.”

Geom Mugeuk opened his aura and released demonic energy.

In that instant, Seo Cheong plunged with a splash into seawater of unfathomable depth.

The pressure that constricted his entire body in an instant left Seo Cheong unable to breathe.

He tried to resist, but it was useless. The internal energy method he had trained in since childhood, his sword art, the methods of controlling his mind—nothing worked.

A scream slipped from his mouth. It felt as though he would either be crushed to death as he was, or suffocate.

‘You bastard! Why the hell are you doing this to me? You must have killed all kinds of people too!’

Unable to endure it, Seo Cheong cried out.

“Please stop!”

Then, as if it had all been a lie, the demonic energy crushing him vanished.

Seo Cheong opened the eyes he had squeezed shut. He saw Geom Mugeuk’s legs before him. While enduring the demonic energy, he had unknowingly fallen to his knees.

His face flushed with humiliation, and he sprang to his feet.

Honestly, Seo Cheong could not understand it. No, he felt wronged. If it were anyone else, perhaps, but shouldn’t the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult understand him better? To him, they should be even more insignificant.

“I said I would compensate them. I will compensate their families with money they could never touch in their entire lives. Wouldn’t that make their deaths all the more valuable?”

Geom Mugeuk looked at him and smiled.

Because Seo Cheong did not know what sort of person Geom Mugeuk was, he naturally misunderstood that smile. He did not know that his fate had already been decided as death, or as suffering even greater than death.

‘Yes, that’s it. No matter how mighty the Demonic Cult is, they can’t touch our Cheonhwa Sect.’

His father had once said it. Our Cheonhwa Sect is a proud sect desired by the Demonic Cult, by the Unorthodox Alliance, and even by the Martial Alliance.

“Call my father.”

“Very well, I will.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s ready answer, Seo Cheong felt relieved.

‘It’s done. I’m fine. It’s nothing. Nothing will happen to me just because I killed a few worthless things. That demonic cultist was a low-ranking martial artist too.’

Thinking it truly fortunate that he had not killed anyone important, Seo Cheong shook off his fear. As always, once his father came, everything would be resolved.

Next, Geom Mugeuk approached Seok Pung.

Seok Pung, still kneeling, trembled in fear as Geom Mugeuk came near. He was confused. Had this situation arisen because of the Young Cult Leader, or had he survived the crisis of nearly being killed by Seo Cheong thanks to the Young Cult Leader? He could not make sense of anything.

“You just need to go and testify exactly as you said earlier.”

At that, Seok Pung’s gaze turned to Seo Cheong, who stood ahead.

Geom Mugeuk drove a wedge into Seok Pung’s heart.

“Yes, you might be afraid of the Cheonhwa Sect and change your testimony. Now, from that moment on, who should you be afraid of?”

Geom Mugeuk slowly clenched his fist in front of his face.

The instant the fist was clenched tight—

Boom!

The sound of thunder rang out from his fist.

“Aaaagh!”

At the thunderclap that burst right before his eyes, Seok Pung nearly lost his soul. It was the sound of real thunder. Thunder came from a fist? If he were struck by that fist, his whole body would be reduced to powder.

“No, I’ll tell everything honestly. There are more evil deeds that bastard committed.”

He thought that if he ratted out all of Seo Cheong’s crimes, he himself would be able to slip away unharmed. Burying bodies together, beating a young man who worked here one-sidedly until he had nearly been crippled—things like that did not even register to him as crimes. Geom Mugeuk did not bother correcting that misconception.

“As expected. You looked clever from the first moment I saw you.”

Watching this, Seo Cheong snorted.

‘Even so, he can’t touch me. If he could, he would have done it already.’

Places like the Soya Gang could be frightened and touched. The thunderclap only made him more certain.

When Geom Mugeuk gave a signal, the demonic cultists of the Hunan Branch came in, sealed the internal energy of Seo Cheong, Seok Pung, and all the others there, then led them out.

Seo Cheong shook off the hands of the martial artists who tried to seize both his arms.

“I will walk on my own feet!”

Seo Cheong remained dignified to the very end.

Because he was so composed, the expressions of the others being dragged away with him also loosened somewhat. They had been terrified, but once they were being taken away without anyone getting hurt, they began to think, ‘This isn’t such a big deal, is it?’ They knew nothing.

In the empty banquet hall, only Geom Mugeuk and his guards remained once again.

Jeok Yeon said to Geom Mugeuk,

“You resolved it without lifting a finger.”

“I told you, didn’t I? This matter is nothing grand.”

“I thought you would challenge Seo Cheong to a martial contest and smash him to pieces.”

“His father is going to come see me. How could I do that?”

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk was thinking of two fathers.

This matter had begun with the father of Haejin, who had been assaulted here, and would end with the father of Seo Cheong, who had assaulted him.

“Let us see how this father comes out.”

* * *

Seo Baekjung (徐伯仲), the Sect Leader of the Cheonhwa Sect, was in the middle of a meeting.

Possessing an intense gaze and a cold impression, he was a self-made man who had raised the Cheonhwa Sect, once nothing more than a small sect in Hunan, into the foremost sect of the province. Having spent his entire life running toward success, he was working even today to make the Cheonhwa Sect stronger.

At that moment, his trusted aide Yu Maeng (柳孟) hurried in and reported through sound transmission.

—The young master has been taken to the Hunan Branch of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

At Yu Maeng’s report, Seo Baekjung showed no agitation at all.

—Have him wait until the meeting is over.

Even after receiving the report that his son had been taken, Seo Baekjung remained calm. He finished the meeting he had been conducting to the very end. His emotions changed so little that it was hard to believe he had received Yu Maeng’s report.

After the meeting ended and he sent everyone who had been with him out, only then did he ask,

“Was it the Young Cult Leader who took him?”

“Yes, that seems to be the case.”

After Yeo Sogwang’s death, rumors had spread widely throughout Hunan that the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult was at the branch.

“Did he take him knowing that he is my child?”

“He did.”

For an instant, an undisguisable displeasure passed around Seo Baekjung’s eyes.

“How did it happen?”

Yu Maeng reported carefully.

“It seems the young master committed murder.”

Seo Baekjung was not surprised at all. His reaction made it impossible to tell whether he had already known, or whether he simply did not care about such a trifling matter.

“Whom did he kill?”

“Several people. The problem is that among them, there seems to have been a martial artist from the Demonic Cult’s Hunan Branch.”

“Foolish!”

He did not mean that killing was foolish. His anger was at the fact that Seo Cheong had killed them and failed to handle the aftermath properly.

“It was revealed in a place where everyone was present, so it seems it will not be easy to smooth this matter over.”

“Then why was he taken by the Demonic Cult?”

“The Young Cult Leader was apparently present.”

“Did my boy invite him?”

“That much has not been confirmed. What has been confirmed is that a fight broke out at the banquet with Young Master Seok of the Soya Gang, and in the process, Young Master Seok seems to have leaked everything.”

Yu Maeng was always amazed by Seo Baekjung’s composure. Even in a situation where his child had been taken by the Demonic Cult, he did not respond emotionally. One might expect him to reveal rage and killing intent toward Seok Pung, but he showed no sign of it whatsoever.

And what was even more astonishing was that he invariably remembered the matters that seemed as if they would be buried and forgotten within that composure, and took revenge for them. Once this matter was over, Seok Pung would never remain unharmed.

“Prepare the carriage.”

* * *

“I pay my respects to the Young Cult Leader.”

Seo Baekjung greeted Geom Mugeuk courteously.

“It is our first meeting, Sect Leader.”

Inwardly, Seo Baekjung was surprised by Geom Mugeuk’s appearance. First, he was surprised by the fact that he was so young, and he was also surprised by his looks, which would stand out anywhere.

“I did not know the Young Cult Leader was such a handsome man.”

“Fortunately, because I did not take after my father, I often hear that.”

At the mention of the Heavenly Demon, Seo Baekjung grew tense inwardly.

“If the Cult Leader hears that, he will feel hurt.”

“In truth, if I had taken after my father, life would be easier. Our Sect Leader would not be able to treat me so comfortably either.”

“It seems I have been rude upon our first meeting.”

“Not at all. Please do not misunderstand. I like things to be comfortable.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled brightly.

Inwardly, Seo Baekjung sensed that the Young Cult Leader was not an easy man to deal with. If one threw out such words before a conversation, the other party was bound to feel uncomfortable, and he could naturally seize the initiative in the conversation and stand in a superior position.

‘Not bad.’

Then again, no matter how young he was, one must not take the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult lightly.

“I heard that my child caused trouble.”

“It would have been better if I had met you over a more pleasant matter.”

“It is my fault. I have been busy with work and failed to educate my child properly.”

Seo Baekjung rose from his seat and respectfully offered a cupped-fist salute.

It was an apology that hoped Geom Mugeuk would also stand and say that it was all right, that such things could happen when one was young, but Geom Mugeuk remained seated. He even said this:

“So it seems.”

Seo Baekjung bowed his head respectfully once again.

‘Look at this little bastard.’

He felt offended, but his tone became even more courteous.

“As you know, our Cheonhwa Sect is the foremost sect in Hunan. We have the greatest number of disciples, and the greatest number of experts.”

After displaying his power in this way, he apologized once more.

“Because of that, I failed to look after my household. I ask that you please understand and show leniency.”

“I would like that as well, but the crimes committed are too great, so it is difficult.”

“There must be many things our sect can do for your cult. We will carry them out and spend a lifetime repaying this debt of sin.”

He had spoken indirectly, but he meant that he would pay money. Since most of the serious problems he had encountered in his life had been solved with money and blades, he was trying to solve this one with money as well. After all, he could not face the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult with a blade.

‘In the end, what you want is money, isn’t it? How much do you want? Two hundred thousand taels? Three hundred thousand taels?’

Then Geom Mugeuk said something unexpected.

“I understand you did plenty of that work with Branch Leader Yeo.”

When the dead Yeo Sogwang was mentioned, Seo Baekjung flinched in surprise.

‘So this was it.’

Now Seo Baekjung understood. In this world, could his child be the only one who had committed sins? Yet the reason he had specifically seized his son was to pressure him into revealing what he had done with Yeo Sogwang.

‘So it isn’t one hundred thousand taels or two hundred thousand taels, but several million taels he wants me to cough up!’

He did not know Geom Mugeuk’s intentions. Even if they were explained, he would not understand. He would not understand that this matter had begun for the sake of a boy who carried liquor in the banquet hall, and that the matter related to Yeo Sogwang was an example meant to establish discipline in the branch of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. Even if he were told, he would suspect there was another hidden meaning.

“I apologize, but I do not quite understand what you mean.”

“When it becomes a situation like this, without fail…”

Geom Mugeuk added with a bright smile,

“Everyone says they don’t understand.”

Their gazes intertwined in midair.

Seo Baekjung realized this was not a problem that could be resolved this way. The other party wanted something he could not give.

“I have taken too much of your valuable time. I will withdraw for today.”

After respectfully offering a cupped-fist salute, Seo Baekjung left the room.

Jeok Yeon entered the place he had left behind.

Geom Mugeuk said to Jeok Yeon,

“I should have beaten that Seo Cheong bastard black and blue.”

“What do you mean?”

“He left without even seeing his son’s face.”

“Perhaps it was because he was angry?”

Geom Mugeuk rose from his seat and walked to the window.

“No. It is because he did not come for his son’s sake.”

No matter how angry he was, no matter how much he hated him, if his son had been taken by the Demonic Cult, it was only proper that he at least see his face and check whether he was injured.

And yet, right now, Seo Baekjung was consumed entirely by how he would deal with me from this point forward. So you two are not looking at each other either. Truly, you are not looking at each other in the most dreadful way.

He could see Seo Baekjung walking away in the distance.

“If he had come as a father, my fight would have been much harder.”

At that moment, Seo Baekjung briefly stopped and looked back this way.

Geom Mugeuk waved from the window. Seo Baekjung bowed his head politely and turned away.

“Instead of this meaningless greeting, you should have met your son.”

Whether to scold him, beat him, or promise to save him, he should have met his son.

Then again, if they had been that kind of father and son, perhaps this would never have happened in the first place.

* * *

Late at night, Seo Baekjung sat alone by the riverside, fishing.

From beyond the thick fog came the sound of footsteps.

Step, step.

With even strides, sounding as if he would walk this way no matter what happened in the world, someone approached.

A man emerged, parting the fog.

His bamboo hat was pulled low, so his face could not be seen, but he gave off a strange impression.

Looking at the lake where his fishing rod was cast, Seo Baekjung said,

“My son was taken by the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult.”

The words Demonic Cult had been spoken, and so had the words Young Cult Leader. Though those two terms combined into the most dangerous phrase in the martial world, the man wearing the bamboo hat answered without hesitation.

“We will resolve it.”

The man vanished into the fog with the same sound of footsteps as when he had come.

Chapter 269: I Saw Your Father in a New Light.
----------------------------------------------
Step, step.

With steady footfalls, the man in the bamboo hat arrived at a small workshop.

The man took off his bamboo hat and tossed it at the wall.

The hat landed lightly atop a yellow long robe hanging there.

The man was middle-aged, with long, narrow eyes. The mysterious gleam in them seemed to have overcome every scheme and stratagem, and the dark cast over his face spoke of the many storms he had endured.

The man sat down on a small chair and began carving a wooden doll.

Scrape, scrape.

In the small space, only the sound of the carving knife shaving wood could be heard. With extraordinary movements of his hands, the man carved the wood, and a wooden doll the size of a finger was taking shape.

Just then, a young man entered without a sound. With bright vitality in his eyes, he was a handsome man with a bold, refreshing air.

He approached the middle-aged man’s back as he carved the wood. His footwork was no ordinary thing; even as he drew his sword and took his steps, not the slightest sound arose.

Swiish.

The young man’s drawn sword came to rest beside the middle-aged man’s neck.

The scraping sound had ceased. It was not because of the sword aimed at his neck. The small carving knife that had been in the middle-aged man’s hand was now floating before the eyes of the young man who had swung his sword.

The young man withdrew his sword and reached out to grasp the carving knife in midair.

But the carving knife did not budge, as if something invisible held it fast.

The young man even used his internal energy to try to move it, but it was no use. At length, when the young man gave up on moving the carving knife, the middle-aged man spoke.

“An expert does not fall to an attack that flies in from behind. That is why he is called an expert. When you kill an expert, you must look him in the eyes as you do it.”

At those words, the young man asked,

“Is that not more difficult?”

“That is easier. Only when you realize that can you become a true expert capable of killing experts.”

Scrape, scrape.

Once again came the sound of the carving knife shaving wood. Before anyone knew it, the carving knife that had been floating in midair was in the middle-aged man’s hand. It was so natural that it felt as though the carving knife had never left his hand in the first place.

“I will keep it in mind, Master.”

The young man bowed his head respectfully.

The two were master and disciple.

Through the window, martial artists could be seen standing around the workshop. The characters embroidered on their chests were the same as those on the young man’s chest: Exterminate Demons.

They were none other than the warriors of the Demon Extermination Unit, the Murim Alliance’s finest elites.

The young man was Jin Hagun, grandson of the current Murim Alliance Leader and commander of the Demon Extermination Unit. He was the elder brother of Jin Haryeong, who had a connection with Geom Mugeuk. After entering the Demon Extermination Unit, he had risen to the position of commander through nothing but his own ability.

The middle-aged man was his master, Baek Cheongyeong.

“You have not been neglecting your training lately.”

Jin Hagun had yet to receive the martial arts of his grandfather, Jin Paecheon, the Murim Alliance Leader. His grandfather wanted him to become the successor proudly through his own skill.

He had publicly declared that he would grant no special favors simply because Jin Hagun was his grandson.

“I am cutting down on sleep to train.”

Jin Hagun walked over to the display stand placed in the center of the workshop. On top of the stand was a scene of martial artist dolls, personally carved by Baek Cheongyeong, engaged in battle.

The sight was so vivid that it seemed as though a real fight was taking place. A battle between martial artists dressed in white and martial artists dressed in black. It looked as if it were depicting a battle between black and white.

Looking down at it, Jin Hagun said,

“The rumors coming from the Demon Cult lately are far from ordinary.”

Baek Cheongyeong remained silent, absorbed only in his carving.

“They say Geom Mugeuk, who newly became the Young Cult Leader, is expanding his power after bringing all the Demon Venerables under his control.”

Even at those words, Baek Cheongyeong appeared unconcerned, turning the wooden doll he was carving this way and that as he examined whether it had been shaped well.

“To be honest, he bothers me.”

At that, Baek Cheongyeong’s hands stopped moving. He rose from his seat and walked over to his disciple by the display stand.

“How does this battle look to you?”

“The martial artists in white appear to be at a disadvantage.”

“The white-clad martial artists are greater in number, so why do you think that?”

“Because of this man.”

Jin Hagun pointed with his finger at the black-clad martial artist who was leading the battle as he swung his sword.

“You saw well. It is because of this man. This man is controlling the board and leading the battle.”

Baek Cheongyeong lowered his body and gazed at the wooden doll with his small, elongated eyes.

“Do you wish to play this role?”

“Yes, I want to stand in that place. No, I absolutely will stand there.”

“Yes. Since you were young, you have always been that way. You never feared a challenge, and you never gave up.”

Whenever his master praised him like this, Jin Hagun’s chest swelled. That was why he trained harder in everything and tried never to give up. After his parents died early, he had lived thinking of Baek Cheongyeong as his father.

“The one standing in that place right now is Geom Mugeuk.”

Jin Hagun could not refute him. He knew that Geom Mugeuk had risen to the position of Young Cult Leader more splendidly than any successor in history.

By contrast, what about himself? He had not even officially risen to the position of successor yet.

Baek Cheongyeong slowly stroked the doll with his finger.

“How will you break this momentum?”

As if he had been waiting for the question, Jin Hagun answered.

“I received a report that Geom Mugeuk is currently in the Hunan region. They say he is pressuring Cheonhwamun while holding the son of the Cheonhwamun Sect Leader in custody there.”

He had been paying attention to the movements of Geom Mugeuk, whom he regarded in his heart as a rival.

“Cheonhwamun is a sect with formidable power, but it is impossible for them to face the Demon Cult alone. If you can save them from the Demon Cult’s grasp…”

Baek Cheongyeong’s gaze turned toward the hero doll.

“You will be able to lead the battles to come.”

Jin Hagun asked cautiously,

“But is Cheonhwamun not originally a sect that moved back and forth between the Demon Cult and the Sado Alliance?”

As it was known to the outside world, Cheonhwamun maintained four parts of its dealings with the Demon Cult, four with the Sado Alliance, and two with the Murim Alliance.

At that, Baek Cheongyeong’s gaze turned to the doll again.

“Do you think you can stand in that place by doing easy things?”

Jin Hagun was confident in everything, but he could not boast about this matter.

“If I followed my heart, I would lead the Demon Extermination Unit, strike the Hunan Branch, and rescue the Cheonhwamun Sect Leader’s son, but…”

If he did that, a war with the Demon Cult would break out. Far from becoming a hero, he might become a sinner for the ages.

After hesitating for a moment, Jin Hagun spoke carefully.

“As commander of the Demon Extermination Unit, I possess a unique authority that can be used during my term when dealing with the Demon Cult. If the Alliance Leader accepts my special authority, we can apply pressure on the Demon Cult in the name of the Murim Alliance. However, I do not know whether Cheonhwamun is a sect worth that much.”

“Cheonhwamun is a great sect in itself, but there are also dozens of small and medium sects that follow it. If you turn Cheonhwamun back to the orthodox faction, there will be no greater merit than that.”

“I understand that the Cheonhwamun Sect Leader is not an easy man. He has managed to walk the tightrope between the orthodox, unorthodox, and demonic factions with infuriating skill, and later he may collude with the Sado Alliance and ignore the fact that we helped him.”

Jin Hagun considered the Cheonhwamun Sect Leader, who had his feet in all sorts of places, to be a foxlike man.

Then came a startling sentence from Baek Cheongyeong’s mouth.

“I will resolve that point.”

Jin Hagun was astonished. His master was well-versed in the state of Jianghu, but he had never personally stepped into Murim’s affairs. And the reason for it was enough to move Jin Hagun.

“If you accomplish this matter, it will become a decisive merit in your becoming the successor of the Murim Alliance. That is why I am stepping forward.”

Jin Hagun cupped his fist respectfully and paid his respects.

“Thank you very much, Master.”

Now that his master had stepped forward like this, there was no longer any reason to hesitate.

“Then I will proceed with the matter at once.”

Jin Hagun, who had been about to leave after offering his farewell, flicked the hero doll with his finger and knocked it down.

After Jin Hagun left, Baek Cheongyeong continued carving the doll.

Scrape, scrape.

How long had he carved the doll?

Baek Cheongyeong came to the display stand and stood the fallen doll upright.

Then he placed the newly carved doll in the center of the board. The doll, whether it was himself or Jin Hagun, stood proudly with its arms crossed.

* * *

Several days had passed since Cheonhwamun Sect Leader Seo Baekjung came and went.

He had taken no action whatsoever to save his son. It certainly did not seem as though he would give up on his son like this. Geom Mugeuk quietly waited to see how the other party would act while training in the Sicheon Secret Art.

The more he trained in the Sicheon Secret Art, the more, little by little, a time difference began to appear. The moment he could use the Sicheon Secret Art and compare the time it took for his internal energy to recover with the time gained, thereby seeing a temporal profit, he would train only while using the Sicheon Secret Art. As a secret art of defying the heavens and reversing time, it was not easy, but he trained and trained again.

After finishing his training in the Sicheon Secret Art, he was drinking tea for a while in the main hall when Jeokyeon entered with Seo Cheong. Seo Cheong had begged to meet him, so Geom Mugeuk had told Jeokyeon to bring him.

As soon as he stepped inside, Seo Cheong shouted as if in protest.

“Why have you not contacted my father?”

He had been imprisoned in the branch’s dungeon. Though he had been locked in the dungeon, his spirit had not been dampened at all.

“I contacted him.”

“Do not lie. If you had contacted him, there is no way he would not have come yet. Young Cult Leader, my father is not such an unreasonable man. If you speak frankly with him, you will be able to obtain what you want.”

That was the conclusion he had come to while imprisoned in the dungeon. The reason the Demon Cult was doing this was to draw Cheonhwamun over to their side. If not, there was no way they would imprison him like this. Surely they would not imprison the successor of Cheonhwamun just because he had killed a few passersby, low-ranking warriors, and such insignificant things, would they?

“Your father has already come and gone.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Seo Cheong was startled.

“What do you mean? Are you saying he came and went without meeting me?”

When Geom Mugeuk nodded, Seo Cheong was shocked and flustered.

“Nonsense!”

After standing blankly for a moment, he began pacing this way and that, babbling incoherently.

“If you are going to sow discord, do it properly. If you are the Young Cult Leader of the Demon Cult, then act like the Young Cult Leader and contact my father as promised.”

He walked to the table and tried to pour himself tea with trembling hands. But when he felt his own hand shaking, he turned back again.

At one moment he did not believe that his father had come and gone, and at the next he did.

“No matter who you are, you are in great trouble now. The fact that my father left without even meeting me means he is truly furious.”

Just then, Geom Mugeuk abruptly asked,

“How much do you know about your father?”

For an instant, Seo Cheong flinched.

“Young Cult Leader, then how much do you know?”

Geom Mugeuk thought Seo Cheong’s appearance was no different from his own before his regression.

If the him of that time had experienced something like this, he would have argued just as Seo Cheong did. He would have lashed out even more fiercely, saying, What do you know that makes you act so high and mighty? Because the fact that he had almost nothing to answer about his father would have driven him into a corner.

It was precisely then. A subordinate of the branch came and delivered an urgent letter that had flown in from Tongcheongak.

He thought it was a message about who had been chosen as the Hunan Branch Leader, but the contents written there were truly unexpected. It was a notice ordering him to release Seo Cheong.

Because he had never expected such a letter to arrive, Geom Mugeuk was astonished. The fact that such a decision had come down from Tongcheongak meant that his father’s permission was also involved. That, in turn, meant that immense power had intervened.

And the fact that the detailed circumstances had not been written and sent was likely his father’s will.

Now then, how will you handle this matter?

Even after he had become the Young Cult Leader, his father’s tests continued.

“Release our Young Master Seo.”

Jeokyeon was also shocked at the order to release him. But soon, Jeokyeon unsealed Seo Cheong’s internal energy.

With a face as joyful as if he might fly, Seo Cheong shouted loudly.

“What did I tell you? Did I not say it would turn out like this?”

He strode over to the table, poured tea, and drank it.

“Would you like a cup as well?”

Seo Cheong guffawed as he drank the tea. As if nothing had ever happened, he had regained his true nature.

He glared at Jeokyeon and spoke in a threatening tone.

“I remember every single face I have seen in this place.”

When Jeokyeon glared back at him even more frighteningly with his one eye, Seo Cheong turned his gaze to Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk was looking at him and smiling.

“Why are you laughing?”

“Because it is interesting. I had been feeling drained until now. Catching our Young Master Seo is not the end, is it? Young Master Yang, Young Master Jin, Young Master Cheon… all sorts of young masters will keep growing back no matter how many I pull out. But someone moved the main cult? I, the Young Cult Leader, was handling this matter, and they overturned it?”

Geom Mugeuk was sincerely delighted.

“This is several times more impressive than storming this place and rescuing you. I am seeing your father in a new light.”

“You realize that only now? I am the successor of Cheonhwamun!”

He strode out while shouting loudly.

“We will never meet again.”

“We will meet again soon.”

As Seo Cheong ran out without even looking back, Jeokyeon asked,

“What happened?”

“It seems the Cheonhwamun Sect Leader moved either the Murim Alliance or the Sado Alliance.”

Otherwise, the Heavenly Demon Hall would never have made such a decision.

“What do you intend to do?”

“They blocked the main cult’s Tongcheongak, so I will have to use what is mine.”

Jeokyeon sensed that, if anything, Geom Mugeuk was excited. That excitement was the joy one felt upon meeting a formidable foe.

In a situation like this, he, as a guard, had to be more rational than ever. And yet he realized that he, too, was just as excited. He was secretly looking forward to what would happen next. Even though, as a bodyguard, he ought to wish for nothing to happen.

While Jeokyeon let out a light sigh, Geom Mugeuk walked outside.

“Where are you going? I will escort you.”

“You cannot escort me. Wait here.”

“I can follow—”

He could not. The instant Geom Mugeuk kicked off the ground and launched himself into the air, he became a dot in the blink of an eye and vanished.

“…It seems I cannot. I will wait here.”

At that moment, Samho, who had been in the yard, said to Jeokyeon,
