What would happen if his master found out? Their relationship would probably become irreparable. If his grandfather found out? He, too, would surely be deeply disappointed. A problem had arisen, yet instead of resolving it himself, he had dragged in a demonic cultist. Even knowing that, he had drawn Geom Mugeuk in. That was how great a sense of crisis he felt.

It was just then.

Someone passing by the window stopped in his tracks and looked inside.

The person whose eyes met Jin Hagun’s was Geom Mugeuk.

“Unit Leader Jin! Why are you here? I was attending the gathering because your sister invited me. I was looking for the privy, but what a coincidence this is. As expected, it seems we are bound together by fate.”

Jin Hagun, who had been staring at Geom Mugeuk, soon let out a sigh.

“Stop with the absurd acting and come in.”

Geom Mugeuk laughed and entered the room.

“How did you know?”

“Because if my sister hadn’t told you, there’s no way you and I would meet here. You’re not the sort of person who wanders around because he can’t find the privy, are you? And you are even less the sort of person one would coincidentally run into like this.”

“As expected! Our Unit Leader Jin is clever. Later, if we’re going to strike the Murim Alliance, we’ll have to rack our brains tremendously too.”

“I’m not in the mood to indulge your jokes right now. So stop.”

Jin Hagun was worried how this moment would be remembered in his life. Would it begin with, “It was a truly foolish choice,” or would it begin with, “If not for that choice then, I would not be who I am now”? He was standing at that crossroads.

“How did you know I was the one who called you?”

“Because I know how important the Murim Alliance is to you.”

“What do you know about me?”

“You’re someone who even gave up Cheonhwa Sect in order to become the Alliance Leader, are you not? That one fact alone tells me how badly you want to become the Murim Alliance Leader.”

Jin Hagun accepted those words with silence.

“Does it trouble you that you called me here? You don’t need to worry about that.”

Jin Hagun waited for his next words with an expression asking what he meant.

Then unexpected words flowed from Geom Mugeuk.

“You are not yet the successor, are you? If a successor had called another successor, that could certainly become a problem. But right now, the leader of the Demon Annihilation Unit called his sister’s friend. He did not call me hoping for help, but because there was something he needed to investigate.”

“Then you came because a mere unit leader of the Murim Alliance, not even a successor, called you? You, the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult?”

“I’m fine. I’m a broad-minded man.”

“You say that so easily with your own mouth.”

“If I don’t tell you, how will you know I’m this broad-minded? You’d be too busy worrying about the eyes of the world when you don’t need to.”

“Truly, you are…”

“A man who knows how to praise himself and insult the other party at the same time.”

How could anyone match Geom Mugeuk with words? In the end, Jin Hagun could only shake his head with an expression that said there was no stopping him.

Geom Mugeuk stood, poured tea from the pot on the table in the corner, and asked,

“Why did you call me?”

When it came time to answer, Jin Hagun hesitated. Should he truly speak about his master? About no one else, but his master.

Geom Mugeuk brought over two cups of tea. The sight was so natural that it seemed almost as though Geom Mugeuk were the one receiving Jin Hagun.

“I’ll try to guess why you called me.”

After looking down at the teacup for a moment, Geom Mugeuk suddenly said,

“You are afraid you may end up killing that person with your own hands.”

“!”

In that instant, Jin Hagun’s eyes flew wide open.

“You called me because you cannot bring yourself to kill him. To use me as your blade.”

They were words Jin Hagun had never expected, yet at the same time, they struck the heart of the matter.

Jin Hagun admitted that, deep in his heart, the words Geom Mugeuk had once spoken had come to mind. That in the end, he might have to kill his master. Once that premonition arose, it had never left his heart.

“Why did you not tell the Alliance Leader about something this important?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s serious gaze, Jin Hagun felt that the time had come to reveal everything.

“Because he would not believe me. Even if he did, I thought that person would notice first in the process of handling this matter. There must be many people within the Alliance who serve as his eyes and ears.”

“Who is that person?”

Jin Hagun finally revealed the existence of that person.

“My master.”

Even Geom Mugeuk was hearing of his master for the first time.

“You had a master?”

“You would not know, because it is not known to the outside. My master was extremely averse to being known to the world.”

One thing was certain. When the Murim Alliance Leader’s Hall had been pushed back by Hwa Mugi and the Murim Alliance Leader and all his blood kin had died, his master had not been among the victims. At the very least, he was not someone who would die protecting his disciple.

“Through this incident, I realized it. That my master is strange.”

“Indeed. Masters are always strange. Mine is trying to bring down a cliff with his fists.”

Whether Geom Mugeuk was joking or serious, that was truly how he felt.

“I would rather he were strange in that way.”

“How was he strange? Tell me in detail.”

“I have no solid proof. Just a feeling. And yet I called you… so perhaps I’m the strange one after all.”

“You’re not strange. It’s because you’re too clever.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Jin Hagun raised his head.

“What do you mean?”

“Even to me, this affair was certainly strange. To begin with, it was odd that the Cheonhwa Sect Leader sought help from your side.”

“Isn’t it possible my master and the Cheonhwa Sect Leader were close?”

“Then let me ask you one thing. Did your master mention revenge?”

Jin Hagun’s heart sank for an instant. Because he had been focusing on the sense of incongruity around his master, he had missed that point.

Jin Hagun shook his head. And that was not the only thing he had missed.

“Did he mourn the Cheonhwa Sect Leader’s death? No, did he even ask who killed him?”

There had been no mourning, nor had he asked who had killed him. Why hadn’t he thought of that? It was truly such a basic thing. Suddenly, he recalled what Geom Mugeuk had said.

—Isn’t it precisely because he’s someone close to you that you fail to see it?

When something happens, rather than such objective judgments, countless memories of the past and one’s relationship with that person are bound to come to mind first.

“So what did your master say?”

“He said we should take back the Cheonhwa Sect. He told me to find a way.”

“With a relationship no deeper than this, why did the Cheonhwa Sect Leader ask your master for help? Just what kind of relationship was it? Even viewed objectively, isn’t it strange? The master of the Murim Alliance Leader’s grandson is in some incomprehensible relationship with the Cheonhwa Sect Leader, who is within the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult’s sphere of influence? Your instincts worked well. And your choice to call me was excellent.”

Geom Mugeuk’s barrage of praise continued.

“That is why I look forward to the day you become Alliance Leader. I want to stand with a wise man like you.”

Geom Mugeuk was endlessly provoking him. Then again, he was the sort of man who could shake people like this; so in the moment of crisis, it made sense that he had been the first to come to mind. That he had even come into his dreams and given him faith.

Jin Hagun no longer hesitated or wavered.

“What should I do now?”

To the very end, Geom Mugeuk was the spokesman for Jin Hagun’s heart.

“Since they tried to play with you, shouldn’t you become a dangerous toy for them?”

Chapter 280: I Must See It for Myself.
--------------------------------------
Jin Haryeong’s body was in the banquet hall, but her mind was not there.

Even as she greeted and conversed with the rising talents, she kept thinking of Geom Mugeuk and Ian. Ian’s beautiful figure came to mind, and she could feel her bearing. She also recalled Geom Mugeuk standing beside her.

‘I wonder if Orabeoni is doing well?’

In such a state, there was no way the rising talents showing her favor would catch her eye.

Among them were handsome men, and among them were those strong in martial arts. But when she thought of Geom Mugeuk, they all looked like children. It was a side effect of coming to know Geom Mugeuk.

After that gathering, which felt especially long today, finally ended and she returned to her quarters, Jin Hagun was waiting for her.

“Orabeoni!”

Who knew she would be this glad to see her brother?

“I was just about to go see you. What happened?”

Judging by Jin Hagun’s expression, which was not bad, it seemed the talk had ended well.

Instead of answering, Jin Hagun asked her,

“How was the gathering?”

“Memorize names, memorize faces, listen to every kind of boasting, pretend to be friendly, then let’s overthrow them, Demonic Cult! Let’s defeat them, Evil Dao Alliance! Tediously the same as always.”

“That tedious work will one day protect the righteous murim.”

“You protect the murim, Orabeoni. My role only goes this far.”

“Do you think there will be anywhere to hide if the Demonic Cult takes control of the murim?”

Her brother was suffering from a side effect too. A side effect that came from living as the Alliance Leader’s grandson. From childhood, the fear that the Demonic Cult and the Evil Dao Alliance might invade had taken deep root in her brother’s heart.

“Do you really think Geom Mugeuk is the sort of person who dreams of unifying the murim? While exterminating all of us?”

No matter how she thought about it, she could not imagine it. Geom Mugeuk standing atop a mountain of piled corpses.

“Who knows what kind of person he is? One thing is certain—if he dreams of unifying the murim, stopping him will not be easy.”

It was not something their grandfather had to stop. That was a matter for the generation of Jin Hagun and Jin Haryeong.

“You can never know whether your faith in someone is justified while you are still in the process. The only time that faith is proven is when the result comes out. And we are still in the process.”

It applied to his relationship with his master, and to his relationship with Geom Mugeuk as well. What did it matter if someone did well a hundred times? If they revealed their true colors at the final moment, it would all end in catastrophe.

“Oh, so you can say things like that too. Impressive, isn’t it? Our Orabeoni!”

After a brief pause, Jin Hagun said,

“Geom Mugeuk said it.”

“Ah!”

She seemed to grow solemn for a moment, then soon burst into loud laughter.

“Our Orabeoni, who was doing so well, has finally met his match.”

Even hearing such words, Jin Hagun did not get angry or displeased at all.

Because she was the only person to whom he could open his heart. The only person with whom he could comfortably have such sensitive conversations.

“What do you think of him, Orabeoni?”

Jin Haryeong was curious about the Geom Mugeuk her brother saw.

As much as she had, Jin Hagun also had much to say when it came to Geom Mugeuk. He was clearly someone worthy of trust, yet also someone one did not know how far to trust.

Even so, Jin Hagun acknowledged the other man.

“He is a person of a different scale from me.”

Jin Hagun held out one hand and curled it as if grasping a ball.

“If I am a person of this size.”

This time, he made a gesture of holding a large ball in front of his chest with both hands.

“Then Geom Mugeuk is a person of this size. If I am to face him and win, I will have to start by acknowledging that.”

After meeting Geom Mugeuk, her brother had certainly changed. The fact that her proud brother could say such a thing meant he had changed.

Jin Haryeong liked that change, yet on the other hand, she was also worried. Worried about what might happen if he became too absorbed in Geom Mugeuk.

“Can you handle it?”

“I have to try. If I can’t do it now, I won’t be able to do it later either.”

Jin Hagun’s gaze was firm. Though he was receiving help for now, she could feel his will never to be swayed by Geom Mugeuk.

“Watch me. Watch how I surpass him.”

Jin Haryeong clenched her fist tightly, her expression saying she believed him.

This time, Jin Hagun asked,

“How was it for you? You saw Geom Mugeuk after so long, didn’t you?”

Her clenched fist opened limply.

“That’s even more repetitive. I’m always surprised and flustered whenever I see him. But today, he wasn’t the problem.”

This time, Jin Haryeong imitated Jin Hagun.

“If I am this beautiful, then this time I met someone this beautiful.”

Looking at her brother, who could not understand, Jin Haryeong smiled.

“I realized that Hubei was a vast well.”

* * *

Jin Hagun arrived at Baek Cheongyeong’s workshop.

At the door, he recalled Geom Mugeuk’s advice.

—Do not watch his mood. Act as you normally do. Rather than trying to look good, subtly reveal that you are dissatisfied because of this matter. Only then will he not suspect you.

But Baek Cheongyeong was not in the workshop.

Jin Hagun was about to leave when he suddenly looked at the display stand. The display stand where the dolls had been placed was covered with a black cloth. Sometimes it was covered like this. But now, the cloth covering it looked different.

Just as Jin Hagun slowly walked over and was about to lift the cloth, he sensed someone behind him.

When Jin Hagun turned around, Baek Cheongyeong was standing at the door.

“Master.”

“You’ve come?”

He was curious about what was beneath the cloth, but Jin Hagun left it as it was and walked toward his master.

Baek Cheongyeong passed by him, sat in his seat, and began carving.

Scrape, scrape.

These days, the amount of time his master spent carving had increased greatly. In the past, it had seemed to be only a hobby.

He had even covered the display case with cloth, so what was he carving so intently?

“So, have you thought about how to reclaim Cheonhwa Gate?”

His master’s gaze was still on the doll he was carving.

“I intend to win over those among Cheonhwa Gate’s experts who support the righteous faction. Cheonhwa Gate has long maintained neutrality, so their loyalty toward the Demonic Cult is not strong. Of course, the same applies to our Alliance and the Evil Dao Alliance. Since the atmosphere of a sect changes according to its sect leader and leadership, it will take a long time, but if we can win over the righteous-leaning experts within Cheonhwa Gate to our side…”

Even as Jin Hagun spoke, he carefully observed Baek Cheongyeong’s every move. He could sense that his master was not listening to him attentively.

Was Master truly waiting for him to come up with a method?

As if to say that was not the case, Baek Cheongyeong cut off Jin Hagun’s words and said,

“Does your pride not hurt?”

In a calm tone, he provoked Jin Hagun.

“The three successors of the righteous, evil, and demonic factions gathered in one place. Two of them returned with lavish gifts, while only you returned empty-handed.”

He was bringing up pride and comparing him to the other successors? Jin Hagun was angry, but he forced himself to answer calmly.

“I do not think this is a matter of pride. Hunan was originally their territory. And the son of Cheonhwa Gate’s leader was a beast in human skin. From the beginning, there was no gift for me there.”

Jin Hagun faithfully followed Geom Mugeuk’s advice. He did not simply admit fault unconditionally and follow his master’s opinion.

“Master, why is Cheonhwa Gate so important to you?”

“Do you think I am doing this because of Cheonhwa Gate?”

“Is that not so?”

“It is not.”

“Then why?”

Then, a startling answer flowed from Baek Cheongyeong’s mouth.

“To prevent war.”

At the word war, Jin Hagun froze.

“What do you mean?”

“Hunan borders Hubei, where the main headquarters of the Murim Alliance is located, and has been a strategic point for generations. Yet a large sect that maintained neutrality there has fallen into the hands of the Demonic Cult. It means the balance has been broken.”

“Even so, is it not too much of a leap to say that war will break out?”

“Why does our Alliance exist? It exists to eliminate even that faint possibility. War can break out from the smallest, most trivial incident. This time, you greatly increased the probability that a war would occur. Because of your desire to become Alliance Leader.”

For a moment, Jin Hagun could not answer at all. If this had been before he met Geom Mugeuk, he would have accepted his master’s words completely on the spot. He might even have blamed himself, thinking he had been greedy.

But not now. No matter how plausible it sounded, it was something to judge later.

Hiding his inner thoughts, Jin Hagun asked with a deliberately flustered expression,

“Then what should I do now?”

His master’s answer was cold.

“Why are you asking me that? You caused this, so you must find the answer.”

Scrape, scrape.

Baek Cheongyeong began carving again. Knowing it was an unspoken order to leave, Jin Hagun quietly bowed his head in farewell and left the place. All the while thinking that he still did not truly understand his master.

* * *

“Lower your arm a little more, and put more strength into your left leg.”

Geom Mugeuk was examining Ian’s sword forms.

Ian’s Flying Heaven Sword Art, which he had not seen in a long time, had advanced another step. Of course, she would still need more effort and time to reach great attainment. She had to train and train again at the nine-star level, letting it ripen. That way, when the time came, she would reach great attainment as if a flower bud were bursting open.

“Put more strength into your waist. If your center collapses, every posture collapses with it.”

The two of them were training at the safe house Jin Hagun had arranged for them.

“Focus! Why can’t you focus today?”

“I’m sorry.”

For some reason, she could not concentrate on martial arts training today. She did not know why.

‘This isn’t an opportunity that comes often. Focus!’

Ian swung her sword with all her might. When that round of martial arts training ended, Ian apologized again.

“I’m sorry, young master.”

She thought he would scold her severely, but Geom Mugeuk showed an unexpected reaction.

“It was better, actually. When I look at you, sometimes you don’t seem human. People have times when they can’t focus like this, and they make mistakes too. That’s how it should be.”

“Thank you for saying that, but I’m still disappointed. It’s been so long since you personally taught me martial arts, young master.”

“I will teach you often until you reach great attainment, so don’t worry.”

Ian swayed her body lightly as if dancing, pleased. Compared to the rigid her from when he first regressed, she felt much freer now.

Yes, you are changing little by little like that. Those changes will pile up and pile up, and someday they will become a different life. Ian, in this life, you will not die in a pool of blood. I will let you live while dancing just as you are now.

Circling around me like a child, Ian asked,

“But young master. Did you really bring me here to introduce me to Miss Jin?”

“No.”

“Then why did you bring me?”

“Why else? I just used it as an excuse because I wanted to come out and have fun with you after so long.”

At that, Ian looked back toward her own backside.

“A few of my tails are gone. You took them, didn’t you, young master?”

Knowing that it meant his answer had been far too excellent, Geom Mugeuk made a show of stroking his own tail.

“To tell the truth, I had more tails from the beginning.”

As they were laughing together, Jin Hagun came to that place.

He had come thinking only Geom Mugeuk would be there, but when he saw a woman with him, Jin Hagun did not approach and stood waiting at a distance.

“Welcome.”

Only when Geom Mugeuk called him did Jin Hagun approach.

By then, Ian had put on her mask. Of course, that mask could not hide her beauty.

“I am Ian, who serves the young cult leader.”

“I am Jin Hagun, captain of the Demon-Annihilation Unit.”

“Then please talk.”

“See you next time.”

Ian bowed politely and entered the residence.

Jin Hagun could tell at a glance. He knew who his sister had meant by a great beauty.

Geom Mugeuk came beside him and asked casually,

“Have you fallen for her?”

Jin Hagun looked at Geom Mugeuk with an expression asking what nonsense that was.

“There is no need to be embarrassed. Is it not natural for a man to fall for a beautiful woman? I understand everything.”

“Do you fall for a woman you meet for the first time so easily?”

If he came at him like this, Geom Mugeuk could not simply let it pass. It was as if Jin Hagun had drawn his sword before him.

“One can fall in love at first sight.”

“I do not. I do not give my heart to someone I do not even know.”

Since it was obvious he was almost completely sincere, Geom Mugeuk attacked from another angle.

“Ah, Ian. You’ll have to try harder. You were not beautiful enough to move this man’s ironclad heart. I shall tell her. That you were not beautiful enough to move his heart.”

At that, Jin Hagun panicked.

“What are you saying! Do not tell her something like that for no reason!”

“But it is true, is it not? How dare she try to move me with mere beauty! Her beauty was not sufficient to move me!”

“No! She is sufficiently beautiful.”

“Then you’ve fallen for her?”

In the end, Jin Ha-gun shook his head with the expression of a man admitting defeat.

“Think whatever you like.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled the smile of a victor.

After they had exchanged such banter, Jin Ha-gun brought up his visit to his master.

“I went to see my master.”

Jin Ha-gun relayed the conversation he had had with his master exactly as it had happened.

Geom Mugeuk fell into thought for a moment. Watching him, Jin Ha-gun felt a strange tension. Geom Mugeuk’s face was completely different from when he had been joking around just moments ago.

In a little while, whatever words came from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth would have to come from his own as well. He had to learn to think that way. Only then would he be able to deal with Geom Mugeuk.

“Did those words perhaps shake your heart?”

“Isn’t it possible that my master truly did it for the sake of peace in the murim?”

“Nonsense. If he had acted for the sake of peace in the murim, he should never have tried to save the son of the Heavenly Flower Sect Master in the first place. Think about it. The Murim Alliance pressured us and took away a man the young cult leader of the Demonic Cult had captured to punish. My father let it pass because he was testing me, but what do you think would have happened if we had reacted harshly to that incident? That would have been the very moment we took another step closer to war.”

Jin Ha-gun nodded. He found himself regretting that he had not been able to say those words to his master on the spot.

“I’ll have to see your master myself.”

“Are you serious?”

Geom Mugeuk nodded. He had judged that it would not be easy to draw out the man’s true nature through Jin Ha-gun. Their opponent was moving far too cautiously.

“How? My master will never agree to meet you.”

His master had lived a secretive life until now. No matter how capable Geom Mugeuk was, Jin Ha-gun thought he would not be able to find a way to meet him.

Just then, another person entered. It was none other than Jin Ha-ryeong.

“So you were here too, Brother.”

“What brings you here?”

“I have no business with either of you. I came to see Ian.”

Geom Mugeuk said to her,

“Ian is in the room. The room at the end of the corridor. Oh, and when you go, tell Ian this. It still isn’t enough to move your brother’s heart……”

“Stop!”

Jin Ha-gun hurriedly cut him off. Jin Ha-ryeong, who could tell at a glance roughly what was going on, said to Geom Mugeuk,

“Even if you play with him, make sure you put him back where he belongs.”

“Of course. He’s the most dangerous toy in the murim.”

Jin Ha-gun gestured for Jin Ha-ryeong, who looked as if she could not understand what he meant, to ignore him and hurry inside.

After she went in, Jin Ha-gun said with a serious face,

“Are you going to keep joking around at a time this important?”

“I thought of a way to meet your master.”

To the startled and flustered Jin Ha-gun, Geom Mugeuk said meaningfully,

“I thought of it thanks to your sister.”

Chapter 281: The One Who Lost Their Way.
----------------------------------------
“Was our reunion too soon?”

Jin Haryeong, standing before the door, greeted her happily.

Water was dripping from Ian’s hair. Having come in after finishing her martial arts training, Ian had just washed up and changed into comfortable clothes.

“Isn’t a reunion with someone you’re glad to see better the sooner it happens? Please, come in.”

Jin Haryeong smiled and stepped inside. There are people who can make you feel better with just a single word. That was probably why she had come to see her.

As they sat down, a bottle of alcohol sat on the table.

“Were you planning to have a drink?”

“Ah, I looked around and found some.”

It seemed to be alcohol prepared for visitors to the inner quarters.

“Could I have a glass as well?”

“Of course. Just a moment, please. I had only planned to drink simply, so I didn’t prepare any snacks.”

“It’s fine. Let’s just drink.”

“Are you sure you’ll be alright?”

“You don’t think the granddaughter of the Alliance Leader only drinks when a feast is laid out, do you?”

Ian smiled and shook her head.

The two poured drinks for each other and clinked their glasses.

“Did something good happen today?”

“Why do you ask?”

“You have alcohol out, and this martial artist’s expression looks bright.”

Something good? Of course there was. She had received martial arts instruction from Geom Mugeuk after a long time, and what had made her feel even better than that were the words he had said to her.

—Then why did you bring me here?

—Why else? I wanted to hang out with you after a long time, so I made an excuse.

To the person who had taught her martial arts that only the bloodline of the Heavenly Demon could master; to the person who always told her that her happiness came first; and to the person who said such things to her—how could she not devote her loyalty?

Ian didn’t answer at all, but Jin Haryeong could feel it. That her good mood was related to Geom Mugeuk.

Ian naturally changed the subject.

“Isn’t it hard? Living as the granddaughter of the Martial Alliance Leader?”

“This martial artist asks the opposite.”

“Hmm?”

“Everyone asks how good it must be to be the Martial Alliance Leader’s granddaughter.”

“I must be a negative person.”

“You’ve never seen a negative person admit they are negative.”

Jin Haryeong emptied her glass and spoke to Ian.

“It’s hard. More than you’d think.”

“I thought so.”

“I think this martial artist must have it hard too? Serving someone who isn’t easy to serve.”

Apart from the question, Jin Haryeong felt envious of her for having been with Geom Mugeuk for so long.

“It’s not hard. When I’m with him, things happen that would never occur even once in an ordinary lifetime. Sometimes I even drink with the granddaughter of the Martial Alliance Leader.”

Jin Haryeong wholeheartedly agreed with Ian’s joking words.

“Right? You really never know what will happen in this world.”

Her relationship with Geom Mugeuk, and this moment right now.

The two shared various stories. Life in the Martial Alliance and life in the Demonic Cult—their lives that each had never experienced crossed back and forth every time their glasses emptied.

At times a subtle tension flowed between them, and they also talked about Geom Mugeuk. They talked about martial arts, and then about Geom Mugeuk again.

With honest feelings, Jin Haryeong had hoped to find lacking parts in Ian. But the more they talked, the more she only thought, this woman is fine.

When the bottle was completely empty, Jin Haryeong rose from her seat. She didn’t suggest drinking more. Instead, she made a proposal that would startle Ian.

“This martial artist, shall we be friends?”

“So impulsively? This is only the second time we’ve met.”

“So what? The martial world is a place where people kill each other upon first meeting.”

The words “let’s be friends” were the first time Jin Haryeong had ever said them to anyone. People lined up to become her friend. But she had never said those words to any of them. Yet here she was, proposing friendship first to Ian, whom she had only met twice. Jin Haryeong thought that she understood herself just as little as she understood the world’s unpredictable affairs.

“Will you really be alright? Being friends with a Demonic Cultist?”

“I’m fine. What about this martial artist?”

Ian gazed silently at Jin Haryeong for a moment, then smiled brightly and said:

“How could I hate having a wonderful friend like you?”

In that instant, the regret Jin Haryeong felt—wondering if she had spoken needlessly—vanished completely.

* * *

Jin Hagon visited his teacher again.

Scratch, scratch.

“Have you found a way?”

His teacher kept asking about the method. Now Jin Hagon knew. His teacher’s goal was not to hear the method, but to drive him into a corner. To get a grip on him. Because the fastest and surest way to bind a person was to make them feel intimidated.

“No. Today I have come for a different reason.”

At the somehow resolute answer, Baek Cheongyeong stopped his hands, which had been carving wood. When he looked up, Jin Hagon revealed his innermost thoughts frankly.

“I know you have been greatly disappointed in me lately. I made excuses to you, but deep in my heart, I am reflecting deeply.”

Baek Cheongyeong’s expression still showed no great change.

“There is something I wish to ask of you, Teacher.”

A slight puzzlement flashed in the corners of Baek Cheongyeong’s eyes. It was because it didn’t seem like Jin Hagon would ask for something in the current atmosphere, as his words suggested. In other words, it also meant that the request was that important.

“Speak.”

“During the previous Little Dragon Tournament, there was a connection between Geom Mugeuk, the Young Leader of the Demonic Cult, and Haryeong.”

Jin Hagon let out a light sigh and added:

“It seems Haryeong has taken a liking to him since then.”

Baek Cheongyeong listened to Jin Hagon’s words in silence.

“This foolish child invited the Demonic Cult’s Young Leader to the upcoming gathering of later-generation talents. And now the Young Leader has come to our Alliance in secret.”

“The Demonic Cult’s Young Leader is in our Alliance?”

Baek Cheongyeong’s gaze intensified.

“I too only learned of this fact a short while ago.”

“Are you saying that Geom Mugeuk has come to see Haryeong?”

“Yes, it seems he too has feelings for my younger sister.”

Baek Cheongyeong didn’t believe him.

“The Demonic Cult’s Young Leader, of whom I’ve heard rumors, is absolutely not someone to be trifled with. You saw him in person and even suffered at his hands, so wouldn’t you know well? Such a person came to our Alliance merely for a woman?”

“That’s right. He is no ordinary man. Precisely because of that, there can be no other reason.”

“What does that mean?”

“Would such a clever man choose to come to the Martial Alliance alone? When a clever man becomes a fool, there is only one reason.”

A few of the words Jin Hagon spoke were ones Geom Mugeuk had taught him.

—Will this work on your teacher?

—It will work because it’s about your younger sister. Matters of blood are beyond understanding and explanation.

Baek Cheongyeong still wore an expression of disbelief.

“So? What is it you wish to ask?”

It was a request Baek Cheongyeong had never anticipated.

“Please meet with Geom Mugeuk.”

He was someone who almost never showed emotion, but this time he couldn’t hide his surprise.

“What are you saying?”

“Meet him and warn him. Tell him to stay away from my sister.”

Baek Cheongyeong made a bewildered expression. He truly hadn’t expected such a request.

“Are you saying that after losing Cheonhwamun, you now want me to prevent your sister from being taken as well?”

Though he rebuked him with painful words, Jin Hagon did not back down.

“Cheonhwamun can be taken, but my sister absolutely cannot.”

“Then shouldn’t you, as her older brother, warn him?”

“He won’t listen to me. While handling the Cheonhwamun matter, he completely ignored and toyed with me. Rather, he would approach her even more just to spite me. The Geom Mugeuk I saw was that tenacious.”

“If you cannot do it, you must ask the Alliance Leader.”

“Don’t you know Grandfather’s temperament? He would immediately drive that man out. We should be grateful if he doesn’t attack him in anger. If driven out, that man will undoubtedly harbor resentment. He’s a madman who came alone to the Martial Alliance just to see my sister.”

“This is not my place to intervene. How am I to handle what the Alliance Leader and you cannot?”

Baek Cheongyeong refused firmly.

Jin Hagon knelt on the spot.

“You know better than anyone what my younger sister means to me. Until now, I have regarded and served you as my father.”

Because it was the truth, this moment must be endlessly painful and suffocating for Jin Hagon.

“Please heed this inadequate disciple’s earnest request.”

Scratch, scratch.

Baek Cheongyeong pondered as he carved the wood. Jin Hagon remained kneeling, not retreating.

How much time passed like that?

What was he thinking?

Having finished the carving he was working on, Baek Cheongyeong spoke.

“Bring him tomorrow.”

* * *

“Teacher has permitted it.”

Jin Hagon said with a happy face.

Geom Mugeuk spoke as if he had expected such a result.

“I knew you would accomplish it.”

Praise was not important now.

“What on earth do you plan to do when you meet?”

“I suppose I’ll have to decide.”

“Decide what?”

“Whether he is someone who can be allowed to live, or someone who must be killed.”

Geom Mugeuk stared into Jin Hagon’s eyes and asked.

“Will you trust my decision?”

“How could I trust you, who loves to jest? I will only trust my own judgment.”

Jin Hagon rose from his seat.

He stopped at the door as he was about to leave the room.

“Have you ever felt that you were lost?”

It was a thought he’d been having lately. Even as he proceeded with matters, another part of his mind tormented him with the thought: “Your teacher is a good man, but you are being used by Geom Mugeuk right now.”

“Never. I have an excellent sense of direction.”

Just as Jin Hagon was about to turn around and leave with an expression that said “jesting again,” Geom Mugeuk spoke from behind.

“Perhaps you haven’t lost your way, but rather forgotten where you are going?”

“Isn’t that the same thing?”

“Do they not say all paths in this world lead somewhere? If you know where you must go, you cannot lose your way.”

“If you’ve forgotten even where you must go?”

“You must sit on the ground and wail.”

“Please don’t jest.”

Geom Mugeuk recalled the days when he had wandered the Central Plains searching for materials for the great technique. There had been times when things were so difficult that he had wanted to sob loudly.

“Ask someone you meet; if there is no one, look at the constellations in the sky; if you still don’t know, throw your shoe and leave it to luck. If you still don’t know, then you must simply walk wherever your feet take you.”

“Aren’t you afraid?”

“Of what? I’ve already lost my way.”

“!”

Jin Hagon fell silent in thought for a moment, then sighed and said:

“You are truly… a strange man.”

“As expected, you people are so proper. Everyone in our cult called me a madman.”

“Then you must be a strangely mad man. See you tomorrow morning.”

With those words, Jin Hagon left that place.

Geom Mugeuk gazed out the window for a moment.

Jin Hagon was still young and didn’t know. What it truly meant to lose one’s way. That a person who has truly lost their way doesn’t even realize they are lost.

* * *

The next day, Geom Mugeuk arrived at the teacher’s workshop with Jin Hagon.

“Teacher, I’m here. I’ve come with the Young Leader of the Divine Cult.”

“Enter.”

When they entered, Jin Hagon was startled. His teacher looked different from usual. His teacher wore a splendid red long robe and carried a long sword at his waist. He remembered that this was the splendid attire he had seen when he first met him.

Geom Mugeuk and Baek Cheongyeong’s gazes met in the air. It was difficult to read each other’s emotions, whether it was Geom Mugeuk’s clear, deep eyes or Baek Cheongyeong’s narrow, long eyes.

Amidst the flowing tension, Geom Mugeuk opened his qi field. It was to verify the opponent’s martial arts. Regardless of the opponent’s will, a deep abyss pulled Baek Cheongyeong in.

Baek Cheongyeong’s qi field that met it was unique.

Geom Mugeuk looked around. Before he knew it, he was standing in a dense forest. It was filled with trees stretching endlessly. A loneliness of being alone and cut off from the world crept over him in that endless expanse.

While Baek Cheongyeong sank into the abyss, Geom Mugeuk walked deeper into the forest. Sunlight shone and birds chirped. A person with such a bright qi field? No. If he had been such a person to begin with, Jin Hagon would never have called for him.

Geom Mugeuk continued walking into the forest. The forest tempted him, endlessly displaying the same scenery, telling him there was no need to go further. Geom Mugeuk shook off the temptation and continued inward.

What guided him was neither his own will nor the opponent’s qi field. At this very moment, what guided him was surprisingly the Nine Transformations Demonic Art.

“There is something over there.”

It was hidden so deeply that one could never discover it without having mastered the Nine Transformations Demonic Art. It also meant that the opponent’s martial arts were formidable.

Soon, Geom Mugeuk discovered a human-shaped wooden statue in the deepest part of that forest. The wooden statue standing with its arms crossed was crafted so vividly that it felt like a living person. But only its face was flat, as it had not been carved. A miraculous feeling emanated from it, as though the wood might bulge and recede to form a human face at any moment.

As Geom Mugeuk tried to approach the wooden statue, the surrounding trees began to move, stretching their branches. Like living things, the branches blocked the path before the wooden statue and surrounded him threateningly.

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk knew.

“Wood Form Thousand Soul Technique!”

The ultimate secret technique that utilized every tree in the world.

In that instant, Geom Mugeuk was overcome with surprise and intense emotion. Not because this secret technique was such a marvelous martial art where all kinds of trees moved at will and small wooden dolls grew in size to charge like experts. But because he knew who used this martial art.

The alliance that his followers had created after Hwamugi went into seclusion was the Under-Heaven Alliance. Twelve absolute masters ruled that Under-Heaven Alliance. They were called the Twelve Earthly Branch Kings by the world! They had hidden their identities in the past and acted as the Twelve Earthly Branch Kings. Perhaps they had feared the revenge of Jeongsama that might occur someday.

Among those Twelve Earthly Branch Kings, the eighth, the Eighth King, had used this Wood Form Thousand Soul Technique.

Geom Mugeuk’s heart began to pound violently.

“It’s the Wood King!”

It was the first time since his regression that he was meeting a follower of Hwamugi.

Chapter 282: What Kind of Person Are You?
-----------------------------------------
Had there ever been a time in recent days when he had been this excited?

His heart pounded like mad.

Hwa Mu-gi.

At last, the moment has come when I will see a fragment of you.

Where are you now, and what are you doing? How strong are you now?

The moment he thought of him, rage boiled up through his entire body like lava. Had that violent emotion been sensed?

Sssssss.

Tree branches stretching out from every direction closed in on Geom Mu-geuk as if to crush him. There were hundreds, thousands of them—an offensive he could neither evade nor cut down in full.

At that very moment!

Thwack! Thwack! Thwack! Thwack!

Something revealed itself in four directions around Geom Mu-geuk—front, back, left, and right. Astonishingly, they were the four evil spirits of the Guhwa Demonic Art’s First Form, the Annihilation Form.

As the evil spirits blocked them like shields, the branches did not dare rush in and instead retreated. They had been frightened by the vicious aura the evil spirits exuded.

Geom Mu-geuk had truly not expected the four evil spirits to appear within the Wood King’s aura and protect him. Those fearsome, handsome, mysterious, and clever beings. If they were already this much, then what in the world would the Heavenly Demon Soul be like?

At the same time, Baek Cheon-gyeong was sinking into an abyss.

‘To possess such an aura at so young an age?’

No matter how far he entered, there was no end. Just as Geom Mu-geuk had walked endlessly into the forest, he swam down into an endless abyss.

He wondered what lay at the end of that darkness.

His instincts tried to stop him. If he went any farther down, it would be dangerous.

But Baek Cheon-gyeong simply could not suppress his curiosity. He had a premonition that something tremendous lay deep within that darkness.

His breath was being choked off, and the pressure growing stronger and stronger felt as though it would burst his body apart, but Baek Cheon-gyeong continued descending.

Just as he was about to give up, thinking he could endure no more—

Flash. It felt as though the world had gone dark and then lit up again.

Baek Cheon-gyeong was standing in the middle of a battlefield. Blood flowed like rivers, and corpses lay scattered everywhere. Among the corpses were humans, evil spirits, and demons. Nothing was alive. No—there was one person.

A man standing with his back turned, looking up at the sky.

Was it Geom Mu-geuk?

But the man’s back was the kind that could only belong to someone who had overcome every hardship in the world. No matter if he was the Young Cult Leader of the Demon Cult, Baek Cheon-gyeong did not think someone so young could possess a back that carried such years.

Then who was it?

The very moment he tilted his head in puzzlement,

that space vanished, and Baek Cheon-gyeong was once again in the deep abyss.

Swaaaaaaash.

And then he was dragged upward toward the surface at terrifying speed. The instant the abyss spat him out, Geom Mu-geuk’s aura vanished.

At the same time, Baek Cheon-gyeong’s aura also disappeared.

Geom Mu-geuk, who had been in the forest, and Baek Cheon-gyeong, who had been in the water, both returned to reality.

The two stared at each other in silence.

Geom Mu-geuk’s chest was still surging.

You who followed Hwa Mu-gi.

What are you doing here now?

There were so many things he wanted to ask him. But he could not ask any of them.

Whether he killed him or spared him, whether he investigated what lay behind him, once Hwa Mu-gi was involved, he had to be cautious.

Turning his boiling rage into a relaxed smile, Geom Mu-geuk greeted him first.

“I am Geom Mu-geuk, from the Divine Cult.”

“Baek Cheon-gyeong.”

Without any explanation, he gave only his name.

“Your disciple told me about you on the way here.”

At that, Baek Cheon-gyeong’s gaze turned toward Jin Ha-gun. In that instant, Jin Ha-gun grew tense inwardly. He was worried about what Geom Mu-geuk would say.

“What did he say?”

“He said you were the person he trusted most.”

Baek Cheon-gyeong’s gaze moved from Jin Ha-gun back to Geom Mu-geuk.

“You said you came to see Ha-ryeong?”

“I came a long way to find the woman I love.”

To Baek Cheon-gyeong, those words sounded like a joke, so he stared into Geom Mu-geuk’s eyes with his small, narrow ones. As he looked into those deep eyes, the back of the man he had seen earlier within the aura overlapped with Geom Mu-geuk.

This young and bright appearance and the man within the aura seemed far too different.

‘Who was that man?’

Baek Cheon-gyeong walked over to the table for receiving guests.

“Sit. Since a guest has come, I should serve tea.”

“I will brew it, Master.”

“I will do it. Sit there.”

“Yes, Master.”

Jin Ha-gun sat together with Geom Mu-geuk. He did not meet Geom Mu-geuk’s eyes at all. He focused thoroughly on his role for the day, while sharpening all his senses toward the current flowing between Geom Mu-geuk and his master.

Baek Cheon-gyeong brought the tea.

“Thank you.”

Geom Mu-geuk tasted the tea he had brewed. Seeing him drink without even checking whether it was poisoned, Baek Cheon-gyeong asked,

“What if there were poison in it?”

“Did you put any in?”

As he said that, he took another sip.

Jin Ha-gun could feel it. Within this seemingly trivial conversation, a strange battle of nerves was unfolding.

As Geom Mu-geuk drank his tea, he looked around the workshop.

“You seem fond of woodcraft.”

All around the workshop were decorations Baek Cheon-gyeong had carved. Arcane energy that he would never have sensed had he not cultivated the Guhwa Demonic Art was seeping from here and there.

How that tiger carved from wood would transform and pounce could only be known by fighting it.

Then Geom Mu-geuk’s gaze turned toward a display stand covered with black cloth.

“What is beneath that? May I open it?”

When Baek Cheon-gyeong said nothing, Geom Mu-geuk rose from his seat and walked over to it. Since he had not stopped him even then, Geom Mu-geuk took it as permission to open it.

Geom Mu-geuk pulled away the cloth covering the display stand.

The dolls were still engaged in battle, but Jin Ha-gun noticed a change.

‘The positions of the dolls have changed!’

The hero doll his master had called Geom Mu-geuk was on the opposite side. It was right beside the Jin Ha-gun doll that had been lying fallen.

In the place where the hero doll had originally stood, a new doll now stood. Judging by its appearance and features, that doll was unmistakably Baek Cheon-gyeong himself.

In the end, Baek Cheon-gyeong was speaking through the dolls. He believed Geom Mu-geuk and Jin Ha-gun were on the same side.

When the cloth was lifted, Baek Cheon-gyeong revealed his true thoughts.

“Young Cult Leader, did you come to kill me?”

Baek Cheon-gyeong did not believe that Geom Mu-geuk had come because of Jin Ha-ryeong. It was a question he could ask only if he believed Geom Mu-geuk was targeting him.

Even at that sudden question, Geom Mu-geuk did not become flustered.

“If you thought that, why did you permit this meeting?”

“If the Demon Cult seeks to kill me, where in this gangho would there be anywhere to hide?”

When Baek Cheon-gyeong’s gaze turned toward him, Jin Ha-gun showed his sense of injustice.

“You still do not trust me to the end.”

Geom Mu-geuk spoke while looking over the dolls on the display stand.

“That must be because your master has never truly liked a woman before. Do not worry. I came to the Martial Alliance only to see Lady Jin. If I had intended to kill you, would I have gone through such an unnecessary and complicated process? Do not forget what kind of Young Cult Leader I am.”

That was also why this meeting had been arranged today. He wanted to know exactly what Geom Mu-geuk’s intentions were.

“By the way, these dolls are truly well made.”

Geom Mu-geuk lowered himself and looked closely at the dolls.

“They seem as though they might come alive and leap out at any moment.”

Geom Mu-geuk knew. More than any other craftwork in this room, these little wooden dolls were the most terrifying existences. He was not making dolls; he was carving subordinates. Surely these were not the only things he had carved in his lifetime.

Then Geom Mu-geuk pointed at one of the dolls and asked,

“Who is this doll?”

The one Geom Mu-geuk, seeing the dolls on this display stand for the first time, pointed to was neither the hero doll, nor the Jin Ha-gun doll, nor even the opposing Baek Cheon-gyeong doll. It was one of the countless dolls standing around them!

“This is the only doll whose face was not carved.”

It was the faceless doll Geom Mu-geuk had seen within Baek Cheon-gyeong’s aura. It was surely a doll with some kind of meaning.

“Who did you make this as? You?”

Surprise flickered across Baek Cheon-gyeong’s face. It seemed he was astonished that, among so many dolls, Geom Mu-geuk had fixed his attention precisely on that one.

“It could be anyone.”

At Baek Cheon-gyeong’s answer, Geom Mu-geuk bent down and looked at the doll more closely.

“It seems this one alone is not caught up in the fight.”

Even without an expression, a sense of ease could be felt from the doll.

Behind him, Baek Cheon-gyeong asked,

“If there are those who fight, there are also those who watch like that. Are you someone who fights, or someone who watches?”

After watching the dolls for a moment, Geom Mu-geuk picked up one of them. It was none other than the Jin Ha-gun doll that had been lying beside the hero doll.

Geom Mu-geuk looked back and forth between the doll and Jin Ha-gun. Anyone could tell it had been made with Jin Ha-gun in mind.

Geom Mu-geuk set the Jin Ha-gun doll upright beside the hero doll.

He had shown directly that he was neither someone who fought nor someone who watched, but someone who raised another back up like this.

Jin Ha-gun looked at the doll of himself that had been set upright. It was nothing at all, yet for some reason, emotion surged in his chest.

No, from the beginning, it had not been nothing. His master, who had taught him for ten years, had made a doll of him and left it fallen over.

“I have seen enough.”

After covering the display stand with the cloth that had originally covered it, Geom Mu-geuk turned toward Baek Cheon-gyeong.

“The reason I came today is because the Demon-Extermination Squad Leader here said that if I wanted to meet Lady Jin, I absolutely had to meet you, so I had no choice but to come. Now, say whatever you have to say quickly. I must go meet Lady Jin.”

Jin Ha-gun sent a sound transmission to Baek Cheon-gyeong.

—Master, please tell him firmly enough to frighten him.

Even at his sound transmission, Baek Cheon-gyeong said nothing. Instead, he turned his gaze away from the two of them and looked out the window.

“If you have nothing to say, then I shall take my leave.”

Without hesitation, Geom Mu-geuk walked outside.

Jin Ha-gun wanted to stay behind and demand why he had not said anything, but Baek Cheon-gyeong’s gaze was still fixed outside the window. It was an order to leave, telling him not to be bothersome, so Jin Ha-gun quietly left as well.

Once outside, Jin Ha-gun walked together with Geom Mu-geuk. When they had moved away from the workshop, Jin Ha-gun finally asked,

“Is he someone who should be killed, or someone who should be spared?”

Jin Ha-gun was curious how Geom Mu-geuk had seen his master.

“I thought you said you did not trust my words?”

“I will only use them as reference.”

Of course, he was someone who had to be killed.

But instead of answering, Geom Mu-geuk showed Jin Ha-gun what he was holding in his hand.

“When did you bring that out?”

The reason Jin Ha-gun was startled was because what Geom Mu-geuk had brought out was the doll from the display stand. It was none other than the faceless doll.

“Why did you bring it out?”

Geom Mu-geuk stared intently at the doll.

“Aren’t you curious? Who he had in mind when he made this doll?”

“If you were curious, you should have left it there.”

“I am thinking of carving this face myself.”

“What?”

What in the world was he talking about? To Jin Ha-gun, Geom Mu-geuk was truly someone impossible to understand no matter how much he tried.

“One thing is certain: your master is no ordinary man. At the very least, I clearly felt that he is someone who should not be the master of the Martial Alliance Leader’s grandson.”

“Did you perhaps exchange sound transmissions with Master?”

Geom Mu-geuk shook his head.

“Then what was it about Master that made you think that?”

“To be honest, after seeing him just once like this, I still do not know whether he is an evil man, and therefore someone who must be killed.”

Of course, he would die by his hand. It was only a question of when and how.

“But his attitude toward you, at least, was a problem.”

Geom Mu-geuk pointed out the part Jin Ha-gun would be able to accept most easily.

“The look in his eyes and his attitude toward you could not be called that of master and disciple. They were cold and detached. Even if you had committed a great wrong, when an outsider is present, he should cover for your flaws, but your master focused only on me. No, perhaps he was focused on himself. That man does not regard you as his disciple.”

Jin Ha-gun had thought that once he heard a conclusion, in whatever form, his heart would feel at ease. But his heart remained exactly as it had been.

This time, Geom Mu-geuk asked,

“What was the master you saw like? You must have had many thoughts as well.”

Jin Ha-gun revealed his honest feelings.

“I do not know. I still find Master suspicious, and I still find you suspicious.”

The feeling of being lost came precisely from this point. He had felt that his master had changed, and his instinct for danger had stirred, yet without any proof that his master was a bad person, he had called Geom Mu-geuk. What if he was being used? What if he had judged wrongly? Those thoughts kept trapping him in fog.

As if he understood him, Geom Mu-geuk said calmly,

“That is why a definite enemy is better than an ambiguous friend.”

Jin Ha-gun realized that he was suffering because he did not know who was friend and who was foe. Why, of all people, did the one to awaken him to this have to be the Young Cult Leader of the Demon Cult?

Geom Mu-geuk stopped at a fork in the road.

“Go on ahead. I have somewhere to stop by for a moment.”

“Where are you going?”

At that, instead of answering, Geom Mu-geuk said something unexpected.

“What I said about having a good sense of direction and never getting lost was a lie.”

This was what he wanted to tell Jin Ha-gun.

“I panic and wander too. I lose my way often. Maybe because I am like that, people who loudly insist that this path is definitely the right one… I do not like them very much.”

“Why are you suddenly saying that?”

“It just suddenly came to mind.”

After saying that, Geom Mu-geuk launched himself away with lightness skill.

Watching him vanish into a dot in an instant, Jin Ha-gun looked up at the sky. He remembered what Geom Mu-geuk had said. To find the way, ask people, look up at the sky, and if even that did not work…

This is madness, he thought as he took off his shoe.

Then he threw it upward. The direction the falling shoe pointed was the direction in which Geom Mu-geuk had just disappeared.

‘Of all directions.’

Even coincidence was saying that following Geom Mu-geuk was right? Not a chance. The first round was always a practice round.

Jin Ha-gun threw the shoe once more. He threw it higher than before. But coincidence overlapped again. He threw it again, and again.

Only when it finally pointed in the direction he intended to go did he put his shoe back on.

“As if you would really panic and wander. Do not comfort me for nothing. I will find my path with my own strength.”

Jin Ha-gun strode vigorously toward his residence.

Even though he tried not to think about it, the image of his doll that had been lying down and then raised back up kept coming to mind.

Chapter 283: Whose Seat Is This?
--------------------------------
Moonlight settled over the inner quarters of the Cheonma Divine Cult.

Hyeolcheon Doma had planted the Myeolcheon Daedo in the courtyard and was drinking while leaning against it. Nothing beat the pleasure of drinking while leaning against the Myeolcheon Daedo.

Just then, a welcome voice came from behind.

“You always had a book in your hand; why a cup of alcohol today?”

A smile formed at the corners of Hyeolcheon Doma’s lips as he drank.

Geommu Geuk sat down beside him. At first, they had always sat facing each other, but now sitting side by side was more comfortable.

“Give me a glass too.”

Geommu Geuk drank the alcohol Hyeolcheon Doma passed him.

“Kya, this is good.”

“Must be busy with your affairs?”

“No matter how busy, you have to see the people you want to see. You were bored without me, weren’t you?”

“If I’m bored, I read a book; if I’m still bored, I drink.”

“What about Master?”

“He’s training in the rear courtyard.”

Since he couldn’t go to the late-night training gathering, it seemed he was training alone. Come to think of it, Cheon Sohui must be training alone as well. Or rather, perhaps she was enjoying her freedom and playing around.

“What troubles you?”

How quick he was to notice.

“Are you reading the will of heaven?”

“An old man ought to have some perceptiveness. Why? Has the Orthodox Murim mobilized in full force to capture you? Are they spreading a heavenly net?”

“If only it were that simple; then it wouldn’t even be worth worrying over. I’d just run, Elder! One word would be enough.”

When his heart grew complicated, the person he found himself seeking was Hyeolcheon Doma. Not to find some answer, but simply because being with him like this put his mind at ease. And often, talking like this, he would find the answer too.

Hyeolcheon Doma poured him a drink and spoke.

“Don’t try too hard to do everything right.”

“Do I look like I am?”

Hyeolcheon Doma nodded. It seemed he had stiffened without realizing it. Since it was related to Hwamugi, he was truly being cautious.

“Later, when you die, do you think you’ll die thinking, ‘I should have done better then’? Absolutely not. ‘I should have seen that person one more time.’ ‘I should have said one more good word.’ That is life.”

The more he learned of Hyeolcheon Doma, the more unexpected sides he saw. When he first met him, could he have imagined this was the kind of person who would say such things? Of course, meeting him had changed him somewhat, but he thought the ability to say such things came from reading many books.

“Then I am doing well. I came to see you like this so I wouldn’t have regrets.”

Though inwardly pleased, Hyeolcheon Doma did not forget the line he always said.

“As if you think kindly of me. Masked rascal, drunkard, brawler, fight-maniac—that’s probably all you think of.”

Geommu Geuk laughed and looked up at the moon.

“You probably can’t see because it’s hidden behind this great blade, but you are always at the very foremost front, Elder.”

Hyeolcheon Doma also smiled and looked up at the moon with him.

“Elder, what do you think you would regret the most?”

His gaze deepened slightly. Was he recalling his youth, when things could have gone well with Ilhwa Geomjeon? Or was he thinking of another lingering attachment he could not speak of?

“I have neither regrets nor lingering attachments.”

A faint smile formed on Geommu Geuk’s lips. Yes, he wished for him to live a life without regrets. Not a life without regrets only in words, but truly a life without regrets.

“Today, I met Jin Hagun’s master.”

At Geommu Geuk’s words, Hyeolcheon Doma’s eyes grew sharp. He knew Baek Cheongyeong was the one who held the key to this matter.

“How was he?”

“He was no ordinary man. Both his mind and his martial arts.”

Hyeolcheon Doma nodded.

“If he weren’t, there would be no reason for us to have come this far.”

“No matter how we handle this, I believe we must be cautious.”

“It won’t be easy. Because even if you are cautious, your opponent may not be.”

“What do you mean?”

Then Hyeolcheon Doma said something unexpected.

“If they make you their enemy, they will inevitably grow anxious and afraid.”

“It feels good to hear praise from you after so long, Elder. Please praise me more! I won’t grow conceited, so don’t worry.”

“You ought to, a bit.”

Geommu Geuk laughed loudly, and Hyeolcheon Doma laughed along.

The two of them drank.

He wished they could simply drink in such good spirits, but Geommu Geuk had come today for a reason.

Geommu Geuk took something from his bosom.

“Have you seen this before?”

It was a doll he had taken from Baek Cheongyeong’s workshop.

Hyeolcheon Doma shook his head.

“An unpleasant-looking fellow.”

Its body was perfectly carved, yet its face was empty, making it look all the more so.

“Do you know of the Mokhyeong Cheonhonsul?”

At the words Mokhyeong Cheonhonsul, Hyeolcheon Doma was startled. His gaze immediately turned to the wooden doll.

“Surely this doll is used for that martial art?”

As expected, Hyeolcheon Doma knew of the Mokhyeong Cheonhonsul. He possessed outstanding knowledge and insight into the various sects and martial arts of the Murim.

“That man Baek Cheongyeong was practicing it. He carved this doll as well.”

“Impossible!”

There was a reason for Hyeolcheon Doma’s shock.

“The Mokhyeong Cheonhonsul was a martial art of a mysterious sect called the Mokcheon Family. But the Mokcheon Family was already destroyed; you’re saying someone who learned that art is alive?”

“Destroyed?”

Geommu Geuk knew of the Mokhyeong Cheonhonsul, but he had not known their family had been destroyed.

“They were destroyed over ten years ago.”

“How were they destroyed?”

Hyeolcheon Doma shook his head.

“Almost nothing is known about it. True to their nature as a mysterious sect, their end was shrouded in secrecy.”

Baek Cheongyeong is a descendant of the destroyed Mokcheon Family? It was a moment when an unexpected fact was revealed.

“The master of the Murim Alliance Leader’s grandson is a descendant of the destroyed Mokcheon Family? This is certainly no ordinary matter.”

“Jin Hagun knows nothing of this. I suspect the Murim Alliance Leader does not know either.”

“I’ve heard that the Mokcheon Family’s secret arts are as secretive as the breath of a forest. If they deliberately hid it, it might have been impossible to know. But how did you find out?”

“The Guhwamagong reacted. It seems the martial roots of the Mokhyeong Cheonhonsul stem from demonic arts.”

It was the Guhwamagong, the absolute demonic art that superseded all demonic arts, standing at the apex of demonic arts.

A descendant of a destroyed mysterious sect has infiltrated the Murim Alliance? Not just any ordinary status, but the master of the Alliance Leader’s grandson, and even Hwamugi behind him to boot?

“What must be done? Give me an answer, Elder!”

No matter how much he feigned distress, Hyeolcheon Doma had no answer to give.

“I don’t know. I’ve lived a life killing everyone without thinking of the consequences.”

Hyeolcheon Doma tilted his cup and added.

“That is why for the inferior, killing is difficult, and for the superior, keeping alive is difficult.”

* * *

At the same time, Jin Haryeong was meeting with Jin Hagun.

“What happened?”

At his younger sister’s question, Jin Hagun hesitated in answering for a moment. The words that his master did not regard him as a disciple did not come out easily. So he only conveyed part of what Geommu Geuk had said.

“He said it’s the first meeting, so he doesn’t know yet.”

Jin Haryeong simultaneously sensed two emotions from her older brother’s expression: anger and hope. She could infer who each target was.

“Brother.”

“What?”

She wanted to tell him that she believed in him. Therefore, to believe in himself. But she knew Jin Hagun’s personality. In this situation, such words would only hurt his pride and be counterproductive.

“As expected, a martial artist is a martial artist.”

“What do you mean?”

“When a crisis comes, your eyes sparkle.”

Jin Hagun smiled bitterly. It was his younger sister’s eyes that were sparkling. Because even without looking in a mirror, he knew what his own eyes looked like.

“But don’t try to shoulder that crisis too much alone. ‘Doing it alone is cool’—I think that’s just bravado.”

The little brat who used to cry and beg to be carried everywhere had already grown so much and was now comforting him.

“If by any chance you’re struggling, couldn’t it be because your faith is half-hearted?”

“What do you mean?”

“If you’re going to trust Geommu Geuk, you have to trust him completely, but you can’t. Why don’t you just trust him outright? You asked for his help because you trust him anyway, didn’t you?”

“What if I end up losing everything?”

“If you’re going to lose everything anyway, wouldn’t you lose it even if you didn’t trust him?”

“!”

“When I look at that man Geommu Geuk, I’ve never once felt the door was closed. Because it was wide open, it actually felt like one couldn’t enter carelessly. If the other side can open it anyway, wouldn’t it be better to remove the clumsy lock? Ah, I’m talking too much. Then rest.”

As she was about to leave, Jin Hagun spoke.

“Thank you.”

Jin Haryeong felt that her brother was growing. He didn’t flare up, nor was his pride hurt. If it were the past… though at that time, this wouldn’t have been the atmosphere for such words anyway.

Jin Haryeong pretended to hold a ball to her chest, just as Jin Hagun had done when comparing himself to Geommu Geuk before—no, one much larger than that.

“To me, Brother is this big of a person!”

* * *

The next day, I went to find Baek Cheongyeong.

He didn’t seem surprised at all, as if he had expected my visit.

“The Young Cult Lord is quite free.”

Now that I knew he was a descendant of the destroyed Mokcheon Family, he felt different from when I had seen him yesterday.

Baek Cheongyeong, what are you doing here?

Even the same question felt different. And I would not hear the answer to this question from him.

After leaving Hyeolcheon Doma’s quarters last night, I had immediately sent an urgent message to Go Wol. It was a message to cooperate with the Tongcheongak and find out all information related to the Mokcheon Family and send it. I would hear the answer from Go Wol.

“Unless a war broke out with your lot, what would the Young Cult Lord have to be busy with?”

“Do you have time to see me? Shouldn’t you be busy meeting Haryeong?”

“Jin Daeju is furiously blocking it. He says before Master’s permission is granted, you absolutely cannot meet your younger sister. He says the moment you contact her, war will break out.”

“So you’re afraid war will break out while the Young Cult Lord of the Demonic Cult strides through the Murim Alliance.”

“It wasn’t that I feared such an obvious threat. As a fellow man, I understand Jin Daeju trying to protect his younger sister.”

“Even so, this isn’t right. The invitation was clearly extended by his own younger sister.”

Baek Cheongyeong pretended not to hear and sat down, beginning to carve wood. Since I arrived, he had only shown me the sight of him carving wood. But now I knew. How terrifying this moment was. Because every time we met, he was creating subordinates.

He asked while carving the wood.

“If I tell you not to meet her, will you go back?”

I strolled around the workshop nonchalantly and asked back.

“Would I?”

I didn’t think so. He would try to use me in whatever way benefited him.

“Naturally. I must accept my disciple’s will.”

“You must cherish your disciple greatly.”

Baek Cheongyeong neither affirmed nor denied it. What did he truly think of Jin Hagun deep in his heart? Was he planning to use him to the fullest and then discard him? Or did he have genuine feelings of considering him a disciple?

“I won’t back down until you let me meet her. I’m someone who does what he says he will.”

As he spoke, he lifted the cloth covering the display stand. The dolls’ positions had changed again from yesterday. All the dolls stood in a battle formation, having drawn a circle to surround someone. They were wielding weapons and attacking—clearly attempting to strike an opponent. But at the very center, there was no doll at all.

“Whose place is this?”

“That depends on how you turn out. It could be yours, or it could be Hagun’s.”

“Why are you excluding yourself?”

Baek Cheongyeong raised his head and looked straight at me.

“Didn’t you say so yesterday? That you didn’t come to kill me.”

“That’s right. I did.”

“Then it is most likely yours.”

I took a doll from my bosom. It was the faceless doll I had taken yesterday.

“Or perhaps it could be this person’s.”

He didn’t mention it separately, perhaps because he already knew I had taken it.

“I’d like to try carving this doll’s face.”

But at these words, he reacted immediately. Baek Cheongyeong looked at me and asked.

“Who will you carve?”

“Since the one who made this is you, you should know who it is.”

Our gazes tangled in midair.

“He can become anyone.”

“Would you teach me how to carve? I’ll try finishing it.”

As if it wasn’t worth answering, he began whittling the wood again.

Scrape, scrape.

I put the faceless doll back inside my robe.

Then I took another carving knife and a piece of wood from the workbench and copied what he was doing.

“This carving knife is still a knife in the end, isn’t it? I’m someone with a fair bit of expertise when it comes to blades.”

I roughly made it by following what he did.

Would he be able to move the one I made too? He probably could, right? This was ultimately made of wood as well. Or perhaps, since he hadn’t made it himself, it might not move according to his commands.

“This is harder than it looks.”

I spent quite some time carving the doll like that. Once I had roughly made the shape of a person, I showed it to him.

“What do you think? It looks fairly convincing, doesn’t it?”

Baek Cheongyeong seemed utterly unable to understand me.

“What is your true purpose?”

“To win Miss Jin’s heart.”

He was half in doubt. If he tried to believe me, it would be unbelievable; if he did not, he would wonder why I was doing this.

“If you permit me to meet her, I will repay you.”

“Repay me?”

“If something comes, should something not go in return?”

“I would not accept anything from a man of the Demonic Path, not even for a thousand gold.”

“What if I gave you something more important than a thousand gold?”

Curiosity flickered across Baek Cheongyeong’s face.

“For instance, the Cheonhwa Sect?”

Surprise passed over Baek Cheongyeong’s eyes, as if he had not expected those words.

“I know that you brought the Cheonhwa Sect Leader and Unit Leader Jin together. Why did you act for the Cheonhwa Sect Leader?”

I mentioned the Cheonhwa Sect at an unexpected moment and watched his reaction.

“I was merely trying to save the Cheonhwa Sect Leader’s son from your grasp.”

“You must have known that son was an evil man. Are you not of the orthodox faction?”

“Good and evil are relative concepts. To be honest, are you not the more evil one?”

“True. It does seem to be relative. I have the feeling that you are more evil than I am.”

A faint smile formed at the corner of his mouth. It seemed like a sneer of denial, and also like a smile of acknowledgment.

With that, Baek Cheongyeong’s gaze returned to the wooden doll he had been carving.

Scrape, scrape.

He was endlessly making subordinates. What did he intend to do with so many dolls? Just what was he planning to commit?

“I will come again tomorrow to learn.”

* * *

After Geom Mugeuk left the place, Baek Cheongyeong at length finished the doll he had been carving.

He looked down at it for a moment, toying with it, then rose from his seat and walked slowly toward the display stand.

“That doll can become anyone, and…….”

Baek Cheongyeong set the doll he had made down in the center of the encirclement, speaking in a tone as sunken as his gaze.

“Anyone can come here.”

The completed doll was a young woman.

Chapter 284: Not a Probe, but a Single Strike.
----------------------------------------------
Deep into the night, Baek Cheongyeong was crossing through the forest.

Illuminating the pitch-black darkness were fireflies. They circled around him, guiding his way.

Whenever he passed, trees and grass moved of their own accord to create a path. A path formed where no one would have imagined one could exist. Once he passed, the grass and trees returned to their original state.

How long had he walked like that?

Baek Cheongyeong stopped deep within the forest.

Ssssssh.

Though the wind did not blow, the grass swayed and the branches stirred.

Then, figures revealed themselves between the bushes. Their clothes, and even their skin, seemed one with the forest.

A man walked out from among them. This man, who felt like a slender blade of grass swaying in the wind, was named Cao Lin. He was Baek Cheongyeong's true disciple. Or rather, strictly speaking, he should be called the Wood King's disciple.

While Jin Hagun had learned the sword, Cao Lin had mastered the Wood Form Thousand Soul Technique, Baek Cheongyeong's true unique martial art.

Baek Cheongyeong spoke to Cao Lin.

"The time has come."

At his words, a faint green light flowed from Cao Lin's eyes.

"Is this not too soon compared to the original plan?"

"A variable has emerged."

"We are not yet ready."

"It cannot be helped."

"What in the world is the reason?"

Baek Cheongyeong recalled Geom Mugeuk, who had visited him over the past three days.

Geom Mugeuk had sat beside him and imitated him whittling wood.

Chattering nonsense, he had begged to be introduced to Jin Haryeong. He had also pestered him to buy drinks and snacks to share, and out of nowhere praised his appearance, remarking that his narrow, elongated eyes were utterly charming.

Now and then, he conveyed various news from the martial world. He knew much and possessed wit and insight. The lad's mouth never stopped. When with Geom Mugeuk, time flew by unnoticed.

On the third day, when the sudden thought arose that it would be nice to have such an amusing fellow as a subordinate or disciple, Baek Cheongyeong realized.

'This boy is poison.'

It was no ordinary poison. It was a poison that slowly addicted you without you realizing it was poison, leading you to death before you knew it—a poison far more terrifying than a lethal toxin that killed in an instant. Because it was a poison that killed you without you ever knowing why.

His instincts told him.

'This one knows something.'

How? I had hidden myself well. I had deceived even the Alliance Lord for all these long years.

But if he assumed that Geom Mugeuk knew about him, then Geom Mugeuk's behavior became understandable.

His instincts spoke again.

'That one smiling so amiably is a reaper who has come to devour you.'

At that, Baek Cheongyeong resolved.

'If fate has sent him to me, I shall accept it as my fate.'

Yes, a full ten years of waiting was enough. Now was the time to move.

Baek Cheongyeong handed something to Cao Lin.

It was a woman's doll he had made earlier.

* * *

Ian and Jin Haryeong stood facing each other in a field.

Their gazes were sharp. On their third meeting, they had decided to spar.

It had already been unusual to agree to become friends at their second meeting, but sparring at the third was even more so.

At first, Ian had doubted Jin Haryeong's intentions. What could the granddaughter of the Martial Alliance Lord possibly lack that she would go out of her way to befriend her?

'It must be because of the Young Master.'

To extract information about Geom Mugeuk. Or to keep her in check.

But at their second meeting, Ian had felt it. That Jin Haryeong truly wanted to become her friend.

This sparring at their third meeting was possible because she knew that sincerity. Otherwise, she would have suspected that Jin Haryeong was trying to kill her through the match.

The two women drew their swords and rushed at each other.

And the difference in their skill was clear.

Before even twenty moves had passed, the sword flew from Jin Haryeong's hand.

A surprised Jin Haryeong asked.

"What kind of martial art is that?"

The moment she asked, Jin Haryeong realized her mistake. Bluntly asking about someone's martial art right after losing a spar was unforgivably rude.

"Ah, sorry."

She cupped her fists in a polite salute.

"I have learned a fine lesson."

With an apologetic expression, Ian returned the salute.

She wanted to speak of her swordsmanship, but she could not.

The fact that she had inherited the Flying Heaven Sword Technique was not something that could be revealed to outsiders, even if the Heavenly Demon had permitted it.

"I knew you were stronger than me, but I didn't know you were this strong."

"I thought it only proper not to hide one's strength from a friend."

She had wanted to win, at least in martial arts.

She had lost to Seo Daeryong at the previous Little Dragon Tournament, and now she had lost to Ian. She kept losing to young martial artists.

'If this fight had been a life-or-death battle rather than a spar, I would be dead.'

What little comfort there was came from the fact that both of them were very close to Geom Mugeuk. Because of that, both Seo Daeryong and Ian were special people.

She shook off the feeling of defeat, picked up the fallen sword from the ground, and sheathed it. She did not pick it up with pointless jealousy to stab her own pride. Yes, a sword belonged in its scabbard, and pride was something others kept safe for you.

"Pretty face, good personality, strong martial arts. Isn't this cheating?"

She said it like a joke, but Jin Haryeong meant it.

Then Ian threw the words back at her.

"You're the one who's heard that all her life, aren't you?"

Ian truly thought Jin Haryeong was beautiful. Because among all the women she had ever seen, Jin Haryeong was the most beautiful.

Jin Haryeong felt that Ian's words were not the victor's consolation, but sincere. It was simply wondrous that such a beautiful woman considered her even more beautiful.

That was why her steps were light on the way back.

"Ah! This path is so pretty."

"It's a place I come to when I feel stifled sometimes. My secret walking path."

As they talked about this and that on the way back, Jin Haryeong revealed her inner feelings.

"Honestly, I'm worried about my brother."

"Don't worry. Calling the Young Master here is something you'll never regret."

She had brought it up because she wanted to hear exactly that.

"You really trust your Young Master, don't you?"

"I trust him more than myself."

"Having someone you can trust so much in your life is truly a great blessing."

"Then you should trust him too."

"What?"

"Didn't you agree to be friends with the Young Master? Then trust him."

These were the very words Jin Haryeong had said to her brother.

To not half-believe Geom Mugeuk, but to believe in him completely. And now she was hearing them from Ian.

An absurd image flashed through her mind: a large fish devouring smaller fish one after another, each representing faith.

Thanks to Ian's faith, Jin Haryeong felt a little more at ease. If all of this turned out to be a scam by the martial artists, she would look like a fool taken in by a pair of con artists. The fact that she could think such a thing meant she still couldn't believe in them completely.

Yet seeing herself say this, she would deserve it even if she were deceived later.

"Let's meet again tomorrow. If you go back, who knows when we'll see each other again?"

"Sure, see you tomorrow."

Ian accepted gladly. Perhaps they might never see each other again. Or they might meet on a battlefield where they had to kill each other. That was the nature of her relationship with Jin Haryeong.

"Same time, same place."

"Sure."

Would Geom Mugeuk have known? That by introducing them to be friends, they would truly become such friends.
