How long had she walked after parting with Jin Haryeong? Ian stopped and turned around due to an inexplicable chill.

By then, Jin Haryeong had already vanished from the beautiful trail.

Ian gazed at the path, unchanged from moments before, then resumed walking.

* * *

Today, too, he had gone to see Baek Cheongyeong without fail.

But he was not in the workshop. He had been there every day for the past several days; where could he have gone?

At any rate, thanks to that, he could devote the entire day to training today.

His main training was the Temporal Heaven Secret Art.

Could it be because of the Wood King's appearance?

His concentration was different from usual, and as a result, the Temporal Heaven Secret Art showed better results than his usual training.

Repeat, and repeat again. Even when his whole body resisted from boredom, he did it again and again.

Training was akin to the process of filling a huge barrel with a single drop of water each day.

You had to endure the boredom. You had to add that single drop of water, arduously produced throughout the day, to a place where no result showed. You must not sigh as you stared at that huge barrel unchanged from yesterday or today, and you had to resist the temptation to wonder if you would spend your life filling it, to stop now and seek other pleasures.

Only by enduring could you meet that one special drop among the countless ones.

Today, that special drop came in the form of the state of no-self.

Forgetting even that he was training at that very moment, his entire conscious self was immersed in the Temporal Heaven Secret Art.

The state of no-self was truly a gift from the heavens.

The trigger for this state of no-self had been Hwamugi. The thought that he might appear had heightened his concentration even further, and that had led him to the state of no-self. Water gushed into the barrel.

When ten shichen—which had felt like a brief moment—had passed.

'Finally!'

The Temporal Heaven Secret Art had ascended to a stage more effective than training martial arts in reality.

Even accounting for the time to recover depleted internal energy, training in martial arts within the Temporal Heaven Secret Art was now more effective. He had finally begun to have more time than others.

"I did it!"

The moment he confirmed this fact, a thrill ran through his entire body. He was so happy he felt he could fly. He was as happy as when he had inherited the Nine Splendor Demonic Art from his father. So happy that he hopped and jumped around inside the Spacetime Conversion Technique.

Now the true training had begun. His time would gradually increase. Because he had been learning the Temporal Heaven Secret Art within the Temporal Heaven Secret Art.

* * *

Geom Mugeuk had come out of the Spacetime Conversion Technique and was resting when Ian entered the room.

"Huh? When did you get back?"

Geom Mugeuk had been training inside the Spacetime Conversion Technique the whole time, so he seemed to have assumed she had gone out and returned.

"Young Master. Did you by any chance meet Lady Jin?"

"No. Why?"

"We promised to meet today, but she didn't show up."

"Is that so?"

"Could something have happened? She's not someone who would break a promise."

"And she didn't send someone to give notice?"

"No. That's why I'm worried and concerned."

He recalled that Baek Cheongyeong had left his workshop. She wasn't someone to break a promise, and he wasn't someone to leave the workshop. By coincidence, both situations had occurred simultaneously.

"When did you last meet?"

"Yesterday afternoon."

"Where?"

"We met in a field to the west and sparred."

"And then you parted?"

"We took a walk along a trail together and then parted ways."

"Was there anything strange then?"

At that, Ian conveyed what had been bothering her with a slightly uneasy expression.

"On the way back, something felt off, so I did look back."

Another suspicious circumstance was added. If Ian had sensed something at her level, something must certainly have happened.

And then, the final event that completed this unease occurred.

From afar, he could feel countless martial artists surrounding the residence. If such a large force was surrounding the residence, they would be Martial Alliance warriors. Geom Mugeuk could guess what had happened.

"If it was a trail, there must have been many trees around, right?"

If it was Baek Cheongyeong in the forest, he could have deceived Ian's eyes and kidnapped her. Because right now, the only one who would kidnap her was Baek Cheongyeong.

'It wasn't a probing attack, but a decisive strike.'

At Geom Mugeuk's grave expression, Ian asked worriedly.

"Lady Jin will be alright, won't she?"

"For now."

After kidnapping the Martial Alliance Lord's granddaughter, there was no way to resolve this matter smoothly. That meant he intended to see it through to the end, one way or another.

Fortunately, Baek Cheongyeong was a cautious man. Even if he would kill her in the end, he was not the type to kill from the very beginning. But if they could not rescue her in time, her life could not be guaranteed.

'What does he want? To kill me? Or?'

How on earth did he plan to deal with the aftermath?

"The Martial Alliance warriors will descend upon us soon."

"I felt it just now too."

Geom Mugeuk opened the door and went out to the courtyard.

After lifting her mask to cover her face and pulling down her bamboo hat, Ian followed him out.

Dozens of experts surrounded the place from all sides, layer upon layer. The pressure was no ordinary thing.

It was the first crisis Ian had faced since coming to the Central Plains, but she remained calm. Because she was with Geom Mugeuk, her mind was astoundingly at ease.

Shortly after, a group of martial artists entered the courtyard.

Leading them was Martial Alliance Lord Jin Paecheon. High-level warriors guarding the Alliance Lord followed behind him.

"It has been a while since we last met."

But Jin Paecheon asked bluntly instead of returning the greeting.

"Where is Haryeong right now?"

When he had come and why were not important. Right now, Jin Haryeong was Jin Paecheon's priority.

"I do not know."

"I shall ask again. Where is Haryeong right now?"

It felt as though Jin Paecheon's furious aura would burst forth at any moment. Had it not been for their past ties, he would have already attacked to subdue Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk sent a voice transmission to Jin Paecheon.

—Alliance Lord.

—Why do you send a voice transmission instead of speaking?

—Because that man's eyes and ears may be in this place.

—Who?

—Baek Cheongyeong.

In an instant, Jin Paecheon's expression hardened.

—Do you even know who he is?

—Yes, he is the master Young Master Jin studied under for ten years. Young Master Jin sensed danger from that master and asked me for help.

Jin Paecheon wore an expression of disbelief. How could he believe it? That his own grandson had sensed danger from his master and asked the Demonic Cult's Young Master for help.

—It may be hard to believe, but it is the truth. Where is Young Master Jin now?

—He has gone out to handle matters regarding the Demon Extermination Corps.

—He even timed it to match Young Master Jin's absence. He would have wanted a clash to occur between you and me right here.

—At the time Haryeong disappeared, that man Baek Cheongyeong was with me.

Now he knew why Baek had not been in his workshop. He had been with the person who could most certainly clear him of suspicion in this incident. Which meant he had a subordinate capable of kidnapping her.

—He planned this quite well, but Baek Cheongyeong overlooked one decisive fact.

—What is that?

—That the Alliance Lord trusts me.

Jin Paecheon asked coldly.

—Why do you think I trust you?

—Because you know that if I had orchestrated this, I would never have found myself in such a situation. Just as before, you must trust me this time as well.

Geom Mugeuk looked at Jin Paecheon with exceptionally clear eyes and added calmly.

—This time as well, only I can rescue Lady Jin.

Chapter 285: Trust with Trust.
------------------------------
Jin Paecheon, leader of the Murim Alliance, was confused.

He certainly had faith in Geom Mugeuk. But how was he supposed to believe that Baek Cheongyeong had kidnapped his own granddaughter? He was someone he had been drinking tea and chatting with just yesterday.

“First, please send everyone around us away. In any case, with you here, Alliance Leader, I couldn’t run even if I wanted to. And even if I were lucky enough to escape, you would know exactly where I went.”

It was true. There was no way he would abandon his position as the young cult leader of the Demonic Cult and hide in the mountains. Geom Mugeuk was a man whose destination was clearly fixed.

Jin Paecheon dismissed everyone except the guards he kept closest to him.

Geom Mugeuk slowly looked over the remaining guards. Jin Paecheon could tell that Geom Mugeuk truly believed there might be one of Baek Cheongyeong’s people near him.

“Why? Do these people seem suspicious too?”

“No. I trust your eye for people, Alliance Leader. Now I’ll speak plainly. At Young Master Jin’s request, I was looking into what sort of man Baek Cheongyeong was. I used Miss Jin as an excuse for coming here. From the start, when Young Master Jin asked for my help, we made it look as though I had come because Miss Jin invited me.”

After that, he briefly explained what had happened, but Jin Paecheon still could not believe the situation.

“Do I trust the man who has taught my grandson without incident for ten years, or do I trust the young cult leader of the Demonic Cult? If you were in my place, whom would you believe?”

“Then why are you leaving me be?”

The answer lay in the last thing Geom Mugeuk had said.

—If I had staged this, I would never have ended up in a situation like this.

Jin Paecheon had experienced Geom Mugeuk before, and he knew what kind of person he was. In the rumors that had reached him afterward, Geom Mugeuk had been accomplishing deeds that made one naturally say, “As expected!”

Moreover, for the Demonic Cult to kidnap his granddaughter meant they were willing to risk war, yet there were no signs of war from the Demonic Cult at all.

“Are you truly saying this was Baek Cheongyeong’s doing?”

“Yes.”

“Can you confront him face-to-face?”

“Of course. But he won’t be at his residence.”

Jin Paecheon sent a sound transmission to his swiftest subordinate, ordering him to bring Baek Cheongyeong here.

“If what you say is true, why did Hagun not ask me for help?”

“He must have thought you wouldn’t believe him. You still don’t believe it now, do you?”

“If Hagun had told me!”

“You would have believed him even less. You would have assumed there had been some conflict between the two of them.”

Jin Paecheon could not deny it. It was exactly the sort of situation Geom Mugeuk always spoke of. Because they were close, he could not see properly.

“There is one more reason. Young Master Jin suspected that Baek Cheongyeong had eyes and ears inside the Murim Alliance. It’s the same reason I spoke to you by sound transmission earlier.”

Jin Paecheon decided to consider the matter from the side of believing Geom Mugeuk. After all, it did not seem likely that the man who had already saved his granddaughter once would suddenly try to harm her now.

“Very well. Then why did Baek Cheongyeong kidnap Haryeong?”

“At first, I thought he was aiming for me. But… now I’m not so sure. No matter how much of a thorn in his side I may be, I have doubts as to whether he would make such a huge incident just to drive me away. Since he doesn’t know about the relationship between you and me, Alliance Leader, he must have expected a great fight to break out here.”

If that happened, it would not be a problem between Geom Mugeuk and Jin Paecheon, but would expand into a problem between the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult and the Murim Alliance. There was no way he could have failed to know that.

Geom Mugeuk believed there had to be some reason he did not know.

“Please let me go. I will rescue Miss Jin and find out that reason as well.”

Jin Paecheon closed his eyes and said nothing. Last time, too, he had had no choice but to entrust his granddaughter’s safety to Geom Mugeuk. And now he had to entrust her to him again?

Some time passed like that, and the subordinate he had sent to Baek Cheongyeong’s residence returned, reporting that, just as Geom Mugeuk had said, Baek Cheongyeong was absent.

“Why must it be you?”

“Because this matter was aimed at me. If you step forward, Alliance Leader, he will deny everything by any means necessary. If that happens, we may never find Miss Jin.”

At that, Jin Paecheon shouted angrily.

“How dare you threaten me? I will find my granddaughter myself.”

Jin Paecheon suddenly shot out finger winds and sealed the acupoints of Geom Mugeuk and Ian.

Ian, caught helplessly, was startled, but Geom Mugeuk was not surprised at all.

“Your granddaughter isn’t my blood kin, so what more can I do? Do as you please.”

Jin Paecheon snorted and shouted.

“Now you reveal your true colors. Escort these two to the Alliance.”

“If you touch me, you won’t be able to handle what comes after.”

“Shut your mouth!”

At the Alliance Leader’s shout, the martial artists standing at a distance looked toward the scene.

The nearby guards came and took the two of them to a carriage. As they were being dragged away, Ian spoke urgently.

“What are we going to do if we’re taken away like this?”

“It’s not as if we can fight the leader of the Murim Alliance, is it?”

Unlike her whispering, Geom Mugeuk spoke loudly enough for everyone to hear, as though he were just as angry as the Alliance Leader.

“What about Miss Jin?”

“She’s his granddaughter, so he’ll find her himself.”

“Even so, you have to ask him! You have to persuade him. What are we going to do if we’re dragged away like this?”

“You saw him too, didn’t you? He’s too closed off to do anything.”

Once the guards put the two of them inside, the carriage immediately set off.

Ian could not understand the situation. No matter how frustratingly the Alliance Leader was acting, was he really giving up on Jin Haryeong this easily? Reacting with such irritation? It was not like Geom Mugeuk.

In the moving carriage, Ian asked cautiously.

“That’s not it, right?”

Once only the two of them were inside the carriage, the irritation vanished from Geom Mugeuk’s face, and his usual expression returned.

“Of course not.”

Only then did Ian let out a sigh. Right, of course it wasn’t.

A short while later, the carriage stopped in a deserted place. When they got out, Jin Paecheon was waiting there.

He had acted in case one of the people he had brought along was someone planted by Baek Cheongyeong. If there had been one, they would report that Geom Mugeuk had been escorted to the Murim Alliance.

Jin Paecheon had truly chosen to believe Geom Mugeuk’s words.

Jin Paecheon again shot out finger winds and released the sealed acupoints.

The reason Geom Mugeuk had not dodged the finger winds in the first place and had let them strike him was also because he trusted the Alliance Leader.

Trust for trust. Jin Paecheon said nothing else.

“Save Haryeong.”

Their gazes met in midair.

Geom Mugeuk knew. As the leader of the Murim Alliance, how could it be easy to entrust his granddaughter’s life and death to the young cult leader of the Demonic Cult? Twice, no less.

Geom Mugeuk did not say anything unnecessary either.

“I will bring her back.”

At the sincerity in Geom Mugeuk’s eyes, Jin Paecheon nodded once, then climbed into the carriage. He felt he had made the right choice.

Of course, though he had entrusted this matter to Geom Mugeuk, he himself intended to move in every possible way to save his granddaughter.

Watching the departing carriage, Geom Mugeuk said to Ian,

“Go straight to our cult’s safe house and tell the two Demon Venerables exactly what happened.”

“What about any other message?”

“Leave what comes after to the Demon Venerables’ judgment.”

This was the trust he had in the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and the Fist Demon. A trust that, even without telling them what to do, they would judge the situation properly and move accordingly. His trust in Ian was the same. A trust that she would relay everything that had happened without leaving anything out.

Once Ian left, Geom Mugeuk also launched himself in the opposite direction. From the Alliance Leader to Ian, everyone was moving on trust.

* * *

Jin Haryeong opened her eyes in an unfamiliar place.

It was a house built of logs. Everything in the room was made of wood. The bed she had been lying on was also made by joining whole pieces of timber together, and all the furniture and household items were wooden as well.

‘Why am I here?’

Then she remembered the final moment.

After parting from Ian, she had been walking when the wind blew, and leaves came pouring down upon her. The instant the leaves clung to her body and brushed against her, her vision had suddenly gone black, and she had lost consciousness. Either a soul-bewitching drug had been mixed into those leaves, or some sorcery had been performed using them.

She first checked the condition of her body. Her internal energy had been sealed, but there were no other injuries.

She immediately got up and examined the door first. Even if she could not use her internal energy, she had trained in martial arts since childhood. Thinking that a mere wooden door was nothing, she put all her strength into it, but the door did not budge.

‘This is no ordinary door.’

She realized that it was not simply a common log door.

She examined the walls, floor, and ceiling in detail, but there was no way a room meant for confinement would have a passage leading outside.

‘Was this Baek Cheongyeong’s doing?’

In the current situation, he was the most likely person to have kidnapped her. But even after suffering it herself, she still could not believe it.

‘No matter how much conflict he’s in with my brother, he kidnapped me?’

Just then, she heard someone walking toward the door.

Jin Haryeong lay down again and pretended to sleep. She hoped the person entering through that door would not be Baek Cheongyeong. If he showed his face to her, it would mean he intended to kill her in the end.

‘Please!’

The door opened, and someone came in. A man’s voice soon followed.

“If you’re awake, get up.”

The other party already knew she was awake.

Jin Haryeong sat up and looked at the man. Fortunately, it was a man she had never seen before. He was Chorim, Baek Cheongyeong’s disciple.

When Jin Haryeong stared at him without saying anything, Chorim asked,

“Why do you say nothing?”

“If you were someone worth talking to, you wouldn’t treat another person like this, would you?”

Even with her internal energy sealed, she was dignified. If they had intended to kill her from the start, they would already have done so. They had to be keeping her alive in order to use her.

“Aren’t you curious who I am?”

Jin Haryeong realized that the man before her was the one who had kidnapped her. The scent she had smelled when the leaves flew in was coming from him.

“I’m not curious who you are. What I’m curious about is whether you know what you’re doing. Have you properly staked your life on this?”

Chorim merely smiled leisurely and did not rise to the provocation at all.

“You must be thirsty. Have some tea.”

She was thirsty, but Jin Haryeong did not touch the teapot placed beside her.

“Why did you kidnap me?”

Instead of answering, Chorim personally poured from the teapot into a cup.

“It is tea that is good for the body. You may drink it.”

As if to show that it was not poisoned, he drank the tea first.

Then he poured tea into another cup and held it out.

But instead of taking the cup, Jin Haryeong carefully examined his face, hands, and clothes. Whether it was a clue or a weakness, she tried to find something that might help her escape this place.

Chorim set the teacup down on the table and turned away.

“I will prepare a meal for you soon.”

Only after he left did Jin Haryeong open the lid of the teapot. It was not plain water, but tea mixed with medicinal herbs.

She was so thirsty she wanted to gulp it down, but she closed the lid again. She could not drink it when she did not know what kind of herbs they were.

But she could not keep enduring forever while refusing water and food.

She had truly never dreamed that something like this would happen near the main headquarters of the Murim Alliance. Because she was having a spar with Ian, she had made her guard, Chuho, wait far away. By now, there was probably an uproar as they searched for her.

In that moment of crisis, the first person she thought of was one man. That person was not her grandfather, nor her brother.

But soon, Jin Haryeong rose from her seat and began searching the inside of the room for anything that might help her escape.

Because the people who survived in murim were not those who had reliable people around them, but those who found their own way to live.

* * *

The place I arrived at was Baek Cheongyeong’s workshop.

It was empty, and the first thing I did was pull away the cloth draped over the display stand.

All the dolls had been removed, and only a single doll was left standing there forlornly.

Lying on the floor was the hero doll Baek Cheongyeong had called Geom Mugeuk. All the other dolls had disappeared.

It had undoubtedly been left behind to show me. This was not a doll, but leisure and mockery.

After taking the doll with me, I immediately left the place.

How would I find where Baek Cheongyeong had gone?

First, one thing was certain. At the very least, he would not be that far from the Murim Alliance.

The fact that he had spent time with the Alliance Leader when Jin Haryeong was kidnapped was an attempt to claim his own innocence—in other words, it meant he had no intention of leaving the Murim Alliance.

If so, Baek Cheongyeong was definitely nearby. And he would be in a place where he could best display his power. The forest.

“I’ll find you with Rapid Step!”

I ran at terrifying speed and descended into a nearby forest. After landing, I released my qi.

What I was looking for was not Baek Cheongyeong. What I was looking for was the wooden dolls he had made.

When I first visited his workshop, I had sensed a certain energy then. A profound energy that I never would have felt if I had not cultivated the Nine Flames Demonic Art was smeared here and there.

I was trying to find the energy that had flowed through those wooden dolls. Since there had not been just one or two dolls, I believed I would surely be able to find it.

When I sensed no energy, I ran again with Rapid Step and moved locations. I released my qi again to search for the energy, then moved locations again. In the blink of an eye, I was releasing my qi ten li away. Today, the Rapid Step I had mastered to great completion was truly showing its worth.

How long had I searched like that?

‘Found it!’

I had finally found the energy I had sensed in the workshop.

I slowly walked toward the place where I felt the energy. The dense forest blocked the way, but neither trees nor grass could stop me.

I continued deeper, forcing my way into places where one would wonder whether there could really be a path. The farther I went, the stronger the profound energy became. At the end of that path was a broad space like a plaza.

The moment I stepped inside, I was startled. In that place, arranged like a circular sparring arena, small wooden dolls stood densely packed on all sides.

They were the wooden dolls Baek Cheongyeong had carved in his workshop over the past ten years. Those thousands of dolls looked as if they might grow larger at any moment and rush at me.

I placed the hero doll I had brought with me in the center of the plaza. Then the thousands of dolls took on the formation of attacking the hero doll.

At that moment, a voice sounded.

“I told you, didn’t I? That place is yours.”

Baek Cheongyeong revealed himself and slowly walked out.

Instead of looking at him, I looked down at the hero doll standing alone and answered.

“I thought it would be me too. I do rather like being the center of attention, you see.”

Chapter 286: When I Hear the Word Revenge.
------------------------------------------
“This was the place, where I parted with Miss Jin.”

Ian pointed to the end of the narrow path.

“And Miss Jin walked that way.”

The ones listening to her were the Blood Heaven Saber Demon and the Fist Demon. For fear of being seen by anyone else, they had pulled bamboo hats low over their faces. The Heaven-Destroying Great Saber, too, was wrapped round and round in white cloth. Fortunately, it was a deserted place, and they met no one.

When Ian came to the safe house and told them what had happened, the first thing the Blood Heaven Saber Demon said was this:

—Leaving that fellow alone is helping him.

That was the Blood Heaven Saber Demon’s assessment of Geom Mugeuk. But his actions differed from his words and judgment.

—There likely won’t be anything we can help with, but you never know, so shall we go get some fresh air?

The Fist Demon, as blunt as ever, followed the Blood Heaven Saber Demon’s will.

The Fist Demon began examining the path here and there. The Blood Heaven Saber Demon merely stood and watched him. Ian had wondered why only he was being entrusted with the search, but there was a reason.

“It seems she was seized here.”

Ian asked the Fist Demon,

“How did you know?”

Ordinarily, he was someone so frightening she would not even have dared speak to him, but after training together late at night, she had grown a little more comfortable with him.

Ah! To think that if she had to choose the most comfortable among the Demon Venerables, she would pick the Fist Demon, who looked the most terrifying!

“Look at the footprints here.”

There were footprints left on the ground, but they were so faint that she would never have recognized them if the Fist Demon had not pointed them out.

“How did you make these out?”

“When I was young, I once took an interest in tracking arts.”

At that, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon praised him.

“If you had not become a Demon Venerable, you would have become the man with the greatest tracking arts under heaven.”

The thought that such a frightening, huge man had learned tracking arts did not suit him at all, and yet another thought occurred to her.

‘Not just anyone becomes a Demon Venerable.’

When one thought about it, they were the eight people who had risen to the highest positions among the demonic martial artists, second only to the Heavenly Demon. Because Geom Mugeuk was so outstanding, it made the Demon Venerables look as though they were being swept along by him, but if one looked at them separately like this, each and every one of them was someone capable of shaking the martial world.

“Fortunately, the kidnapper let his guard down, thinking no one would follow. This way.”

“Lead the way.”

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon and Ian followed behind him.

Their pace grew slower as they went. Even if he had learned tracking arts in his youth, it was no easy thing to display that skill properly now.

Just when Ian wondered whether they would have to give up, the Fist Demon found the trail.

They had discovered traces of someone forcing a path through the forest.

“It’s Mugeuk.”

They were traces Geom Mugeuk had deliberately left behind.

Shaking his head, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon said,

“Doing whatever he can to work an old man to the bone!”

Unlike his words, a flash of relief crossed his face. Ian could tell. Though he had said leaving Geom Mugeuk alone was helping him, he was sincerely worried about him.

And that blunt Fist Demon was trying to find Geom Mugeuk, even if it meant drawing on the skills of his youth.

Ian felt pleased by their concern for Geom Mugeuk, and at the same time, envious.

‘I want to make people like this in my own life too.’

Geom Mugeuk had taught her not only martial arts. He was influencing even the direction of her life like this.

With a faint smile, Ian said to the Blood Heaven Saber Demon,

“Let’s hurry, so you can scold him!”

And so the three of them began cutting across the forest.

* * *

“How did you find this place?”

Though he pretended to be calm on the outside, Baek Cheongyeong was inwardly greatly shocked by Geom Mugeuk’s appearance. This place was his own hiding place, one that had never been discovered by anyone until now. Of course, over the past ten years, there had been one or two herb gatherers who had happened to set foot here and died, but he had never imagined someone would deliberately track this place down.

“I tossed my shoe and read the direction it pointed. It pointed right here.”

“Yes, you were always one to spout nonsense.”

Geom Mugeuk gazed at him and asked,

“And what sort of person are you? Someone who kidnaps women to set up his schemes?”

“You saw correctly. I am a man who will achieve his aim even if he has to kidnap a woman.”

The aura emanating from the thousands of dolls gradually began to grow stronger. It was clear Baek Cheongyeong’s emotions were rising.

“Where is Miss Jin?”

“Haryeong is well.”

“Haryeong? Are you qualified to call Miss Jin by name?”

“Qualified? I have never seen anyone who speaks of qualifications actually possess them. What about you? Are you qualified to question my qualifications?”

“If it is qualifications, I have them in abundance. The Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult who came to save the granddaughter of the Martial Alliance Leader—how is that? Have you ever seen such a person? Being someone like that, what qualification could I possibly fall short of?”

A sneer appeared at the corner of Baek Cheongyeong’s mouth.

“Yes, I suppose you slipped away after bewitching the Alliance Leader with sweet words, just as you are doing now.”

Geom Mugeuk had expected that he would not easily fall into a trap. After all, this was the man who had forced him to move up his plan by several days.

“What is this all for? Haven’t we reached the point where you can tell me? Say it. Why are you doing this?”

“Since you brought up qualifications, let us first see whether you are qualified to hear my story.”

When Baek Cheongyeong extended his hand, deep green light seemed to pour from the eyes of the dolls near Geom Mugeuk.

Ssssss!

Ten of the dolls grew larger.

Like living people, they drew their weapons. The sight of wooden dolls moving so naturally was truly bizarre and chilling.

Their weapons, too, were made of wood, except for the bladed portions. Those parts were neither iron nor wood, yet they shone more keenly than any sharp weapon.

It was the moment he directly encountered the Wood-Effigy Heavenly Soul Art, which he had only heard of in words.

Of course, Geom Mugeuk was neither surprised nor flustered.

At his reaction, Baek Cheongyeong felt both doubt and admiration at the same time.

‘He is not surprised at all.’

How could he remain so composed when small wooden dolls grew to the size of people and moved like them? Was it because he had experienced all manner of demonic arts since childhood?

Of course, that did not mean Baek Cheongyeong thought Geom Mugeuk would overcome the Wood-Effigy Heavenly Soul Art. No matter how outstanding the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult might be, that youth of his could not overcome Baek Cheongyeong’s years of experience.

Geom Mugeuk knew the quickest and most certain way to break this martial art. It was to kill Baek Cheongyeong himself. Of course, Baek Cheongyeong would protect himself in all sorts of ways, but at least Geom Mugeuk would not need to face every single one of these dolls.

However, Geom Mugeuk did not charge in to kill Baek Cheongyeong. His main purpose was not to kill him, but to rescue Jin Haryeong and find out about Hwa Mugi.

And so he respectfully received Baek Cheongyeong’s first move.

The moment Geom Mugeuk drew his sword, the ten wooden dolls rushed at him all at once.

When Geom Mugeuk unfolded the Wind God’s Four Steps, no matter how fast the dolls were, they could not keep up with those wondrous movements.

Without using the Flying Heaven Sword Art, Geom Mugeuk cut them down. He treated them as if they were people, not dolls, and cut them down decisively.

How could ten ordinary experts possibly kill Geom Mugeuk? Of course, they had frightening aspects different from people. First of all, they had no emotions. They had no fear and no mercy toward their opponent. No matter who the opponent was, they obeyed only orders.

Even more frightening was the fact that they could not feel pain.

On top of that, they even possessed regenerative ability. If one arm holding a sword was cut off, a sword grew from the other arm and attacked.

If both arms were cut off, they charged in and rammed him with their torsos.

In the end, only when he split their bodies in two or cut off their heads did they stop moving. The dolls thus neutralized returned to their original size.

As Geom Mugeuk faced these ten dolls, he thoroughly grasped their characteristics and movements. Seeing him not waste even a single move, Baek Cheongyeong inwardly admired him.

‘He truly is no ordinary man.’

After eliminating all ten dolls, Geom Mugeuk exaggerated his distress.

“What will you do if I die at this rate?”

“If you die, you die. Why? Do you think I cannot kill you?”

“I am the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult. The Young Cult Leader.”

“You may be an important person in the martial world, but to me, you are nothing more than an obstacle that suddenly sprang into my life. Like a carriage that suddenly comes charging when I turn a corner.”

“Not a carriage, the Demonic Cult. At this rate, there will be war. War, I tell you.”

He had slipped in the word war to probe him, but Baek Cheongyeong did not waver at all. Perhaps that was what he wanted. After all, his purpose in causing a clash with the Alliance Leader had been to incite conflict between the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult and the Martial Alliance.

“I showed you my qualifications, so answer my question.”

“Did I not just answer you? If you wish to speak with me again, show me your qualifications again.”

This time, twenty wooden dolls grew larger.

Twenty dolls out of thousands. It was a number that amounted to no more than a drop in the bucket. The frightening thing was not the twenty dolls, but all the dolls that remained.

Of course, Geom Mugeuk was not afraid. To use the Wood-Effigy Heavenly Soul Art, Baek Cheongyeong must also expend internal energy. He could not move the dolls endlessly, and in a contest of internal energy, Geom Mugeuk was confident against anyone in the world.

And this time, Geom Mugeuk dealt with them in a different way.

Swaaaaaaak!

Before they could charge in, Geom Mugeuk’s sword energy swept over them first. It was the first form of the Flying Heaven Sword Art, the Heaven-Balancing Form, which had reached great completion at the twelfth level.

It was not sword energy that could be avoided simply by trying to dodge. It was so fast and powerful that the twenty dolls were cut apart all at once.

“Kuh.”

Baek Cheongyeong let out a low cry. When the Wood-Effigy Heavenly Soul Art he had created was cut apart in one stroke like this, the shock rebounded into his body.

‘Twenty bodies in a single move?’

Baek Cheongyeong could not believe it. Since he had begun using the Wood-Effigy Heavenly Soul Art, this was the first time he had been defeated so helplessly.

Though he had displayed an astonishing move, Geom Mugeuk was instead calm.

He slowly walked over and sat down in the place where the dolls that had just come out to attack had been.

Then he picked up the doll beside him. Without the slightest fear, he toyed with the thing, which might grow and attack at any moment.

“What on earth happened to you?”

With the playfulness gone, Geom Mugeuk gave off a different impression. His clear, deep eyes seemed as though they would listen to any story. Though he was an enemy, it felt as if he might offer a comfort that Baek Cheongyeong had never received from any ally.

Was it because of those eyes? A scene rose in Baek Cheongyeong’s heart.

That place on that day, filled only with corpses. That day’s events, branded into his chest like a fire mark.

Then Geom Mugeuk suddenly asked,

“Are you trying to take revenge?”

Baek Cheongyeong looked at Geom Mugeuk. He had been determined never to speak of his circumstances, but looking at Geom Mugeuk, he felt a desire to tell him. He wanted to hear what Geom Mugeuk would say. What this strange, silver-tongued Geom Mugeuk would say.

“And what are you trying to do? Are you aiming to become the Martial Alliance Leader’s grandson-in-law? Or are you trying to win the Alliance Leader’s favor and then kill him?”

Toying with the doll, Geom Mugeuk said,

“I am trying to take revenge too.”

Baek Cheongyeong was startled. It was the greatest shock he had felt since meeting Geom Mugeuk. He had truly never imagined the word revenge would come from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth.

“Against whom?”

“See? When you hear the word revenge, you immediately become curious who it is against, do you not?”

Geom Mugeuk set the doll he was holding back in its place and said,

“Shouldn’t I hear your story first, in order?”

At that instant, four dolls around Geom Mugeuk suddenly grew larger and launched an attack.

Shiiiiik!

The four dolls were cut apart at the same time.

Geom Mugeuk reacted as though he had known the attack was coming. His level of martial arts already overwhelmed the dolls’ attacks.

Baek Cheongyeong could tell. With this method, he could not harm even a hair on Geom Mugeuk’s head. Only by using his trump card could he decide the match.

Thud, thud.

The dolls returned to their original size and fell, severed.

Even after the surprise attack, Geom Mugeuk did not curse him for it. He picked up the fallen pieces from the ground and placed them back where they had been.

“Are you mocking me?”

“If I were mocking you, I would have trampled this or pissed on it. At the very least, I know this much, do I not? How diligently you carved each and every one of these. I know because I tried imitating you. Carving these is no ordinary task.”

“No matter what you say, you will not return alive today.”

“Then can you not tell me? I am going to die anyway. The reverse is also true. Even if you are the one who dies, it is the same. You are going to die anyway, so what reason is there not to speak? What worries burden you so heavily that you carry even matters after death upon your shoulders?”

Those words struck Baek Cheongyeong in the chest. Yes, he was holding on to all kinds of lingering attachments and worries. Even while he did not actually fear death.

“You are good at spouting sophistry.”

“Tell me. I want to know what happened to you.”

Their gazes tangled in midair.

Baek Cheongyeong could feel that Geom Mugeuk sincerely wanted to know, and that his reason was not to gain an advantage in this fight.

At last, Baek Cheongyeong slowly extended his hand.

Then the dolls some distance away from Geom Mugeuk grew to the size of people. They did not rush toward Geom Mugeuk. Instead, they began fighting among themselves in the center of the square.

Geom Mugeuk could tell. Baek Cheongyeong was trying to show what he had experienced in this way. Now the square had become a stage displaying his past.

One masked doll was cutting down the other dolls. Male dolls and female dolls alike, all were struck down by his sword. They were helpless. Even on the stage, the Wood-Effigy Heavenly Soul Art was being displayed. It showed small dolls growing larger. It showed them trying to stop him even by using the Wood-Effigy Heavenly Soul Art.

‘The Mokcheon Clan.’

He was now showing the destruction of the Mokcheon Clan. The sight of everyone in the clan dying. More than anyone else, it was a scene that would enrage Geom Mugeuk. Familiar faces overlapped the fallen dolls.

The masked doll cut down everyone, even the last person. With this, Geom Mugeuk could tell. The destruction of the clan that day had been carried out by the hand of a single person.

“You wanted to know why I spent ten years in the Martial Alliance carving these things? Why I do not care whether war breaks out or not? Why I dared to kidnap the Alliance Leader’s granddaughter without fear? Why I waited only for the perfect moment of revenge? Watch closely!”

The moment those words ended, the doll that had carried out the massacre turned its head toward Geom Mugeuk.

The instant Geom Mugeuk saw him, he was startled.

Though the doll wore a mask, he knew whose sharp and intense eyes those were. It was someone he knew as well.

It was none other than the Martial Alliance Leader, Jin Paecheon.

Chapter 287: Who handled that matter?
-------------------------------------
Silence fell.

Jin Paecheon, made of wood, was staring at Geom Mugeuk. Because it had no expression at all, it felt all the more chilling and intense.

Ssssst.

The wooden doll returned to its original size, like an actor leaving a stage after the curtain had fallen.

Looking past the wooden doll that had dropped to the floor, Geom Mugeuk asked Baek Cheongyeong,

“Did the leader of the Murim Alliance annihilate your clan?”

Geom Mugeuk’s voice trembled. He had experienced all manner of things, but even so, this shocked him deeply.

Baek Cheongyeong, on the other hand, was savoring Geom Mugeuk’s reaction.

Now then, oh clever Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult! What will you say now?

“Why? Is it so hard to believe that the Murim Alliance Leader would do such a thing?”

Geom Mugeuk answered firmly.

“It is. I cannot believe it. The Alliance Leader I saw was not someone who would do such a thing.”

Jin Paecheon was certainly a domineering man, but he was not someone who would massacre an entire clan. However unknowable the human heart might be, Jin Paecheon had done such a thing? No. That could not be.

“Very well. Then why did the Alliance Leader do it?”

Baek Cheongyeong refused to answer.

“I have said enough.”

As if the next conversation would only be possible if he withstood this attack, Baek Cheongyeong once again unleashed the Wooden Figure Thousand-Soul Art.

Ssssst.

This time, three dolls grew larger.

As they slowly walked forward, their movements and presence were different from the dolls before them.

Geom Mugeuk could tell. Among the dolls, these were special ones. If they were people, they would not be ordinary experts, but exceptional ones.

And yet, though the enemies were stronger, Geom Mugeuk returned his sword to its sheath.

Just as Baek Cheongyeong looked at him in puzzlement—

Whoooosh.

Geom Mugeuk charged first with the Myeongwang Step. In the literal blink of an eye, Geom Mugeuk crossed space and thrust out his fist.

Boom!

Rumble!

An explosion rang out along with the roar of thunder. Before it could even launch a proper attack, the head of the doll standing in the middle was blown clean off.

It was the Fist Demon’s martial art, the first fist of the Thunderclap Asura Fist: Black Cloud Asura.

The remaining two dolls attacked without the slightest agitation.

Whoosh! Whoosh!

Two swords flew in, aiming for Geom Mugeuk’s vital points.

‘I’ve got him!’

The attacks were so fast and precise that Baek Cheongyeong was deluded into thinking so.

At the very moment the two swords pierced Geom Mugeuk’s body—

Geom Mugeuk’s form vanished.

‘Form-Shifting Transposition!’

At the same time, a peal of thunder erupted, burying Baek Cheongyeong’s astonishment.

Ruuumble!

A second thunderclap burst out. Before anyone knew it, the head of the doll on the left was exploding.

Even amid the flying fragments, the last doll did not hesitate at all and launched its attack.

But Geom Mugeuk was already no longer there. This time, he had used the Dark Shadow Step.

Ruuuumble!

The third peal of thunder rang out even louder, and the last doll’s head was smashed apart as well. No matter how well-trained a hunting dog might be, it could not bring down a tiger. All three were finished in a single blow each.

The three dolls returned to their original size as well and fell to the floor.

Baek Cheongyeong’s entire body trembled. He had never imagined he would feel this kind of fear here. Here, of all places, in a place filled with forests and dolls.

Geom Mugeuk picked up the severed doll again.

“These dolls are different.”

“They were. Until you made them ordinary.”

“I did not want to waste time for no reason.”

“You are trying to turn ten years of my life into wasted effort.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk stared into his eyes and said,

“What about Miss Jin’s time? Have you thought about the years she has lived and the days she has left?”

Certainly, when he thought of Jin Haryeong, there was something that weighed on his heart. But before that, there was rage.

“What about the time of my family, who died unjustly! The time of my elder brother, my sister-in-law, and my nephews! The time of the children my nephew had? Can you even imagine it? The hearts of parents who died clutching their child, trying to protect them! How I felt when I unfolded that bloodstained swaddling cloth! In that swaddling cloth! Do you know what I saw in that swaddling cloth?”

Baek Cheongyeong’s eyes were bloodshot.

Sssssst.

His rage was contained in the doll growing larger once more.

This time, there were two dolls. The fewer their number became, the more extraordinary the aura they revealed.

This time, the two dolls attacked first. They were truly fast; there was no doubt they had been made by imitating masters of the swift sword.

They were so fast that even Baek Cheongyeong, who had created the two dolls, could not clearly see the fight.

Since he could not see their movements, sound was everything. The clash of sword against sword rang out in succession, and the end was marked by three distinct sounds.

Whoooosh! Slash! Clack!

The moment Geom Mugeuk’s sword returned to its sheath—

Thud, thud!

At the same time, the heads of the two dolls fell and rolled across the floor.

It was the Flying Heaven Sword Art, Swift Sword Style, Fifth Form: Azure Heaven Form.

It had been a long time since he had used the Azure Heaven Form, and it had become faster than before. Even at the same great completion of the Twelfth Star, as his realm gradually matured, it manifested as an even more powerful technique.

Baek Cheongyeong was so shocked that he could not say a word.

No matter how he was the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult, he should not have been able to neutralize the swift sword dolls’ attacks this quickly. Baek Cheongyeong had expected at least five hundred exchanges. In this fight, he had thought that even Geom Mugeuk would be unable to withstand them and would be gravely injured.

Geom Mugeuk’s reaction was even more astonishing. He could have boasted about his martial arts, yet he did not mention them at all. It was as if he considered a fight of this level to be meaningless. His attention was fixed solely on Baek Cheongyeong’s life.

“Answer the question I asked earlier. Why did the Alliance Leader annihilate your clan?”

“Why is that so important to you? Why does it matter whether the Alliance Leader did it because he went senile or because he was bored? What does it matter whether the Murim Alliance Leader is a hypocrite or not! In the end, all you Demonic Cult bastards only intend to wipe everyone out and devour the whole of murim anyway, don’t you?”

“Even if we devour it, we will cut away the rotten parts first. So tell me.”

He deliberately called it rotten. Whatever the truth of this incident was, Baek Cheongyeong believed it so.

“Did you see it yourself?”

“Are you trying to say it was not the Murim Alliance Leader’s doing because I did not see it myself?”

Every word he spoke was laced with rage. Because Geom Mugeuk knew better than anyone just how immense that rage must be, he calmly soothed his emotions.

“I am not on the Murim Alliance’s side. I merely want to know the truth.”

“If you are not on the Murim Alliance’s side, then why are you standing before me?”

Because of Hwa Mugi. If he had not learned of the connection to Hwa Mugi, he would never have approached him to this extent.

“Because of Miss Jin.”

Baek Cheongyeong snorted once, but in the end he answered.

“I did not see it myself. When I returned, it was already…”

As if that moment had come back to him, Baek Cheongyeong could not continue.

“Then why are you so certain it was the Alliance Leader’s doing?”

“There was someone who witnessed him.”

“Who?”

“The chief steward of my family. He was dying, drenched in blood. Those were the last words he left. That the murderer was the Murim Alliance Leader.”

Geom Mugeuk said nothing. The Murim Alliance Leader might have killed them, or he might not have. The human heart was something no one could ever truly know.

But the reason Geom Mugeuk considered the possibility that he had not done it first was because Baek Cheongyeong was someone who, in the future, would follow Hwa Mugi.

“No matter how I think about it, I cannot see why the Alliance Leader would annihilate your clan…”

At that moment, a thought flashed through Geom Mugeuk’s mind.

Geom Mugeuk recalled a fact he had forgotten. There was one reason the Alliance Leader might have annihilated them.

“You killed the Alliance Leader’s son and daughter-in-law!”

Baek Cheongyeong’s expression hardened, and he said nothing. From his reaction, Geom Mugeuk could tell.

“You knew.”

“If I knew, does that lighten his crime? If he wanted revenge, he should have taken it only on the person who committed the deed. Why did he kill everyone?”

His protest was justified. The grief of losing one’s children was understandable, but annihilating the other party’s entire clan was unquestionably wrong.

“But there is one thing I wonder about. If that is the case, why did the Alliance Leader leave you alive? It would have been obvious you would try to take revenge like this.”

“Because after that incident, I was recorded as dead as well.”

So he had entered the Murim Alliance under a new identity.

“Who handled those matters? Did you do it yourself?”

Faking one’s death and becoming the teacher of the Murim Alliance Leader’s grandson under a new identity—this would not have been easy even if the cult’s Tongcheon Pavilion had taken great care in arranging it.

“Of course.”

His answer came after a hesitation. That meant it was a lie. Geom Mugeuk was certain there was someone behind him.

“So? What is it you want to say?”

Geom Mugeuk spoke words that startled him.

“What if that incident was not the Alliance Leader’s doing?”

“!”

“What if a third party was involved? What if someone deceived you and deceived the Alliance Leader as well?”

To Baek Cheongyeong, whose face was deeply contorted, he added something even more shocking. If he had not known of Hwa Mugi’s existence, he would never have made such an assumption. But the Alliance Leader’s character and Hwa Mugi’s existence called forth this possibility.

“What if even the deaths of the Alliance Leader’s son and his wife were planned from the very beginning?”

Baek Cheongyeong stood blankly for a moment, as if struck by a blow. It was something he had never once thought of. What was this bastard saying? Nonsense.

“And what would they gain from that?”

Geom Mugeuk gave the final conclusion.

“You.”

“!”

“You, who went mad with revenge, endured ten years, and embedded yourself deep within the Murim Alliance. You, who would one day become the decisive dagger that kills the Alliance Leader.”

Killing intent erupted from Baek Cheongyeong’s body. He denied everything. No, he had no choice but to deny it. If Geom Mugeuk’s words were true, then what he had carved for the past ten years had been his own foolishness. It meant a doll had been carving dolls.

“What would the likes of you know! Do you think every word that spills from that flapping mouth of yours becomes truth? What do you know!”

It was rage born from bewilderment. What if those words were true? That deep anxiety was sweeping through him. If they had come from someone else’s mouth, he would not have paid them any mind at all. He would have dismissed them as lies made up to shake him.

But they were words from Geom Mugeuk, who had overwhelmed him in martial arts. Words from the mouth of an extraordinary person, extraordinary enough to make him advance his own plan.

Feeding on shock, fear, and unease, the trees began to grow.

Around the two men, branches rose from the ground.

They were no ordinary trees. Trees that could not be cut by blades or swords—the secret technique of the Wooden Figure Thousand-Soul Art was being unleashed. The growing trees formed walls and then a ceiling. There was nowhere to flee. The two faced each other inside a sealed space made entirely of wood.

Other branches wrapped around Baek Cheongyeong’s body, forming armor for him. At least in this place, even sword qi and sword aura could not cut through this armor.

“You ruined everything. Because of you, Jin Haryeong will die as well.”

Geom Mugeuk asked him calmly,

“What did you say to me earlier? Did you not shout and ask why he killed not only the person involved, but the family as well? And yet what are you doing now?”

“They were the ones who did it first!”

“The ones who began this were you people, were they not?”

No, perhaps it had been Hwa Mugi.

“Release Miss Jin. I know you never planned to kill her originally.”

Vines rising from the ground climbed up Geom Mugeuk’s feet. Then they began slowly binding his entire body so he could not move.

“The person who helped you infiltrate the Murim Alliance—this entire matter was that person’s scheme.”

Geom Mugeuk saw it. Baek Cheongyeong’s pupils trembled. The branches climbing up his body paused for a moment. There had definitely been someone.

“Tell me who it is.”

“There is no one! No such person exists! Do you take me for a fool?”

Around Geom Mugeuk, more trees grew and became long spears. Aimed like the arms of an octopus, they stabbed toward Geom Mugeuk from all directions with the speed of scorpion tails.

Geom Mugeuk raised his protective aura and blocked the attacks.

Clang! Claaang! Clang!

The wooden spears collided with his protective aura and rang out like metal.

Even when he cut them away with his sword, they grew back again. Even when they shattered against his protective aura, other trees grew and attacked. They attacked endlessly, trying to tear through the protective aura. The only way to stop this assault was to kill Baek Cheongyeong, who was hiding inside the armor.

Clang! Claaang! Claaang!

It was an attack within this space. It was several times stronger than it would have been outside.

“It is not too late. Face him together with me!”

Because he felt Geom Mugeuk’s sincerity, Baek Cheongyeong wavered. What if his words were true?

“Please! Do not make me kill you.”

But in the end, he did not believe Geom Mugeuk.

Baek Cheongyeong drew up his inner force to the limit. Blood flowed from his mouth, but he did not stop. More than ten spears attacked only one place: Geom Mugeuk’s heart.

Clang! Claaang! Clang!

At this rate, the protective aura would eventually be pierced.

“If you die, war with the Demonic Cult will break out. Even if I cannot kill him, the Heavenly Demon will kill him.”

The wooden spears merged into one. Turning an even deeper color, it became Baek Cheongyeong’s final attack, into which he poured all the inner force he possessed. At least within this space, that attack could no longer be stopped.

Shraaaaaak!

It flew toward Geom Mugeuk’s heart.

At that very moment—

Flash!

The wooden spear rushing toward him had stopped before Geom Mugeuk’s heart. Soon after, the sharpness of the spear vanished, and it dropped to the floor with a thud.

Far ahead, standing before Geom Mugeuk, there were five in all.

Four evil spirits stood around Baek Cheongyeong. The first technique of the Guhwa Demonic Art, the Annihilation Form, had been unleashed.

The wooden armor that could not be cut even by sword qi was sliced apart and began falling to the ground in clattering pieces. His martial art, rooted in demonic arts, could not withstand the Guhwa Demonic Art.

When the evil spirits disappeared, Baek Cheongyeong’s entire body was soaked in blood.

Baek Cheongyeong looked at Geom Mugeuk with vacant eyes. Geom Mugeuk sensed that he did not have much time left.

Geom Mugeuk took a doll from his bosom. It was the very doll whose face he had once said he would carve himself.

And now, a face had been carved onto that doll. Over the past few days, as he had gone to and from Baek Cheongyeong’s workshop, Geom Mugeuk had carved Hwa Mugi’s face into it.

“Was the man this person?”

Chapter 288: You Gave Me Hell.
------------------------------
Baek Cheon-gyeong looked at the doll.

Geom Mugeuk focused on the change in his expression. Had the doll been the cause? The vacant look in his eyes cleared. It was the dying flash of lucidity that came just before death.

Baek Cheon-gyeong’s reaction was unexpected. He smiled when he saw the doll.

“Why are you laughing?”

Baek Cheon-gyeong’s gaze shifted from the doll to Geom Mugeuk.

“I should have taught you when you asked me to teach you carving.”

It meant he had made it that badly.

Geom Mugeuk smiled and said,

“Isn’t this excellent for a first work?”

“Just don’t go around saying I taught you.”

Even as he spoke clearly, blood kept spilling from his mouth. He was not even aware that he was coughing up blood. Geom Mugeuk felt a pang of regret. He had not wanted to kill him.

“I feel dizzy.”

As Baek Cheon-gyeong’s body slowly collapsed, the trees that had filled the surroundings began to sink back into the earth. The two were freed from the sealed space created by his final secret technique, and sunlight shone upon Baek Cheon-gyeong’s pale face.

“Just tell me if it was that man!”

Baek Cheon-gyeong, on his knees, looked at the grass around him. As his life faded, the grass and trees around them were dying as well.

“You gave me hell… Why should I tell you anything?”

It was not simply resentment at being killed. To die while harboring the suspicion that he might have lived as someone’s puppet for the past ten years—that was surely hell.

He raised his head and looked at the wooden dolls set up all around them. Those dolls, too, were losing their mystic force. The dolls he had carved over the past ten years had not been carved for his own sake?

“……Don’t spout nonsense.”

He denied Geom Mugeuk’s words.

The grass and trees around them shook violently, as if a wind had blown through. It was because his rage had seeped into them, yet they looked as though they were mourning his death.

Geom Mugeuk slowly walked over and sat facing him. Their eyes met.

“I will take revenge. I’ll take your revenge too. So tell me!”

Geom Mugeuk knew it. The more desperate he became, the more Baek Cheon-gyeong would be enraged. But he had no choice. Whether he heard those words or not was far too important.

Baek Cheon-gyeong’s eyes began to close.

“No! Don’t die!”

Geom Mugeuk placed his palm against Baek Cheon-gyeong’s chest and poured internal energy into him. Baek Cheon-gyeong coughed, splattering blood across Geom Mugeuk’s face and body, but Geom Mugeuk did not stop infusing him with energy.

Baek Cheon-gyeong opened his eyes again.

“Right… You said you wanted revenge too, didn’t you?”

Geom Mugeuk held the doll out in front of him again. He said nothing, but Baek Cheon-gyeong understood. The face carved into the doll was Geom Mugeuk’s enemy.

At last, the truth flowed from Baek Cheon-gyeong’s mouth.

“Your revenge has nothing to do with me. The one who approached me… was not that man.”

Geom Mugeuk was startled.

“It wasn’t this man?”

“He… did not look like this.”

In the final moments of his life, he did not appear to be lying.

“Then who was it? How do I meet that person?”

But Baek Cheon-gyeong did not tell him about that person. The last person he thought of before death was Jin Ha-ryeong.

“……I’m sorry to Ha-ryeong.”

Deep regret filled his face.

“I told them to kill her if I did not return on time.”

Geom Mugeuk, shocked, shouted urgently.

“Where is she?”

He had never expected that Baek Cheon-gyeong would have given an order to kill her.

“……It’s already too late.”

“Where is she!”

Baek Cheon-gyeong used the last of his strength to look in one direction. At the same time, Geom Mugeuk flashed away, flying toward that place with Swift Steps.

“……I will apologize to that child in the underworld.”

With those words, Baek Cheon-gyeong’s head dropped.

When he died, every tree around them withered and died with him.

Now only the wooden dolls, their mystic force completely gone, silently watched over his death.

* * *

The moment Jin Ha-ryeong saw Chorim enter the room, her heart dropped. The look in Chorim’s eyes as he gazed at her was different from usual. It would have been better if they had held killing intent; instead, there was apology in them.

“You’ve come to kill me.”

Chorim readily admitted it. Since his master’s strict order had been given, he had to kill her.

“I bear you no personal grudge.”

For the first time, Jin Ha-ryeong felt death. I’m going to die? It did not feel real.

“I don’t want to die in such a stifling place. Let me see the sky just once before I die.”

When Chorim hesitated to answer, she pleaded again.

“Please.”

“Very well.”

The instant Chorim turned around and opened the door, Jin Ha-ryeong lunged.

Shiiik! Paat!

Jin Ha-ryeong swung what she held and stabbed at Chorim’s neck. What she held in her hand was a wooden peg that had been embedded in the bed.

The moment she realized Chorim had dodged and her strike had missed, she kicked open the door and threw herself outside.

After rolling across the ground, she sprang up and tried to run, but then she froze.

From the hut behind her, Chorim walked out, touching his neck.

“I understand.”

Blood was flowing from his neck, but the wound was not deep. Chorim was an expert, and no matter how sudden the ambush had been, it was not easy to kill him with a body that had no internal energy.

Even as he walked out behind her, Jin Ha-ryeong did not flee. No, she could not flee.

Dozens of warriors were waiting in front of the door.

Jin Ha-ryeong turned back to Chorim.

“What exactly do you understand? You’re not the one dying. I am. Are you trying to ease your conscience by acting so refined? You’re worse than some vile street thug.”

“I understand it all.”

“Fine. If you really do understand me, as you say……”

Jin Ha-ryeong raised the wooden peg. With her internal energy sealed, she was no match for them anyway, but she had not the slightest intention of dying while simply staring up at the sky.

“Surely you’re not going to kill me while my internal energy is sealed. Release it. Let’s fight properly.”

Even if he released her internal energy, there were too many enemies, so the chances of her surviving were slim. Still, she tried to find some way out.

“If you release my internal energy, I’ll fight with this as my weapon. Your subordinates are watching too. You should act like a leader.”

She hoped to provoke his pride and make him release her internal energy, but Chorim had no intention of doing so.

“You are Hubei’s finest rising star. If I carelessly release your internal energy, how could I handle you?”

“So? You’re going to kill a woman resisting with a wooden peg while her internal energy is sealed, like a coward?”

Even if she died, she would not die quietly. She would at least scratch at his pride and leave a wound in his heart.

Chorim said in a slightly irritated tone,

“This makes me feel a little better.”

Jin Ha-ryeong charged. Even with her internal energy sealed, she had the skill to reach the finals of the Little Dragon Tournament. The wooden peg in her hand stabbed sharply through the air. Naturally, she aimed for vital points that could subdue an opponent at once, such as the eyes and throat.

After they exchanged a dozen or so moves, Chorim’s fist flew straight into Jin Ha-ryeong’s face.

Thud!

Jin Ha-ryeong was sent flying.

“My apologies. I struck the face of Hubei’s greatest beauty.”

The subordinates watching burst into laughter. Chorim and his men alike were all enjoying this situation.

Touching her face, Jin Ha-ryeong rose from the ground.

“Live honestly from now on. This is who you are. Kidnapping, beating, killing, and gleefully mocking people.”

Chorim did not deny it. As she said, he was not a good person. Not only he, but everyone here was the same. They were people who had committed crimes and were being hunted. They bore deep grudges against the Murim Alliance, and they were people who could not live in the light. If not, they could not have hidden here for the past ten years.

Baek Cheon-gyeong had promised them. Once he killed the leader of the Murim Alliance, he would pardon all their crimes.

Shiiik! Shik!

Jin Ha-ryeong’s attacks only grazed the empty air.

Amid the laughter of those watching, Jin Ha-ryeong aimed for only one move.

A lucky strike.

But Chorim was not someone against whom luck would work.

Thud!

This time, she was struck in the stomach and sent flying. It hurt so much that even getting up was difficult, but she sprang to her feet and charged again.

After toying with her a few more times, Chorim decided to end it. Tree roots grew from the ground, binding her legs and arms and restraining her so she could not move.

When Chorim raised his hand, a leaf flew from somewhere into his grasp.

When it had flown over, it had been an ordinary leaf, but once it was in his hand, a chilling aura coiled around it.

“Your death will not be in vain. After you die, you will become fertilizer for these trees.”

Jin Ha-ryeong shot back coldly,

“Why? Because filthy things like you aren’t even fit to be fertilizer?”

At those words, Chorim revealed his true nature. With an evil look in his eyes, he said to her,

“If I hadn’t been ordered to be respectful to you and never lay a hand on you, you would have died after receiving the love of all of us.”

Regret flickered across Chorim’s face. Because she knew what that meant, Jin Ha-ryeong felt the greatest killing intent of her life. She truly wanted to kill him. It was too unjust to die at the hands of someone like this.

Chorim raised his hand high, intending to cut off her head with the leaf sword.

Even at the moment of death, Jin Ha-ryeong did not close her eyes and glared straight at him. She tried not to show fear, but her heart was filled with despair.

“So this is how I die.”

She had not known her end would be like this. She had thought she would die at least a little more splendidly than this.

When she died, she had hoped to look back on her life and die with dignity. Thinking of how her grandfather and elder brother would grieve made her even angrier.

If her grandfather lost his granddaughter now after already losing his child, his heartbreak would be beyond words. Her elder brother, too, would lose the only person to whom he could open his heart.

If they had done this to her, then they must have been prepared to harm them as well.

When that thought occurred to her, the person who came to mind was Geom Mugeuk.

“You’re the only one I can trust.”

To think she would face death while thinking of him at the last moment.

Just as the leaf sword was about to fly in and cut her neck—

Swaeeeeeeek!

Along with a wind that tore at the ears, screams erupted.

Puh-puh-puh-puh-puhk!

A gigantic great saber swept through the warriors as it flew toward them.

Chorim retreated and dodged the great saber that had flown in, cutting down his subordinates as it came.

Boom!

The great saber embedded itself in the stone wall behind him.

Startled, Chorim looked in the direction from which the great saber had come. A path had been carved through his subordinates, and a dozen or so of them lay collapsed on either side, mangled masses of blood and flesh.

“Raise the Ironwood Barrier!”

Chrrrrrrrk!

In the direction from which the blade had flown, wood rose from the ground and began to form shields.

The wall erected in front of Chorim was the largest and thickest. After raising the wall, he approached Jin Ha-ryeong again.

“No matter what happens, I will kill you!”

At that moment, Jin Ha-ryeong saw it.

From the other side of the great saber embedded behind Chorim, someone lightly leaped up toward them.

The movement was so light and nimble that it made not a whisper of wind.

Only when that person landed directly behind Chorim did he finally sense their presence and turn around.

What he saw was a single line.

Shiiiiik!

Slice.

The swiftly drawn sword struck his neck and sent it flying.

Chorim could not even let out a death scream before his head fell.

The person who appeared before the fallen Chorim was none other than Ian.

The great saber that had flown in was the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s Heaven-Destroying Great Saber.

Ian had been clinging to the back of the great saber and had flown in with it. Because the blade was larger than a person, they had missed the fact that she had been pressed against the far side of the blade as she came in.

It was a feat possible only because it was powered by the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s internal energy, and possible only because Ian had reached the ninth form of the Flying Heaven Sword Art.

At Chorim’s death, one of the warriors shrieked as if having a fit.

“Kill those bitches first!”

They rushed at Jin Ha-ryeong and Ian. Ian had no leeway to release Jin Ha-ryeong’s internal energy.

Ian cut down the warriors charging at them. After she cut down four in succession, the enemies changed tactics. They used as her weakness the fact that she was trying to protect Jin Ha-ryeong.

“Release sword energy!”

The warriors capable of unleashing sword energy released it all at once.

Ian shouted to Jin Ha-ryeong,

“Behind me!”

She could not dodge. To save Jin Ha-ryeong, she had to neutralize all the incoming sword energy.

Swaeeeeeeek!

At the same time, over a dozen streams of sword energy surged toward Ian. Ian was not confident she could block so much sword energy at once, but she did not dodge. She could not let Jin Ha-ryeong die.

At that desperate moment—

Shiiiik!

Thuuud!

Something came down and embedded itself in front of the two women, blocking the incoming sword energy.

Boom! Kwaang! Kwakwa! Kwaaang!

The sword energy struck one after another, but it could neither pierce nor break through it, and all of it dispersed and vanished.

Ian saw it. The enormous great saber blocking the way. And the man who had driven it into the ground.

Astonishingly, it was the Heaven-Destroying Great Saber.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon, who had flown there at some point, had pulled the great saber from the wall and landed firmly before the two of them.

At the same time, a thunderous roar exploded.

Ruuuumble! Kwaaang!

The Ironwood Barrier they had raised behind them shattered, and those nearby were swept away along with it. The one who had destroyed the Ironwood Barrier, which they had been certain would never break, with a single punch was none other than the Fist Demon.

In front was the Blood Heaven Blade Demon; behind was the Fist Demon.

One of the enemies shouted while looking at the Fist Demon.

“Kill that monster first!”

The moment those words ended, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon said briefly with a look of pity,

“Oh dear.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon grabbed Ian and Jin Ha-ryeong, whose bodies had been outside the Heaven-Destroying Great Saber, and pulled them behind it.

The next instant—

A thunderclap that tore at the ears sounded.

Kurururukwakwaang!

It was the first time in their lives that Ian and Jin Ha-ryeong had heard thunder so loud and terrifying. It was enough to make them want to cover their ears.

The next moment—

Huaaaaaaaang!

Beside the upright Heaven-Destroying Great Saber, an immense torrent of force energy swept past. If the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had not braced the Heaven-Destroying Great Saber with both hands and poured internal energy into it, even the great saber would have been ripped free and sent flying.

A single peal of thunder.

And then, silence.

Ian and Jin Ha-ryeong cautiously poked their heads out from behind the Heaven-Destroying Great Saber.

The scene spread outside shocked both women.

No one there had survived. With a single blow, everyone there had been swept up by the force released from his fist and turned into corpses.

The Fist Demon stood alone, his massive fists hanging at his sides.

At that sight, even Ian, who was on the same side as him, felt fear. It was completely different from when they trained together. The Fist Demon in real combat was truly another person. Today, Ian saw clearly why the Heavenly Demon trusted him the most among the Demon Venerables.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon pulled the Heaven-Destroying Great Saber from the ground and slung it over his back, and the Fist Demon began walking toward them through the corpses.

Chapter 289: I Wouldn't Have Done That.
---------------------------------------
The joy of having survived, compounded by the presence of the two Demon Lords, caused Jin Haryeong's heart to begin beating faster and fiercer.

Whether it was the Blood Heaven Blade Demon standing at her side with his great blade, or the Fist Demon slowly walking toward them, both men were truly terrifying.

'So these are the Demon Lords!'

Until now, the Demon Cult had been vague and distant to her. She had only heard that it was fearsome and cruel; the only member of the Demon Cult she had actually met was Geom Mugeuk. Young, handsome, and good with jokes.

But today, she felt as though she had seen the true Demon Cult.

The Fist Demon's face, now close by, was truly frightening, but Jin Haryeong tried her utmost to maintain a calm expression.

"Thank you so much for saving my life."

It was gratitude conveyed to both Demon Lords and I-an.

"I will surely repay this grace."

She feared the Demon Lords, but she did not hate the two men who had saved her.

The Demon Lords merely nodded once, making no show of having saved her life.

Her brother would scold her, asking if she were crazy, but she found herself curious about what kind of people these Demon Lords were.

After greeting the two, Jin Haryeong looked at I-an.

She knew how dangerous it was to fly here clinging to a blade. Moreover, it was a blade that had flown here while killing enemies.

That Demon Lord, and that I-an—neither of them made a single remark showing off that they had saved her life.

I-an released Jin Haryeong's sealed internal energy while cracking a joke.

"Friend! We agreed to meet—how could you break your promise?"

More than any word she had heard in her life, that word 'friend' now resonated deeply with Jin Haryeong. It was a moment where the friendship between the two women advanced one step further.

Jin Haryeong touched her face and spoke.

"That's why I look like this? There's nothing to see but my face."

At her reply, half-joking and half-self-mocking, I-an said something unexpected.

"Then starting today, you'll have to show something else."

Jin Haryeong could understand. I-an did not use her beautiful appearance as a weapon. That was why she was so strong, and that was why she could tell Jin Haryeong that she was beautiful. And she was saying it again: appearance is not important to them.

That's right. From today, there is no Greatest Beauty of Hubei. She would become the Greatest Swordsman of Hubei, and advance toward becoming the World's Greatest Swordsman. Never again would she face a life-or-death crisis against someone like Chorim.

Moreover, she had learned a great lesson today.

Life and death had truly been separated by a hair's breadth. If she had simply given up and resigned herself to death, she would have died before I-an and the Demon Lords arrived.

But because she had tried to fight until the very end, she had survived. She had truly felt that even heaven's will, when choosing someone, selects from among those who do not give up until the last moment.

Just then, something as fast as light came hurtling toward them.

Swhaaaaaak.

Geom Mugeuk descended upon the spot with a rapid step. His posture upon landing was so natural that he seemed like someone who had been there from the beginning.

Geom Mugeuk looked around and soon let out a sigh of relief. He could tell that the Demon Lords and I-an had arrived in time to save Jin Haryeong.

"You got here fast. If it weren't for them, I'd already be dead."

Geom Mugeuk laughed at Jin Haryeong's joke and said,

"That's why I'm close with them."

Geom Mugeuk looked at the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and the Fist Demon. His faith in leaving the judgment to the two men had been correct. They were men who had repaid faith with faith.

"You can put more strength into your shoulders. You look truly magnificent!"

"Where have you been loitering until now? Making an old man suffer."

However, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon's worried gaze was fixed on the blood staining Geom Mugeuk's clothes.

"Was it worth making the old man suffer?"

"You brat!"

Then the Fist Demon asked Geom Mugeuk,

"Are you injured?"

"It is not my blood. I am fine."

The Fist Demon nodded.

Watching their conversation, Jin Haryeong finally realized that Geom Mugeuk was the Young Cult Leader of the Demon Cult. The rumors that he had seized control of the Demon Lords were wrong. It was not that he had seized control, but rather that he had made them his own people. The gazes the two men directed at Geom Mugeuk were like the gaze of a grandfather toward her, and also like that of an older brother.

She could understand. Though I-an and the two Demon Lords were the ones who had saved her, she had ultimately survived thanks to Geom Mugeuk. If it were not for him, these people would not have moved.

"The granddaughter of the Martial Alliance Chief, indebted to a member of the Demon Cult twice over! I'm completely ruined."

At her quip, Geom Mugeuk and I-an laughed together.

Geom Mugeuk spoke to the two Demon Lords.

"Please wait at the inner quarters with I-an. I will take Young Lady Jin to the Martial Alliance and return."

"Is everything finished now?"

"No, there is one thing left to confirm before I go."

He had to confirm whether the Martial Alliance Chief had truly annihilated the Mokcheon family.

No matter how much he thought about it, Jin Pae-cheon did not seem like the type to commit such an act, but if he had been caught in someone's scheme, it was hard to say.

Geom Mugeuk gazed at the Martial Alliance, visible far in the distance, and spoke a joke he had made earlier as though it were serious.

"This time I might really have to run away, sir! I may have to."

* * *

Jin Hagun strode into the Alliance Chief's residence.

Jin Pae-cheon, who had been standing by the window staring outside, turned around urgently.

"What is the situation?"

"We mobilized the Demon Extermination Corps and searched every inch of the Dongho region, but could not find her. Has there been no word from the Blue Dragon Squad yet?"

Jin Pae-cheon shook his head.

"What about the Flying Tiger Squad?"

Another shake of the head. No matter how they searched in all directions, dispatching the elite of the Martial Alliance, they could not find Jin Haryeong's whereabouts.

There had been no demands from Baek Cheongyeong, who was presumed to be the kidnapper. He was truly burning with worry.

"It would be better to make it public to the martial world."
