Currently, the search was being conducted in secret, placing limitations on the investigation; if they made it a public search, witnesses might come forward.

But Jin Pae-cheon shook his head. Those bold enough to kidnap the granddaughter of the Martial Alliance Chief would not have been seen by anyone. Moreover, if they rashly made it public, the Sa Do Maeng or other villains might get involved.

Jin Hagun thought that his grandfather might say, "This is all your fault!"

But as if to say that was merely the narrow-minded worry of a child, Jin Pae-cheon said nothing of the sort. He was not someone who would shift blame onto others just because he was driven into a corner.

"Is there still no word from Geom Mugeuk?"

Jin Pae-cheon nodded silently.

The situation was gradually making Geom Mugeuk the last hope.

But the more that happened, the more complicated Jin Hagun's heart became.

'Could I really have been deceived?'

What if all of this was Geom Mugeuk's scheme?

The image of his younger sister caught in Geom Mugeuk's clutches came to mind.

After taking his sister, he thought that Geom Mugeuk might threaten him at some decisive moment.

In his fear of losing his sister, all sorts of thoughts came to him. His sister was the only person in this world who truly understood him.

Never in his life had he thought he would lose his sister. But now that it had become reality, he could not control his fear.

If he were to lose his sister?

His life from then on would change greatly as well.

And at this moment, Jin Pae-cheon was thinking the exact opposite.

"That child was confident that only he could save her."

Jin Hagun felt that his grandfather trusted Geom Mugeuk.

When he had rushed back upon hearing that his sister was kidnapped during the Demon Extermination Corps' mission, his grandfather had not trusted Geom Mugeuk at that time.

When asked who was responsible, he had answered thus.

—It was either Geom Mugeuk or Baek Cheongyeong.

And staring at him, his grandfather had asked,

—Who do you think it was?

Even then, he had not fully trusted Geom Mugeuk, and had hoped the Martial Alliance's warriors would find her.

But now his grandfather trusted Geom Mugeuk. It was not that he trusted him because he believed in him, but because the situation was so desperate that he had no choice. Though he was stronger than anyone in the world, with his granddaughter's life on the line, he had become an ordinary grandfather.

"I will go search again."

Just as Jin Hagun was about to leave, a warrior of the Alliance Chief's residence ran in and reported,

"Lady Haryeong has returned."

Jin Pae-cheon and Jin Hagun were startled.

Jin Pae-cheon sprang up from his chair and leaped down.

Soon after, Jin Haryeong entered.

She walked in first, and Geom Mugeuk followed behind her.

"Grandfather!"

Jin Haryeong ran and embraced Jin Pae-cheon. The moment she saw her grandfather, tears poured forth. Having passed through a brush with death this time, she realized anew just how great a presence her grandfather was in her life.

"Are you hurt anywhere?"

"I'm fine."

Her face and body were injured, but she had not sustained internal injuries.

"You've come well, you've returned well."

Tears welled in Jin Pae-cheon's eyes. Though he possessed a heart of iron, seeing his granddaughter return safely caused his heart to surge. He could not even remember the last time he had shed tears.

Jin Haryeong also reunited with Jin Hagun.

"Brother."

Jin Hagun held his younger sister tightly.

"I'm sorry."

All of this had happened because of him. It had started because he suspected his master.

"Why are you sorry, brother? It's my fault for getting captured so incompetently."

"Don't say that."

When the reunion that taught them how precious they were to each other came to an end, the heated gazes of Jin Pae-cheon and Jin Hagun turned toward Geom Mugeuk.

Saving Jin Haryeong was more important to the two of them than anything else. It was a greater deed than saving their own lives.

"Thank you truly for saving Haryeong."

Jin Pae-cheon formally pressed his fists together to convey his gratitude.

Geom Mugeuk also returned the courtesy politely and answered,

"Young Lady Jin is not only the Alliance Chief's granddaughter, but also my friend. Please do not mind it too much."

A life debt is repaid by saving a life in return. Jin Pae-cheon resolved that someday, he would save Geom Mugeuk's life.

Meanwhile, Jin Hagun was ashamed of himself for having doubted Geom Mugeuk even for a moment.

'I still have a long way to go.'

In the past, he would have ended with shame and a sense of inferiority, but now it was different. He felt that he had to work harder and strive more. Seeing that Geom Mugeuk stimulated him even at this moment, he truly was a person whose very existence was a stimulus.

"First, go receive treatment and rest. We can talk about what happened later. And you, come out with me for a moment."

Jin Hagun and Jin Haryeong noticed that their grandfather wanted to speak with Geom Mugeuk alone. Since they could speak with Geom Mugeuk later, the two withdrew obediently.

"Yes, Grandfather."

Walking with Jin Hagun, Jin Haryeong turned around and said,

"I'm so happy to see Grandfather again."

Jin Pae-cheon smiled and nodded. The thought of what might have happened had he not seen that child again caused his heart to sink.

And so, only two people remained in the Alliance Chief's residence.

"What happened to that Baek Cheongyeong?"

"He is dead."

He had expected as much since his granddaughter had returned, but hearing it directly from Geom Mugeuk put him in a strange mood.

"Did he say why he kidnapped Haryeong?"

Then, astonishing words flowed from Geom Mugeuk's mouth.

"Baek Cheongyeong is the younger brother of the Mokcheon family head."

In that instant, Jin Pae-cheon's eyes widened.

How could he have forgotten about the Mokcheon family?

"They are all dead."

"That is not so. One person, the family head's younger brother, survived."

"I believed him to be dead as well."

"He faked his death."

Now Jin Pae-cheon finally understood Baek Cheongyeong's dual nature. Could all of this really be related to his son's death?

And the most important question of today's conversation came out.

"Allow me to ask you, Alliance Chief. Was it you who annihilated the Mokcheon family?"

A heavy silence fell.

Geom Mugeuk thought that this silence was not a confession. If he had truly committed the deed, he would not fall into a silence that invited such suspicion. This was a silence born of circumstances.

Soon, Jin Pae-cheon let out a short sigh and began his story.

"That day, I went to find them for revenge. It was my family's vengeance, so I went alone."

His expression furrowed as if the scene of that day was vividly rising before his eyes.

"When I arrived, they were already all dead."

Jin Pae-cheon proved that he was speaking the truth in the most powerful way he could.

"I swear upon my dead son and daughter-in-law. I did not kill them."

For him, who valued honor above all, to swear upon his son meant his words were true. He too knew how important it was to reveal the truth.

And that day's events had remained a great wound in Jin Pae-cheon's heart.

"Seeing that scene snapped me back to my senses. Do you know what I thought as I, who had rushed there like a madman for revenge, looked upon that sight?"

After a brief pause, Jin Pae-cheon poured the emotions of that day into a long sigh.

"I thought it was a relief."

Geom Mugeuk listened in silence.

"I, the Martial Alliance Chief, thought it was a relief. If this had not happened first, I might have committed such deeds myself—that is truly what I thought. I, who should have mourned and raged at the deaths of the innocent!"

Jin Pae-cheon's face hardened. Pain seeped from his expression.

"You would not have annihilated them, Alliance Chief."

Jin Pae-cheon stared at Geom Mugeuk and asked,

"Do you truly think so?"

No, one could not know. How could anyone be certain how far a parent who had lost a child would go? Nevertheless, Geom Mugeuk spoke firmly.

"Yes, because you are not that kind of person."

Jin Pae-cheon let out another long sigh. It did not suit his usual domineering demeanor. One could see how much this matter had remained a deep wound in his life.

Torn between the thought that he might have annihilated them and his disappointment in himself for feeling relieved at the sight, he must have suffered all along. Because he had lived his entire life upholding righteousness, that wound must have been deeper than anyone else's.

Geom Mugeuk repeated his earlier words once more.

"You would not have done that, Alliance Chief."

Jin Pae-cheon's pupils trembled. At this moment, he realized. He had wanted to hear these words from someone for a very long time. That was why he had wanted to say them himself.

'Yes, I would not have done that.'

Jin Pae-cheon looked at Geom Mugeuk with deepening eyes.

"Why do you trust me so much? You hardly know me."

"Yes, I do not know you well, Alliance Chief. But is there not one thing that is certain?"

Geom Mugeuk calmly added,

"Is it not because you are someone who would not have done such a thing that I am able to stand alone in the very center of the Martial Alliance?"

Chapter 290: If the Demon Cult Is Cooler Than Us.
-------------------------------------------------
Deep within Jin Paecheon's heart, something stirred.

It was an emotion he had felt for the first time in his life. Having lived as the Martial Alliance Lord, he had met countless people and experienced all manner of things.

And now, his emotions dried up like stone, functioned only out of habit. Smiling out of habit, raging out of habit, grieving out of habit.

But the stirring he felt now was real.

He had lived while deliberately averting his eyes from that deeply wounded place. He had wanted to carve out the wound, but the more he tried, the larger the scar became. So he had covered it and lived on. He knew it was festering inside yet pretended otherwise. After all, he thought everyone lived carrying such wounds. Because he had much to do. Because he was the Martial Alliance Lord.

And now Jin Paecheon felt it. That the pus of his wound was being washed away as it began to heal.

He had said the same words to himself dozens, hundreds of times.

*I wouldn't have done that.*

But the wound in his heart had not healed. Yet when someone else uttered those words, emotions surged within him. He had a premonition that he could finally break free from that wound.

To think he would feel this way from just a few words.

Normally, his pride would have been wounded. He would have desperately denied it.

But looking at Geom Mugeuk gazing at him with clear, deep eyes, his pride—which had been writhing as if to awaken—quietly fell back asleep.

"Thank you for saying that."

Geom Mugeuk smiled pleasantly. The Alliance Lord was someone capable of saying those words at this moment. Whether one could acknowledge one's wound and speak those words was truly important. Because the final treatment of a wound had always belonged to the one wounded. The Alliance Lord had now wrapped the last bandage around his injury.

"Unlike the Alliance Lord, Baek Cheongyeong refused to acknowledge it until the very end."

Geom Mugeuk naturally steered the conversation back to what they had been discussing.

"I had believed until now that the one who annihilated them was the younger sibling of the Mokcheon-ga clan leader. Because he was not present during the annihilation that day, and was later found as a corpse who had taken his own life."

"In conclusion, that corpse was not him."

Since it concerned the Mokcheon-ga, how meticulously must Jin Paecheon have investigated the matter at the time? And yet he had been deceived.

"Then who annihilated them?"

"There is someone who exploited Baek Cheongyeong's desire for revenge. They helped him fake his death and infiltrate the Martial Alliance under a new identity. I suspect he is the one who annihilated the Mokcheon-ga."

Jin Paecheon's thoughts deepened. Manipulating a corpse, infiltrating the Alliance. This was not simple personal enmity or malice. This was war.

The opponent was not someone who merely schemed. By annihilating the Mokcheon-ga, he had proven his martial might as well.

"You must bear in mind that there is someone targeting the Martial Alliance."

To avoid wasting mental energy wondering if it might be someone from the Demon Cult, he added a word.

"This time he even tried to kill me to scheme for a war with our cult. Thus, he is also an enemy of our cult."

Geom Mugeuk did not mention that the death of the Alliance Lord's son and daughter-in-law might have been part of that man's scheme from the beginning. Because that was not yet certain.

"I shall investigate thoroughly to uncover the mastermind."

"I will find him as well. If you require my aid, call upon me anytime. At least in capturing this mastermind, I wish to help."

Geom Mugeuk limited the offer to himself rather than the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. There was no need for the Martial Alliance to join hands with the Demon Cult; by offering his own hand, he lessened the Alliance Lord's burden.

Jin Paecheon accepted readily.

"Do that."

"Then I shall see you again. I will meet Young Master Jin and Young Lady Jin, then depart at once."

"Wait."

Calling out to Geom Mugeuk, who was turning to leave, Jin Paecheon slowly approached.

As an expert of Jin Paecheon's caliber drew near, Geom Mugeuk's Heavenly Demon Guardian Divine Art activated on its own. But for this moment, at least, that effort was in vain.

Jin Paecheon embraced Geom Mugeuk tightly.

"Not as the Martial Alliance Lord, but as Haryeong's grandfather, I am truly grateful to you."

Geom Mugeuk also returned the embrace.

"Please take care of yourself until we meet again."

Naturally, he did not forget a jest.

"If there is a Demon Cult Young Lord whom the Martial Alliance Lord has embraced, tell him to step forth!"

Jin Paecheon laughed heartily, then spoke honestly.

"So a hug is enough to settle it? In my heart, I wanted to repay you with something grand. Our Alliance's treasure vault is filled with all manner of spirit medicines and divine weapons."

"Then please give them to me. I beg you!"

Jin Paecheon laughed at Geom Mugeuk's shameless reply.

"I'm sorry, but I must refuse. I fear you would add that power to your arsenal and strike us."

"Are you not more afraid of leaving a debt of the heart?"

"!"

Geom Mugeuk spoke with a smile.

"That is why I decline material compensation."

Watching Geom Mugeuk's retreating figure as he politely paid his respects and turned to leave, Jin Paecheon truly thought it a pity.

What if he had not been the Demon Cult's Young Lord?

He recalled the time when Jin Haryeong had brought Geom Mugeuk, declaring she would marry a servant.

*If only he had been a servant.*

It was Jin Paecheon's true feeling, which he could tell no one.

* * *

Jin Haryeong screamed and sat up with a start.

Turning her head, she saw she was in the infirmary.

"Are you alright?"

It was Jin Hagun, who had rushed in startled.

"I dreamt I died."

It was a dream in which, after she died at Chorim, the Demon Lords arrived.

She had thought he might shed at least a tear, if not wail, but Yi An had been too busy chatting with the Demon Lords to care about her death. That was more of a nightmare than dying.

*We've only truly seen each other three times, so what's there to feel slighted about?*

Since when were those three ordinary meetings? The first as friends, the second in a duel, the last as a rescue. Such a connection was truly not easy.

"It won't happen again."

"How can you say that for sure?"

"Because I will make sure of it."

"That's what I should do."

Though she was smiling, there was a new strength in Jin Haryeong's gaze. Through this incident, Jin Hagun felt his younger sister had grown.

"How long did I sleep?"

"About half a shichen."

Jin Haryeong rose. She felt uneasy that Geom Mugeuk might have left while she slept. Even if she had to let him go, she should bid him farewell.

"What about Geom Mugeuk?"

"He's waiting outside. He said he would greet you when you woke up, then leave."

At the news that Geom Mugeuk had waited for her, Jin Haryeong was inwardly moved.

As she tried to go outside, Jin Hagun spoke.

"You know?"

"What?"

"That it's impossible. Don't give your heart in the first place."

"Why? Are you worried? Worried that your only sister might have her heart stolen by the Demon Cult's Young Lord?"

Surprisingly, Jin Hagun nodded.

"Yes, I'm worried about that."

"Aren't you supposed to say you trust your sister and not to worry?"

"No, I'm worried."

Had it been anyone other than Geom Mugeuk, she would have been offended, but Jin Haryeong actually felt pleased. Because it was about whether she would be paired with Geom Mugeuk or not.

"Brother, I'm not as naive as you think."

"Then that's fine."

Jin Haryeong, who had been about to go outside, instead worried about Jin Hagun.

"What about you, Brother? Are you okay?"

"What do you mean?"

"I'm telling you not to get swept up by that person."

Then Jin Hagun revealed an unexpected thought.

"Forcibly trying not to get swept up is worse. Even if you get swept up, do it selfishly."

"Selfishly?"

"Take all the good influence he gives, without letting your own core waver."

Jin Haryeong wondered if that could really work. If human relationships could be severed as cleanly as he said, what worry would there be?

By that logic, she was the same. She had told him not to worry because she wasn't naive, but could she truly endure Geom Mugeuk to that extent?

"I suppose we really do share the same blood. Brother, you are far from naive too."

Thus, the two of them came outside.

Geom Mugeuk was standing in the clinic courtyard, looking up at the sky.

Jin Haryeong stood beside him.

"What are you looking at?"

"Do you see that sky color there? That is the color of my sword gang."

"Better than a dull color. To die struck by such a beautiful hue."

At her words, Geom Mugeuk laughed and turned his head.

"Is your body alright?"

"Thanks to you."

"I waited to see your face before leaving. See you next time."

Jin Haryeong was so reluctant to part with him. She wanted to stay with him a little longer.

*Stay and hang out a while longer. Even a few days.*

But being unable to say even such words was the relationship and fate between Geom Mugeuk and herself.

"Before you go, answer this. How can I become strong like you?"

"By training as if your life depends on it."

"But it looks like you're always playing."

"Training is done where no one can see."

Then Jin Hagun slowly walked over.

"I heard from Grandfather about the mastermind."

He could not believe there had been someone manipulating his master.

Geom Mugeuk passed the credit to him.

"It was thanks to you that this matter was uncovered. Had you not noticed something strange about your master, disaster might have befallen us later."

Jin Hagun found it horrifying to imagine. Had he let it pass unknowingly, Baek Cheongyeong would have driven them to ruin at a decisive moment.

Jin Hagun redirected the credit back to Geom Mugeuk.

"It was thanks to you. Because of your words to look properly rather than carelessly, I was able to realize."

From now on, Jin Hagun would strive to look everyone in the eye properly. Not only the Demon Extermination Corps, but the Martial Alliance's organization as well would be reformed.

Geom Mugeuk offered his final farewell.

"Be careful in all things."

"You as well."

Jin Hagun expressed his final gratitude.

"Thank you for saving my sister."

"Do not forget. Repay me later without fail."

Thinking it was an answer befitting Geom Mugeuk, Jin Hagun smiled faintly.

"Then let us meet again."

Geom Mugeuk also took his leave of Jin Haryeong.

Jin Haryeong asked with a smile.

"We're still friends, right?"

At Jin Haryeong's words, Geom Mugeuk nodded.

"There is a tavern near our cult's main altar with fine liquor and a good innkeeper. I shall invite you next time."

Would such a day truly come?

The moment Jin Haryeong nodded, Geom Mugeuk kicked off the ground and soared into the air, instantly vanishing from sight.

"Brother, I think we need to work hard. The Demon Cult can't be cooler than us, right?"

Jin Hagun walked away with large strides without answering.

*I know Brother's personality better than anyone. He won't lose, our Brother.*

Jin Haryeong, who had been following him, glanced back toward where Geom Mugeuk had disappeared.

But soon she too strode forward with vigor. As if she were the only one who had gained enlightenment?

It was her first step toward becoming not the Hubei First Beauty, but the Hubei First Sword.

* * *

In the An family kitchen, Yi An was busy cooking.

She did not know why she had suddenly felt like cooking for the two Demon Lords herself.

There were only three or four dishes she knew how to make, and she prepared the one she was most confident in.

"Then please enjoy."

"You eat too."

"I'm fine."

Because Gwonma insisted, even gesturing for her to join, Yi An dined with them.

Sharing a meal with Hyeolcheon Doma and Gwonma?

Wasn't this a punishment by any standard? She was extremely nervous when Hyeolcheon Doma spoke while eating.

"It's bland."

Hyeolcheon Doma was not one to offer false praise out of consideration for the cook.

"Ah! I'm sorry. I under-seasoned it. I'll go prepare it again."

Then Gwonma grabbed the plate Yi An was about to take away.

"He said eating bland is healthy."

"Who?"

"The Demonic Physician."

Yi An was inwardly grateful, not having expected Gwonma to say such a thing.

She realized the Demon Lords were truly different up close from how they appeared. Then again, her outward impression and actual personality were completely different too.

Hyeolcheon Doma asked, as if curious.

"Why did you suddenly cook for us?"

Of course, there was a reason.

She had been moved and filled with respect by the two Demon Lords in battle. The strength and intensity they showed gave her tremendous inspiration as she began her full activities as the Ghost Shadow Great Lord. So she wanted to treat them to a sincere meal.

"It's not an opportunity that comes often."

She added what Seo Daeryong always said at drinking gatherings.

"I want to tell my child later. That this mother personally cooked for Lord Hyeolcheon Doma and Lord Gwonma when she was young."

"Make sure to say it was bland."

Yi An laughed awkwardly at Hyeolcheon Doma's words.

Then Gwonma suddenly spoke.

"A child resembling both Mugeuk and you would be incredible."

Yi An was startled. At first she thought she had misheard. Flustered, she quickly said.

"How could I marry the Young Lord? Even if I've become a Great Lord, I was merely an ordinary martial artist."

"What does it matter? Neither the Cult Lord nor Mugeuk is someone who cares about such things."

At Gwonma's words, Hyeolcheon Doma nodded in agreement.

She was truly grateful to Gwonma for saying so. But there had always been something weighing on her mind. It was the first thought that came to her whenever marriage was mentioned.

Words she normally would not bring up, yet somehow she wanted to speak them before these two frightening people. No, the words spilled out naturally.

"I don't even know who my parents are."

Then Hyeolcheon Doma offered a word of comfort.

"Family is the enemy. You don't need to know."

She was also grateful to Hyeolcheon Doma for speaking thus.

Then Gwonma said something completely unexpected.

"If that truly weighs on your mind, I shall become your father."

Hyeolcheon Doma was startled by those words. So how startled must Yi An, the person in question, have been?

So surprised and flustered was she that she dropped the chopsticks she held.

She had never spoken privately with Gwonma. So why? Did he take a liking to her at the late-night training gathering? Or was there another reason? Was it a joke? Had she misheard this time for sure?

All manner of thoughts filled her mind, leaving her speechless.

Then Geom Mugeuk entered.

"I have returned."

Geom Mugeuk immediately grasped the frozen atmosphere.

"Nothing happened when the Demon Cult's Young Lord was at the Martial Alliance Lord's residence, so what in the world happened at the An family's dinner table?"

It was Hyeolcheon Doma who broke the ensuing silence.

"The food was bland."

Chapter 291: What If That Scary Person Were My Father-in-Law?
-------------------------------------------------------------
“If Ian wanted, I told her I would become her father.”

At the Fist Demon’s bombshell declaration, everyone turned their eyes to Geom Mugeuk. They were curious how he would react.

Unexpectedly, he merely blinked, showing no surprise.

“You’re not surprised?”

At the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s question, even Geom Mugeuk seemed surprised by his own reaction.

“So it seems. Why am I not surprised?”

Why? Because you wanted to tease the Fist Demon.

Geom Mugeuk looked back and forth between the Fist Demon and Ian.

“A father and daughter so opposite in every way? Maybe that’s why I’m not surprised.”

If anyone else had said that, the Heaven-Destroying Great Saber might have needed a shield again, but the Fist Demon readily nodded even at Geom Mugeuk’s teasing. Even he had to admit that their appearances were polar opposites.

“Do you think anyone will believe it?”

“Would Master believe it?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon slipped into the conversation, making a sincere remark sound like a joke.

“He’d think she’d been kidnapped.”

The Fist Demon nodded as if he agreed with that as well.

Just then, Ian suddenly spoke.

“Father!”

The surprised gazes of the others gathered on Ian. Of the three men, the most startled was the Fist Demon. Gazing at him, Ian said,

“If a daughter like me is all right with you, please take me as your daughter.”

The Fist Demon’s eyes widened. Since meeting him, this was the first time Geom Mugeuk had seen him look so astonished.

“Would you really be all right with a father like me?”

In a voice that did not suit him at all, the Fist Demon’s voice trembled. The voice of the Fist Demon, who would not so much as blink even with ten thousand enemies arrayed before him, was trembling.

“You are far too good for me.”

Tears welled in Ian’s eyes. The thought that such an incredible person would become her father overwhelmed her. She was truly, tearfully grateful.

Ian looked at Geom Mugeuk and asked with her eyes,

Is this really all right?

It had been an impulsive decision on her part as well. It might have been the most important decision of her life, yet she had made it in an instant.

Is this really all right?

The answer came in the form of Geom Mugeuk’s radiant smile.

“Congratulations, Ian.”

After taking a deep breath, Ian asked the Fist Demon again.

“May I ask you one thing?”

“Ask me anything.”

“Why did you say you wanted to take me as your daughter?”

The Fist Demon’s answer was simple.

“Because I would like to have a daughter like you.”

It was an answer Ian liked more than any other.

Ian rose from her seat and bowed to the Fist Demon.

“I look forward to your guidance from now on… Father.”

At the word “father,” her emotions surged.

“That’s the first time I’ve said the word ‘father’ in my entire life.”

She had never once called anyone “Dad” or “Mom.”

She had resented the parents who abandoned her, tried to understand that they must have had their reasons, missed them, hated them, and tried to forget them. I must never become parents like them. No, I must never become a parent at all. I’ll simply live alone and die quietly.

The emotions she had kept buried came surging up, and tears spilled down her cheeks.

Geom Mugeuk, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, and the Fist Demon silently watched her shoulders shake. They let her cry to her heart’s content. The strongest people in the world stood there for a long time, protecting her.

When her sobs finally subsided, the Fist Demon helped her to her feet.

Seeing her face covered in tears, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon made a joke.

“Now that you’ve become ugly, you finally look like father and daughter.”

Geom Mugeuk burst out laughing. The Blood Heaven Blade Demon was not the sort of person who usually meddled in other people’s affairs. Yet when it came to the Fist Demon, he was especially fond of joking and making lighthearted remarks. It meant that he and the Fist Demon were a good match, and that he liked him. This was the same man who made such a fuss about being ostracized whenever he attended the Demon Venerables’ gatherings.

A powerful aura radiated from the Fist Demon as he looked at Ian.

“When we return to the Order, I will officially announce that you have become my daughter. From now on, as long as I am alive, no one in the martial world will dare mistreat you.”

At the Fist Demon’s declaration, tears streamed down Ian’s eyes once more.

“This is the last time I’ll cry. I won’t cry anymore from now on.”

She forced a smile, but the tears would not stop.

The very existence of parents had always been her deepest wound. She had never wanted to tell anyone about it, speaking of it only to Geom Mugeuk. From now on, she would live boasting about her father to her heart’s content. What does your father do? Mine is the Fist Demon!

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon spoke with an astonished expression.

“When you first brought it up, I thought you were joking.”

Geom Mugeuk picked up his words.

“You’re not the sort of person to joke about something like this, are you?”

“That terrifying man is going to be your father-in-law?”

“Indeed. I wonder what son-in-law could possibly handle him.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon stared at Geom Mugeuk with an expression that clearly said, I was talking about you!

Whether he knew what the older man was thinking or not, Geom Mugeuk teased Ian.

“Ian, what are you going to do? You’re already married.”

Then the Fist Demon suddenly spoke.

“You could marry him.”

Ian waved her hands frantically.

“Please don’t say things like that. Young Master, don’t worry about it.”

The Fist Demon glared at Geom Mugeuk, waiting for his answer.

“I eat my food salty, you know?”

“Then you’ll start eating it bland from now on!”

So that’s how you’re going to play it?

Geom Mugeuk gave him a meaningful smile.

“Can you handle having the Heavenly Demon as your in-law?”

At that, the Fist Demon flinched. He had thought only of Ian and had not considered that far. The one person in the world who could make him utterly helpless—and now his in-law? The Cult Leader’s in-law?

“Hey! I’m right here! I’m a bride with absolutely no intention of getting married!”

Amid the chaos, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon stroked his chin and imagined the day.

“If they really do get married, there’ll be absolute pandemonium.”

* * *

That night, the Fist Demon was alone in the rear courtyard of the annex, practicing his martial arts. Perhaps he was soothing his complicated feelings through training.

His bare torso was perfectly sculpted, as though painted by a master artist.

“When will I have a body like yours, Master?”

I had also taken off my shirt and stood beside him, throwing out punches from the Thunderclap Asura Fist. The reason we trained bare-chested was to observe every movement of our muscles.

Training with him again after so long left me feeling refreshed.

The Fist Demon had me repeatedly focus on the fifth technique, Vajra Asura. It was a move that temporarily hardened the body like steel to protect it, and could save one’s life in a crisis. If Vajra Asura were used together with the Heavenly Demon Bodyguard Art, it might even produce the temporary effect of an indestructible vajra body.

“Why are you focusing so heavily on Vajra Asura?”

“Judging by how things are unfolding, I think you need to protect your body first.”

Now that the mastermind behind the attempt on the Martial Alliance Leader had appeared, he had decided that I could not afford to relax simply because I was the Young Cult Leader.

The Fist Demon repeated the key formulae of Vajra Asura for me. Some of it was material I had heard before, while other parts were new.

Whenever I failed to understand something, I asked immediately, and the Fist Demon explained it to me with utmost sincerity.

“All right. Endure this!”

“You’re not going to hit me with that fist, are you?”

“Why wouldn’t I?”

The instant he finished speaking, the Fist Demon’s fist came flying at me.

Hwaaaaaaaaaang!

Kwaaang!

I was knocked backward without even attempting to block the attack. In the process, the Heavenly Demon Bodyguard Art and Vajra Asura activated together, dispersing the impact.

When I walked back to him unharmed, the Fist Demon was delighted.

“Well done. Vajra Asura will save your life someday. Never neglect it.”

“I’ll keep that in mind.”

After finishing a round of training, we sat side by side and rested for a while.

“May I ask why?”

Even though the question had come out of nowhere, the Fist Demon knew what I was asking.

“Didn’t I tell you? I wanted to have a daughter like Ian.”

“I mean the real reason.”

After a brief pause, the Fist Demon spoke.

“I don’t know what my parents looked like either. That’s why I understand that girl’s feelings better than anyone.”

It was the first time I had heard anything about his personal history.

“When I was young, I gritted my teeth and denied them. I didn’t even allow myself to think about revenge. To harbor a desire for revenge would mean acknowledging them. I tried desperately to erase them from my life completely.”

“And now? Don’t you want to find your parents? Don’t you want to tell them, ‘I’ve lived well even without you’?”

The Fist Demon shook his head, revealing a hollow bitterness.

“I wish even a trivial desire for revenge remained in my heart.”

It sounded as though he had become desolate, devoid even of such feelings.

“And yet here I am, trying to make someone my family.”

I was grateful that he was showing this human side of himself. It meant he trusted me that much.

“No. You did the right thing.”

“Do you truly think so?”

“I want Ian to be happy. And I believe becoming your daughter will bring Ian the greatest happiness of all. You made the right choice—for Ian and for yourself.”

A smile appeared on the Fist Demon’s terrifying face. I liked seeing him at times like this. A smile blooming amid that fearsome expression—the Fist Demon looked rather impressive then.

“Thank you for saying that.”

Who could have imagined that the Fist Demon and I would one day be able to have conversations like this? Normally, he was a man of few words, but when circumstances required him to speak, he spoke better than anyone.

“Things might be difficult for you from now on.”

“Why?”

“You’ve never been a father before, have you? What will you do if your daughter rebels?”

The Fist Demon raised a fist.

“Do you think this won’t work?”

“If that worked, would people say raising children was harder than becoming a Sword God?”

The Fist Demon lowered the fist he had raised playfully. A precious fist like that should not be allowed to droop so helplessly.

“It’ll work somewhere else, at least.”

“Where?”

“You’ll have the most beautiful daughter in the world. You’ll have plenty of chances to use that fist.”

Understanding what I meant, the Fist Demon clenched that enormous fist again.

“That sounds fun.”

“It will be fun for you, Master. I’ll pray in advance for your opponents’ souls.”

Who could have imagined that life would turn out this way? Who could have imagined that Ian would become the Fist Demon’s daughter?

“Perhaps you really can bring down that cliff, Master.”

At my words, the Fist Demon asked with a puzzled look,

“Wouldn’t it be the other way around?”

How could someone so soft and vulnerable to affection bring down a cliff? That must have been what he was thinking.

“A person has to change before martial arts can change.”

The Fist Demon mulled over my words.

“If I change, I’ll be able to destroy it?”

“Did you ever imagine taking someone as your foster daughter?”

The Fist Demon slowly shook his head.

“You’ve changed, Master. I believe that change will probably allow you to break through your limits.”

The Fist Demon looked down at his own fist and suddenly spoke.

“Perhaps I’ll be able to bring down the cliff when my fists no longer make the sound of thunder.”

I could not imagine the Thunderclap Asura Fist without the sound of thunder.

But there was an even more frightening image.

A fist that flew in without a sound and overturned heaven and earth!

The moment we imagined it, neither the Fist Demon nor I could remain still. We sprang to our feet and threw our punches. Our training continued without pause until dawn.

* * *

The next day, I said farewell to everyone.

“I’m going to the Jiangxi Branch.”

The original purpose of this trip outside the Order had been to visit the Hunan Branch and Jiangxi Branch before returning.

Until new clues emerged concerning Hwa Mugi, I had to live my own life. Quietly and steadfastly, without allowing myself to be swept along.

“You’re going alone?”

“Yes. Take the two of them back to the Order. Oh, and tell all the guards waiting along the way to return as well.”

“You’re going alone after leaving even the guards behind?”

“This time, I’m conducting a covert inspection.”

I had gone openly to the Hunan Branch with a retinue of guards, so this time I planned to visit in secret. Hopefully, the Jiangxi Branch Lord was not as corrupt as the Hunan Branch Lord.

“I’ll be off first. Don’t expect to work this old man from now on.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon said only what he wanted to say and walked toward the door. Watching his back, I called out,

“Considering what I’ve achieved by working you, I don’t think that will be easy.”

Don’t worry. If I need one of the Demon Venerables for something, I’ll call on a different one this time.

Next, it was time to part with the Fist Demon and Ian.

“Thank you for yesterday’s instruction.”

“Be careful.”

The Fist Demon also walked toward the door. Although there was now more for that broad back to protect, it seemed to leave in an even better mood as it filled the doorway.

Only after the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and the Fist Demon had gone outside did Ian finally reveal what was in her heart.

“I’m sorry.”

“What’s wrong?”

“For deciding such an important matter without discussing it with you beforehand.”

“You hadn’t made up your mind beforehand either, had you?”

“I think I should have asked you, even if only through telepathic transmission, at the moment of decision.”

That must have been weighing on her mind enough to keep her awake all night. Yes, it was precisely because she was this kindhearted that she had to become the Fist Demon’s daughter.

“Because it’s such an important matter, you make the decision that way. You do as your instincts tell you.”

“What if it’s the wrong choice?”

“Then you bear the consequences. You’ll suffer under a terrifying father.”

Ian made a face as though imagining that future, then mimed clutching her head.

“He wouldn’t just be a terrifying father! He’d be the most terrifying father in the world.”

“Take comfort in the fact that he’s only the second-most terrifying. There’s a poor child who has the number-one father standing before him.”

Ian laughed at my joke.

“Trust your instincts. Your martial arts, and your heart. The stronger you become, the more correctly your instincts will function.”

“I’ll keep that in mind, Young Master.”

“See you later at the Main Sect.”

“Be careful.”

Ian, who had been about to walk away, turned back at the door.

“To think I’m going to become the Fist Demon’s daughter. I’ve come this far thanks to you, Young Master. Thank you so much.”

“Ian, our journey may not have even begun yet.”

Ian looked startled, as though she could not even imagine what lay beyond that.

“Just how far are you planning to go?”

“I don’t know that myself.”

“It doesn’t sound like a joke anymore.”

“Because it isn’t a joke.”

Killing Hwa Mugi was not the sole goal of my life. My goal was to live this life happily and well. Killing Hwa Mugi was merely something I had to do in order to achieve that. My life would continue even after Hwa Mugi was dead. Mine, and the lives of my people.

“You told me that, didn’t you, Young Master? That if things became too difficult, I should rest and then continue.”

“I did.”

“You look very tired right now.”

It probably wasn’t only because of training until dawn. Lately, thinking about Hwa Mugi had kept me on edge. No matter how well I concealed my feelings, Ian alone could sense and read them.

I smiled at her and nodded.

“All right. I’ll rest a while.”

And so, even she left.

I stood in the courtyard of the safehouse for a moment and looked up at the sky. I tried to do so whenever I had the chance. Every time I looked, it seemed as though the sky was saying this: Yes, no matter how busy you are, let’s live while looking up at me once in a while.

I knew better than anyone how difficult it was even to lift one’s head once life began pushing one along. Just as my life had been before my regression.

Back then, I walked around looking only at the ground, looking only for materials to fill my pockets, looking only for ways to kill my enemies. The sky must have been just as blue then…

Hoping that this journey to Gangseo would be a somewhat comfortable and peaceful one, I left the safehouse.

So, you villains, get some rest too! Please!

Chapter 292: If Only All Guests Were Like This.
-----------------------------------------------
Bian Zhong was lucky.

He had come from Jiangxi to Hubei as a coachman, and for two days he had been unable to catch a carriage heading back.

Then, just in time, he found a carriage going to Jiangxi.

At places that rented out carriages, they hired coachmen like this, connected them with work, and took a portion of the money the coachman earned.

Bian Zhong asked the manager of the carriage rental office.

“How far is the passenger going?”

“To the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult’s Jiangxi Branch.”

Bian Zhong lowered his voice and asked quietly.

“Could he be a demonic practitioner?”

“Didn’t look like one on the outside.”

Even so, Bian Zhong grew tense and afraid. How many coachmen had died after getting caught up in fights between unorthodox faction members and demonic practitioners?

Bian Zhong cleaned the carriage and waited, and before long, the passenger arrived. He was a young and handsome man.

“My name is Bian Zhong. I’ll see to your comfort.”

“I’ll be in your care. It’s nothing urgent, so there’s no need to drive too fast.”

“Understood.”

Seeing the young man greet him, Bian Zhong could tell.

‘He’s not a demonic practitioner.’

He had carried several demonic practitioners before, but he had never seen one so courteous.

Of course, Bian Zhong was mistaken. The other party was the most famous demonic practitioner among all demonic practitioners. The young man was none other than Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk intended to rent a carriage and ride to Jiangxi. He would practice the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art in the carriage, and if there was a place with fine scenery, he would stop and look around before continuing on. He meant to go leisurely, resting as he pleased.

“Now then, we’ll be setting off.”

And so the carriage departed.

After they had been traveling for quite some time, Bian Zhong glanced back through the window connected to the coachman’s seat. Geom Mugeuk had his eyes closed. Bian Zhong could not tell whether he was sleeping or lost in thought.

‘Yes, this is the best kind of passenger.’

Inside the carriage, Geom Mugeuk was cultivating the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art. At times like this, when he was on the move, he actually found it easier to concentrate. The sound of wind brushing past the carriage, birdsong from afar, the voices of passersby chattering. Sometimes such sounds heightened his focus all the more.

And when he grew tired of training, he would look out at the scenery, as he was doing now.

‘This is what resting is.’

The rest Ian had spoken of surely had not meant cultivating the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art while taking brief pauses, but in his current situation, even this sort of rest was a luxury.

After gazing at the scenery outside for a while, Geom Mugeuk closed his eyes again.

He began to recite the endlessly tedious verses of the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art with care, as though learning them for the first time. Another drop of water was added to the water jar.

* * *

That evening, the carriage stopped at an inn. Every coachman had different places where he rested, but they had one thing in common.

“I’ve heard the inns where coachmen rest are good at cooking.”

“That’s right. Since we travel so often, we know all the inns with good food.”

The two of them entered the inn.

“Then please rest well.”

“Let’s eat together.”

“I’m all right.”

As a rule, coachmen ate separately from their passengers. There were passengers who bought them meals, but for the most part, each person took care of eating and sleeping on their own until departure.

“I don’t like eating alone. Join me.”

Bian Zhong sat down with him, pretending he had no choice. From Bian Zhong’s perspective, it was a good thing; if he could get even one meal out of it, that was money saved.

“You must know this place well, so recommend something. What’s the best dish here?”

Bian Zhong recommended a few dishes. Geom Mugeuk ordered everything he mentioned.

“Now, we’re hungry, so let’s eat plenty.”

To a coachman, Geom Mugeuk was the finest kind of passenger.

He had invited him to eat together, yet did not ask this and that or nag him.

There were people like that. People who, while showing kindness, made a show of every bit of it; people who bragged about themselves until your ears ached; even people who asked how much a coachman earned and advised him that he ought to live a different life.

But Geom Mugeuk merely stared blankly outside the inn.

If anything, Bian Zhong wanted to speak to him.

He wanted to ask what the young hero did for a living, and why he was going to a branch of the Demon Cult.

Of course, Bian Zhong asked nothing. He had reached an age where he knew well enough that nothing good came from getting entangled with martial artists.

A short while later, the dishes came out, and Geom Mugeuk ordered wine as well.

He poured a cup for Bian Zhong too.

“I have to drive the carriage, so I’ll have just one cup.”

After drinking wine for the first time in a long while and eating delicious food, Bian Zhong truly felt alive again. Lately, he had been barely staving off hunger every day in order to save money.

‘Truly, if I only had passengers like this, being a coachman would be a decent job.’

Rather than lucky days like today, a coachman’s life was a succession of anxious, nerve-racking days.

Since the man had even bought him a meal, it felt shameless to say nothing at all, so he cautiously asked one question.

“Is this perhaps your first time going to Jiangxi?”

“No.”

“I see.”

The question of why he was going to the Demon Cult’s branch rose all the way to his throat, but he forced it back down.

“Thank you for the meal.”

Geom Mugeuk did not deliberately start a conversation, fearing it would make him uncomfortable. During his life as a wandering warrior, he had experienced enough to know that at times like this, simply remaining quiet was what best served the other person.

After finishing the meal, Geom Mugeuk was heading up to his guest room when he asked Bian Zhong,

“Why aren’t you going?”

“I can sleep in the carriage. Please go and rest comfortably.”

Bian Zhong usually snatched a little sleep in the carriage. He had to save even a single coin.

Geom Mugeuk booked a room for him as well with his own money.

“I’m fine.”

“I want to ride in a carriage driven by someone who slept comfortably.”

When he put it that way, Bian Zhong could not refuse again. Truly, if only there were passengers like this!

Upon entering the guest room, Bian Zhong felt wonderful.

‘How long has it been since I slept in a bed?’

That night, he slept very comfortably. He did not even envy the leader of the Martial Alliance.

The next day as well, Geom Mugeuk’s kindness continued.

He invited him to eat together and booked him a room too. Bian Zhong waved his hands and said he was fine, but Geom Mugeuk put forth the logic that taking care of the person holding the reins was the same as taking care of his own safety.

In the end, they ate together, and he slept in a good bed.

On the first day, he had eaten and slept happily, simply pleased by it all, but on the second day, while he was eating the dishes, he thought of his wife and children. It was because the dish was one his children liked. It was the dish he had bought for his eldest on his birthday last year. No, had it been the year before?

If only he could pack this up and take it with him. If he truly could, he wanted to take it home.

Then he raised his head and saw Geom Mugeuk looking at him. As if he had read his thoughts, Geom Mugeuk asked whether he had children.

“Do you have children?”

“I have two.”

“How old are they?”

“Seven and nine.”

“You must miss them.”

Bian Zhong grinned and nodded. Miss them? Of course he did. At times, he even felt the impulse to drive the carriage back to them at once.

And that night, his children appeared in his dreams.

* * *

Now and then, Geom Mugeuk had the carriage stop at places with good scenery.

Far off in the distance, the sun was setting beyond the river. The sight of the crimson sunset spreading over the glittering river was truly magnificent.

Geom Mugeuk stood with his arms crossed, gazing at it in silence.

Thanks to him, Bian Zhong also looked at the sunset for the first time in a long while. Come to think of it, it had truly been a long time since he had watched the sun set like this. So long that he could not even remember.

Whenever the sun went down, he was too busy driving the carriage with the thought that he had to reach an inn before it grew dark. To him, the sunset had instead been a signal that made him anxious. Today, he felt it anew. The sunset was this beautiful.

“My heart told me so. It said I looked tired, and that I should rest a bit. So I intend to listen to my heart.”

“Ah, I see.”

Bian Zhong answered as though he understood, but he did not understand what that meant. His heart? What did that mean? It only led him to the mistaken notion that perhaps this man had a heart condition.

They traveled on and on like that, until it was the day before they would reach their destination.

Bian Zhong was caught by Geom Mugeuk while refunding the room he had borrowed from the innkeeper and getting the money back. Geom Mugeuk, who had happened to come out for some fresh air, saw him. Pretending not to have seen, Geom Mugeuk went outside.

“Ah!”

Bian Zhong let out a sigh. Considering the other man’s sincerity, it was something he should not have done. Even if the other party understood, he himself felt terribly wretched.

The next day, troubled by it the entire time he drove the carriage, Bian Zhong approached Geom Mugeuk when they stopped in a field to let the horses rest for a while.

“I’m sorry about what happened yesterday.”

“It’s all right.”

It probably would have been enough to leave it at that and move on. But suddenly, a thought occurred to him.

He would have explained himself in detail to someone with a foul temper out of fear, yet to such a good person, he was going to say only that he was sorry and let it pass. That felt cowardly.

Fine. At least let me make my excuses properly.

“Last year, my second child suffered from a terrible fever. Back then, I borrowed money to pay for medicine. We were already living hand to mouth, and in my urgency, I borrowed money I should never have borrowed.”

“You borrowed from the Yama lenders.”

“That’s right.”

Bian Zhong let out a sigh.

“It wasn’t even much. I borrowed ten taels for the treatment costs… but now, after a year has passed, it has become seventy taels. Even though I’ve paid more than twenty taels in interest in the meantime.”

That was why he slept in the carriage. He had to pay the interest and feed his wife and children. But the debt only kept growing.

“It’s my fault. I borrowed it even after hearing how expensive the interest was from the start.”

He did not regret it. If he had not borrowed that money then, his second child would have died or become crippled.

Then Geom Mugeuk said,

“How could that be your fault, Mr. Bian? The fault lies with those who make Yama loans with interest beyond all common sense.”

At the words “Mr. Bian,” Bian Zhong’s heart surged. No one had ever addressed a coachman with such respect before.

And whenever he told people about borrowing money, they all said it was his own fault. Why had he borrowed money from a place like that in the first place?

“What is the name of the place where you borrowed the money?”

“It’s a place called Tongqiansuo.”

“Let’s go there first.”

“We mustn’t. They are violent and vicious people. Some of them know martial arts, and I’ve heard rumors that terrifying people are behind them. They have been making Yama loans in the region for a very long time, yet they’re still going strong.”

Bian Zhong felt hope that he might be able to escape this hell, and at the same time fear that this good man might be killed by them.

Geom Mugeuk stared at him blankly and said,

“It seems there’s been some misunderstanding. I’m going because I need money.”

For a moment, Bian Zhong wondered whether he should believe those words or not. This was someone who had spent money so freely on the way here, and now he was going to take out a Yama loan? So suddenly?

“Let’s go.”

What could he do when the passenger said they should go? The carriage changed direction from where it was originally supposed to go.

* * *

Surprisingly, after arriving at Tongqiansuo, Geom Mugeuk really did borrow money.

“How much can you lend me?”

Zhou Yang, the man in charge of lending money, looked Geom Mugeuk up and down.

“We don’t often lend money to outsiders…”

He did not state it firmly, trailing off instead. It meant they would lend the money after all.

“Do you have collateral?”

“No. Shall I leave this sword?”

“What would a sword be worth? We don’t take swords as collateral.”

Most of the swords martial artists tried to leave as collateral were miserable things. More than a few would buy some cheap sword from somewhere and pass it off as a famous blade.

“My sword would be offended to hear that.”

Zhou Yang looked at the sword. When he saw the sword wrapped round and round in bloodstained cloth, a snort escaped him. He had dressed it up well to make it look impressive.

“If you have no collateral, someone who will stand surety for you will do.”

At Zhou Yang’s words, Geom Mugeuk turned to Bian Zhong, who had come with him.

“Would you stand surety for me?”

Bian Zhong jumped in fright. For a moment, he wondered whether the man had brought him here to squeeze blood from a stone and make him stand surety.

“I’m joking. My father said you shouldn’t stand surety even for family.”

Bian Zhong, who had been tense, let out a sigh of relief.

“If I have neither collateral nor a guarantor, how much can I borrow?”

“We can lend you up to ten taels.”

“Good. Let’s borrow ten taels.”

“Now, write your personal details on this loan document and press your thumbprint.”

Geom Mugeuk wrote as instructed and even pressed his thumbprint. When lending the money, they made it easy—truly fast and simple.

“If you don’t pay it back on time, interest will be added. There are always bastards who later throw a fit, saying the interest is expensive or whatnot. Shameless wretches who came here on their own two feet to borrow money, then spout that kind of nonsense.”

Bian Zhong lowered his head. Zhou Yang had said it for Bian Zhong to hear.

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk took Bian Zhong’s side.

“How desperate must someone be to borrow money even at such high interest? Those who take advantage of desperate people’s hearts are the shameless ones.”

For an instant, Zhou Yang’s expression hardened. But Zhou Yang, seasoned as he was, did not get angry. That was because he knew better than anyone what kind of end awaited men who acted cocky like this.

‘You damned fool. Let’s see if you can still say that later. I’ll show you what it means to be unable to repay money even when you want to.’

Zhou Yang checked the contents written on the loan document.

“Your hometown is Guangxi City, I see. You’ve come from far away. I trust you wrote the address properly. Your father’s name is Geom Ujin—what a fine name. I don’t know if you’re aware, but we have branches in every province. We’ll soon confirm what you wrote and receive word back.”

“Do that.”

“I’ll say this in advance: don’t think about running away without paying back the money. Your family will have to pay it back in your place. I’m saying we’ll go find your father.”

“My father has plenty of money.”

He left the place together with Bian Zhong. Many men wearing blades were coming and going all around the courtyard. It was a fairly large-scale Yama lending house.

Once they left, Bian Zhong asked the question he had been holding back.

“Why on earth did you come here?”

He had worried that there might be a bloody fight. He had been worried, and he had also hoped for it.

He had hoped this young hero would draw his sword, sweep them all away, and say, “From now on, you have no debt.” It was selfish and worldly of him, but honestly, that was how he had felt.

“Didn’t I say I came to borrow money?”

“You came just to borrow ten taels? Young hero… don’t you not need that money?”

Considering how Geom Mugeuk had spent money on the way here, it made no sense that he would borrow ten taels.

“I do need it.”

It was not solely because of the Yama lenders that he had come. He intended to begin his secret inspection of the Jiangxi Branch from here. Corruption was always connected to money.

Far behind Geom Mugeuk, two men attached themselves to their trail. It was an open tail, meant to show they were being watched and that they should not think of doing anything else.

“If they lend ten taels and use two men to watch you, isn’t that a loss for them instead?”

Geom Mugeuk showed him the ten taels in his palm and asked in return,

“This isn’t ten taels, is it?”

Bian Zhong let out an exclamation. He had understood what Geom Mugeuk meant.

Starting with these ten taels, they would insert a tube and suck blood for a lifetime. That was why the money borrowed just now was not ten taels. It was hundreds, thousands of taels. Just as he himself was suffering now.

“Let’s see how they collect these ten taels.”

Chapter 293: Where the Man Named Geom Woojin Lives.
---------------------------------------------------
“Let’s have a meal together before we part ways.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s suggestion, Pyeonjung stepped back, saying there was no need.

“I’m fine.”

“We ate together every day on the way here. Would it not be strange to skip it on the last day?”

It would be strange indeed. But Geom Mugeuk was already tugging at his sleeve cuff.

“Which inn around here has good food? Let’s go there.”

Thus, led by Geom Mugeuk, the two walked down the main street. Having met a good customer, his mouth would feast until the very last day.

As they were walking, a fierce-looking man blocked Pyeonjung’s path and held out his hand.

Pyeonjung handed over two-thirds of the money he had earned this time.

The man who took the money left immediately. No greeting, no curses, no threats. Geom Mugeuk found this even more heartless. Before any emotion like anger or sorrow could be felt, the hard-earned money had vanished.

Walking a few steps further, Pyeonjung sighed and spoke.

“At first, I protested that it was unfair and resisted.”

A difficult past followed.

“I was even beaten in front of my wife and children. I bore that, too. But when that bastard put a knife to my son’s neck, I gave up everything.”

Now, with a resigned heart, he endured each day by saving more and working harder.

Pyeonjung glanced at Geom Mugeuk. Geom Mugeuk said nothing.

“It’s right over there. That place has the best cooking.”

Entering the inn, Geom Mugeuk secured a room to stay for a while, then ate with Pyeonjung on the first floor. He ordered the dish the inn did best, and they ate together.

‘He has so much money, so why did he borrow ten nyang? He’s only going through unnecessary troubles.’

Pyeonjung spoke cautiously.

“If you are thinking of doing something to Tongjeonso, please do not. They are not ordinary bandits. Their organization is large. There is nowhere in the Central Plains they do not reach.”

Geom Mugeuk refilled his wine cup.

“You need not worry about me.”

As always, Geom Mugeuk silently ate and drank, and Pyeonjung also quietly ate his food.

After they finished eating, Geom Mugeuk bade farewell.

“You must be tired, so go and rest. Thank you for driving the carriage all the way here.”

Geom Mugeuk called for the innkeeper. There was a separate reason he had insisted on bringing him here. The innkeeper brought out a packaged dish.

“I asked for food that children would enjoy, so take it and feed them.”

Geom Mugeuk had noticed that Pyeonjung fell into thought with every bite, realizing he must be thinking of his children.

Being cared for down to his children moved Pyeonjung’s heart.

“Why do you treat me so well?”

“It is my gratitude for bringing me here safely.”

Pyeonjung choked up. One meets good people and has lucky days in life, but he had never before met someone who showed such kindness.

After thanking him repeatedly, Pyeonjung headed home with both hands full of food. His steps quickened at the thought of how much his children would truly enjoy it.

Pyeonjung would think that the relationship which began as coachman and passenger ended upon reaching the destination, but the bond was not yet severed.

Watching him leave, Geom Mugeuk silently took out ten nyang from his bosom and looked down at it.

* * *

Deokchul was nervous.

Having spent three months as a Tongjeonso probationary investigator, he was finally going on his first official investigation.

‘Let’s do this splendidly and be acknowledged.’

An investigator’s job was to go to the debtor’s address and confirm whether their parents or family truly lived there. This task was more important than collecting money. Because if family was not held hostage, the debtor could run away.

The closer he got to the address, the more tense Deokchul became.

‘They say the Demon Cult headquarters is in this area. This place really is a demonic den.’

He had lost count of how many demonic ones emanating demonic energy he had met on the way here.

He was afraid, but he could not turn back. He had to do this job well to become an official investigator.

Deokchul checked the address written on the paper and walked on. He acted as naturally as possible. It was obvious that if he made a mistake for no reason in the demonic ones’ territory, he would not get out alive, so he walked leisurely, like someone who came here often.

Thus, Deokchul entered Magachon. As he passed through the marketplace, peddlers were selling various goods.

“We have many fine wares, come and see!”

Unable to refuse the goateed peddler’s solicitation, Deokchul was drawn in. He feared that if he refused, the peddler would twist his goatee and some retired demonic one would jump out asking, “Do you know who I am?”

The display had all kinds of things: from dolls carved from the Heavenly Demon Spirit to products related to the Demon Lords. The white mask of the Extreme Evil Young Demon was also hanging there.

‘These Demon Cult bastards are truly mad. To think they sell things like this.’

The Heavenly Demon Spirit doll was truly terrifying. If he saw it while half-asleep, he thought it might trigger convulsions. Come to think of it, had the person who made this even seen the Heavenly Demon Spirit? Thinking about it, how could someone who merely made and sold dolls have seen it?

Beside it stood the Eight Demon Lords dolls.

What caught his eye first was undoubtedly the Fist Demon doll. With a build twice that of others, its face was as impressive as the Heavenly Demon Spirit.

The goateed peddler quickly explained.

“This is the Fist Demon, sir. It is really well made to actual scale.”

His fist was the size of a human head, and he called it actual scale? Who did he take for a fool?

Deokchul set down the Fist Demon doll and examined another doll beside it. It had caught his eye because its appearance was the complete opposite of the Fist Demon.

“That one is the Poison King, sir. He really is handsome, is he not? Look at those twelve poison pouches there. Every single wrinkle was made with such care.”

Though the goateed peddler spat saliva as he introduced it, Deokchul inwardly doubted.

‘The Poison King is this young and handsome? No matter how no one’s seen the real thing, is it really okay to scam people like this?’

And what of the Ilhwa Sword Venerable doll? Clad in snow-white martial robes, it fully radiated the beauty of middle age. How could Deokchul believe it was the Sword Venerable when he did not even know the Sword Venerable was a woman?

‘They really made these haphazardly.’

What decisively fueled Deokchul’s disbelief was the Demon Buddha doll. About half the size of the other dolls, it glowed golden.

‘They cut the cost by making it half-sized.’

Moreover, how could such a golden glow come from a human body?

This time, Deokchul picked up the Blood Heaven Blade Demon doll with its well-captured crooked, stubborn gaze, then drew the Heaven-Destroying Great Sword he carried on his back. Oh, this is cool.

When he saw the Extreme Evil Young Demon doll with its arms crossed arrogantly while wearing a white mask, his heart chilled, and he ended up trying on the hanging mask for no reason.

The Soul-Seizing Demon Lord doll manipulating a puppet, or the Drunken Demon doll sprawled on a rock drinking wine, were also quite cool in their own way. They were all made to their own taste, if nothing else.

“If you buy all Eight Demon Lords at once, I’ll give you a thirty percent discount.”

“I’ll buy them next time.”

As he was about to leave, the goateed peddler blocked him. He had already noticed that Deokchul was a greenhorn from out of town.

The goateed peddler’s eyes said: You looked at all this and you’re just leaving? You even drew the Heaven-Destroying Great Sword and you’re not buying? You even tried on the mask? Really?

Feeling like he had to buy something or else, Deokchul picked up the Blood Heaven Blade Demon doll.

“I’ll buy this.”

Only then did the goateed peddler break into a wide smile.

Just as Deokchul, having been forced to buy the Blood Heaven Blade Demon doll, was turning around.

From up ahead, a golden radiance walked this way.

Deokchul squeezed his eyes shut and opened them again. He had not been seeing things. The real Demon Buddha was walking this way.

Deokchul looked at the dolls on the display stand.

‘He looks exactly the same!’

As the Demon Buddha walked past, he glanced at Deokchul. The Blood Heaven Blade Demon doll in Deokchul’s hand trembled.

‘Should I have bought the Demon Buddha doll?’

His heart pounded wildly. Surely he wouldn’t be killed for buying a different doll?

Just then, a voice came from the opposite direction.

“Welcome.”

Deokchul turned to see a young man standing there.

He was none other than Great Young Lord Geom Muyang.

“Great Young Lord.”

Deokchul held his breath.

‘Great Young Lord? Don’t tell me I’m standing at a meeting between the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult’s Great Young Lord and the Demon Buddha right now?’

Geom Muyang spoke to the Demon Buddha.

“The Demon Lord treated last time, so I’ll buy the drinks today.”

“Very well.”

The Demon Buddha walked with a bright smile. Rather, after Geom Mugeuk became the Young Cult Leader, the relationship between these two had improved.

The Demon Buddha had protected Geom Muyang to the end, though he had failed to become the Young Cult Leader. Having stepped away from power, a new friendship had begun between the two.

As the Demon Buddha passed in front of Deokchul, he glanced at him. Before Deokchul knew it, the goateed peddler had also thrust a Demon Buddha doll into his hand.

Having ended up buying the Demon Buddha doll as well, Deokchul hurriedly escaped that place.

Dawdling near the Demon Cult. He must be crazy wanting to die. Come on, let me check quickly and go back.

“Pass Magachon, walk five hundred paces west, turn the corner of Rock Mountain…”

When he arrived there, Deokchul’s mouth fell wide open. He checked the paper he was holding again.

‘It should be right here?’

Deokchul raised his head and looked at the scene before him.

The place where magnificent buildings stretched endlessly and giant evil ghost statues stood tall was the main headquarters of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. He had never been here before either. He checked the address written on the paper again, but indeed, it was this place.

‘What the hell!’

What kind of madman writes down this place as his address after borrowing money?

‘Ah! No way. Could it be someone who works at the Demon Cult headquarters?’

If so, he should go and check if the person written here was there.

Go to the main gate and ask, “Does a man named Geom Woojin live here?” And then they’d say, “We have so many people living here, why ask such a thing?” and cut his throat in one blow?

‘Ah, what should I do? It’s my first official mission!’

As Deokchul was clutching his head in worry beneath the huge wall of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, two people were walking toward him from afar.

‘If they look like good people, I’ll ask them.’

But they were people he could never ask.

The two were none other than Fist Demon and I-an, who had just returned to the headquarters.

As the gigantic frame approached, Deokchul realized where he had seen this man before. Where had he seen that build? The moment he recalled, the doll the peddler had sold came to mind. That doll whose fist was the size of a human head.

‘It’s the Fist Demon!’

Startled out of his wits, he stepped aside and bowed his head deeply. The two continued their conversation as they walked.

“Father.”

“What is it?”

Father and daughter? No way! Ah! She must have been kidnapped. Damn demonic bastards.

“I just called for no reason.”

“How dull.”

“Father!”

“Stop fooling around!”

Surely she wasn’t calling that scary man because she liked him. Could she be asking me for rescue?

I’m sorry, young lady! There are probably only a few people in the entire martial world who could rescue you from the Fist Demon!

Trying to steal another glance at the woman, he inadvertently raised his head and locked eyes with the Fist Demon.

It was truly the most terrifying face he had ever seen in his life. His knees buckled and all the hair on his body stood on end at once.

The terrifying doll the peddler had sold had not done the real Fist Demon justice. The real thing was far more frightening.

The dolls in his hands were the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and the Demon Buddha.

‘I should have bought the Fist Demon doll!’

In the meantime, the two passed in front of Deokchul.

Just as Deokchul was letting out a sigh of relief and turning around, this time an old man holding a massive blade walked toward him.

Deokchul looked at him, then looked again at the doll in his hand. They were identical.

‘Blood Heaven Blade Demon?’

Deokchul truly felt like screaming.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon looked at Deokchul with his crooked, stubborn gaze and abruptly spoke.

“What are you doing there?”

Deokchul was startled out of his wits at the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s question.

He had not expected the Blood Heaven Blade Demon to speak to him. Not knowing what to answer, Deokchul hesitated, then held out the Blood Heaven Blade Demon doll he was holding.

‘Please spare me! I bought your doll too.’

Just then, a voice came from above his head.

“I’m waiting for my drinking buddy to return.”

Turning around, he saw a man sitting on the high wall, drinking wine. Deokchul understood. The question had not been directed at him, but at the man on the wall.

The man on the roof was the Drunken Demon.

“I ran out upon hearing that you two had returned, but the person I wanted to see hasn’t come.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon walked on again.

“Don’t you know? He’s someone who sticks his nose into all sorts of worldly affairs.”

Then the Drunken Demon looked off into the distance where the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had come from.

“I know. Isn’t that why I miss him all the more?”

“If you miss him that much, stop drinking and go find him.”

In the past, he would have told him to stop acting like a drunkard, but the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had grown much more lenient toward the Drunken Demon as the three of them spent more time together with the Ilhwa Sword Venerable.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon walked toward the main gate of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, and the Drunken Demon drank again.

Deokchul did not know how he got out of there. He ran and ran, dashing into some tavern in Magachon.

“Give me some wine.”

He ordered wine the moment he sat down. His throat was dry and his legs trembled. Yet he could not simply leave. If he returned without even confirming his first official mission, his probationary period could be extended.

The one who brought the wine was Jo Chunbae. The tavern he had dashed into was a pleasure tavern.

As he set down the wine, he spoke in a low voice.

“I took your order because you seemed to be in a hurry, but we have an honored guest present now, so you must drink quietly.”

Deokchul gulped down the wine. Honored guest or not, he had to save himself first. His heart had been trembling so much he thought he might die.

As Jo Chunbae was turning to leave, Deokchul asked.

“Proprietor, do you know where a man named Geom Woojin lives around here?”

In an instant, silence flowed through the tavern.

Everyone’s gaze turned to Deokchul, then to the second floor of the tavern. A man sat there with his back turned, drinking wine.

Judging by the surrounding reaction, Deokchul quickly asked.

“Is that him?”

Jo Chunbae spoke with a trembling voice.

“It is indeed him, but… I don’t think he is the one you are looking for.”

“That’s what everyone says.”

It was exactly what he had seen and heard daily during his probationary period. I don’t know such a person. He is not my child. We lost contact long ago. What do you mean he isn’t?

Without giving him time to intervene, Deokchul hurried up to the second floor.

‘There’s no law saying I’ll die.’

To think he would meet Geom Woojin here.

‘It is the will of Heaven for me to become an official investigator!’

Deokchul approached Geom Woojin, who was drinking alone on the second floor, and held out the paper as he asked.

“Are you the one who lives at this address?”

Chapter 294: Why Didn't You Buy My Doll?
----------------------------------------
Geom Ujin raised his head and gazed steadily at Deokchul, then checked the address written on the paper.

“This is my house, all right.”

“Ah, then I’ve come to the right place.”

Deokchul smiled and sat down across from him. He had become the first ordinary person ever to sit alone with the Heavenly Demon, but he had no idea how extraordinary this moment was.

“Your son is Geom Mugeuk, correct?”

From now on, this was Deokchul’s mission. He had to coax and cajole him with kind words, get him to confirm that he was the boy’s parent, and secure his signature as guarantor.

“Your son borrowed money. So, sir, you’ll need to stand surety for him.”

For a moment, silence flowed through the tavern. Jo Chunbae, the customers on the first floor, and even the guards concealed nearby were all sharing in this astonishing moment in history.

The next instant, Geom Ujin burst into loud laughter.

Jo Chunbae, who was downstairs, was startled. It was the first time today he had seen the Cult Leader laugh.

Even Hwi, the personal guard hiding nearby, had not seen the Heavenly Demon laugh so pleasantly in a very long time.

Still with laughter lingering on his face, Geom Ujin said,

“I have never had a son who would make his father stand surety for his debt.”

As if he had expected that reaction, Deokchul rose from his seat, then quietly moved over and sat beside Geom Ujin.

Jo Chunbae, watching, clenched his fist in alarm. The other customers’ hearts pounded at what was about to happen.

Please, let his severed head not roll down to the first floor!

Everyone knew it except the man himself. He alone had no idea how dangerous a moment he was in.

Deokchul poured Geom Ujin a drink and consoled him.

“Everyone says that. But what can you do? He may be a wretch of a son, but you still have to pay it back for him.”

The one great mercy was that he did not pat Geom Ujin on the shoulder.

Don’t touch his body!

Jo Chunbae prayed desperately.

“I’ll be honest with you. If your son can’t pay back the money, people will come looking for you, sir. The people who collect debts are frightening people, so you need to be careful.”

Geom Ujin seemed to be enjoying this situation he was experiencing for the first time in his life.

“There’s no need to worry about me.”

“Then please read this first. Take your time reading it, and then you can sign.”

After placing the document in front of Geom Ujin, Deokchul rose from his seat as if giving him a moment to read.

“Huh?”

The words written on the wall entered his eyes. It was the wall where the Demon Venerables had left their sayings and signatures.

“A lot of famous people have visited.”

Starting with Poison King was here, there were traces of the Golden Great Arhat Art left by the Demon Buddha, and beside it was a line from the Blood Heaven Blade Demon as well.

Hyeolcheon, drank with his disciple and left.

“Ooooh! Even the Blood Heaven Blade Demon left something!”

He looked down at the Blood Heaven Blade Demon doll hanging at his waist. Since he had seen the man in person today and even bought his doll, he felt an odd intimacy, as if someone he truly knew had left those words behind.

Then one signature caught his eye. A hand more powerful than any writing in this place.

Heavenly Demon, Geom Ujin.

Deokchul was so startled that he jumped.

“Oh! The Demon Cult Leader came here too. Waaah! Did you see this?”

Deokchul looked around.

The people on the first floor were staring at him with anxious eyes. When had he ever received this much attention from others? Deokchul felt needlessly nervous and pleased.

“The cook here must be very skilled.”

After saying it loudly toward downstairs, he added in a voice only Geom Ujin could hear,

“Well, real famous eateries are always shabby places like this, aren’t they?”

Then he spoke loudly again toward Jo Chunbae on the first floor.

“A place the Demon Cult Leader visited! You could even change the name of this tavern to The Tavern the Heavenly Demon Visited. If it were me, I’d change it at once!”

Jo Chunbae shook his head with an expression that said, Please, stop!

Then Deokchul flinched and looked back at the wall with the writing on it.

“Huh? Come to think of it, the Demon Cult Leader’s name is Geom Ujin too.”

Deokchul looked once at Geom Ujin, then at the wall.

Jo Chunbae, along with the people on the first floor, waited expectantly.

At last, he’s realizing who the other party is! Hurry up and beg for forgiveness!
