Deokchul seemed to be staring at Geom Ujin.

“You must be uncomfortable sharing the same name as the Demon Cult Leader.”

A soundless sigh spread like a wave across the first floor.

Deokchul had not the slightest thought that the man before him might be the Heavenly Demon.

“Now, please sign. You’ll have to do it anyway. What parent can ever beat their child?”

“I have no such son.”

“I’m telling you, if you don’t, your son will be dragged off by frightening people.”

“To do that, they would have to be more frightening than the people written on that wall.”

Of course, Deokchul took it as a joke. The most frightening people in the world were all there, so anyone more frightening could only be seen after death.

“I understand. That’s right. Raise children and they all think they grew up on their own. Now, now, hold this brush. You can sign here. Only after I have a child myself and suffer the same thing will I think, Ah, so this is how my parents felt. Don’t agonize over it, just write your three characters here.”

At that, Geom Ujin signed the document as if he had no choice.

“You did well. This is all for your son’s sake.”

Deokchul, looking down at the document, flinched again.

Jo Chunbae and the customers on the first floor waited in anticipation once more.

Yes, compare it to the writing on the wall! Turn your head! Please!

Was everyone’s wish granted? Deokchul slowly turned and looked at the wall.

His eyes widened. Just when it seemed he had finally realized the Heavenly Demon’s identity,

“It’s not just the name that’s the same, the handwriting is the same too! Wow, how fascinating!”

An “Good grief” escaped Jo Chunbae’s mouth on its own. The customer beside him lost strength in his hand and dropped the chopsticks he had been holding, while the customer beside him clutched his head, sighed, and spilled his drink.

“Surely, your face isn’t similar too, is it?”

In Deokchul’s mind, the very possibility of meeting the Heavenly Demon in a place like this simply did not exist.

“Be careful. At this rate, you might be misunderstood as impersonating the Heavenly Demon.”

Just then, someone entered the tavern.

At his appearance, everyone on the first floor stopped breathing.

The man strode forward and came up to the second floor.

Deokchul, who had glanced that way without thinking, greeted him with a happy bow. He had done it instinctively, thinking the man looked familiar, then started in fright and let out a short scream. He did know him. The man who had come upstairs was none other than the Fist Demon.

The Fist Demon remembered seeing Deokchul earlier beneath the wall of the main headquarters. That was why the look in his eyes was exactly this:

Who are you, and why do you keep appearing before my eyes?

Jo Chunbae and the customers watching were once again engulfed in tension. Now the danger had doubled.

The Fist Demon greeted Geom Ujin respectfully.

“I heard you were here, so I came to see you. I have just returned.”

“Did you have a good trip?”

“Yes, Cult Leader.”

At the words Cult Leader, Deokchul blurted without realizing it,

“That man isn’t the Cult Leader, he just resembles him!”

Sighs erupted again from the first floor.

Geom Ujin laughed, and the Fist Demon wore an expression of utter disbelief.

Why, exactly?

Deokchul blinked at the reactions around him, and his expression gradually changed. Thinking about it, there was no way the Fist Demon would fail to recognize the Cult Leader and come to someone who merely resembled him, calling him Cult Leader.

The reaction of the lookalike was the same. Did you have a good trip?

Why would he say something like that? Why else? Because he wasn’t a lookalike, but the real one.

By the time Deokchul’s thoughts reached that point, his face had already gone deathly pale. He let out a low groan and trembled all over like an aspen leaf.

The Fist Demon asked Geom Ujin,

“Who is that man?”

“He came to tell me I must stand surety for my son’s debt.”

The Fist Demon stared at Deokchul with a dumbfounded expression.

“No! Absolutely not! There was some kind of mistake. I was wrong! Please spare me!”

He threw himself flat on the floor. In the moment it was revealed that the other party was the true Heavenly Demon, he achieved the astonishing record of seeing four Demon Venerables and even the Heavenly Demon in a single day.

Then Geom Ujin suddenly asked,

“Why did you not buy my doll?”

Since there was no Cult Leader doll to begin with, he was asking why Deokchul had not bought the Heavenly Demon Soul doll. It was truly the greatest ordeal Deokchul had suffered today. Jo Chunbae and the customers on the first floor grew tense again.

On top of that, even the Fist Demon joined in.

“And my doll?”

Deokchul looked down at the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and Demon Buddha dolls tucked at his waist. That enormous blade and golden radiance stood out far too clearly.

Ah! So in the end, today I should have bought the Heavenly Demon Soul doll and the Fist Demon doll!

Was he going to die because of a single moment’s choice? Goat Beard, I won’t forget you even in death.

Deokchul had no idea how to answer. In the end, he said in a crestfallen tone,

“Your dolls were too frightening, so I couldn’t buy them.”

His choice to answer honestly was excellent. Geom Ujin laughed, and the Fist Demon laughed along with him. The reward for making the Heavenly Demon laugh so heartily after such a long time was something he had never imagined.

Geom Ujin took a piece of paper from his robe and flicked it out. It drifted slowly through the air and entered Deokchul’s hand.

Amazingly, it was a note worth one thousand nyang.

“On your way, buy one of my dolls and one of this friend’s dolls.”

So shocked, Deokchul’s eyes went wide. Soundless astonishment spread through the first floor as well.

“You don’t want to?”

“What? No! I’ll buy them! I’ll buy them right away.”

Deokchul, who had wandered in misunderstandings all day long, grasped the other party’s intention accurately at this final moment.

“I’ll quit this work at once, go down to my hometown, and live honestly! With the money you have granted me, I’ll buy land, farm it, and live serving my parents dutifully.”

Having given the correct answer, Deokchul flew through the sky. Not his heart, but his body truly flew.

With one wave of Geom Ujin’s hand, Deokchul floated up and landed lightly on the first floor.

Admiration burst out from the first floor. All day long, he had received only pitying sighs, but at the end, he won everyone’s envy.

Jo Chunbae said with a good-natured smile,

“I’ll have to get your signature too. I think I should leave you here as the luckiest man in the world.”

“Thank you truly for accepting me as a guest. Someday, I will definitely come again!”

With tears brimming in his eyes, Deokchul bowed respectfully toward the second floor, then left the place.

I once worked at a Tongjeonso when I was young. I went out on my first assignment then, you see... He had spent a day no one would ever believe.

Jo Chunbae also looked up at the second floor. Though Deokchul had committed rudeness several times, the Cult Leader had bestowed mercy upon him. Beyond that, he had even opened a new life for him. Jo Chunbae felt sincere respect for the Cult Leader.

And more than anything, he was overjoyed that the Heavenly Demon had laughed loudly today.

Jo Chunbae wrote today’s date on the wall behind the counter, then left a line.

The day the Cult Leader laughed heartily.

In all his life running a tavern, today was a day he would never forget. It was the first time he had ever felt this good.

On the second floor, the conversation continued between the two manliest men in the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. Because the Fist Demon was the person Geom Ujin trusted the most, their conversation was comfortable and natural.

“You seem pleased.”

“Would there ever be a day in my life when I become indebted to a creditor?”

“When one is with Mugeuk, one experiences things one could never have imagined. For example, suddenly gaining a foster daughter one never expected.”

The Fist Demon had come to inform the Heavenly Demon of this fact before anyone else.

“I have decided to take Ian, who was Mugeuk’s guard, as my foster daughter.”

Geom Ujin was not easily surprised by most things, but this news surprised him.

Geom Ujin filled the Fist Demon’s cup to the brim.

“Congratulations.”

The Fist Demon emptied the drink in one refreshing gulp.

This time, the Fist Demon filled Geom Ujin’s cup.

“I gained a disciple and gained a daughter as well. In my late years, I have been blessed with much fortune.”

“A daughter is better than a son. Raise a son, and all you get is…”

Geom Ujin’s gaze turned far away, beyond the window.

Even so, today he missed, just a little… the son who would try to make his father stand surety for his debts.

* * *

Geom Mugeuk shut himself in his guest room and devoted himself solely to training in the Sicheon Secret Art.

Now, all his training was done unconditionally within the Sicheon Secret Art. Naturally, he could train for more time. The more time he gained, the more he could train, and the more time he gained again.

As he was training like that, Ju Yang of the Tongjeonso, who had lent him money, came to see him.

“In all my years doing this work, I’ve seen every kind of madman, but you’re the first person to write the Demon Cult Leader’s name as your father.”

When Deokchul left the Tongjeonso, he had reported the result of his first and last investigation as: a madman who wrote the Demon Cult Leader’s name. Naturally, there was no way the Heavenly Demon’s son would have used a King of Hell loan.

“You probably didn’t even meet the Demon Cult Leader, so how can you call it impersonation?”

“You may have been counting on that, but we did meet him.”

“My father?”

“They said he was drinking in a shabby tavern.”

This time, Geom Mugeuk laughed aloud. It was a laugh exactly like the one Geom Ujin had laughed at Pungnyu Tavern.

“Why are you laughing?”

“I laughed because I thought of my father.”

He pictured his father drinking alone at Pungnyu Tavern. When he thought of his father’s back, bearing everything on it, he missed him.

“Will you only come to your senses after you’re dragged to the Demon Cult, flayed, and thrown into a barrel of salt?”

“Do you have the courage to take me to the Demon Cult?”

Ju Yang’s eyes turned fierce.

“You’re treating us like mere louts running some King of Hell loan operation.”

“Are you not?”

A cold sneer formed at the corner of Ju Yang’s mouth.

Things have been dull lately, and now an amusing fellow like this appears.

His head filled with thoughts of how to crush this brat, but Ju Yang did not show those emotions. After all, the greatest pleasure was politely guiding someone into hell.

“I was told to reveal who my father was, so I revealed it. I was told to write an address, so I wrote one.”

Even at his insistence to the end, Ju Yang did not get angry. In any case, what would punish him was the clause written on the promissory note.

“You can be like that in the rashness of youth. I understand. Many people put down false addresses. In exchange, from now on, it is one hundred nyang.”

“One hundred nyang? Didn’t I borrow ten nyang?”

Ju Yang took the promissory note from his robe and showed it to him.

“Look here. Isn’t it written at the bottom of the note? If the address or family relationship is written falsely, the borrower must repay ten times the amount borrowed.”

“The letters are so small I can’t even see them. And the wording is too difficult.”

“Writing proper information on a promissory note is only basic, is it not? In any case, the money you borrowed is now one hundred nyang. Naturally, the interest will also be calculated as if you borrowed one hundred nyang.”

“Why do you keep saying I wrote falsely? I clearly wrote the truth.”

Ju Yang said in a gentle tone,

“What will you do if I bring a real member of the Demon Cult here?”

Clearly, he seemed to have something to rely on.

Asking directly would not get the answer he wanted. So Geom Mugeuk muttered softly, as if talking to himself.

“What member of the Demon Cult would have anything to do with someone who merely gives out King of Hell loans?”

Chapter 295: Can You Afford My Interest?
----------------------------------------
That was the one thing Juyang hated hearing most.

You’re nothing but peddlers of Hell King loans.

It was what people usually said when they looked down on them.

But even if everyone in the world said it, there was one person who absolutely must not. The very person who had come to borrow a Hell King loan.

“I said, if you’ve got a demonic practitioner, bring him here.”

Geom Mugeuk provoked him once more.

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk read Juyang’s emotions. What showed in his eyes and expression was this:

If I could really do as I pleased, I’d just—!

He clearly had someone on the Demonic Cult’s side he was relying on. Was it the Jiangxi Branch Leader? Or some other demonic practitioner? Tell me! Who is it you’re counting on?

But Juyang, who had dealt with all manner of debtors for many years, was no pushover.

He had a firm conviction when it came to tormenting others.

The Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult? What would be the point of bringing swordsmen to beat up a bastard who spouted such ridiculous lies? What plunged slippery bastards like this into hell was not violence or threats. It was giving them the suffocating reality of having to work for the rest of their lives to repay the money—of having no choice but to work.

“With the Demonic Cult Leader as your father, I suppose you can afford to be this bold.”

It was sarcasm, but Geom Mugeuk took it purely.

“You people had even greater nerve, going to see my father.”

“You intend to insist to the very end that you really are the Demonic Cult Leader’s son?”

“That’s right. I am the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult. I’ve told you clearly, so don’t say anything absurd later. How could I have believed that, would you have believed it if you were me? Let me say this clearly. If it were me, I would have believed it. My life is precious, after all.”

Of course, Juyang did not believe him. He had already come today after receiving word from the investigator on that side. This bastard was an unprecedentedly shameless man who had impersonated the Demonic Cult Leader as his father.

“Very well, our Young Cult Leader.”

“So you finally believe me.”

“Starting tomorrow, I’ll send someone every day to collect the interest.”

“Every day? Wasn’t it supposed to be collected once a month?”

“The rules have changed.”

Starting tomorrow, this hateful bastard’s life would change from shameless to threadbare.

“Can you change it so arbitrarily?”

“It’s written on the loan contract. The method of collecting interest can be changed however we please.”

After checking the clause written in letters as tiny as eye grit on the contract, Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

“Did you lend money to an ant? This is truly too much.”

“We’re acting according to the established rules. You should have read it carefully before signing.”

The poisonous clauses had truly been hidden with astonishing skill. This was how usurers sucked the marrow from people who had no money.

“For someone who is, after all, the Young Cult Leader, this much money should be easy to repay if you put your mind to it, no?”

“Can you really handle my interest?”

At that, Juyang smiled as if asking what that was supposed to mean, and asked back.

“Have you ever seen anyone who hates money?”

* * *

The next day, while Geom Mugeuk was eating at the inn, a vicious-looking man strode over and abruptly held out his hand.

“I’m here to collect the interest.”

When he looked up, it was the very man who had taken the money from Pyeonjung before. The very bastard who had beaten Pyeonjung in front of his wife and children, and had even held a dagger to the child’s throat.

Among the customers eating nearby, a few set down their chopsticks, quietly paid their bills, and left. Those who remained turned their eyes away, avoiding his gaze. Just from that, one could tell how this bastard usually behaved.

“My nickname is Mad Dog. Then you should know very well what to do from now on. When you see me walking over from a distance, take out the interest in advance. Got it?”

This Mad Dog, who knew how to torment people and enjoyed tormenting them, was the hardship Juyang had bestowed upon him.

Geom Mugeuk looked up at him and asked.

“But aren’t you afraid? Your opponent is a martial artist wearing a sword.”

Mad Dog was carrying a blade, but he was not a martial artist with internal energy.

“Why? Because you’re some pathetic martial artist, you’re going to draw that sword and kill me? Go on, kill me! Then you and your family are finished that very day. You think our organization will let you live? We start by tearing your family limb from limb.”

As if to make his attempt at intimidation meaningless, Geom Mugeuk said with a smile.

“Then you won’t even be able to start.”

Mad Dog’s face hardened fiercely. At that, his face truly looked like that of a savage dog.

“They said you were a bastard lost in the delusion that you’re the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult. Looks like it’s true.”

Mad Dog threw the bowl of food Geom Mugeuk had been eating from onto the floor and shattered it. A sense of crisis flowed through the place, as if a fight might break out at any moment.

The innkeeper and the waiters watching all wore looks of pity. They knew better than anyone that no matter how kind and diligent a person was, once they got tangled up with the Tongjeonso, they could never return to their original life.

And of all people, Mad Dog was in charge of him? There was almost no one in the village who had not been beaten by that Mad Dog, so violently did he go around throwing his weight about.

“Young Cult Leader. What will you do now?”

“What else? He broke the Young Cult Leader’s rice bowl, so he must be punished. Now, when I send a signal, the Demon Venerables outside over there will make their entrance.”

At the words Demon Venerables, not only Mad Dog but everyone in the inn flinched. There was a primal fear carried by that title.

“Not there. Look over there.”

Mad Dog’s gaze followed where Geom Mugeuk was pointing. The customers in the inn looked that way as well.

“One, two…”

Geom Mugeuk began counting as though about to send the signal. At that, Mad Dog tensed for an instant. Surely they wouldn’t really appear?

Just then, as a tension flowed through the room as though someone truly might appear—

“Three!”

But no one appeared. Everyone’s eyes turned to Geom Mugeuk.

“You believed that? You think the noble Demon Venerables would come here to deal with the likes of you?”

With the tension released, Mad Dog was dumbfounded. The fact that he had been tense even for a moment made him furious to the tips of his hair. Just as he was about to spew curses, Geom Mugeuk took money from his robes.

“Here, here is the interest!”

At his courteous attitude, Mad Dog’s urge to explode in anger subsided. Yet for some reason, he felt as if he was being swept along by the other man.

You bastard, I’ve got my eye on you now.

“You should have done that from the start. A young bastard like you, not knowing how scary money is.”

Geom Mugeuk dropped a coin onto his palm.

It seemed as if the coin fell lightly.

Whoooosh.

Bang!

The moment the coin landed on his palm, it was too heavy to withstand. The coin crushed down on his hand and slammed it into the ground.

“Aaaaargh!”

From the impact, the bones in Mad Dog’s hand shattered and were crushed.

He tried to use his other hand to lift the coin away, but the coin did not budge.

“It hurts, it hurts! Someone get this off me! Aaaaagh!”

His hand hurt terribly. The weight grew heavier and heavier, to the point where it felt as if a hole would truly be punched through his palm.

“There’s still interest left. Give me your other hand.”

Mad Dog looked up at Geom Mugeuk while lying face down. In that instant, he understood. There was no way a coin could be this heavy, so Geom Mugeuk had used some sort of trick.

Mad Dog said in a trembling voice.

“Please spare me.”

The dog that had been incomparably savage until just now had turned into a puppy that looked ready to burst into tears at any moment.

“No! You said if I didn’t pay the interest, you’d go looking for my father. Take it.”

Mad Dog tried to flee, but because of the hand pinned by the coin, his body would not move.

An unknown force compelled his other hand forward.

“Uuuugh! Please spare me!”

“What are you talking about? I’m telling you to take the interest.”

He lightly placed a coin on the other hand.

Whoooosh!

Bang!

This time as well, unable to withstand the weight of the coin, his hand was slammed into the floor. An even louder scream burst from Mad Dog’s mouth.

Mad Dog ended up flattened on the floor as if bowing, both palms pressed to the ground. Those watching looked at one another. Unconcealable joy flickered across their faces. Truly, even for someone throwing his weight around, the bastard had gone too far.

“Uuuugh. It hurts so much.”

Geom Mugeuk tilted back his cup of wine, using his screams as a side dish.

“That’s right. You should know how scary money is.”

* * *

Upon hearing the news, Juyang rushed to the inn with his hired blades.

He had expected that Geom Mugeuk would not hand over the money meekly, but he had not imagined an incident would occur from the very first interest payment.

“If you all swarm over here just to collect two coins, aren’t you taking a loss?”

Pretending not to hear Geom Mugeuk, Juyang first kicked Mad Dog, who had collapsed unconscious on the floor, to wake him.

“What are you doing?”

When Mad Dog came to, the coins on his palms had become light.

“Uuuugh. My hands hurt so much.”

Juyang examined Mad Dog’s hands. The bones in both hands were crushed, and he would no longer be able to live normally.

“Please treat my hands first.”

“It’s too late. You’ll have to live as a cripple for the rest of your life.”

At that, Mad Dog let out a monstrous cry.

Juyang slapped Mad Dog across the face without mercy.

“Get a hold of yourself! What exactly happened? Were they smashed with stones? Or trampled underfoot?”

“It’s just that the coins were too heavy, so I couldn’t lift them.”

“What are you talking about? Were you dreaming?”

If he had simply been beaten, that would be one thing, but he could not understand the claim that the coins had become heavy at all.

Juyang glared at Geom Mugeuk.

“What trick did you pull?”

“Trick? He came to collect the interest, so I gave it to him. Then he rolled around on the floor by himself, making a huge fuss about how much it hurt.”

On top of that, Geom Mugeuk added another spoonful.

“Do you people commit fraud and extortion too? Trying to squeeze money out of me with this kind of stunt? How much will you raise it from one hundred taels this time?”

Juyang calmly asked the innkeeper and the waiters what had happened. They answered exactly as Geom Mugeuk had said.

“He gave him the coins, and then he rolled around on the floor saying it hurt.”

It was not something only one or two people had seen.

Juyang could tell that Geom Mugeuk had done something with the coins. After all, he had never in his life heard of coins becoming so heavy that one could not move.

“Do you know who we are, acting like this?”

When Juyang lowered his voice, Geom Mugeuk imitated him.

“Do you know who I am?”

But he soon spoke playfully.

“Ah, right, you do know, don’t you? That I’m the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult.”

Juyang’s expression hardened. He had thought him a madman lost in delusion.

‘So he’s someone with a move up his sleeve, is that it?’

As the hired blades tried to carry Mad Dog out, Geom Mugeuk picked up what had fallen on the floor.

“You have to take the interest with you.”

Geom Mugeuk held out the two coins Mad Dog had dropped again.

Because of what he had heard, Juyang did not dare extend his hand.

“It’s the interest you like so much, isn’t it? You’re not going to take it?”

There were many eyes watching nearby. If they returned like this, the authority of the Tongjeonso would suffer greatly. In particular, the head of the Tongjeonso was a man who believed that if a debtor ever looked down on them, the business was finished. It was obvious that the thunderbolt would fall on Juyang himself.

Juyang gave a look to one of the hired blades beside him. The man who had been chosen stepped forward with a reluctant expression.

“Now, here comes the interest!”

At the very moment Geom Mugeuk dropped the coin—

Whoooosh!

Bang!

His hand, too, was slammed into the floor. This time as well, he could not withstand the weight of the coin.

“Aaaaaaargh!”

At the pain of his bones being crushed, the hired blade screamed. Another hired blade rushed in and struggled to lift the coin away. But the coin did not budge.

Seeing this with his own eyes, Juyang was startled out of his wits. It was the first time in his life he had witnessed such a sight. This was no simple trick.

‘He was an expert!’

The hired blades all drew their swords at once. It was not because they intended to avenge their comrade, but because they were frightened and had drawn them reflexively.

Juyang raised his hand to stop them. With the opponent being an expert like that, there was no way mere Tongjeonso swordsmen could handle him.

Geom Mugeuk held out the remaining coin to him.

“You came to collect the interest, so why are you drawing swords instead of taking the money?”

Juyang asked in a trembling voice.

“What was your purpose in approaching us?”

There was no way an expert like this had borrowed money from them because he lacked ten taels.

“Purpose? I simply borrowed because I needed money, didn’t I? You were the one who increased it to one hundred taels, and you were also the one who said you’d collect the interest every day. If you hadn’t changed the rules, we wouldn’t have met today.”

Juyang could not refute him. It was indeed he who had created this situation.

“Now, take the interest.”

Geom Mugeuk held out the remaining coin.

Since the hired blade who had taken the coin before was screaming that he was going to die with his hand stuck to the floor, no one dared step forward to receive that money.

Juyang looked around at the hired blades, but no one stepped forward. They all avoided his gaze.

In the end, Juyang passed it off to the hired blade lying on the floor and screaming.

“That man agreed to take it, so give it to him.”

“Let’s do that.”

Geom Mugeuk strode over.

“Aaaaagh! Please! No!”

The coin fell, and the man’s other hand was crushed as well. The man, who had been screaming, fainted on the spot. Everyone was frightened by that merciless, unhesitating sight.

“How could you people, who carry swords around and do nothing but extort money, ever know? Money earned through labor is this heavy.”

Juyang instinctively knew that things had gone wrong—terribly wrong.

But this was not the end. Geom Mugeuk picked up the coin from the man’s palm.

“You said there’s no one who hates money, so why is everyone refusing to take it?”

Geom Mugeuk once again held the coin out in front of Juyang.

“You have to take the interest.”

The moment his eyes met Geom Mugeuk’s, goosebumps rose all over Juyang’s body. He had dealt with all manner of bastards until now, but this was the first time he had ever felt this frightened.

“Hold out your hand.”

Geom Mugeuk’s attitude had changed completely from before.

Juyang did not dare disobey. The fear that he would die if he refused dominated him.

Juyang’s outstretched hand trembled violently.

The coin fell into Juyang’s hand. Juyang squeezed his eyes shut, but fortunately, this time it was not heavy.

Opening his eyes, Juyang spoke urgently.

“I won’t take the interest, nor even the principal. Let’s consider it all repaid.”

As he hurriedly turned away, Geom Mugeuk spoke coldly behind him.

“Says who?”

When Juyang turned back, Geom Mugeuk had newly placed two coins on the table.

“Come collect the interest tomorrow too. If you don’t come, I’ll go find you.”

Chapter 296: Who on Earth Did You Lend Money To?
------------------------------------------------
Juyang, who had been on his way back to the Tongjeonso, stopped in his tracks.

His heart kept pounding as if it might burst at any moment.

The look in Geom Mugeuk’s eyes as he had stared at him rose in his mind, and the image of the coin on the table boring through his palm would not leave his head.

One of the swordsmen returning with him asked,

“Are you not going?”

“Go on ahead. And keep your mouths shut about what happened today.”

“Yes!”

Juyang sent the swordsmen on first and sat down for a while by the roadside. He had never experienced anything like this before, so he was angry, his pride was wounded, and he was afraid.

The work of the Tongjeonso was his calling.

He felt the thrill of becoming an absolute master when he saw people bow their heads to him and borrow money. When he coaxed them with sweet words, there truly was nothing more amusing.

And what about the sight of them weeping and begging because they could not pay a few coins of interest? He found comfort in other people’s misfortune.

This work, controlling and manipulating someone else’s life, was a source of such happiness to him.

But for the first time, he felt a sense of crisis shaking the life he had lived with such arrogance. More frightening than the fear of having his hand pierced was the fear that he might no longer be able to do this work.

‘How do I deal with this?’

He had to resolve it before word reached the ears of Jeokpae, the head of the Tongjeonso. If Jeokpae found out… Juyang shook his head. Jeokpae was a man who always made someone take responsibility for losses.

Geom Mugeuk might pierce his hand, but Jeokpae would pierce his throat. That was the kind of man he was.

Just then, his eyes met those of some villagers passing by. They flinched in surprise, bowed their heads in greeting, and hurried away. From their quickened steps, he could feel that they feared him.

Was punishment finally coming down for the evil deeds he had committed, living by ruining other people’s lives?

But Juyang was not the sort of person to reflect like that. If he had been, he would never have suffered something like this in the first place. He was the sort of person who thought this instead.

‘I have to get rid of that bastard somehow.’

Thinking of someone suited to the task, he sprang to his feet and started walking. And today, more than ever, he shot irritable glares at everyone he passed.

* * *

The next day, Juyang did not come to collect the money.

“I told him I’d come find him if he didn’t show up.”

Geom Mugeuk went straight to the Tongjeonso. Today, someone else was sitting in Juyang’s place, lending out money.

When he asked where Juyang was, he was told Juyang had fallen ill and had not come out.

Geom Mugeuk went out to the training yard and shouted.

“Juyang! I’ve come to give you your interest, so come out at once! Juyang! Master Ju! Great Hero Ju!”

At the commotion, the Tongjeonso’s swordsmen came out to watch. He had come to pay interest? A debtor who was usually busy running away whenever they met?

Since there had never been such a disturbance since the Tongjeonso was established, everyone watched with curiosity.

“Juyang! Come out at once!”

Upon hearing the news, Juyang hurried out of the building.

“You!”

Unlike yesterday, when he had treated him coldly, Geom Mugeuk looked at him warmly and smiled.

“They said you were sick, but you look perfectly fine.”

“Do you know where you are, making such a racket?”

“Did I not say I would come find you if you didn’t come?”

Even so, Juyang had not expected him to come all the way here and make a scene. Did he have any idea who might be here? This was a place that handled large sums of money. Did he think there was not a single expert here capable of killing him?

And so the very first thought that arose in Juyang’s mind was this. Setting aside how skilled he was, what his intentions were, and everything else.

‘This bastard is insane.’

Geom Mugeuk held out his hand to him.

“Here, take the interest.”

Otherwise, there was no way he could do this.

The swordsmen watching began to murmur. Those who had been at the inn yesterday retreated a step in tension, while the others chuckled, excited by the amusing spectacle.

It was just then.

“Is that him?”

A man with a heavy voice appeared.

He was dragging a blood-soaked man out with one hand.

When he appeared, the swordsmen all bowed their heads in greeting. He was Janhak, the one who could be called the true blade of this Tongjeonso.

Janhak was the person who stepped forward when trouble arose with martial artists, and he was also the first person Juyang had gone to meet after returning yesterday.

What kind of person he was could be explained by one simple fact. Everyone joked that his name, Janhak, was short for “brutal and inhumane.” There were even people who thought that was actually true.

That was how cruel Janhak was. Everyone who had exposed and opposed the evil deeds of the Tongjeonso had been killed by him.

“Yes, that is him.”

At Juyang’s answer, Janhak threw the man he had dragged out forward.

The man rolled limply across the ground.

Geom Mugeuk approached and examined him. The man, whose entire body was a wreck, barely opened his eyes.

“…Kill me.”

Those were his first words.

How terrible must the pain have been for him to say that as his first words, without even knowing who the other person was?

By contrast, Janhak, the one who had made him that way, was relaxed.

“At first, that bastard was just as full of spirit as you. He flew over that wall as if he had wings and landed right in the middle of the yard. Truly, a hero had descended.”

At that, the watching swordsmen burst into laughter.

“Standing there, he launched into a grand speech. Something about justice, something about chivalry. He treated us like money-grubbing insects, like trash. Not even knowing who the trash was.”

At that moment, the man reacted to the word trash.

“…I am trash. I am trash.”

It was a phrase Janhak had forcibly driven into him. The man was half out of his mind from the brutal torture.

Geom Mugeuk examined his body.

His dantian had been shattered, and the meridians of all four limbs had been severed, leaving him bedridden for the rest of his life. A body not even a divine physician could restore. The bastard had committed an act no human being should commit.

As Geom Mugeuk was examining him, Janhak approached without a sound. He had already heard from Juyang what the opponent had done with a coin yesterday. If what he had heard was true, then this man was quite an expert.

He thought he would first subdue him with a surprise attack, then play with him in place of that bastard for a while.

At this moment, while he was defenseless, his attention taken by that dying piece of trash.

‘Idiot! What is that little bit of sentiment, that little bit of righteousness worth? I’ll fix that mind of yours completely!’

Just as Janhak launched himself and was about to bring down a palm strike—

Piiing!

Thud.

A streak of wind, followed by the sound of something being pierced.

Bang!

Something embedded itself in the wall of the building behind him.

Craaaack.

The force of the impact was so great that cracks spread like a spiderweb around the point where it had struck.

When Juyang confirmed what had lodged there, he recoiled in shock.

What was embedded in the wall was a coin.

Blood trickled down from the coin, running along the wall.

‘Blood? Why blood? Don’t tell me?’

Juyang instinctively looked at Janhak.

Janhak was still standing exactly where he had been. He was touching his forehead, as though it itched. He seemed to be trying to say something, but he only muttered indistinctly, impossible to understand.

A moment later, his body slowly collapsed.

Thud!

Blood was flowing down from the forehead of the already-dead Janhak.

The coin had pierced Janhak’s forehead, passed through the back of his head, and flown on to lodge in the wall.

Everyone was horrified by his death.

How many times had they seen Janhak until now? How many martial artists had he killed and toyed with?

And yet he had died with a coin through his forehead, without even swinging his sword once? It felt so unreal that it seemed as if he had conspired with that young man to put on an act.

At Janhak’s death, tears welled in the eyes of the man who had been tortured. He had endured with one thought alone: that if he ever escaped, he would kill that bastard.

“I imagine you would want to repay him with the same torture, but he is not even worth that. Would that not mean treating that bastard like a human being?”

As always, Geom Mugeuk granted no value whatsoever to the death of an evildoer. Only that way could he face the endless stream of villains. Like moving a stone blocking the road to the side and walking on again.

But he granted value to this man. Though he was seeing him for the first time and did not even know his name, he understood him through the actions he had taken.

“No one else climbed over that wall for the sake of others. Not the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, not the Evil Dao Alliance, not even the Righteous Dao Alliance. They were all either uninterested, in collusion, or calling it an unavoidable necessary evil.”

Geom Mugeuk held the man’s hand tightly.

“You have lived a noble life. Thanks to you, many people will be freed from suffering.”

A faint smile formed on the man’s lips. With his eyes, he made his final request to be killed, and Geom Mugeuk accepted that request. It was a gesture of respect for him.

“May you go in peace, great hero.”

Geom Mugeuk placed his hand on the crown of the man’s head.

There was no pain in that touch. With a peaceful expression, the man breathed his last.

After laying him down as he was, Geom Mugeuk rose to his feet.

Those who had been chuckling just moments ago were all standing there, holding their breath.

It seemed as if he would rage like a storm and sweep them all away, yet Geom Mugeuk was calm. And that made him even more frightening to Juyang.

Geom Mugeuk held out the remaining coin to Juyang and said,

“There is one more left. Shall I give it to you? Or will you come and take it?”

Juyang understood that when the other man said he would give it to him, it meant another coin would be embedded in the wall.

“No. I will receive it.”

Juyang ran over and held out his hand. His heart pounded, and he felt dizzy.

In the tense situation where everyone was watching, Geom Mugeuk slowly placed the coin down.

This time, surely his hand might be smashed to pieces…

But today as well, the coin was placed lightly on his palm.

“…What is it you want?”

As if to say there was nothing he needed to tell the likes of him, Geom Mugeuk lifted the man’s corpse into his arms and strode away, speaking as he went.

“Make sure you come to collect the interest tomorrow.”

* * *

Juyang went straight to see Jeokpae, the head of the organization.

If he wanted to preserve his life, he had to go and report it himself before the report reached him through someone else. If he did not take the beating that came first, the beating that came later would take his life.

Jeokpae did not reside at the Tongjeonso. He did not consider that place safe, with countless people coming and going every day.

He lived in a manor built like a fortress in a remote place. Experts guarded him in layer upon layer, and deep within the inner compound was a palace of beauties. He was a man who knew very well how to spend the money he earned.

Juyang took a deep breath in front of Jeokpae’s residence and steadied himself. Having worked under Jeokpae for the past ten years, he knew well what kind of person he was. As far as Juyang knew, he was the most selfish man alive. He regarded other people’s lives as no more than the lives of flies. Perhaps that was why he had been able to make this business flourish.

A madman claiming to be the young cult leader of the Demon Cult turned out to be an expert.

If he made this report, Jeokpae would throw whatever was within reach at him. If he happened to be peeling an apple, a fruit knife would fly at him; if he happened to be painting, an inkstone would fly at him. He was the sort of man who threw the inkstone, not the brush.

Fortunately, Jeokpae was looking over the ledgers and flicking an abacus. He looked like a ferocious wolf, but when calculating money, he became a fox.

When he was calculating money, it was wise to keep one’s mouth shut.

The sound of the abacus beads stopped for a moment.

“An expert intervened, and there was an incident.”

Jeokpae was not particularly shaken. While running the Tongjeonso, had there only been one or two times when martial arts experts interfered? They had resolved them all somehow and overcome every crisis.

“A young man borrowed ten nyang.”

“Ten nyang?”

Jeokpae raised his head and looked at Juyang with that gaze. He could not have come to him over a mere ten-nyang problem.

“Because of that matter, Janhak died.”

Whoosh. Thwack!

The abacus flew and struck Juyang on the head. Unfortunately, the corner of the abacus hit him, and blood streamed down from his forehead.

“Did I not tell you to properly distinguish from the start the bastards who would become a problem?”

“I am sorry.”

“And after eating Yeomwangchae’s rice for so long, you could not even handle that properly.”

Repeating his apologies, Juyang bowed his head.

“What kind of bastard is he?”

“A madman claiming to be the young cult leader of the Demon Cult.”

Thwack!

Something else flew and struck Juyang in the face.

“How does a madman kill Janhak?”

Juyang lowered his head deeply again and said he was sorry. But it was not as though the young cult leader of the Demon Cult killed him, was it? He forced himself to swallow the words he wanted to say.

“How will you take responsibility?”

“I will accept any punishment.”

“You are certain he is not one of the Demon Cult’s men?”

“The investigator sent word that he is not. From what I have seen as well, he is not a demonic practitioner.”

Jeokpae gestured for Juyang to leave, as if he could not stand the sight of him. It was probably only ending at this much because Juyang had at least been running the Tongjeonso well lately without any mistakes.

Left alone, Jeokpae said in a low voice.

“Look into it yourself and take care of it.”

From behind the wall came a brief reply: yes.

Jeokpae took a new abacus from his desk drawer and began calculating again.

“Why won’t anything add up?”

When writing the ledgers, there were days when the numbers simply refused to match. Wrong, and wrong again. Today was one of those days.

* * *

The next day, at Jeokpae’s summons, Juyang once again hurried to his residence.

Jeokpae stood in the middle of the courtyard with a grave expression.

Before him lay a corpse. It was the very man who had gone out yesterday after receiving Jeokpae’s orders—one of his own hands and feet.

Juyang knew very well just how skilled he had been. He was a subordinate like Jeokpae’s right arm, one Jeokpae had spent an enormous sum to keep at his side, and he could have dealt with someone like Janhak with ease.

And Juyang also knew something else.

He knew who had killed him.

Two coins had been placed on the corpse’s forehead.

The coins seemed to be saying:

—I’ve paid today’s interest too.

Juyang’s heart sank. The fear he had felt until yesterday had not been fear at all. He felt a primal terror surging up from deep within his chest.

Juyang could not tear his eyes away from the coins.

“What are these coins?”

The two coins had now become the most terrifying things in the world.

“They are today’s interest.”

Jeokpae glared at Juyang with frightening intensity.

“You… just who did you lend money to?”

Juyang had expected Jeokpae to react this way. Whenever a problem arose, he was the sort of man who always looked for someone to blame.

You’re the one who sent a man to kill him!

Swallowing the words he wanted to say yet again, Juyang spoke.

“I will go and find out what he wants.”

“You still don’t know after seeing this? What he wants is our money. What other business would an expert like this have with us?”

Jeokpae was a man convinced that anyone who opposed him was after his money. Unexpectedly, that theory was often correct.

“You said he claimed to be a demonic practitioner?”

“He boasted that he was the young cult leader of the Demonic Cult.”

“You’re certain he isn’t?”

“He absolutely is not.”

Now that things had come this far, Juyang even began to think the investigator’s report might have been wrong.

But he still answered with the word “absolutely.” Because he absolutely had to be wrong.

In that case, Jeokpae’s solution was already decided.

“The real thing should deal with the fake.”

That was why he had spent so much money for a day like today.

“We’ll show him just how terrifying the real Demonic Cult is.”

Chapter 297: Why Me?
--------------------
Jeokpae, who had lost his subordinates, was not sad.

Dead was dead. If this was how he acted even after losing his right arm, there was no need to ask how he regarded him—it was obvious.

“Until I return, you must hold onto him.”

Because he knew Jeokpae’s personality well, Juyang understood. That if he let Geom Mugeuk slip, he would die. He already stood between life and death.

For now, the faithful task he had to carry out was going to collect the interest.

The next day, the blade hand who went to collect the interest came back with his hand ruined.

The day after was the same. He had hoped Geom Mugeuk might go easy on them on the second day, but Geom Mugeuk showed no mercy.

When they returned with ruined hands two days in a row, the blade hands of the Tongjeonso were shaken. Several suddenly fell ill and took to their beds, and several even hid their tracks claiming urgent business had suddenly come up.

Among the blade hands they cursed Geom Mugeuk as heartless, but Juyang thought the opposite. The opponent was showing mercy. If he were truly heartless, no one would have survived at the Tongjeonso.

*Why didn’t he kill everyone that day?*

When closing the eyes of the person who had been tortured by Jeokpae, Juyang had despaired, thinking, *Ah, we’re all going to be massacred now.* But Geom Mugeuk did not do so. There was clearly a reason he had kept them alive.

In any case, he had to go collect interest today as well.

“Today, I will go myself.”

At Juyang’s words, the blade hands who had been anxious about being chosen themselves brightened considerably. As a manager, there was a limit to blindly sending blade hands, so he stepped up himself.

Watching that not a single person offered to accompany him as a guard, he left the Tongjeonso with bitterness. Yes, he understood. He wouldn’t have followed himself either.

Geom Mugeuk welcomed him with a face even more pleased than his subordinates.

“Why did you come? Have your subordinates already run out?”

“They are all terrified.”

“And yet you are not one to worry about your subordinates?”

“What do you know about me?”

Geom Mugeuk stared at him intently.

“You are overflowing with confidence. Did you go call some great expert?”

Though struck in a vital point, Juyang tried hard not to show surprise.

*How quick-witted he is.*

He had been putting on an act of fear. How on earth had he noticed?

“It won’t be easy to find an expert greater than the one sent to kill me that day.”

Juyang could tell that the person Geom Mugeuk spoke of was Jeokpae’s right arm. He was right. That was why he had gone to ask the Demon Cult for help.

Instead of answering, Juyang threw out a question to change the subject.

“Why haven’t you ruined my hands?”

It was what he was most curious about. He had never particularly done anything good for him.

Geom Mugeuk took out two coins and placed them on the table.

“Because I may need those hands of yours.”

At those unexpected words Juyang was bewildered, but Geom Mugeuk said no more and went up to his room.

Juyang picked up the coins on the table.

He had been about to leave when he suddenly looked down at his own hands. He needed these hands? Why?

* * *

“It seems the Branch Leader has begun digging into the Chief’s background.”

At those words, a groaning sound burst out from beneath a boulder the size of a house.

Surprisingly, a person was shouldering that massive boulder. No matter how much internal energy he used, how could he... It was a sight that naturally evoked such thoughts.

Thud.

The boulder fell to the ground, and from behind it a man revealed himself and walked out.

He possessed a robust physique and a sharp impression. If forced to compare him to an animal, he was a tiger.

Demon Sword Chief Jungak.

One line was all it took to explain this man.

The Demon Sword Squad was the elite of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, a martial force deployed at the front-line Gangseo Branch in preparation for war.

Jungak, as one responsible for leading an elite organization, was a master possessing powerful martial arts and abundant real combat experience.

“Reporting such things while I’m holding this heavy thing—are you trying to kill me?”

The subordinate who had reported was Chahoe, first squad leader of the Demon Sword Squad and Jungak’s right-hand man.

“Don’t I need to serve as Chief once and then retire too?”

Jungak had a cold and resolute personality that even the martial artists of the Demon Sword Squad feared, yet the only person with whom he spoke without reserve was Chahoe. Their loyalty ran so deep that nothing was hidden between them.

“That human is investigating my background?”

“Yes, he has secretly dispatched people.”

By rank, the Branch Leader was higher than the Chief. But elite organizations like the Demon Sword Squad possessed authority that transcended rank. Therefore, in normal cases, they would maintain an appearance of mutual respect.

However, at the Gangseo Branch, this was not being maintained well.

“That person sure likes making enemies. Yet we’re the ones doing all the fighting.”

Jungak removed his sweat-soaked clothes and washed his body in the flowing water. His entire body was covered in scars. It was a body that made one feel what kind of history was required to become Chief of the Demon Sword Squad.

As he washed, he continued speaking.

“Wouldn’t it be nice to live amicably? Comfortably among people who are neither enemies nor friends.”

“Who knows what intentions lie behind that pretense of integrity?”

“No, that person is simply sick. Someone who can’t stand not acting superior.”

Having finished washing, he put on new clothes prepared in advance.

“So? Is there a problem?”

“I’ve muzzled them for now.”

Jungak was indeed a corrupt martial artist, as the Branch Leader suspected. He was secretly taking bribes from various sects within Gangseo.

On the other hand, the new Branch Leader who arrived last year had an upright personality and never tolerated bribes. Because of this, even more bribes were offered to Jungak. Jungak accepted every single one he was given.

“They say they’ll share overflowing money, yet I don’t know why they make life so difficult. Just take what those vermin give, pocket it well, and decide as I please.”

He actually did just that. He didn’t accept every request just because he took bribes. What he thought unnecessary he ignored, and what he deemed acceptable he granted.

The four characters that made it possible: Demon Sword Chief.

Of course, most of the bribes they offered were in preparation for things that had not yet occurred.

“We fight their anxieties for them, don’t we? How wonderful.”

At Jungak’s joke, Chahoe smiled faintly.

The problem was that this matter was not something to laugh off so lightly.

“As you know, he is not one to usually be picky, so if he starts digging like this, he will eventually find out.”

Jungak’s eyes gleamed coldly. The fact that he had resolved to investigate his background meant he was betting everything. At first, their relationship had been quite decent. Where had it gone wrong?

Jungak slowly walked to the boulder and drew his sword.

He swung lightly, as if cutting a twig.

Swhooooosh.

The massive boulder split in half.

At this astonishing display, Chahoe cupped his hands in respect.

“If only for the peace of the martial world….”

Jungak stroked the smooth cross-section of the boulder and added.

“The Branch Leader may change, but we must remain.”

And right then, as if by fate, a visitor arrived.

Another subordinate came and reported.

“A guest has arrived.”

* * *

The one waiting in the secret room was Jeokpae.

This was the place Jungak used for secret meetings.

Jeokpae too wore a bamboo hat and shabby long robes to hide his identity.

When Jungak entered, Jeokpae jumped up and greeted him respectfully.

“It has been a long time.”

“I believe I told you not to come before I called you.”

Jungak’s reaction was cold. He had always openly disdained Jeokpae, who ran the moneylender’s ring.

*Then you should have disdained my money too.*

Jeokpae hid such inner thoughts and prostrated himself flat.

“Would I have come like this if things were not desperate?”

“What is it?”

“Someone falsely claiming to be Main has intervened—a master we cannot handle.”

He deliberately omitted the part about impersonating the Young Leader of the Demon Cult. Given Jungak’s personality, if he had mentioned it, Jungak would have said they were telling him to face such a madman and would use it as an excuse to withdraw.

“Impersonating our cult is a grave crime. How great is his skill?”

“We were forced to flee all the way here. Please, help me!”

Under normal circumstances, with the Branch Leader already suspecting him, Jungak would have refused unconditionally. But a thought crossed Jungak’s mind.

“Understood. I shall handle it.”

“Thank you.”

Jeokpae was rather bewildered by how readily he agreed.

“However, there is one thing you must do.”

“Please command me.”

“See that word reaches the Branch Leader’s ears that there is someone falsely claiming to be Main. Such matters cannot be handled arbitrarily by me alone.”

Jeokpae understood.

*So because the opponent is a master, he won’t move himself but will use the Branch Leader to eliminate him. As cunning as ever.*

In any case, it didn’t matter. As long as the bastard was eliminated.

“I shall do as you command.”

In truth, Jungak was scheming a far more ruthless plan than Jeokpae anticipated.

The Gangseo Branch Leader dies while going to catch someone impersonating the Demon Cult? Would the Demon Sword Squad avenge him?

Would that work? If he had lived a life of doubt, he would never have reached his current position.

He would make it work.

This was the life Jungak had lived.

* * *

The next day too, Juyang came to find him.

“Our Mister Juyang, now you come alone boldly without fear.”

“Since you said at least that you need my hands, surely my hands can be kept safe?”

Juyang had been unable to sleep all night from curiosity.

“Why on earth do you need my hands?”

Then the most astonishing words Juyang had ever heard in his life flowed from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth.

“I am thinking of making you the new head of the Tongjeonso.”

Juyang shouted without realizing it.

“That’s absurd!”

Geom Mugeuk, on the other hand, was calm.

“Why do you think it’s absurd?”

“Well….”

There were many reasons. But they were reasons he had never once thought about or spoken aloud.

“Weren’t you going to kill me?”

The Geom Mugeuk he sensed was not one to leave villains be.

“Correct. I was originally going to kill you, but I found myself in need of a villain.”

Geom Mugeuk revealed his plan.

“Since you people are a necessary evil anyway, I intend to make you a truly proper necessary evil. From now on, the Tongjeonsos of the Central Plains will lend money at reasonable interest. For now it’s only the Tongjeonso, but someday I will change every money house in the Central Plains that way.”

Only now did he understand. Why he had kept him alive. Why he had left his hands intact. It meant he was to continue clicking the abacus with these hands.

The man before his eyes was truly dreaming an absurd dream. The words burst out unbidden.

“Just what are you?”

The answer he had heard several times came again.

“The Young Leader of the Demon Cult.”

“You are simply a madman!”

This was a man who had drilled a hole in someone’s forehead and shattered the hands of blade hands, yet he made Juyang shout like this. He kept making him lower his guard.

“In the end, even a loan shark hears that from me. If the Demon Venerables heard your words, they would roar with laughter. They would be beside themselves making fun of me for a while.”

Juyang shook his head. Either this was a truly proper madman… or he was putting on a performance before the real Young Leader of the Demon Cult.

“Do you think that is possible?”

“I wouldn’t know. You are the one who must take charge and carry it out.”

How could he not be bewildered by this?

“No matter how strong your martial arts are… you will die anyway.”

“Then help me.”

“What sort of Young Leader of the Demon Cult asks a loan shark for help?”

Perhaps because the joke sounded absurd even to himself, Juyang laughed alone then quickly returned to a serious expression. Associating with a madman, he was going mad too.

“Fine, assuming that’s true. Why me? You could appoint someone fitting, couldn’t you? Not someone like me, but someone without greed for money.”

This was what Juyang was personally most curious about.

“It is a place handling enormous sums, so that person too would eventually fall to corruption for money. On the other hand, you have nothing left to fall from. If you break your promise with me and act foolishly, it is also easy to kill you without any compunction.”

To think it was for such a reason.

“You go too far before a person.”

“You are mistaken if you think I am saving you. This will not be easy for you. You will have to manage the organization meticulously, and you will not be able to embezzle a single penny carelessly.”

This was the punishment Geom Mugeuk had handed down to him.

“Just as those who paid interest to you did, you too must work yourself to the bone until the day you die. While properly collecting, for the rest of your life, the interest you wrongly took.”

“If I refuse?”

“You will die together with Jeokpae on the day he dies.”

Geom Mugeuk took out two coins one by one and placed them on the table. His action seemed to say: This coin is Jeokpae’s; this one is yours.

At the sight of the coins, Juyang’s heart began to race madly again. Nowadays, perhaps his heart would burst and he would die before his forehead was pierced by those coins.

Was it because he feared death? Because of vanity at the possibility of becoming owner of the Tongjeonso? Because of the suffocation of having to live doing boring things for a lifetime? When with this interest, his heart kept raging like this.

As if listening to the sound of Juyang’s heart, Geom Mugeuk calmly asked.

“Are you afraid?”

“Would I not be?”

“Even if you fall into the sea, you can survive if there is just this much space to expose your nose and mouth. No matter how vast the sea is, if you have just this much.”

Geom Mugeuk curved his hand like a cylinder in front of his face.

“Think well whether that breathing hole for you is me, or whether it is your position as head. You must decide quickly. A great wave will soon come crashing down.”

Juyang looked down at the coins placed on the table. These two coins were trying to change his fate.

What were his feelings? He wanted to live. If he had to atone for a lifetime, he would rather choose death. But he lacked such spirit or courage.

He was someone who would soon be torn apart and killed once the real Demon Cult stepped in. So why did his heart keep wavering? Why did he want to say, even now, *I will follow your will, so please spare me!*? Why did he feel he had to?

Juyang pocketed the coins and spoke.

“That wave is crashing down on you first. So you had better find your own breathing hole.”

Chapter 298: You bastards! This person here is.
-----------------------------------------------
Hogyeong (護景), the Jiangxi Branch Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, was examining the documents stacked on his desk one by one.

The task Hogyeong spent the most time on each day was carefully reviewing the reports coming in from each branch office.

Just then, Jungak, the Demonic Sword Unit Leader, visited his office.

“Are you busy?”

“Welcome. What brings you here without notice?”

“I stopped by because I wanted to see the Branch Leader.”

“You’ve come at a good time.”

One was digging into the other’s back, while the other knew it full well, yet they exchanged greetings more warmly than usual.

Jungak spoke while looking at the piles of documents on the desk.

“So this is why the complaints about you are no small matter.”

“What do you mean?”

“Because you handle work so meticulously, there are complaints from those below that it’s hard on them. Please look after your subordinates’ morale too.”

Jungak said it like a joke, smiling, and Hogyeong replied calmly.

“Isn’t the reason most people suffer calamities because they fail to pay attention to small things?”

“As long as our Branch Leader is here, those Orthodox Alliance bastards won’t even dare think of provoking us.”

“How could that be thanks to me? It’s thanks to our Unit Leader and the Demonic Sword Unit.”

Their gazes intertwined in the air. Both were smiling with their mouths, but their eyes were not.

“By the way, have you heard the rumors about someone going around impersonating an official of our cult?”

“I have. I’ve already given orders to investigate the matter.”

“I also sent men to look into it, and it seems to be fact, not rumor.”

Hogyeong’s expression naturally grew grave. Impersonating the Divine Cult was a matter the main cult took very seriously.

“Do you happen to know who he is?”

“I don’t know their identity, but there is someone who can approach them.”

“If you provide the information, I’ll send men to capture them.”

“You shouldn’t think so lightly of it. I hear he is quite a formidable expert.”

Hogyeong made an expression as if he could not understand.

“Such a master impersonating our cult? That’s strange.”

“We’ll know once we capture and question him. But can your branch handle it?”

It was a clever way of speaking that prodded at Hogyeong’s pride. If you can’t handle it, we’ll solve it.

“We will take care of it.”

It was not because he had risen to the bait, but because it was the duty of the Jiangxi Branch Leader—himself—to resolve it. Only when the branch could not solve it should they ask the Demonic Sword Unit for help.

“Your subordinates could be in danger.”

This was the reason Jungak had visited personally today. He was worried that Hogyeong might not go himself and only send his subordinates to handle it.

“I will move out personally with my men.”

“Ah, if the Branch Leader moves personally, then I can rest assured.”

Jungak thought inwardly.

‘That’s your weakness. That empty pride.’

If it were him, he would have answered like this. Isn’t the Demonic Sword Unit here to handle such dangerous matters?

“Then I shall have him drawn out through my secret agent.”

“I’d be grateful if you did.”

As Jungak was about to leave, he added with a smile.

“Let’s have a drink once this matter is finished.”

“Very well.”

Hogyeong also saw Jungak off with a pleasant expression.

‘That drinking table will be your last.’

He had obtained information that Jungak was taking bribes. If it had been once or twice, he might have turned a blind eye. The Demonic Sword Unit was an important elite organization indispensable to the Jiangxi Branch.

But there were too many places making periodic tribute payments. And the amounts were too large as well. Exactly how far did he think they would turn a blind eye? It felt like this: I’ve committed corruption. So what? What are you going to do about it? You think you can cut me down?

‘I’ll be the one to sever your head.’

Jungak, turning to leave, was thinking the exact same thought. The only difference was whose neck it truly was.

* * *

“The cult has finally decided to move.”

Juyang flinched in surprise at Jeokpae’s words.

Geonmugeuk was going to die, so he should have felt relieved, but he did not.

“You bring him to the abandoned temple in the west tomorrow. Can you do it?”

“I can. But will I be all right?”

“I’ve put in a good word for you, so when you arrive, they’ll look after you.”

“Understood.”

“If you make a mistake, we all die. Keep that in mind!”

That night, Juyang had a dream.

It was a dream of lying on the sea, floating gently.

He was floating happily in the warm sunlight.

The moment he imagined Geonmugeuk being hacked apart by demonic practitioners, strength entered his body and he began to sink.

He floundered and fell from his bed, unable to fall back asleep until morning.

* * *

When Juyang went to find Geonmugeuk, he was meeting someone at the inn.

It was the first time he had seen Geonmugeuk with anyone at this inn, so Juyang observed them closely.

The other man handed Geonmugeuk what looked like a letter.

Geonmugeuk examined the contents carefully and said a few words to the man.

At times like this, he looked so serious that he truly seemed like the Young Cult Leader of the demonic cult.

The man who had handed over the letter left the inn.

Juyang, standing at the entrance, glanced at the man brushing past him.

He was an ordinary person commonly seen anywhere.

Juyang approached Geonmugeuk and asked.

“Who was that?”

“A man from the Tongcheongak.”

“Tongcheongak?”

“The Heavenly Demon Divine Cult’s greatest information organization.”

“You really start bragging the moment we meet again!”

Juyang shook his head, yet he couldn’t help but ask.

“So? What on earth were you talking about with that supposedly greatest intelligence organization?”

“I looked into the Jiangxi Branch. About the Branch Leader, and the Demonic Sword Unit Leader too.”

When the two were mentioned, Juyang flinched.

“If it’s real, let me see that letter too.”

Geonmugeuk extended the hand holding the letter.

The moment Juyang tried to take it.

Fwoosh.

Flames rose from Geonmugeuk’s hand and the letter burned up.

“If you saw our cult’s confidential information, you wouldn’t survive.”

Surprised, Juyang stared blankly at the paper turning to ash and flying away.

He had heard of it. That true masters burned paper like this with Samadhi True Fire.

“Do you believe my true identity now?”

He expected an affirmative answer.

“If you’re this strong, why are you crazy?”

Geonmugeuk laughed as he swirled the rising ashes this way and that.

“To make you believe, I’d have to line up demonic venerables behind me, wouldn’t I?”

Juyang stared at Geonmugeuk in silence.

‘There won’t be an opportunity for that. Because you’ll die today.’

Geonmugeuk also stared intently at Juyang and asked.

“You don’t seem to have slept well. Is something weighing on your mind?”

By now, Juyang was no longer surprised at Geonmugeuk guessing things like a ghost.

“There’s somewhere I need you to go with me today.”

“Let’s go.”

Geonmugeuk followed readily, and Juyang was the one thrown off.

“You say let’s go without even knowing where?”

“We’ll know when we get there.”

“What if I had laid a trap? Are you looking down on me? Are you saying my trap is nothing?”

“It’s not that. If you kill me, you can’t collect my interest, can you? You are a man who collects money, not one who takes lives, aren’t you?”

At the unexpected answer, Juyang shook his head.

“You really are impossible to talk to.”

Juyang walked ahead and Geonmugeuk followed behind him.

Juyang’s heart was complicated.

His relationship with this strange person would soon be over.

He would never see this madman again.

Juyang glanced back at Geonmugeuk.

Unaware he was going to his death, he followed leisurely, looking at the merchants.

“That clothing over there would suit you well. Shall I buy you a set?”

“Use that money to pay your interest properly. Damn it! Stop talking nonsense and just follow me.”

Why was he so irritated? The reason was simple. He didn’t want to kill Geonmugeuk.

What was so pitiful about that bastard smiling so foolishly!

Juyang suddenly thought of the coin.

The coin he had placed atop his palm.

Yes, it must be a feeling born from gratitude that he hadn’t ruined his hand.

When they left the main street and arrived at a crossroads, Juyang stopped and sat on a rock by the roadside.

“Let’s rest a moment.”

“You should do some physical training too. You just sit around counting money all day; no wonder your body is in such shape.”

Geonmugeuk sat down beside him.

After sitting in silence for a moment, Juyang abruptly asked.

“If you really are the Young Cult Leader of the demonic cult… what on earth are you doing here?”

After a brief pause, Geonmugeuk spoke.

“As you can see, I’m meeting people. I’m meeting a coachman who is trapped serving your kind for life because he borrowed ten nyang for his child’s treatment fees. I’m meeting you, who insert a straw into him and suck his blood. And I’m meeting a nameless hero who jumped over the wall to save him.”

As he listened to those words, Juyang’s heart began to tremble.

It wasn’t something that should make his heart tremble, but when he was with Geonmugeuk, he often experienced this.

“Why would a demonic practitioner try to straighten out Yeomwangchae? Even the orthodox practitioners are letting it be. You’re a demonic practitioner!”

“There may be those with the face of a man but the heart of a beast among the orthodox, and there may be demonic practitioners who can’t stand to see such sights. Isn’t that how it is? Besides, the Yeomwangchae matter isn’t something I’ll do—it’s something you’ll take care of from now on.”

“How could a guy like me do such a thing? As you said, I’ve lived my whole life sitting around counting money, enjoying the sight of others’ misfortune. How could I do something so grand?”

“It’s not grand. It’s normal.”

Geonmugeuk looked at him calmly.

“If you’ve lived a life like a pine caterpillar, now try living a soaring life too. Even if you can’t become a butterfly, couldn’t you become a moth?”

At that moment, Juyang wished that he really was the Young Cult Leader of the demonic cult.

So much so that even if that matter were a punishment given to him, he wanted to try living a new life.

It was a once-in-a-lifetime opportunity.

That made him even angrier.

Because he wasn’t the Young Cult Leader of the demonic cult.

Because the Young Cult Leader of the demonic cult wouldn’t be saying such things to a mere loan shark.

“I’ve had enough of your mad talk! Leave this place by this road!”

Juyang’s voice trembled.

“Leave by this road and never come back to Jiangxi Province again. If you come back, you’ll die.”

Juyang resolved to save this mad master.

Never once in his life had he lived for someone else.

It had been a life lived selfishly, only for himself.

He didn’t know why the target of this one-time favor had to be such a madman, but what could he do when his heart went out to him?

“There are people waiting to kill you. So go. I’ll say that you sensed it was a trap and ran far away. I don’t know if they’ll believe me.”

Had he ever taken such a risk for someone else before? Absolutely not.

“Why are you telling me this?”

“I suppose it’s because I’ve never seen someone as strangely mad as you.”

Then Geonmugeuk spoke as if he knew the reason.

“That’s not it.”

Geonmugeuk was looking into the deepest part of Juyang’s heart.

“It’s because you are truly a selfish yet strong survivor. Your instinct knows. That this is how you survive.”

“You really—! Even when I’m showing you kindness! The real demonic practitioners are waiting!”

“I know.”

“You know?”

“Didn’t you see me receiving that report earlier? I have full sight of the Jiangxi Branch’s current movements.”

Juyang threw his hands up.

“Right. This is what makes you a real madman. You’re perfect.”

“And if you go alone, you’ll die.”

Geonmugeuk stood up first and began to walk.

Those words somehow sent a chill down his spine, so Juyang silently followed him.

‘I don’t even know anymore.’

Even this much was an excessive favor.

Someday he would look back on today.

He would recall that that madman was the only person he had ever wanted to save in his life. The recollection would begin like this.

And so the two arrived at the temple, the promised place.

As they entered the temple’s front courtyard, dozens of Jiangxi Branch demonic practitioners who had been lying in ambush revealed themselves.

They drew their swords in unison and unleashed their demonic energy.

Branch Leader Hogyeong walked out from among the demonic practitioners.

“For what reason have you been impersonating a demonic practitioner?”

Hogyeong did not recognize Geonmugeuk.

They had met before, but it was when Geonmugeuk was young, a very long time ago.

Afterward, Hogyeong had traveled among various branches in the Central Plains, so he had no opportunity to see Geonmugeuk again.

“I have never impersonated anyone.”

Hogyeong’s expression hardened coldly.

“There is already a witness.”

Hogyeong looked at Juyang, who was with Geom Mugeuk, and said,

“Did that man impersonate our cult?”

At the icy question, Juyang was flustered. This was not the situation he had expected. Jeokpae had said so. He had said that once he arrived, he would take care of everything.

Neither that question nor the demonic energy flying toward him showed the slightest consideration for him. They simply saw him as an accomplice of someone impersonating a demonic practitioner.

‘Damn it!’

The target of his anger was Jeokpae. He had not looked after him. Perhaps, seeing him mingling too closely with this Geom Mugeuk, he had simply wished for them to die together.

“Stick close behind me. Wouldn’t it be unjust if you were struck by some stray blade?”

The only person here who was thinking of him was Geom Mugeuk.

“I asked whether the one who impersonated our cult was that man!”

When Hogyeong asked again, Juyang answered Geom Mugeuk instead.

“The demonic practitioner who colluded with the Tongjeonso is not that man.”

At those words, even Hogyeong grew tense as he waited for him to continue.

“The person who colluded with the Tongjeonso is none other than……”

It was precisely then.

Someone leaped over the roof and landed there.

It was none other than Jungak, the captain of the Demon Sword Unit. On the rooftops of the buildings and atop the walls on all sides, the martial artists of the Demon Sword Unit revealed themselves. Even Hogyeong was surprised by his sudden appearance, but Jungak, exuding cold demonic energy, focused on Juyang.

“Who is the demonic practitioner who colluded with the Tongjeonso?”

Juyang knew that the moment he revealed it was Jungak, he would die without question. No—even if he did not reveal it here, he had a premonition that he would end up dying in the end.

Just then, as he was gripped by terror, Geom Mugeuk took two coins from his bosom and held them out to him.

“Come to think of it, I haven’t paid today’s interest. Take it.”

The two coins were placed in Juyang’s palm.

Juyang silently looked down at them, then handed the coins back to Geom Mugeuk.

“The interest is too much. The interest I’ve already paid is more than enough to cover the principal.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at him and smiled brightly.

Juyang drew upon the courage he had carefully saved up all his life without ever using it, and even all the courage he would ever muster until the day he died.

“The one who took a bribe from Jeokpae, the head of the Tongjeonso, and promised to kill you is that man there—the captain of the Demon Sword Unit.”

Cold demonic energy burst from Jungak’s body. At the same time, the demonic practitioners of the Demon Sword Unit surrounding the place also released their demonic energy.

He was going to die anyway! Like a runaway carriage, Juyang shouted at the top of his lungs.

“You bastards! This man here is the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult! All of you, kneel and worship him!”

Ah, so I’m going to die as a madman!

Chapter 299: You Won’t Come and We Won’t Wait.
----------------------------------------------
Far from worship, laughter burst out.

The one laughing was Jungak. Even after being singled out as having taken bribes, he did not look flustered in the slightest.

“Don’t tell me you’ve been going around impersonating the Young Cult Leader?”

Even Hogyeong, who had been listening gravely, let out a hollow laugh. But Hogyeong had clearly heard what Juyang had said earlier.

—The one who took a bribe from Jeokpae and said he would kill you is that man, the Leader of the Demonic Sword Unit.

If those words were true, then this Juyang had now become someone he had to protect.

Hogyeong asked Jungak,

“What brings you here, Unit Leader?”

“Since the opponent is an expert, I thought you might need support.”

Hogyeong did not believe him. If that had truly been his intention, he would have made a show of offering to lead the Demonic Sword Unit from the start.

He had appeared the moment his own name was about to come out of Juyang’s mouth. It was obvious he had come to pressure him into keeping silent.

“As you heard, Unit Leader, that man has named you. So it would be best for you to withdraw for now.”

“Surely you do not believe his words?”

“Of course not. However, since he testified in front of everyone, we must at least conduct a formal investigation.”

“That man runs a Yeomwangchae operation.”

At that, Hogyeong asked Juyang,

“Is that true?”

Juyang shrank under the cold question.

“It is, but what I told you is the truth.”

Hogyeong did not trust those who ran Yeomwangchae.

Seeing his expression harden, Juyang realized anew just how extraordinary Geom Mugeuk was. Everyone frowned at him like that, everyone looked down on him, yet no one stepped forward to get rid of him.

Just then, Geom Mugeuk slipped into the conversation.

“When one cannot refute the contents, one attacks the messenger.”

At those words, Jungak’s eyes turned fierce.

Hogyeong placated Jungak.

“If it proves false, I will punish him severely for false accusation. Do not worry.”

Outwardly, Jungak looked composed.

But he was searching for any chance to eliminate Juyang. If they returned to the branch and began a proper interrogation, the fact that he had taken bribes would become public knowledge. If the other bribes were exposed as well, he would not only be forced to step down as unit leader, but be locked in the dungeon.

Jungak’s gaze turned toward Geom Mugeuk.

“And how will you deal with our Young Cult Leader?”

“I will handle him personally as well.”

“Would that not be treason?”

No one laughed at his joke.

In the midst of it all, Juyang looked at Geom Mugeuk. Even if everyone else mocked him, Juyang did not. He had already resolved himself for death anyway.

“Young Cult Leader, what do you intend to do now?”

Fine. Since he had started this, he would be the Young Cult Leader to the very end.

As if he had been waiting for that very moment, Jungak drew his blade.

“How dare you put the Young Cult Leader’s title in your mouth!”

Just as he was about to rush in and cut Juyang down on the spot,

Hogyeong stepped in front of him.

“Stop.”
